```markdown

Scope: <component/subsystem/files>

Constraints: <limits, assumptions, non-goals>

```

## Lesson: ncu-export-overwrite-pre-clean
Lesson ID: BL-20260528-ncu-export-overwrite-pre-clean
Scope: any orchestrator that drives NCU via `label_driver.sh` (or invokes `ncu --export <path>` directly) and re-runs against the same cell key (e.g., `tools/local_labels/orchestrate_sm86_label_recapture.py::recapture_cell`, `tools/local_labels/run_sm86_label_recapture_sweep.py`); future bulk-sweep drivers that recover from partial failures by re-running.
Problem Description: NCU's `--export <path>` refuses to overwrite an existing report file (NCU emits `==ERROR== File ... already exists. Use '-f' for overwriting` and returns non-zero). The first bulk-sweep invocation succeeds, but every subsequent re-run silently fails every cell with returncode-1 unless the operator manually deletes the stale `.ncu-rep` files. The cell-level orchestrator records `repeats_failed = n_repeats` but the typed_failure_reason can be empty stderr (the driver redirects NCU stdout to `/dev/null`). The sweep ledger then reports the entire sweep as `recapture_failed` with `typed_failure_reason=null` for every cell, masking the real cause.
Root Cause: NCU's CLI safeguards against accidental overwrite of report files; the driver wrapper does NOT pass `-f`/`--force` because the design contract is one-report-per-(repeat, launch) tuple. Without idempotent cleanup at the orchestrator layer, re-running the bulk sweep against an already-populated quarantine path silently fails every cell. The failure is invisible because NCU's overwrite-refusal error is on stderr which the driver redirects to its parser's input — the parser then sees an empty CSV and reports a generic non-zero exit.
Solution: At the orchestrator layer, before each repeat invocation, pre-clean the canonical `.ncu-rep` path: `<out-dir>/<arch>/<slug>/<shape>/run_<repeat>_<launch>.ncu-rep`. The Python pattern is `stale_report.unlink()` wrapped in `try/except OSError` so a race or permission issue does not crash the sweep. Apply at the per-repeat granularity so partial re-runs (one cell at a time) also benefit. Do NOT modify the driver to pass NCU `-f` because the overwrite refusal is a useful safety check for the operator-CLI surface; the bulk-orchestrator layer is the right place to make sweeps idempotent.
Constraints: The pre-clean only removes the canonical `.ncu-rep` path; companion files (`label_run_manifest_*_*.json`, `*.parser_diagnostics.json`, the label sidecar `label_*_*.json`) are written by the driver and overwritten naturally. The orchestrator layer is the right granularity; per-cell scripts that delegate to the orchestrator inherit the idempotency. A future change that moves NCU export to a different path convention must update this cleanup helper in lockstep. The cleanup is per-repeat, not per-cell, so a cell with `n_repeats=5` does 5 unlinks; this is intentional so retry-one-repeat workflows benefit.
Validation Evidence: Round 12 bulk sweep returned `recapture_failed: 36` (silently masked by stale `.ncu-rep` files). After adding the pre-clean in `recapture_cell()` and rebuilding the 36 CPU-incompatible binaries, Round 13's re-run returned `admitted: 44, repeats_partial: 0, recapture_failed: 0`. The aggregation pass admitted 20 cells (24 failed the per-cell stddev gate, which is the orthogonal `BL-20260526-typed-exclusion-stddev-threshold` path).
Source Rounds: round-12 (defect surfaced as silent 0/44 admission); round-13 (resolved via per-repeat pre-clean + bulk sweep yielded 44/44 sweep-level admission and 20/44 cohort-admitted post-aggregation).

## Lesson: ncu-csv-thousands-separator-strip
Lesson ID: BL-20260528-ncu-csv-thousands-separator-strip
Scope: any consumer of `ncu --import --page details --csv` output that parses numeric values via `float(value_str)` or `int(value_str)` (`tools/local_labels/parse_ncu_metrics.py`; future NCU consumers in the project).
Problem Description: NCU 2026.1.x CSV output formats raw integer counter values with locale-dependent thousands separators (e.g., `lts__t_sectors_lookup_hit.sum` rendered as `2,481.00`) regardless of the `--print-fp` flag. A parser that calls `float(value_str)` on those rows fails with `ValueError: invalid literal for float(): '2,481.00'`, which `tools/local_labels/parse_ncu_metrics.py` then surfaces as `ncu_csv_metric_non_numeric`. The `label_driver.sh` invocation returns non-zero and no label sidecar lands, blocking every live NCU re-capture invocation on cells whose counters exceed 1000 (effectively every non-trivial kernel).
Root Cause: NCU's `--print-fp` flag targets floating-point metric values only (per `references/NVIDIA_Manuals/ncu_guide/04_nsight-compute-cli.md`); it does NOT affect integer counter rows. Integer counters reaching ≥1000 in absolute value are rendered with US-locale thousands separators (`,`) followed by the `.00` floating-point trailing zeros that NCU appends to make the column look uniform. Python's `float()` treats `,` as a syntax error.
Solution: Strip `,` from every NCU CSV value before the numeric conversion: `float(value_str.replace(",", ""))`. The comma is purely cosmetic in NCU's CSV output and carries no semantic information. Apply the strip uniformly at every `float()` call site in the CSV-parsing path — both the auxiliary-counter loop (lookup-hit / lookup-miss sums) and the primary metric loop (`.pct` percentages, `.sum` counters). Do NOT widen `--print-fp` discipline or attempt to disable NCU locale formatting with `LC_ALL=C` — both are partial workarounds that miss other call sites and are NCU-version-dependent. The strip is a defensive one-line fix at the parser, not a configuration knob at the producer.
Constraints: The strip is locale-agnostic only when the input is US-format (comma as thousands separator, period as decimal). If a future NCU release switches to European format (`.` as thousands, `,` as decimal — e.g., `2.481,00`), the parser would silently miscompute. The strip should remain `,` -> empty until that locale shift is observed; at that point a more sophisticated locale detector (try-comma-strip-then-period-strip with sanity bound) would be needed. The lesson's scope is `ncu --csv` consumers; it does NOT apply to `ncu` non-CSV outputs (which use their own formatting).
Validation Evidence: Live smoke test on `bf16_hmma_gemm_traceable / trace_l2_accuracy_xl` (sm_86, RTX 3090 Ti, driver 595.71.05, CUDA 13.2, ncu 2026.1.1.0): pre-fix the driver returned non-zero with `parse_ncu_metrics: ncu_csv_metric_non_numeric: raw-counter metric 'lts__t_sectors_lookup_hit.sum' value '2,481.00' is not numeric`; post-fix the driver returned zero and `data/local_labels/sm_86/bf16_hmma_gemm_traceable/trace_l2_accuracy_xl/label_0_0.json` landed with `cache_hit_rate_l1tex_sector=0.5`, `cache_hit_rate_l2_sector=0.8476`, and `binary_sha256` matching the on-disk binary.
Source Rounds: round-10 of the sm_86 cohort regeneration loop (2026-05-28); discovered during the smoke test for the per-cell label re-capture orchestrator.

## Lesson: merge-with-prior-when-fresh-evidence-absent
Lesson ID: BL-20260528-merge-with-prior-when-fresh-evidence-absent
Scope: any regenerator that re-emits an artifact whose downstream consumers pin specific result_kinds (e.g., `tools/sm_86_predictor_accuracy_recovery_v2/compute_predicted_l1_per_admitted_cell.py`); future per-cell predictor/sidecar regenerators that may run before their upstream evidence inputs exist.
Problem Description: A regenerator that fail-closes to `typed_unavailable` when its evidence inputs are absent (classifier manifests, trace `.pb` files, etc.) silently regresses downstream consumers that pin specific `result_kind` values on the prior-committed artifact. Round 8 of the sm_86 cohort regeneration loop fixed an identity-binding drift by re-running `compute_predicted_l1_per_admitted_cell.py`; the regenerator emitted `typed_unavailable` for every cell (because no traces existed yet), dropping 9 previously-committed scalar rows. Five same-package consumer tests then broke: a carveout L1 budget-compliance test that explicitly required `result_kind=scalar` on `carveout_sensitivity_probe / trace_l2_accuracy_xl`, plus four task12 substitution-preflight tests that compared scalar rows from `predicted_l1_per_admitted_cell.json` against the diagnostic sidecar's cached scalar predictions.
Root Cause: A producer that re-emits a closed-set artifact multiple times over a lifetime where the upstream evidence churn rate exceeds the consumer artifact's regeneration cadence loses every previously-computed scalar value on each regen. The artifact remains schema-valid (all rows are well-formed `typed_unavailable` records) but the artifact graph silently regresses: dependent diagnostic / preflight / interim-report artifacts continue to cite the prior scalar values; the new artifact does not. The "restored from the diagnostic sidecar" pattern documented in the pre-regression artifact's per-cell `evidence.note` fields was a one-off manual fix from a prior loop; absent producer-side support, every future regen would reintroduce the same regression.
Solution: Extend the regenerator with a merge-with-prior pattern. Accept an optional `existing_artifact_path` argument (default to `args.out`'s path); load the existing artifact before emission; build a `(slug, shape_point) -> row` lookup. For each cell, compute fresh; when the fresh result is `typed_unavailable` AND the existing artifact has a `scalar`/`bounded` row for the same key, preserve the prior row's prediction fields (`predicted_hit_rate`, `result_kind`, `evidence`, `input_digests`, `typed_reason`) and stamp the current admitted identity (`binary_sha256`, `source_commit`, `kernel_signature_sha256`, `ncu_metric_version`, `arch`, `cache_layer`) onto the row. Mark every preserved row with `evidence.preserved_from_prior_artifact = True` so future audits trace the preservation lineage. The merged-row pattern generalizes the manual restoration into the producer.
Constraints: The merge is keyed on `(slug, shape_point)`; a future cohort change that drops or renames a cell key surfaces correctly as a missing row in the merged output (no silent preservation across renames). The audit marker is producer-side; downstream consumers that need to distinguish preserved-from-fresh rows should consult the marker rather than re-deriving the distinction from evidence shape. The merge does NOT apply when the existing artifact is absent or unreadable; the regenerator falls back to fresh-only emission (which is the producer-correct behavior when there is no prior baseline). Identity-binding tests that compare `cells[N]` to `label_admitted` continue to pass because every emitted row carries current identity regardless of whether the prediction fields came from fresh compute or preservation.
Validation Evidence: `tools/sm_86_predictor_accuracy_recovery_v2/compute_predicted_l1_per_admitted_cell.py::_preserve_prior_row_with_current_identity` + `_build_existing_lookup` + `_IDENTITY_FIELDS_STAMPED_ON_PRESERVED_ROW`. Round 9 regen restored the artifact to 9 scalar + 35 typed_unavailable (matching pre-Round-8 result_kind distribution) with current identity on every row. `pytest tools/sm_86_predictor_accuracy_recovery_v2 -q` reports 418 passed, 13 skipped, 0 failed (was 5 failed in Round 8); the Round 8 combined 10-test gate continues green at 171 passed, 711 subtests.
Source Rounds: round-8 (regression introduced); round-9 (merge-with-prior pattern landed).

## Lesson: portable-lcd-arch-gate-lcd-alias-expansion
Lesson ID: BL-20260528-portable-lcd-arch-gate-lcd-alias-expansion
Scope: any tool that parses `ARCH_GATE` from per-slug Makefiles under `workloads/portable_lcd/<slug>/Makefile` or compares the gate set against a target arch (e.g., `tools/local_labels/emit_sm86_cohort_preflight.py`, future arch-coverage scanners, future buildability preflights crossing portable_lcd cells).
Problem Description: A preflight emitter that parses `ARCH_GATE` from a slug's Makefile and tests whether `sm_86` (or any other arch) appears in the parsed token list misclassifies EVERY portable_lcd slug as `arch_gate_excludes_sm_86` because every portable_lcd Makefile declares `ARCH_GATE = lcd` (a single alias token), not a space-separated list of `sm_*` tokens. The misclassification routes 28 of 44 sm_86 cohort cells into a `TERMINAL_TYPED_FAILURE` action — the inverse of the truth — and would have escalated the loop to operator review under DEC-1's `admitted_sm86_count >= 8` floor.
Root Cause: The portable_lcd workload tree uses an alias token `lcd` in its Makefile gate (`workloads/portable_lcd/Makefile.template` line 14: `ARCH_GATE = lcd`). The alias expands inside `workloads/portable_lcd/common.mk` to the LCD base-arch triple `sm_86 sm_90 sm_120` (plus a compute_80 PTX fallback). A naive parser that reads only the literal tokens after `ARCH_GATE =` and matches against `sm_86` sees the single token `lcd` and concludes sm_86 is absent.
Solution: When parsing ARCH_GATE for any portable_lcd-owning slug, expand the literal token `lcd` to the tuple `("sm_86", "sm_90", "sm_120")` BEFORE comparing the gate set against a target arch. simbench_bw slugs continue to use literal arch tokens directly (e.g., `ARCH_GATE = sm_86 sm_90a sm_120`). The expansion is documented in `workloads/portable_lcd/Makefile` and `workloads/portable_lcd/common.mk`; the project source-of-truth is the Makefile.template. Architecture-conditional variants (`sm_90a`, `sm_120a`, `sm_120f`) are FORBIDDEN under the portable_lcd discipline and would never appear in a portable_lcd ARCH_GATE.
Constraints: The `lcd` alias is portable_lcd-tree-specific. simbench_bw slugs MAY decorate their ARCH_GATE with arch-conditional variants (e.g., `sm_90a`, `sm_120a`) — those tokens stay verbatim and the parser must NOT silently map them. If portable_lcd's base-arch set ever widens (e.g., adds a future `sm_140` base arch) the expansion tuple must update in lockstep; the regression test that catches misclassification is the per-workload preflight action-summary scanner (e.g., a portable_lcd cohort that lands `TERMINAL_TYPED_FAILURE` for every cell is a defect signal).
Validation Evidence: `tools/local_labels/emit_sm86_cohort_preflight.py::_LCD_ALIAS_EXPANSION` carries the expansion tuple. Without expansion: the preflight emits `TERMINAL_TYPED_FAILURE: 28` for the 44 sm_86 cohort cells (all 28 portable_lcd cells); with expansion: `TERMINAL_TYPED_FAILURE: 0`, `REBUILD_TRACE_LOCAL: 36`, `REBUILD_BINARY_LOCAL: 8`. The `python3 tools/local_labels/emit_sm86_cohort_preflight.py` invocation in Round 0 confirms the corrected counts before and after the fix.
Source Rounds: round-0 of the sm_86 cohort regeneration loop (2026-05-28); the defect surfaced during the preflight emitter's initial verification run and was fixed in the same round.

## Lesson: cohort-row-level-not-source-row-level
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-cohort-row-level-not-source-row-level
Scope: data/local_labels/sm120_cohort_expansion_roster.toml; tools/local_labels/sm120_cohort_roster_validator.py; future cohort plans that filter source rosters by `level`
Problem Description: A plan AC of the form "level == L2 for simbench_bw rows" reads ambiguously — does the level constraint apply to the COHORT ROW's level field or to the SOURCE ROSTER ROW's level field? Strict source-row reading combined with the BUILDABLE-ONLY constraint (no building new scaffolded simbench_bw L2 rows) yields an infeasible cohort: portable_lcd alone has 2 memory_stress slugs (8 candidates) and the 30-cell 10/10/10 family-bucket split needs 10. The loop would silently relax constraints or fail closure.
Root Cause: simbench_bw historically structures kernels under `l1/` and `l2/` directories matching the source roster's `level` field, but the cache_layer that a kernel MEASURES (the field joined on in `comparison_contract.toml`) is determined by the SHAPE_POINT chosen, not by the source row's `level`. Many simbench_bw L1 microbenches (dram_stream_stride_mix, l2_persisting_window_sweep, working_set_capacity_sweep, …) carry `trace_l2_accuracy_xl` shapes that produce L2-cache-layer cells AND are already buildable on disk.
Solution: Interpret `level == L2 for simbench_bw rows` as the COHORT-row level (the cohort IS L2), not the SOURCE-row level. The validator enforces cohort-row level == "L2" on every row; the inventory tool admits source level == "L1" rows whose shape catalog includes `trace_l2_accuracy_*`. Document the interpretation in the Plan Evolution Log and the round summary the round it lands. The buildability preflight verifies the binary is actually on disk (or makes it via `make`).
Constraints: The interpretation is bounded to source rows whose shape catalog (workloads/simbench_bw/shapes/<slug>.toml) declares a `trace_l2_accuracy_*` entry. Source rows without such a shape stay out of the cohort. Family-bucket mapping must be extended in lockstep (raw families `memory`, `cache`, `atomics`, `shared_mem` map to `memory_stress`). Per-arch behavior may differ (a future arch's cohort must re-justify this reading because the available L1 microbenches and their shape catalogs differ).
Validation Evidence: `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_cohort_roster_validator.py` 11 cases (test_ac2_positive_schema_and_diversity_admit covers the cohort including 6 simbench L1 rows in memory_stress with cohort-level=L2); `data/local_labels/sm120_cohort_buildability_witness.json` records buildable=40/40 for the admitted cohort.
Source Rounds: round-0 (2026-05-26)

## Lesson: prose-and-test-lockstep-on-cap-widening
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-prose-and-test-lockstep-on-cap-widening
Scope: load-bearing constants in `tools/cloud/trace_budgets.py` (and any analogous module with a published "cap history" prose); `tools/test_nvbit_slowdown_budget_prose_lockstep.py` and any analogous prose-lockstep guards; downstream test files that hardcode "above-cap" values relative to the cap.
Problem Description: A round raised the runtime constant `NVBIT_SLOWDOWN_BUDGET_MAX_RATIO` from 400.0 to 1200.0 + updated the verifier-side test file's hardcoded "above-cap" values. Codex Round 4 review flagged that two more surfaces remained stale: (a) the cap-history prose in `trace_budgets.py` still described the active cap as 400x in current-tense statements (lines 450, 459, 468 of the original module); (b) the dedicated prose-lockstep guard `tools/test_nvbit_slowdown_budget_prose_lockstep.py` still asserted `_runtime_cap() == 400.0` and kept `_PERF_COUNTER_FALLBACK_IMPLIED_SLOWDOWN = 500.0` (no longer above the new cap). Running the lockstep guard failed 2 tests; the verifier-side tests passed in isolation but the integrated test set was red.
Root Cause: A cap widening has THREE coupled surfaces, not two. The actor doing the raise tends to remember the constant + the verifier-side test (the obvious surfaces) and miss the cap-history prose + the dedicated lockstep guard. The lockstep guard exists precisely to catch this gap; missing its existence means the guard fires the next round.
Solution: Treat cap widenings as a three-surface refresh checklist: (1) the constant value; (2) the cap-history prose in the same module, where the new evolution lands as a new bullet and prior values are re-tagged as historical with explicit markers (e.g., "(historical)" + "post-round-N" suffix); (3) the dedicated prose-lockstep guard test + its internal self-binding sentinels (e.g., `_runtime_cap()` expectation, `_PERF_COUNTER_FALLBACK_IMPLIED_SLOWDOWN`, forbidden-token list catching stale current-tense prose). The historical-exempt marker list in the guard MUST be extended in the same round so prior-round prose references stay admitted.
Constraints: The cap-history paragraph format is established by prior rounds (round-9, round-25); new evolutions extend the format without breaking prior framings. The forbidden-token list in the lockstep guard catches stale "currently <old>" / "active <old>" prose; new entries must be specific enough to not catch historical references. Re-raising the cap in the future will require the same three-surface refresh.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/test_nvbit_slowdown_budget_prose_lockstep.py workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_trace_budget_c_ac_h_verifier.py -q` → 63 passed, 2 skipped (was 2 failed pre-Round-5). The full focused test suite (5 files) → 140 passed, 2 skipped, 4 subtests passed.
Source Rounds: round-4 (defect introduced), round-5 (resolved)

## Lesson: typed-exclusion-stddev-threshold
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-typed-exclusion-stddev-threshold
Scope: tools/local_labels/sweep_repeatability_matrix.py + tools/local_labels/repeatability_aggregator.py + cohort sweep discipline.
Problem Description: A sweep can complete cleanly (all NCU invocations succeed, all sidecars land) yet still produce zero admissions for a cell when the cell's L2-hit-rate stddev across the 5 repeats exceeds the contract's `stddev_threshold = 0.03` admission gate. This is a TYPED EXCLUSION, not a sweep bug. It surfaced in Round 4 on `dram_stream_stride_mix / trace_l2_accuracy_xl` (stddev 0.07-0.39 across all 6 (warmup, cache_control) configs).
Root Cause: Some kernels exhibit high inherent variance in L2 hit rate at the chosen shape. DRAM streaming kernels with stride-mix patterns are particularly volatile because the L2 working set depends on which strides happen to fall into the same set lines on each launch, which depends on the launch-time DRAM allocator behavior. The matrix axes (warmup_count, cache_control) cannot suppress this — the source of variance is the kernel/shape combination itself.
Solution: Treat the cell as a typed exclusion and substitute from the same-bucket replacement pool. The plan's AC-2 frozen roster reserves 10 replacement cells (4 memory_stress + 3 tensor_compute + 3 activation_norm) for exactly this case. Do NOT relax the stddev_threshold (the `0.03` is contract-pinned and a source-text scanner asserts the literal survives — see `tools/local_labels/SWEEP_RUNBOOK.md` "Critical Rules"). Do NOT admit a noisy cell.
Constraints: The substitution must come from the same family_bucket as the excluded cell (preserves the AC-2.2 family-bucket distribution). The matrix axes already explored in the failed sweep (`warmup_counts 0,1,5` × `cache_controls all,none`) should be the starting set for the substitute cell too; if the substitute also fails, escalate per the plan's Lower Bound discipline before relaxing.
Validation Evidence: Round 4 sweep at `data/local_labels/sweeps/sweep_20260526T104200Z_c31bd4171074/verdict-table.csv` shows the per-cell-per-config stddev values; `dram_stream_stride_mix` has 0 admitting configs while `carveout_sensitivity_probe` (5/6), `l2_persisting_window_sweep` (2/6), `l2_prefetch_hint_sweep` (6/6), `working_set_capacity_sweep` (2/6) all admit. The merge tool admitted 4 cells and rejected the noisy one; the cohort progressed 5 → 9 sm_120 cells.
Source Rounds: round-4 (typed exclusion observed; substitution queued for round-5)

## Lesson: nvbit-slowdown-budget-is-tunable
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-nvbit-slowdown-budget-is-tunable
Scope: tools/cloud/trace_budgets.py NVBIT_SLOWDOWN_BUDGET_MAX_RATIO; workloads/simbench_bw/run_suite.py --mode trace; workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_trace_budget_c_ac_h_verifier.py
Problem Description: When generating predictor traces via `run_suite.py --mode trace`, the NVBit tracer can exceed the slowdown budget for cells whose baseline wall-clock is small and whose instruction trace is large. Round 4 hit this with `l2_prefetch_hint_sweep / trace_l2_accuracy_xl` at `actual_slowdown_ratio = 1010.85x` against the original 400x cap, producing a typed `nvbit_slowdown_budget_exceeded.json` sidecar and no trace JSON. Without a trace JSON the downstream sweep `--predictor-trace-jsons` requirement cannot be satisfied.
Root Cause: The budget cap is a hard-coded constant `NVBIT_SLOWDOWN_BUDGET_MAX_RATIO: float = 400.0` in `tools/cloud/trace_budgets.py`. It was originally 200x and was widened to 400x at Round 25 of a prior loop. Some Blackwell sm_120 kernel + shape combinations exceed even 400x because the per-instruction NVBit overhead scales with the kernel's instruction count, and trace_l2_accuracy shapes intentionally pack large access counts into small thread counts to magnify L2 evictions.
Solution: The constant is OPERATOR-TUNABLE. Round 4 raised it from 400.0 to 1200.0 with operator confirmation. The change requires lockstep updates in `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_trace_budget_c_ac_h_verifier.py` because that test file pins specific "above-cap" actual_slowdown_ratio values (e.g., 500x against the 400x cap, updated to 1500x against the 1200x cap). The cap should NOT be raised silently and should NOT be removed entirely; the safety mechanism catches truly runaway traces (the runbook discusses the `NVBIT_SLOWDOWN_BUDGET_MAX_RATIO` pattern as a guardrail). Document the raise as a Plan Evolution Log entry on the loop's goal-tracker so downstream rounds know the new ceiling.
Constraints: When raising, choose a ceiling that gives a finite headroom over the observed peak (Round 4 chose ~19% over 1010.85x = 1200x). Re-raising should require a fresh operator confirmation, not silent inflation. The test file's "above-cap" assertions must update in lockstep; otherwise the trace-budget test suite breaks.
Validation Evidence: `tools/cloud/trace_budgets.py:445` constant change; `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_trace_budget_c_ac_h_verifier.py` 46 pytest cases pass after the 500 → 1500 substitution; `l2_prefetch_hint_sweep` trace regenerated cleanly post-raise (at `actual_slowdown_ratio ≈ 1011` < 1200 cap) and admitted in the Round 4 sweep.
Source Rounds: round-4

## Lesson: stable-session-anchor-name-not-mtime
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-stable-session-anchor-name-not-mtime
Scope: RLCR loop harness time-anchor patterns; tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py; any future tool that needs to distinguish "produced during this loop" vs "produced before this loop".
Problem Description: A tool used the session directory mtime (`.humanize/rlcr/<session>/`.stat().st_mtime`) as a cutoff for "did this artifact land during this loop?" The cutoff worked at first measurement but failed silently as the loop progressed: every time `setup-rlcr-loop.sh`, the harness, or the agent wrote a new round file into the session dir (round-N-prompt.md, round-N-contract.md, round-N-summary.md, round-N-review-prompt.md, etc.), the directory mtime advanced. An active comparison bundle emitted EARLIER in the same loop than the latest round file was then OLDER than the directory mtime and got filtered out, leaving the strict guard with nothing to scan. Codex Round 1 review caught this in the sm_120 L2 cohort loop's claim guard.
Root Cause: Filesystem directory mtimes record the most recent metadata change of any direct child of the directory, not the directory's creation time. They are mutable; they advance every time a file is added, removed, or has its metadata changed. They are useless as a stable "session-start" anchor.
Solution: Derive the session-start cutoff from the session directory NAME, which `setup-rlcr-loop.sh` sets at session creation and never modifies. The receiving session directory carries an ISO-like timestamp (`YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS`). Parse it as a NAIVE datetime (no `tzinfo`) and call `.timestamp()` so the conversion uses the host's local timezone — matching the `date +%Y-%m-%d_%H-%M-%S` semantics that `setup-rlcr-loop.sh` uses to write the name. Treating the parsed datetime as UTC instead (an earlier bug fixed in Round 3) shifts the cutoff by the host's UTC offset and silently excludes legitimately-active artifacts on any non-UTC host (e.g. Asia/Singapore +0800 produces an 8-hour shift). Fall back to the directory mtime ONLY when the name fails to parse so older session-naming schemes still get a cutoff (just not a stable one).
Constraints: The session directory name format is controlled by `setup-rlcr-loop.sh`; if the format ever changes, the parser needs to land in lockstep. The fallback to directory mtime preserves backward compatibility but degrades to the broken behavior; the parser should be regression-tested. Local-time parsing assumes the receiving host has the same local timezone as the originating host; cross-host pivots that preserve the same session directory (rather than creating a fresh one) would produce a wrong cutoff. CLAUDE.md's cross-host survival semantics require a fresh `.humanize/rlcr/<new-session-id>/` on the receiving host, which sidesteps this caveat. The lesson is RLCR-harness-general — any tool that distinguishes "active" vs "historical" artifacts should NOT use directory mtime as the anchor.
Validation Evidence: `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` six cases covering this lesson: the four original cases (test_session_start_epoch_parses_session_directory_name; test_session_start_epoch_falls_back_to_dir_mtime_when_name_invalid; test_default_discovery_includes_bundle_older_than_dir_mtime_but_newer_than_session_start; test_default_discovery_excludes_historical_bundle_older_than_session_start) plus two Round 3 timezone-regression cases (test_session_start_epoch_respects_local_tz_not_utc; test_active_bundle_with_local_mtime_after_session_start_is_included_under_asia_singapore_tz). The Round 3 cases manipulate `TZ` directly and assert that local-time parsing produces a cutoff that matches the originating host's clock.
Source Rounds: round-1 (defect introduced), round-2 (partial resolution with UTC-parsed name), round-3 (timezone-aware resolution)

## Lesson: predicted-bytes-validator-checks-every-shape-in-slug
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-predicted-bytes-validator-checks-every-slug-shape
Scope: `workloads/simbench_bw/trace_size_estimator.py::validate_slug`; `workloads/simbench_bw/run_suite.py::handle_trace`; per-slug shape TOMLs at `workloads/simbench_bw/shapes/<slug>.toml`.
Problem Description: When trying to run `run_suite.py --mode trace <slug> --shape-point <target_shape>`, the trace generation can fail with `trace_prediction_stale` even when the TARGET shape's `predicted_bytes` is correct, because the validator iterates EVERY shape in the slug's TOML and returns ALL stale shapes. Round 5 reported "warm_cold_pair_relaunch / trace_l2_accuracy_xl is blocked by predicted_bytes mismatch" but the actual stale shape was `trace_00` (predicted_bytes=40960 against estimator=1310720); trace_l2_accuracy_xl was already correct at 655360. The misattribution wasted operator time on the wrong shape.
Root Cause: `validate_slug` walks `shape.get("trace_shapes", [])` and tolerates ±10% drift per shape; any shape outside that band fails. `handle_trace` collects all stale errors and returns EXIT_UNSUPPORTED on the slug as a whole, not on the target shape. The error message includes the shape name, but a casual reader can miss it when the cell tracker names only the target shape.
Solution: When `run_suite.py --mode trace` fails with `trace_prediction_stale`, read the stderr CAREFULLY for the specific `<slug>/<shape_name>` token in the error — it may name a sibling shape, not the target. Fix every stale shape in the TOML (not just the target). The R49 axis-product fix (excluding `_bytes` and `carveout_percent` / `prefetch_distance` axes from the multiplicative product) changed the estimator output for many slugs; shapes added before that fix can have stale predicted_bytes that only surfaces when validate_slug runs against the current estimator. To rediagnose: `python3 -c 'import importlib.util, sys; sys.path.insert(0, "workloads/simbench_bw"); spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("est", "workloads/simbench_bw/trace_size_estimator.py"); est = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(est); print(est.validate_slug("<slug>"))'`.
Constraints: The ±10% tolerance is the canonical drift threshold; widening it is a contract change. The fix is to update the stale `predicted_bytes` value in the TOML, not to relax the validator. The static estimator's formula is deterministic given the shape's axes + launch_config, so the new value is reproducible (no operator pinning a magic number).
Validation Evidence: Round 6 fix: `workloads/simbench_bw/shapes/warm_cold_pair_relaunch.toml` `trace_00.predicted_bytes` updated 40960 → 1310720; `validate_slug("warm_cold_pair_relaunch")` returns `[]` post-fix; `run_suite.py --mode trace --kernel warm_cold_pair_relaunch --shape-point trace_l2_accuracy_xl` succeeds and emits the trace JSON + measurement_manifest under `workloads/simbench_bw/results/warm_cold_pair_relaunch/rtx_5060_laptop/sm_120/`. Subsequent NCU sweep admits the cell at warmup=0, cache_control=none, stddev_l2_sector=0.018379.
Source Rounds: round-5 (initial mis-diagnosis), round-6 (resolved)

## Lesson: deferral-guards-from-prior-loops-must-reconcile-when-active-loop-changes
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-deferral-guards-from-prior-loops-must-reconcile
Scope: in-loop deferral guards under `tools/*/test_*_deferral_guard.py`; multi-loop RLCR sequencing on a project; any test that asserts "this file is not modified between round-start commit X and HEAD."
Problem Description: A prior RLCR loop landed a deferral guard at `tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator_sm120_deferral_guard.py` that pinned a specific round-start commit (`2b77180e3e32...` of the Cross-Arch Bounded First sm_120 Label Admission Loop) and asserted `tools/local_labels/activation_trace_orchestrator.py` does NOT appear in `git diff` between that commit and HEAD. The next loop's plan (sm120_l2_observability_30_cell_cohort) explicitly REQUIRED modifying that file to add sm_120 host support. The deferral guard from the prior loop fired as a red test in the current loop because the round-start commit was still reachable in history.
Root Cause: A deferral guard authored under loop A pins loop A's round-start commit. Loop B running on the same branch sees loop A's commit and treats the guard as still active. The guard has no mechanism to know loop A has closed and loop B's plan has explicitly admitted the previously-deferred work. Resolution requires manual reconciliation: the guard's author intended to LIFT it when the follow-on plan landed, but the lift was missed when loop B's plan was generated.
Solution: When a follow-on plan ADMITS work that was deferred by a prior loop's guard, the round that lands the admitted work MUST replace or remove the deferral guard. Don't just modify the deferred file and leave the guard red — that turns the guard into a permanent red signal. Two options: (a) DELETE the guard file outright; (b) REWRITE the guard file as positive coverage of the previously-deferred behavior (preferred, because it preserves the same import surface for adjacent test runners and documents WHAT the deferral was about). The replacement test file should explain in its docstring that the prior loop's deferral has been formally lifted by the current loop's Plan Evolution Log entry, and cite both the prior loop's round-start commit and the current loop's plan path.
Constraints: The replacement must keep the same path/filename so that any external test runner config (CI, pytest collections) continues to find it. Tests in the replacement file should EXERCISE the previously-deferred behavior so a regression that re-introduces the deferral (e.g., a developer rolls back the orchestrator change) is caught. The git-history invariant test that pinned the prior round-start commit can be deleted; the structural invariants (module imports, constants present) should be REPLACED by positive tests for the new behavior (e.g., `_RTX_5060_LAPTOP_PROPS` constant present + correct values + reachable via dispatch table).
Validation Evidence: Round 6 fix: `tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator_sm120_deferral_guard.py` rewritten from 173 lines (negative guard) to 311 lines (5 positive test classes, 14 cases) covering structural invariants, device-property values, CLI surface, output-path discipline, and manifest payload fields. Pre-fix `pytest tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator.py tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator_sm120_deferral_guard.py` failed 1 test; post-fix passes 25 tests.
Source Rounds: round-5 (defect introduced when modifying deferred file), round-6 (resolved)

## Lesson: roster-aware-path-resolver-for-cross-workload-cells
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-roster-aware-path-resolver-cross-workload
Scope: `tools/local_labels/roster_path_resolver.py`; `tools/local_labels/activation_trace_orchestrator.py`; `tools/local_labels/stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py`; the frozen `data/local_labels/sm120_cohort_expansion_roster.toml`'s `trace_json_path` field; any future producer/staging surface that consumes cohort rows.
Problem Description: The sm_120 cohort roster mixes two `roster_source` values: `portable_lcd` rows whose cells live under `workloads/portable_lcd/results/<slug>/<sku>/<arch_dir>/` and `simbench_bw` rows whose cells live under `workloads/simbench_bw/results/<slug>/<sku>/<arch_dir>/`. The pre-Round-7 producer (`activation_trace_orchestrator.py`) and staging tool (`stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py`) both hardcoded the simbench_bw prefix. portable_lcd cells were silently routed to the simbench_bw tree, leaving the frozen `trace_json_path` field unhonored and the sweep harness reading whichever path it found first. Codex Round 6 review caught the miss-routing.
Root Cause: Two separate workloads exposed similar producer-side trace mechanisms (simbench_bw's `run_suite.py --mode trace` for L1 microbenches; portable_lcd's `activation_trace_orchestrator.py` for activation/tensor/loss kernels). Each producer was authored against its own canonical tree and the staging tool was authored before portable_lcd cells joined the cohort. The frozen roster's `trace_json_path` is a candidate-contract field meant to pin the on-disk path, but no shared resolver enforced it; both tools constructed their paths independently.
Solution: Introduce a single roster-aware path resolver module (`tools/local_labels/roster_path_resolver.py::resolve_cell_root`) that loads the frozen roster TOML, finds the matching `(slug, shape_point)` row, and dispatches on `roster_source`. portable_lcd rows map to `workloads/portable_lcd/results/`; simbench_bw rows map to `workloads/simbench_bw/results/`. Both producer and staging tools import the SAME helper; a regression that re-hardcodes either prefix is caught by source-pin tests (`"resolve_cell_root("` substring in source) plus end-to-end resolver checks that probe a known portable_lcd cell + a known simbench_bw cell. The fallback parameter (`fallback_source = "simbench_bw"`) preserves the legacy sm_86 / 3090 Ti emission path for cells not in the active loop's roster.
Constraints: The resolver loads the frozen roster TOML on every call (cheap; ~40 rows). For a future ten-thousand-cell cohort this would need caching but is fine here. The `(slug, shape_point)` key must be unique; if the same slug appears twice with the same shape_point in different roster sources, the resolver returns the FIRST match (a TOML order accident). The plan body's frozen-roster discipline prevents this.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator.py tools/local_labels/test_activation_trace_orchestrator_sm120_deferral_guard.py tools/local_labels/test_stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py -q` → 36 passed (5 new orchestrator path tests + 6 new staging tests). Round 7 live verification: `cross_entropy_fwd_fp32_sm80 / trace_l2_accuracy_xl` (portable_lcd) emits traces to `workloads/portable_lcd/results/...` and `warm_cold_pair_relaunch / trace_l2_accuracy_xl` (simbench_bw) emits to `workloads/simbench_bw/results/...`; both traces sweep-admit.
Source Rounds: round-7 (introduced + verified). Closes Codex Round 6 Blocking Side Issue #1.

## Lesson: staging-tool-must-preserve-rich-producer-manifest
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-staging-tool-preserves-rich-manifest
Scope: `tools/local_labels/stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py`; the schema-v2 `measurement_manifest_<shape>.json` contract; any future "stage operator manifest" tool that runs AFTER a producer that already wrote a richer artifact.
Problem Description: Round 6 added a per-shape staging step that wrote a 4-field manifest (`schema_version`, `slug`, `shape_point`, `binary_sha256`, `kernel_signature_sha256`) at the same path the orchestrator had ALREADY written a rich manifest (with `driver_version`, `gpu_sku`, `cuda_version`, `gpu_uuid`, device props, compiler flags). The staging tool's `write_text` overwrote the rich manifest with the stub. The sweep harness then read the stub; downstream consumers saw missing device fields. Codex Round 6 review caught the regression specifically: the on-disk manifest after staging carried `driver_version = "13.2"` (a CUDA toolkit value misrouted into the NVIDIA driver slot) OR no driver field at all.
Root Cause: A staging step authored on the assumption that NO prior artifact exists at the target path is asymmetric with respect to the producer pipeline. The 4-field stub was the MINIMUM required by the sweep harness's `--measurement-manifests` contract, but the orchestrator was already emitting the MAXIMUM (full schema-v2). The staging tool did not check for an existing rich manifest before overwriting.
Solution: Three-branch dispatch in the staging tool: (1) if no manifest exists at the target path, BUILD a full schema-v2 payload from the trace JSON + the SKU device-property record (defined in the staging module to avoid a cross-module dependency on the orchestrator's constants); (2) if a RICH manifest exists (detected by presence of `cuda_version`, `driver_version`, `gpu_sku`, `device_name` — the orchestrator's signature device fields), PRESERVE the rich payload and merge only join-key fields from the trace; (3) if a PRE-Round-7 4-field STUB exists, treat it like case (1) and upgrade it in place. A new end-to-end regression test (`StageManifestEndToEndOnDiskFieldsTest`) runs `main()` on a stub fixture and asserts the final on-disk manifest has `driver_version = "595.71.05"`, `gpu_sku`, compiler_flags, and the device props.
Constraints: The rich-manifest detector uses presence of the four device fields as the signature; a future regression that drops `cuda_version` from the orchestrator manifest would route through the "build full" branch instead of "preserve" — that's acceptable because the build branch reconstructs the same fields from the SKU table. A future loop that REMOVES `driver_version` from the schema would need to update both the detector and the orchestrator. The SKU device-property table is duplicated between the orchestrator and the staging tool; that duplication is intentional (each tool is independently usable; consolidating into a shared module would couple their release cycles).
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py -v` → 6 passed (build-from-absent, preserve-rich, upgrade-pre-Round-7-stub, source-references-resolver, full-build-for-3090-ti, end-to-end-on-disk). Round 7 live verification: re-staging `warm_cold_pair_relaunch/trace_l2_accuracy_xl` upgraded its pre-Round-7 stub at `workloads/simbench_bw/results/.../measurement_manifest_trace_l2_accuracy_xl.json` to a full schema-v2 manifest with `driver_version = "595.71.05"`.
Source Rounds: round-7 (introduced + verified). Closes Codex Round 6 Blocking Side Issue #2.

## Lesson: schema-additionalproperties-false-requires-lockstep-extension
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-schema-additionalproperties-false-requires-lockstep
Scope: `workloads/simbench_bw/schemas/measurement_manifest.schema.json`; any JSON schema with `additionalProperties: false`; producer-side tools that emit fields beyond the canonical schema (`tools/local_labels/activation_trace_orchestrator.py`, `tools/local_labels/stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py`).
Problem Description: Round 7 added `kernel_signature_sha256` to the staged `measurement_manifest_<shape>.json` payload (per Codex Round 6's "preserve rich manifest" requirement) but did NOT update the canonical schema. `additionalProperties: false` then rejected the field at the canonical-validator layer even though the operator-side `validate_measurement_manifest.py` admitted it via its own allowlist. The two validation surfaces drifted: producer-side OK, canonical-schema FAIL. Codex Round 7 review caught the conflict.
Root Cause: A schema with `additionalProperties: false` is a closed-world contract — any new field MUST be declared in the schema before the producer can safely emit it. Producer-side tools that adopt new fields without lockstep schema updates create a silent validation split: the operator-side tool admits the manifest, but any canonical-schema check rejects it. The pre-Round-7 state had this latent: the orchestrator's `_build_activation_manifest_payload` carried a comment block (lines 473-485) explaining that `kernel_signature_sha256` was DELIBERATELY excluded because the schema had `additionalProperties: false`. Round 7's "preserve rich manifest" requirement then added the field via the staging tool without updating either the schema or that comment.
Solution: Treat any `additionalProperties: false` schema as a three-surface change checklist: (1) the canonical schema, (2) the producer-side tool that emits the field, (3) the operator-side validator that admits it. New OPTIONAL fields (not in `required`) preserve backwards-compat with historical artifacts; do not promote to required unless every legacy artifact carries the field. The pattern: declare the property in the schema with its pattern/type/description; update the producer to actually emit the field (not just discard it); update any orchestrator comments that referenced the "schema-forbidden" status; add jsonschema validation tests for every producer-side code path (e.g., the three staging branches).
Constraints: Optional fields must not appear in the schema's `required` list. The OPTIONAL status preserves backwards-compat with historical run_suite.py timing/correctness manifests that don't emit the field. Future fields added to the schema must follow the same pattern; adding a new REQUIRED field would break every historical artifact and is not a backwards-compatible change.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py -q` → 9 passed (was 6 pre-Round-8; +3 jsonschema branch tests). Direct canonical validation: `jsonschema.validate(instance=manifest, schema=manifest_schema)` passes on all Round 7 + Round 8 staged manifests. Operator-side `validate_measurement_manifest.py` still passes on the same files (1 manifest valid). Both surfaces now agree.
Source Rounds: round-7 (regression introduced), round-8 (resolved). Closes Codex Round 7 Blocking Side Issue #1.

## Lesson: cohort-scanner-test-prevents-uneven-evidence-upgrade
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-cohort-scanner-test-prevents-uneven-upgrade
Scope: `tools/local_labels/test_sm120_admitted_manifest_scanner.py`; the post-loop evidence-hygiene pattern; AC-3 / AC-4 cohort-level provenance.
Problem Description: Round 7 fixed the warm_cold_pair_relaunch cell-level manifest's `driver_version` from "13.2" to "595.71.05", but did NOT re-stage the 4 Round-4-admitted simbench sm_120 cells (carveout_sensitivity_probe, l2_persisting_window_sweep, l2_prefetch_hint_sweep, working_set_capacity_sweep). Their pre-Round-7 5-field per-shape stubs and `driver_version="13.2"` cell-level manifests stayed stale on disk even though the cells were ADMITTED in the cohort. Codex Round 7 review caught the per-cell omission; without a cohort-wide check, similar uneven upgrades would land silently in future rounds.
Root Cause: A round that fixes a producer-side bug touches the NEW outputs the producer makes (the round's own batch). It does NOT automatically touch artifacts the producer made in PRIOR rounds. The asymmetry is invisible without a cohort-level scanner: every cell-level check that runs on the round's-new files passes, but the prior-round files are still wrong. The label_admitted.json append is byte-equivalent so the cohort-membership invariant is preserved; the on-disk-manifest invariant is NOT.
Solution: Add a per-cohort scanner test that walks every admitted `arch == "sm_120"` row in `label_admitted.json`, resolves the per-shape AND cell-level manifest paths via the roster-aware resolver, and asserts: (a) per-shape manifest carries the full canonical device-field set; (b) `driver_version` is the NVIDIA driver value (`595.71.05`), not the CUDA Driver API value (`13.2`); (c) cell-level manifest (when present) has the same `driver_version` discipline; (d) per-shape manifest validates against the canonical jsonschema. The scanner runs at HEAD time so any cohort-level drift fails fast on the next round's test suite, not at the AC-4/AC-5 closure when re-running would require a full sweep redo.
Constraints: The scanner skips rows whose per-shape manifest is absent (legitimate microbench cells use the cell-level manifest layout). The scanner does NOT validate label_admitted.json schema (`merge_admitted_cells.py` does that). The scanner is cheap (~30 file reads + jsonschema validations) so it can run every round. Future cohort columns (AC-4 predictor bundle, AC-5 headline bundle) would add their own scanner tests on the same pattern.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_sm120_admitted_manifest_scanner.py -v` → 5 passed (test_at_least_one_sm120_cell_admitted, test_per_shape_manifest_has_full_device_fields, test_per_shape_manifest_driver_version_is_nvidia_driver, test_cell_level_manifest_driver_version_is_nvidia_driver, test_per_shape_manifest_validates_against_canonical_schema). Verified Round 4 simbench manifests after re-staging: all 4 cells now have full device fields and `driver_version = "595.71.05"`; canonical jsonschema validates each.
Source Rounds: round-8 (introduced + verified). Closes Codex Round 7 Blocking Side Issue #2.

## Lesson: cohort-scanner-must-fail-on-missing-not-skip
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-cohort-scanner-fail-on-missing-not-skip
Scope: `tools/local_labels/test_sm120_admitted_manifest_scanner.py`; any future cohort-wide scanner that walks admitted rows and checks per-cell artifacts.
Problem Description: Round 8's cohort manifest scanner walked every admitted sm_120 row but had a permissive `if not mpath.is_file(): continue` skip when the per-shape manifest was absent. The skip was justified in-comment as "Per-shape manifest may legitimately be missing for historical microbench cells whose runs predate the per-shape-naming convention" — but this rationale was wrong for THIS loop: every admitted sm_120 row must have a roster-resolved per-shape manifest because future AC-4 predictor-bundle production depends on the file existing at that exact path. The skip let 12 admitted cells pass the scanner even though their manifests were missing. Codex Round 8 review caught the hole.
Root Cause: A scanner that skips on missing artifacts is a "best-effort hygiene" scanner, not a contract enforcer. The scanner's purpose IS to enforce a contract; skipping defeats the purpose. The mistake was confusing "historical optional layouts" (the cell-level `measurement_manifest.json` for microbench cells) with "required per-shape manifests for the active cohort". The cell-level manifest IS optional; the per-shape manifest at the roster-resolved path is NOT.
Solution: Split the scanner into two tests with different fail semantics: (1) an EXISTENCE test that HARD-FAILS on any missing per-shape manifest (`test_per_shape_manifest_exists_for_every_admitted_cell`); (2) field-completeness / driver-version / canonical-schema tests that focus on shape and DOCUMENT (in code comments) that the existence test owns the missing-file failure path. The field-completeness tests retain their `if not mpath.is_file(): continue` because the existence test already covers that case — splitting role-clearly keeps each test's failure message specific to its concern (missing vs. malformed vs. wrong driver). The split also makes the contract auditable in source: one test name says "exists for every admitted cell"; another says "has full device fields". Future readers see the contract directly.
Constraints: The cell-level `measurement_manifest.json` check stays permissive (legitimate microbench cells use that layout). Only the per-shape check is hard-failing. Future cohort columns (AC-4 predictor bundle, AC-5 headline bundle) should follow the same pattern: one explicit existence test per artifact class, with hard-fail semantics.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_sm120_admitted_manifest_scanner.py -v` → 6 passed (was 5 pre-Round-9; +1 existence test). Direct walk over all 23 admitted sm_120 cells: 23 checked, 0 missing, 0 schema failures (was 23 checked, 12 missing, 0 schema failures pre-Round-9). The 12 missing manifests were regenerated via the Round-7-widened orchestrator + Round-8 staging tool; the regenerated binary_sha256 values match `label_admitted.json` (binaries unchanged on disk).
Source Rounds: round-8 (defect introduced), round-9 (resolved). Closes Codex Round 8 Blocking Side Issue #1.

## Lesson: same-family-bucket-replacement-rule-on-typed-exclusion
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-same-bucket-replacement-rule-on-typed-exclusion
Scope: typed-exclusion replacement-pool discipline; `data/local_labels/sm120_cohort_expansion_roster.toml` replacement rows (orders 31-40); AC-2.2 family-bucket diversity invariant.
Problem Description: When a primary cohort cell is typed-excluded (stddev > 0.03 across all sweep configs), the replacement-pool substitution MUST come from the same family_bucket. Round 9's tracker entry briefly allowed an activation_norm substitute for the order-17 tensor_compute typed exclusion (the flash_attn_bwd_bf16_sm80 cell), which would have silently broken AC-2.2's bucket-floor invariant (≥6 cells per memory_stress / tensor_compute / activation_norm). Codex Round 9 Goal Alignment Check caught the drift in the goal-tracker prose during review.
Root Cause: Under time pressure when a replacement candidate (order 36 flash_attn_fwd_bf16_sm80, also tensor_compute) ALSO typed-excludes, it's tempting to pull from a different bucket "to keep moving." The replacement-pool design — 4 memory_stress + 3 tensor_compute + 3 activation_norm — assumes substitutions stay in-bucket; cross-bucket substitution silently changes the cohort's family-bucket distribution and breaks the AC-2.2 diversity floor.
Solution: Replacement-pool substitution is STRICTLY same-family-bucket. For tensor_compute exclusions: orders 34 (gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80/trace_l2_accuracy_lg), 35 (gemm_hmma_fp16_sm80/trace_l2_accuracy_lg), and 36 (flash_attn_fwd_bf16_sm80/trace_l2_accuracy_xl) are the three tensor_compute candidates. If multiple from a SINGLE family (e.g., flash_attn) both typed-exclude, EXHAUST the family at the failed bucket before considering any other bucket. Round 10 followed this discipline: order-17 (flash_attn_bwd timing_shape) excluded; order-36 (flash_attn_fwd trace_l2_accuracy_xl) replacement also excluded; order-34 (gemm_hmma_bf16 trace_l2_accuracy_lg, non-flash_attn tensor_compute) selected per Codex Round 9 guidance and admitted cleanly with stddev=0.008936.
Constraints: The 35% bucket cap (AC-2.2) must still hold post-substitution. If a bucket exhausts its 3 replacement candidates, the cohort cannot close without expanding the replacement pool (a future-round plan change). The roster is frozen; substitution operates within the pinned set.
Validation Evidence: Round 10 sweep at `data/local_labels/sweeps/sweep_20260526T144351Z_7507c7b35bb4/` admitted gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80/trace_l2_accuracy_lg (order 34, tensor_compute) at warmup=0, cache_control=all with stddev_l2_sector=0.008936. Cohort family-bucket distribution post-AC-3.1-close: memory_stress=10, tensor_compute=10, activation_norm=10 (the original 30-cell primary split preserved despite the order-17 tensor_compute substitution).
Source Rounds: round-9 (review caught tracker drift), round-10 (rule applied and verified).

## Lesson: ac3-validator-mirrors-contract-not-just-schema-shape
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-ac3-validator-mirrors-contract-not-just-schema
Scope: `tools/local_labels/test_ac3_label_admitted_validator.py`; future AC-N closure-tooling tests; the discipline of mirroring the plan's per-AC sub-criteria in test code.
Problem Description: Rounds 4-9 admitted sm_120 cells via `merge_admitted_cells.py`'s schema validation, which checks the JSON schema's required-field set (schema-shape). That catches missing fields but does NOT enforce the AC-3 contract's value invariants: schema_version must be EXACTLY 4 (not just present); profiler_version must start with `"ncu_2026.1."` (not just be a string); n_repeats must be ≥ 5 (not just an integer); stddev_l2_sector must be ≤ 0.03 (not just a number). Schema-only validation leaves the contract-pinned thresholds enforceable only at sweep time; once a row is in `label_admitted.json`, the cohort scanner doesn't recheck.
Root Cause: JSON schemas describe shape; AC contracts describe value invariants. The two are NOT interchangeable. A schema can require `n_repeats: integer`, but the AC-3 contract requires `n_repeats >= 5`. Relying solely on schema validation lets a row drift below the contract without producing a test failure.
Solution: For every AC sub-criterion that pins a value invariant (a threshold, a family-prefix string, a non-null requirement), the dedicated AC validator must implement that invariant as a positive test AND as a negative-control synthetic fixture. The validator walks `label_admitted.json` directly, partitions cells by "pre-loop prefix" vs "new-cohort admissions" (via the pre-Round-4 pin commit), and applies each invariant to the post-pre-loop slice. The pre-loop slice gets a byte-equivalent preservation check via `git show <pin>:<path>`. Negative-control fixtures construct single-row mutations that violate exactly one invariant; the helper predicates flag them. This dual coverage (cohort walk + synthetic fixtures) keeps each invariant auditable in source: one test name per sub-criterion, one fixture per failure mode.
Constraints: The validator MUST NOT validate the pre-loop prefix against the new-cohort invariants — historical rows may have older schema versions, lower n_repeats, etc., that the loop preserves byte-equivalent. Partition by pin-commit prefix-matching, not by date or arch. Synthetic fixtures must be self-contained (no on-disk file dependency) so they run on any host. Each negative fixture covers EXACTLY ONE failure mode; bundling multiple violations into one fixture masks regressions.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_ac3_label_admitted_validator.py -v` → 14 passed (8 positive: cohort_has_at_least_30, new_sm120_rows_have_required_17_field_schema, new_sm120_rows_have_arch_sm120, new_sm120_rows_have_l2_cache_layer_signal, new_sm120_rows_have_n_repeats_at_least_5, new_sm120_rows_profiler_version_is_2026_1_family, new_sm120_rows_stddev_l2_sector_within_gate, pre_loop_prefix_byte_equivalent_to_pin; 6 negative-control fixtures covering missing n_repeats, stale profiler_version, non-sm_120 arch, non-L2 signal, stddev above gate, historical mutation).
Source Rounds: round-10 (introduced and verified). Implements AC-3.2 / AC-3.3 / AC-3.4 closure tooling per Codex Round 9 mainline gap.

## Lesson: claim-guard-standalone-token-vs-substring-detection
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-claim-guard-standalone-token-not-substring
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py::_scan_round_summary_for_closure_addendum`; future regex-driven detectors that gate on a special token.
Problem Description: The Round-10 claim guard fired `complete_summary_missing_cache_layers_pin` on `round-10-summary.md` because the closure-detection logic used a substring check (`if "COMPLETE" not in text: return`) that matched the hyphenated forms `COMPLETE-emitting` and `COMPLETE-round` appearing in MENTIONS of future closure events (e.g., "rerun the claim guard against the COMPLETE-emitting round summary"). The summary was NOT itself a closure claim, but the guard treated it as one and demanded the verbatim `cache_layers = ["L2"]` / `prior_art_handling = "contextual_only"` pins.
Root Cause: Substring detection of a special token (`"COMPLETE"`) is over-eager because the token appears inside other contexts: hyphenated identifiers (COMPLETE-X), compounds (PRECOMPUTE, INCOMPLETE), and casual prose. The substring check is correct semantics for "does this file MENTION the token" but wrong semantics for "does this file MAKE the claim the token represents." A closure CLAIM is a standalone occurrence of the token (e.g., `Status: COMPLETE`, `**COMPLETE**`, `# Round 23 — COMPLETE`); a closure MENTION is a hyphenated or contextual occurrence.
Solution: Use a regex with explicit word-boundary lookbehind/lookahead to match standalone tokens only. For `COMPLETE`: `(?<!\w)COMPLETE(?![\w-])` requires the token to be preceded by a non-word character (or start of string) AND followed by a non-word character that is NOT a hyphen. The hyphen exclusion is the key insight — `\b` alone matches between letter and hyphen, but the hyphenated form is precisely the false-positive we want to reject. Pair the detector with both a positive control (standalone COMPLETE in a summary lacking pins MUST flag) and negative controls (hyphenated COMPLETE-X in a summary lacking pins MUST NOT flag).
Constraints: The standalone-vs-substring distinction is principled for ANY single-word token that can appear in compounds or hyphenated identifiers. The same pattern applies to future guards that detect words like "DEPRECATED", "RETIRED", "FROZEN", etc. Always pair the tightened detector with negative-control tests that exercise the false-positive cases.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py -q` → 59 passed (was 56 pre-Round-11; +3 new tests: hyphenated_complete_emitting_does_not_trigger, hyphenated_complete_round_does_not_trigger, standalone_complete_word_still_triggers). Direct guard run: `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` → 26 paths scanned, 0 defects (was 1 defect on round-10-summary.md pre-Round-11).
Source Rounds: round-10 (defect introduced when round-10 summary prose referenced future closure events), round-11 (resolved at the guard layer).

## Lesson: contract-validator-must-use-canonical-schema-not-hand-rolled
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-contract-validator-uses-canonical-schema
Scope: `tools/local_labels/test_ac3_label_admitted_validator.py`; future AC-N validators or any contract-checking test that walks a JSON artifact.
Problem Description: The Round-10 AC-3 validator advertised "17-field schema" but enforced only 13 fields via a hand-rolled `_AC3_NEW_ROW_REQUIRED_FIELDS` tuple. Missing: `cache_hit_rate_l1tex_sector_pct`, `l1tex_label_status`. The negative-control fixtures used inlined local predicates (e.g., `ok = isinstance(row.get("n_repeats"), int) and row.get("n_repeats", 0) >= 5`) instead of calling the production validator path, so they could stay green while the hand-rolled list drifted from the canonical schema. Codex Round 10 review caught the discrepancy: "Stop calling the validator 17-field unless the schema and rows are made to require all 17 fields truthfully."
Root Cause: A hand-rolled required-field list is a duplicate source of truth that silently drifts from the canonical JSON schema. Inlined negative-control predicates compound the problem because they exercise the duplicate logic, not the production validator. A future schema extension (e.g., adding a new required field) updates the schema but NOT the hand-rolled list, so the validator's "passes" do not actually witness contract compliance.
Solution: Load the canonical schema (`workloads/simbench_bw/schemas/local_label_admitted.schema.json`) and use `jsonschema.validate()` as the production path. The validator's role is to BIND the artifact to the schema, not to reimplement schema semantics. Negative-control fixtures call the same `_validate_new_row_against_schema(row, schema)` helper that the cohort walk uses — so a fixture's expected violation goes through the production code path. The hand-rolled list is appropriate only for invariants the schema CANNOT express (e.g., the contract-pinned `stddev <= 0.03` threshold, which is a value invariant not a shape invariant) and even then those should be implemented as separate single-purpose tests with their own production helpers.
Constraints: The schema's required fields are the floor, not the ceiling. Value invariants (thresholds, family-prefix matches) live in the validator's helper functions. The validator should partition cells by pin-commit prefix-matching so historical rows aren't validated against new-cohort invariants (older schema versions, lower n_repeats, etc., that the loop preserves byte-equivalent). The byte-equivalence check for the historical prefix is a SEPARATE concern from the schema-shape check on new admissions.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_ac3_label_admitted_validator.py -q` → 18 passed (10 positive over actual cohort + 8 production-path negative fixtures exercising `_validate_new_row_against_schema` and `_row_has_roster_l2_match`). The validator now bypasses the "13-field subset" discrepancy by loading the schema directly.
Source Rounds: round-10 (defect introduced via hand-rolled list), round-11 (resolved by switching to canonical schema validation + production-path negative fixtures).

## Lesson: byte-equivalence-vs-dict-equality-for-historical-preservation
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-byte-equivalence-not-dict-equality-for-prefix-preservation
Scope: AC-3.3 (historical-cell byte-equivalence preservation); any future "this prefix is immutable" preservation check over a JSON artifact.
Problem Description: The Round-10 AC-3.3 preservation check parsed both the pin-commit's JSON and the current JSON, then compared the first-49 cell dicts with `==`. That catches VALUE mutations (a number changed, a key flipped) but does NOT catch BYTE-level changes such as key reordering, whitespace churn, trailing comma drift, or float-formatting drift. Codex Round 10 review explicitly demanded byte-equivalence: "Implement a deterministic prefix byte scanner. Use the pin commit's exact bytes."
Root Cause: Dict equality is reflexive over JSON parsing — two byte sequences with the same parsed structure compare equal even if the bytes differ. AC-3.3's "byte-equivalent preservation" promise requires the BYTES to be the same, not just the semantic content. A reformatter, a key-reorder regression, or a whitespace-normalization tool would silently break the artifact's byte hash while keeping the dict-equality check green.
Solution: Implement a JSON-aware byte-span scanner. Walk the bytes of both the pin and current artifacts, locate the `"cells":` array, and use brace-depth tracking (with string-literal escape handling) to identify each top-level cell object's byte range. Concatenate the first N byte ranges from each and compare bytes directly. Keep the dict-equality check as an auxiliary diagnostic (more readable error messages on value mutations) but make the byte comparison the AC closure gate. Pair with a negative-control fixture that synthesizes two JSON byte sequences with the same parsed dicts but different key order or whitespace; the byte-span scanner must flag them.
Constraints: The byte-span scanner must handle nested objects (brace depth), nested arrays, and string literals containing braces/brackets. The walking loop in `_extract_first_n_cells_byte_span` does this with a small state machine (string-literal flag, escape flag, depth counter). For a future N (e.g., 100 cells), the linear walk is still O(file size) and fast enough for routine pytest. The scanner identifies the byte range by content (the first/Nth `{`/`}` pair at top-level) rather than by line/column to be robust against whitespace variations.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest tools/local_labels/test_ac3_label_admitted_validator.py::Ac3LabelAdmittedValidatorTest::test_pre_loop_prefix_byte_equivalent_to_pin -v` → passed. Negative control `test_synthetic_byte_reorder_flagged_by_byte_span_scanner` builds two byte sequences with identical parsed dicts but reordered keys + extra whitespace; the scanner correctly flags them as non-equal.
Source Rounds: round-10 (dict-equality check landed as AC-3.3 closure approximation), round-11 (byte-span scanner replaces dict-equality as the actual AC-3.3 closure gate).

## Lesson: claim-shape-context-not-token-shape-for-summary-claims
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-claim-shape-context-not-token-shape
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py::_summary_makes_closure_claim`; future "is this artifact making claim X?" detectors over markdown summaries.
Problem Description: Round 11 tightened the COMPLETE-claim detector from substring (`"COMPLETE" in text`) to standalone-token regex (`(?<!\w)COMPLETE(?![\w-])`). The regex fixed the hyphenated false-positive (`COMPLETE-emitting`) but still false-positived on TWO new patterns in `round-11-summary.md`: (a) standalone COMPLETE inside inline code spans (e.g., `` `Status: COMPLETE` still trigger.`` — the token is technically standalone but appears as an EXAMPLE inside backticks); (b) standalone COMPLETE in parenthetical word lists (e.g., `special word (COMPLETE, DEPRECATED, RETIRED, etc.)` in a lesson note). Codex Round 11 review caught both.
Root Cause: A token-shape detector ("does the token look standalone?") only constrains what the TOKEN looks like, not what the SURROUNDING CONTEXT looks like. A claim is fundamentally a contextual signal: a summary CLAIMS closure when it puts COMPLETE in a HEADING or in a STATUS DECLARATION, not when it mentions the word in body prose. Token-shape detection is a necessary but insufficient condition; claim-shape context is the actual closure signature.
Solution: Two-step detector: (1) STRIP inline code spans (single backticks) and triple-backtick fenced blocks from the text before scanning — content inside backticks is by markdown convention quoted/exemplary, not assertive; (2) require the COMPLETE match to appear in a CLAIM-SHAPE context — either a markdown heading line (`^#+\s+...COMPLETE` matched per-line) or a status/verdict declaration (`^Status:`, `^Verdict:`, `^Mainline Progress Verdict:`, etc. followed by COMPLETE on the same line). Parenthetical lists, body prose, and code spans don't match either pattern. Pair with negative-control fixtures for each false-positive shape (backtick-wrapped, parenthetical list, body discussion) AND positive controls for each claim-shape (heading, status declaration, review-template verdict line).
Constraints: The claim-shape keyword list must include the canonical review-template forms (`Mainline Progress Verdict`, `Mainline Verdict`, `Final Verdict`). The code-span strip uses simple backtick matching; nested or escaped backticks aren't handled but markdown summaries don't usually carry them. The pattern generalizes to ANY token-based status guard (DEPRECATED, RETIRED, FROZEN) — token shape + claim-shape context, with code-span exemption.
Validation Evidence: `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` → 28 paths scanned, 0 defects on active loop state (was 2 defects on `round-11-summary.md` pre-Round-12). `python3 -m pytest workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py -q` → 63 passed (was 59 pre-Round-12; +4 new fixtures: backtick-wrapped + parenthetical-list + status-declaration positive + Mainline-Progress-Verdict positive). Direct probes: `_summary_makes_closure_claim('`Status: COMPLETE` still trigger.')` returns False (code-span strip); `_summary_makes_closure_claim('(COMPLETE, DEPRECATED)')` returns False (no claim-shape context); `_summary_makes_closure_claim('# Round 23 — COMPLETE')` returns True (heading); `_summary_makes_closure_claim('Status: COMPLETE')` returns True (status declaration).
Source Rounds: round-11 (token-shape detector landed, still over-fires on code spans + parenthetical lists), round-12 (resolved with code-span strip + claim-shape context check).

## Lesson: bare-line-claim-shape-for-stop-signal-detection
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-bare-line-claim-shape-stop-signal
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py::_BARE_COMPLETE_LINE_RE`; the RLCR protocol stop-signal convention; future status-detector patterns.
Problem Description: Round 12's claim-shape detector (`# heading ... COMPLETE`, `Status: COMPLETE`) was too narrow. The RLCR loop protocol's terminal stop signal is a bare `COMPLETE` line — `_summary_makes_closure_claim("COMPLETE\n")` returned False pre-Round-13, so a closure summary that simply wrote `COMPLETE` (or `**COMPLETE**`, or `COMPLETE.`) on its own line would bypass the AC-7 pin-restatement requirement.
Root Cause: The Round-12 design implicitly assumed closure claims appear with a contextual prefix (heading, status keyword). But the RLCR protocol's canonical stop signal IS the bare line itself. Heading + status-declaration patterns are necessary but not exhaustive.
Solution: Add a third claim-shape pattern matching a line whose ONLY content (after trim) is the standalone COMPLETE token, optionally wrapped in markdown emphasis (`*`/`**`/`***`) and optionally followed by terminal punctuation. The regex anchors with `^...$` under `re.MULTILINE` so body prose like `(COMPLETE, DEPRECATED)` (additional content on the same line) does NOT match. The Round-12 code-span strip stays in front of the scan, so backtick-wrapped bare COMPLETE remains exempt.
Constraints: The bare-line pattern is the trickiest of the three claim shapes because it's the broadest. Pair it with negative controls for: backtick-wrapped bare COMPLETE (must not match — code spans are explanatory); mid-prose COMPLETE within a sentence (must not match — not a bare line). Future status guards (DEPRECATED, RETIRED, FROZEN) following the same three-pattern shape (heading + status declaration + bare line) should also add the code-span strip first.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py -q` → 67 passed (was 63 pre-Round-13; +4 new bare-line fixtures). `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` → 30 paths scanned, 0 defects on active loop state. Direct probes: `_summary_makes_closure_claim('COMPLETE\n')` → True; `'**COMPLETE**'` → True; `'COMPLETE.'` → True; `'`COMPLETE`'` (backtick) → False; `'once COMPLETE the loop ends'` (mid-prose) → False.
Source Rounds: round-12 (token-shape→claim-shape narrowing landed but excluded bare line), round-13 (bare-line pattern added).

## Lesson: bundle-producer-consumes-existing-traces-no-recapture
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-bundle-producer-no-recapture
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_activation_bundle_producer.py`; any future predictor-bundle producer over admitted-row evidence.
Problem Description: The pre-Round-13 producer (`sm120_activation_bundle_producer.py`) was a 5-cell hardcoded `_AMENDED_COHORT_CELLS` list that re-captured NVBit traces every run (`_capture_trace` wiped the trace_dir + ran the binary under `CUDA_INJECTION64_PATH`). For Round 13's AC-4 work (30-bundle production), re-capturing each cell would (a) require ~5-10 minutes of NVBit wall-clock per cell × 30 cells = ~3 hours, AND (b) risk binary_sha256 drift if the binary was rebuilt against a different HEAD than the admitted cell's pinned binary_sha256.
Root Cause: The 5-cell producer was authored when no per-shape traces existed yet — re-capture was the only way to materialize artifacts. Rounds 4-10 since then captured + staged all 30 admitted-cohort traces via `activation_trace_orchestrator.py` and `run_suite.py --mode trace`; the dynamic_trace.pb files exist on disk and joined the admitted rows at their pinned binary_sha256. Re-capture would produce identical-or-noisier artifacts in the best case and SHA-drifted artifacts in the worst case.
Solution: A predictor-bundle producer over an ADMITTED cohort should consume the existing per-shape trace artifacts that joined the admission, not re-capture. The producer's invariants: (1) on-disk binary_sha256 matches the admitted row (guards against undetected rebuild); (2) `<cell_root>/trace_<shape>/dynamic_trace.pb` exists (the trace IS the admission's evidence); (3) `sector_stream.jsonl` is regenerated idempotently from the pb when missing (the consume_trace tool is deterministic given the pb input). The producer fails closed when any of (1)-(2) doesn't hold — those are roster-side or admitted-row defects that need fixing upstream, not papered over by re-capture.
Constraints: This discipline depends on the per-shape trace artifacts being preserved on disk across rounds. The Round-7+ roster-aware resolver + the Round-9 cohort scanner enforce this — every admitted cell has a roster-resolved per-shape manifest with the matching binary_sha256. Future cohorts that admit cells without preserving the trace would need to either re-capture or skip those cells. The producer's `--re-capture` flag could be added later if needed for diagnostic comparison; Round-13 default is consume-existing.
Validation Evidence: `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_activation_bundle_producer.py` → wrote 30 bundles to `data/local_labels/sweeps/sm120_cohort_bundles_20260526T154829Z/predictor_bundles/`, 0 failures. Each bundle's `manifest_pairing_index.arch == "sm_120"` and `cache_layer == "L2"`. Total wall-clock ≈ 5 minutes (sector_stream generation dominates; cache_predictor is ~1 second per cell).
Source Rounds: round-13 (introduced + verified). Implements AC-4.1 production (≥30 bundles) per Codex Round 12 review.

## Lesson: token-boundary-must-reject-hyphen-on-both-sides
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-token-boundary-rejects-hyphen-both-sides
Scope: any regex token-boundary that must reject hyphenated compounds (e.g., `COMPLETE` vs `bare-COMPLETE`, `COMPLETE-emitting`); `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py`'s four COMPLETE patterns.
Problem Description: Round 13's claim-guard token boundary `(?<!\w)COMPLETE(?![\w-])` only rejected hyphen on the RIGHT side. The left-side lookbehind used only `\w`, which is `[A-Za-z0-9_]` and does NOT include the hyphen. So `bare-COMPLETE` matched: `-` is not in `\w`, satisfying the lookbehind; `\n` after COMPLETE satisfies the right lookahead. Codex Round 13 review caught this on `round-13-summary.md`'s heading `### Blocker — bare-COMPLETE claim-shape detection`.
Root Cause: `\w` (word character) excludes `-` by design — Python's regex `\w` is `[A-Za-z0-9_]`. A token boundary intended to reject hyphenated compounds must explicitly include `-` in the lookbehind class. The Round-11 → Round-12 → Round-13 tightening chain added each new pattern only on the right side because the prior false positives (Round 11: hyphenated `COMPLETE-emitting`) were right-hyphen cases; the symmetric left-hyphen case (`bare-COMPLETE`) only surfaced when Round 13's own summary introduced it.
Solution: Use `(?<![\w-])COMPLETE(?![\w-])` consistently on BOTH sides of the token. Apply the boundary to every claim-shape pattern (heading, status declaration, bare line, standalone). Pair with regression fixtures for each hyphenated direction: left-hyphen (`bare-COMPLETE`), right-hyphen (`COMPLETE-emitting`), and both-sides (`bare-COMPLETE-X`). Preserve positive controls for true standalone forms (`COMPLETE`, `**COMPLETE**`, `COMPLETE.`).
