Constraints: Hyphen is the most common compound-builder in markdown headings and identifiers, so it's the asymmetry that bites first. Other separators (`/`, `.`, `:`) might also need exclusion if compound tokens with those separators become common; the same lookbehind/lookahead pattern generalizes. Future status guards (DEPRECATED, RETIRED, FROZEN) following the same three-pattern shape should also reject hyphen on both sides.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py -q` → 68 passed (was 67 pre-Round-14; +1 new `test_hyphenated_bare_complete_in_heading_does_not_trigger` regression fixture). `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` → 33 paths scanned, 0 defects (was 2 defects on `round-13-summary.md` pre-Round-14). Direct probes: `_summary_makes_closure_claim('### Blocker — bare-COMPLETE claim-shape detection')` → False; `_summary_makes_closure_claim('# Round 23 Summary — COMPLETE')` → True.
Source Rounds: round-13 (left-hyphen false-positive introduced by Round-13 summary content), round-14 (resolved by symmetric token boundary).

## Lesson: target-arch-narrows-comparison-universe-before-bookkeeping
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-target-arch-narrows-universe-pre-bookkeeping
Scope: `workloads/simbench_bw/comparison_runner.py::run_comparison`; any future "single-arch baseline" mode over a mixed-arch label corpus.
Problem Description: Pre-Round-14 `run_comparison(target_arch="sm_120")` was a coverage-gate relaxation: it allowed the top-level admission to fire on the target arch alone (without cross-arch admission). But the comparison universe (label_cells, arch_set, per-arch counters) stayed comprehensive — sm_86 historical labels were still walked, and any sm_86 label with no matching bundle emitted a `comparison_runner_join_key_unmatched` exclusion. Codex Round 13 review caught the Round-13 30-sm_120-bundle run producing 44 sm_86 unmatched records because the 44 historical sm_86 cells in `label_admitted.json` had no Round-13 sm_120-only bundles to join.
Root Cause: A "single-arch baseline" mode has two distinct meanings: (a) the ADMISSION GATE only requires the single arch to meet its smoke-test floor; (b) the COMPARISON UNIVERSE is narrowed to that single arch. The Round-12+ implementation only landed (a). The historical label corpus was still in scope, producing per-arch-tally leakage and join-key-unmatched noise for the non-target arch.
Solution: When `target_arch` is set, narrow the comparison universe FIRST: filter `label_cells` to that arch before any join-key/profiler-version/layer-broadcast bookkeeping runs; narrow `arch_set` to `(target_arch,)` so per-arch counters and diagnostics surfaces contain only the target arch. Add an explicit `arches` list and `scalar_or_bounded_headline_cells_per_arch` mirror to the bundle output so consumers can assert AC-5.2 / AC-4.3 against canonical fields rather than re-derive them per call site. Update the schema to admit the new fields. Pair with two regression tests: (i) mixed corpus → zero non-target arch surfaces + zero non-target arch unmatched records; (ii) empty-target-arch case → target arch counter reports 0 (not omitted; explicit-zero is the signal).
Constraints: When `target_arch` is None, the runner runs the multi-arch comparison universe unchanged. Historical tests that assumed `target_arch` mode kept multi-arch per-arch diagnostics need updating in lockstep (Round 14 updated one such test). The bundle schema's `arches` and `scalar_or_bounded_headline_cells_per_arch` are optional (not in `required`) to preserve backwards-compat with historical bundles. Future cross-arch verification tests should switch from `target_arch=...` to omitting the kwarg.
Validation Evidence: `python3 -m pytest workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_comparison_runner.py -q` → 67 passed (was 66 + 2 new = 67 total; the existing test_synthetic_target_arch_sm86_admits_top_level_when_only_sm86_meets_floor updated to assert single-arch tally). Active sm_120-only L2 comparison bundle at `data/local_labels/sweeps/sm120_cohort_bundles_20260526T154829Z/comparison_bundle.json` reports `arches: ["sm_120"]`, `comparable_cells_per_arch: {"sm_120": 30}`, `headline_cells_per_arch: {"sm_120": 27}`, `scalar_or_bounded_headline_cells_per_arch: {"sm_120": 27}`, 0 `comparison_runner_join_key_unmatched` (was 44 pre-Round-14).
Source Rounds: round-13 (gap identified by Codex when comparison-runner probe showed 44 sm_86 unmatched), round-14 (resolved).

## Lesson: claim-guard-default-scope-active-loop-only
Lesson ID: BL-20260526-claim-guard-default-scope-active-loop-only
Scope: tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py; CLAIM guards over round summaries
Problem Description: A claim guard that scans `.humanize/rlcr/*/round-*-summary.md` by default surfaces false-positive defects against PRIOR LOOPS' summaries (which target different plans with different AC closure semantics and different pin-quoting requirements). The closure-addendum pin check in particular fires on any prior-loop COMPLETE summary that doesn't quote THIS loop's specific contract pins. A token-level scan of the plan body also surfaces false positives because the plan body documents the forbidden CLAIM PATTERNS as illustrative examples.
Root Cause: The CLAIM guard's purpose is to detect when THIS loop's artifacts make a forbidden claim. Prior-loop artifacts and the documentation that defines the rules are out of THIS loop's claim-domain even though they live in scannable file shapes.
Solution: Scope the default discovery glob to the NEWEST `.humanize/rlcr/<session>/` directory (by mtime) only. Exclude the plan body from default discovery. Explicit `--path` overrides remain available for operator-driven cross-loop audits. The negative-fixture marker (`# SM120_CLAIM_GUARD_NEGATIVE_FIXTURE`) gives any artifact an explicit opt-out from the per-line scan, which the claim-guard's own pytest fixtures use.
Constraints: The active-loop scope assumes the loop harness preserves directory mtimes when scp-ing between hosts; raw-scp cross-host transfer touches mtimes. After a host pivot, verify the active session is the receiving host's freshly-created `.humanize/rlcr/<new-session-id>/`.
Validation Evidence: `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` 17 cases (positive + negative pattern coverage); `python3 tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` exits 0 in default mode on the Round 0 state.
Source Rounds: round-0 (2026-05-26)

## Lesson: dual-geomean-holdout-distinct-from-full-cohort
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-dual-geomean-holdout-vs-full-cohort
Scope: `workloads/simbench_bw/comparison_runner.py::run_comparison`; any future "headline + generalization estimate" baseline reporter that mixes a calibration corpus with a reserved generalization slice.
Problem Description: A single "geomean" field on a comparison bundle conflates the headline-statistic over the FULL admitted cohort with the generalization estimate over the reserved holdout slice. Without an explicit holdout-only field, downstream readers cannot tell whether a claimed accuracy is the within-population statistic (rel-err over all included cells, including calibration cells whose labels may have been used in fit/select) or the generalization estimate (rel-err over labels reserved from any fitting / selection / typed-exclusion decisions). The two have materially different methodological weight, and the difference is invisible if only one number is reported.
Root Cause: The headline `geomean` field's population is implicit: it's "whatever cells survived the headline filter." When a contract introduces a SPLIT contract (calibration vs holdout) on the same admitted cohort, the headline field's population is ambiguous on the reading side. The producer side knows the split but discards it before reporting.
Solution: On the admitted arm, emit BOTH a `full_cohort_geomean` (and its bootstrap CI tuple) and a `holdout_geomean` (and its bootstrap CI tuple) computed over the reserved holdout subset of the same included-cells set. Resolve the holdout subset by joining included headline cells to the frozen roster's `(slug, shape_point) -> split` index. The full-cohort field MUST mirror the legacy `geomean` field by construction (same population, same series) so legacy readers continue to work. Record `holdout_cell_count` so a typed-unavailable bubble (a holdout cell excluded from the headline) is visible in the bundle and NOT silently substituted from calibration/validation. Both CIs use the contract's bootstrap pins (the producer MUST pass them explicitly; the per-arm CI is NOT a re-derivation of the legacy CI). The typed_unavailable arm omits all dual-geomean fields, mirroring the legacy geomean's arm exclusivity.
Constraints: The roster's `(slug, shape_point) -> split` index is loaded at runtime; tests that synthesize cells with off-roster slugs see `holdout_cell_count == 0` (the empty-list fallback yields `holdout_geomean == 0.0` with zero CI bounds). Tests that want a positive holdout signal must use roster-listed holdout slugs. The schema additions must be optional (not `required`) so legacy bundles continue to validate. Bootstrap CI on a 1-cell holdout subset collapses (low == high == geomean, half_width == 0); the 0-cell case returns (0, 0, 0) — both must be tested.
Validation Evidence: 4 new tests in `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_comparison_runner.py` cover: full_cohort mirrors legacy geomean on admit; empty-holdout returns count=0 + zeros; 3 roster-holdout cells produce count=3 with the correct subset geomean and a distinct full-cohort series; typed_unavailable arm omits all 9 fields. Active comparison bundle regenerated: `full_cohort_geomean = 0.503`, `holdout_geomean = 1.886`, `holdout_cell_count = 7` (matches the roster's 7 holdout-split cells). Schema validates.
Source Rounds: round-15 (2026-05-27)

## Lesson: closure-claim-summary-scope-tag-additive-not-replacement
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-closure-claim-scope-tag-additive
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py::_scan_round_summary_for_closure_addendum`; any future claim guard that gates closure-claim summaries on multiple verbatim pin strings.
Problem Description: Adding a new required verbatim string to the closure-claim summary contract risks breaking the existing pin-check chain in two ways: (a) the new rule could replace one of the existing rules (silently dropping the pin-quote requirement that other ACs depend on); (b) the new rule's byte-exact match could collide with an existing pin (so the same defect line gets fingered twice). The first failure mode is silent; the second is noisy but harmless.
Root Cause: A "summary must contain X" requirement is monotone — adding a new required string adds, never replaces, an existing requirement. But the implementation can drift if the new check is wired into the same conditional as the existing checks via an `else` branch or short-circuit. Tests that pin both directions (rule fires when string is absent; ALL existing rules still fire when the new string is absent and the existing pins are also absent) catch the drift.
Solution: When adding a new closure-claim verbatim-tag rule, place it after the existing pin checks in the same scanning function and emit its own `ClaimDefect` independently. Pin the additive semantic in tests: (1) closure-claim summary lacking ALL required strings fires the new rule AND the existing rules; (2) closure-claim summary with ALL required strings fires no rules; (3) byte-exact match is required (near-miss variants — truncated, reordered, extra whitespace — still fire); (4) non-closure summary does NOT require the new tag (the rule mirrors the existing closure-addendum gates' scope, which fire only on closure claims). Update any pre-existing "with pins clean" test fixtures to also quote the new tag so the all-clean state stays clean.
Constraints: The verbatim tag MUST be defined as a module-level constant so tests, the rule body, and documentation all reference the same source of truth. The tag's byte-exact match is the contract — tests that build the tag from string concatenation across lines must avoid embedding newlines mid-tag (a multi-line `textwrap.dedent` body that wraps the tag across two lines breaks the byte-exact match in a way that is invisible at the source-text level).
Validation Evidence: 4 new tests in `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` cover: tag absent fires new rule alongside existing two; tag present clears all three; near-miss variants (missing clause, extra space, reordered semicolons) all still fire; non-closure summary does NOT require the tag. 1 pre-existing `test_complete_summary_with_pins_passes` updated to also quote the AC-5 scope tag. Full claim-guard suite: 72 passed.
Source Rounds: round-15 (2026-05-27)

## Lesson: optional-schema-extension-does-not-enforce-presence
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-optional-schema-extension-not-enforcement
Scope: JSON Schema authoring for evidence bundles where AC-specific NEW fields must be REQUIRED on a specific arm/scope, not just permitted.
Problem Description: When a producer starts emitting new bundle fields (e.g., AC-5.3's 9 dual-geomean fields), the obvious schema extension is to add the field definitions and append them to the schema. Many schema authors stop there, leaving the new fields as optional `properties` entries. The schema then ADMITS bundles with the fields present (the producer's output validates) but also ADMITS bundles WITHOUT the fields (the negative test "bundle missing X is rejected" passes by accident at the writer level but silently fails at the consumer level). Codex Round 15 review on this project surfaced exactly this pattern: the AC-5.3 negative test was not enforced, even though the producer-side fields were emitted.
Root Cause: JSON Schema's default semantic is "properties define shape if present"; `required` is what makes a property's PRESENCE mandatory. Backwards-compat with historical artifacts that do not carry the new field is a legitimate constraint, but it does NOT extend to NEW active artifacts emitted by the updated producer. The correct shape is a SCOPED `required` constraint: the new fields are required ONLY when the bundle is on the arm/scope the AC targets.
Solution: For an AC that requires NEW evidence on a specific arm/scope, add a JSON Schema conditional (`allOf` with an `if`/`then` pair) that REQUIRES the new fields when the bundle's arm-discriminator and scope-discriminator match the AC's target. Mirror with a `not.anyOf` extension on the OPPOSITE arm (e.g., `typed_unavailable`) so cross-arm contamination is also rejected. The conditional preserves backwards-compat for arms/scopes the AC does not cover (e.g., a `target_arch=sm_86` admitted bundle is not affected by an `sm_120`-scoped conditional). Validate with negative probes against the ACTUAL active bundle artifact, not synthetic fixtures — synthetic round-trips can pass even when the conditional doesn't fire on real data.
Constraints: The conditional's `if` clause must use `required` for the discriminator fields it inspects so the `if` block matches only when the discriminator is present. `if.properties.X.const = "Y"` alone matches when `X` is absent (vacuously). Synthetic round-trip tests for the typed_unavailable arm need to inject each forbidden field individually because the `not.anyOf` clause fires on the first match; iterating one field at a time catches a future regression that drops a single field's `required` entry from the clause.
Validation Evidence: 7 new tests in `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_comparison_runner.py` (5 schema-negative + 2 roster-load) all pass. Direct CLI probes confirm: unmodified active sm_120 admitted bundle validates; removing `holdout_geomean` alone fails; removing the full holdout CI tuple fails; removing all 9 fields fails. The fail diagnostic names the first missing required field by JSON path.
Source Rounds: round-15 (introduced optional schema) → round-16 (closed enforcement gap).

## Lesson: claim-guard-headline-bundle-emit-vs-loop-closure-scope
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-headline-bundle-emit-vs-loop-closure-scope
Scope: `tools/local_labels/sm120_loop_claim_guard.py::_scan_round_summary_for_closure_addendum`; any RLCR claim guard that gates round-summary tags on multiple scopes (loop-closure vs interim-evidence-emission).
Problem Description: An RLCR plan's AC-N.4-style "round summary for the round that emits the artifact" requirement is broader than "the loop-COMPLETE summary". A guard that gates the required tag only on closure-claim summaries misses the INTERIM round that emits the artifact in question. The plan in this project (line 89) wrote "The Round-N close summary for the round that emits the headline bundle explicitly tags the bundle"; the Round-15 implementation interpreted this as the loop-COMPLETE summary and shipped a closure-only scope. Codex Round 15 review flagged this as an AC-5.4 enforcement gap.
Root Cause: "Closure" and "emission" are different events in the RLCR cadence. A round that emits new evidence is not necessarily the round that closes the loop; those are two distinct triggers and the AC's required tag can apply to either. A guard that conflates them under-enforces the AC.
Solution: Detect "emission" via the AC-specific evidence signature — typically the introduction of new field names (e.g., `holdout_geomean`, `full_cohort_geomean`, `holdout_cell_count` in the AC-5.3 case). These names are AC-unique by construction (introduced in the round that closed the AC) so a summary that quotes them is necessarily reporting that AC's evidence. The detector should NOT key on the generic artifact filename (e.g., `comparison_bundle.json`) because that name is shared across all rounds that produce or reference the artifact (historical bundles, prior-round regeneration commands, plan-documented examples). Combine the emission detector with the existing closure-claim detector via OR: tag required on EITHER condition. Keep closure-only pin checks (e.g., AC-7 closure-addendum re-statements) gated on closure alone.
Constraints: AC-N.4-style tags should match the plan body's exact phrasing for case sensitivity (in this project, plan line 90 specifies case-insensitive substring matching; the Round-15 byte-exact implementation was over-strict). The emission detector MUST NOT strip code spans because round summaries canonically reference field names in backticks (markdown convention); a stripped-span variant would silently miss the common case. The detector is naturally narrow: it only runs on files matching `round-*-summary.md`, so plan-body or lesson-note mentions of the field names never reach it. Tests should pin the precise scope: unrelated in-flight summary does not require the tag; headline-bundle-emit summary without the tag fires only the AC-N.4 rule (not AC-7 pins); detector keys on field names, not on the generic filename.
Validation Evidence: 5 new tests in `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_sm120_loop_claim_guard.py` cover: unrelated in-flight (no tag required); headline-bundle-emit without tag (AC-5.4 fires; AC-7 pins do NOT); headline-bundle-emit with tag (positive control); case-mixed tag (case-insensitive substring); detector specificity (filename alone does NOT trip the detector). Full claim-guard suite: 76 passed. Default discovery against active loop state: 37 paths scanned, 0 defects.
Source Rounds: round-15 (introduced closure-only scope) → round-16 (widened to headline-bundle-emit + case-insensitive).

## Lesson: cli-as-library-via-module-main-for-driver-composition
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-cli-as-library-via-module-main
Scope: Driver/orchestrator scripts that need to compose existing CLI tools in-process (e.g., a headline driver routing to a per-arch bundle producer).
Problem Description: Two existing CLI scripts need to compose: a top-level driver loads a cohort, branches on arch, and needs to invoke a per-arch producer to write the bundles the driver then consumes. Naive approaches (shell out via subprocess; copy the producer's body into the driver) lose either error propagation (subprocess exit codes are coarse, stack traces are lost across the fork) or maintenance correctness (a forked copy of the producer drifts from the original). The driver also needs to feed the producer a caller-chosen output dir so the producer's bundle output ends up under the driver's run-output tree.
Root Cause: A producer with both a `def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:` entry point AND a `if __name__ == "__main__": raise SystemExit(main())` script guard is already designed for both forms — but the script-form pattern (with `argparse.parse_args(argv)` inside `main`) means callers can pass the SAME `argv` they would have passed at the shell, and the function returns the same int the script's exit code would have. This is the cleanest in-process composition surface: the driver builds the argv list with `--output-root <its_own_output_dir>`, calls `producer_module.main(argv)`, and gets back an int return code it can check.
Solution: When composing two CLI tools in-process:
  1. Import the producer module lazily inside the branch that needs it — the legacy non-producer path then does not pay the cost of loading the producer's full transitive import graph.
  2. Build the argv list with explicit flags (no positional smuggling); pass the caller-chosen output dir as `--output-root <str(driver_output_dir)>`.
  3. Check the return code: zero ⇒ continue; non-zero ⇒ propagate a typed-diagnostic abort with both the producer's rc and the driver's context.
  4. Read produced artifacts from the canonical output-subdir the producer documents (`<output_root>/predictor_bundles/` in this case) using a deterministic glob so the in-process consumer matches the producer's CLI behavior byte-for-byte.
  5. Extract the routing decision into a pure helper (e.g., `_route_predictor_stage(headline_cohort, target_arch) -> PredictorStageRouting`) so the routing logic is unit-testable without going through the driver's full main() (which needs a full cohort manifest + label_admitted.json + repo state). The pure helper returns a NamedTuple with `outcome` (enum-like string) and per-outcome detail fields (n_sm120, n_total, diagnostic, detail). Tests then exercise every outcome with synthetic cohort tuples.
  6. End-to-end tests can stub the producer module by registering a fake in `sys.modules` BEFORE the driver imports it (the driver's lazy `import` inside the branch will pick up the stub). The fake writes a synthetic bundle into the requested `--output-root`'s predictor_bundles subdir so the driver's read-back path is exercised.
Constraints: The producer's `main(argv)` MUST return an int (not just call `sys.exit`). A `SystemExit` raise inside `main` would propagate through the driver's `main` and exit the whole process. If the producer has a `if __name__ == "__main__": raise SystemExit(main())` script guard but `main` itself returns int, the script form still works and the in-process form is safe. The driver should NOT rely on producer-specific output-naming conventions beyond the canonical subdir; if the producer writes bundles with arch-prefixed filenames (e.g., `sm_120__<slug>__<shape>.json`), the driver's glob picks them all up via `*.json` and lets the comparison runner do the join.
Validation Evidence: 7 new tests in `tools/local_labels/test_run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py` (5 pure-helper routing tests + 2 end-to-end stub-producer tests) all pass. The end-to-end tests use `sys.modules["sm120_activation_bundle_producer"] = <stub>` to register a fake producer module BEFORE the driver's lazy `import` inside the routing branch; the stub captures the argv it was called with and writes a synthetic bundle into the requested output dir. The driver's `cr.run_comparison` is monkey-patched separately so the test does not depend on a real label corpus. Focused suite: 185 passed, 3 skipped.
Source Rounds: round-17 (2026-05-27).

## Lesson: producer-output-must-be-bound-to-requested-cohort
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-producer-output-bound-to-requested-cohort
Scope: Composed CLI driver/producer chains where a top-level driver delegates artifact production to a per-arch (or per-domain) producer, then consumes the produced artifacts in a downstream stage. Especially relevant when the producer writes into a stable output dir that may also contain stale artifacts from prior runs.
Problem Description: A driver that calls `producer.main(["--output-root", str(out)])` then globs `out/predictor_bundles/*.json` accepts WHATEVER currently lives in that directory: stale bundles from a previous run with a different cohort, partial output where the producer's per-cell fail-closed silently dropped cells, or bundles whose embedded identity (arch, cache_layer, slug+shape) does not match the cohort the driver was asked to compute over. The driver hands the mismatched set to the next stage (e.g., `cr.run_comparison`), which produces a publication-grade artifact bound to the WRONG population. The producer's per-cell rc-check is necessary but insufficient: rc=0 only says "the producer did not crash", not "the producer wrote exactly the cohort the driver requested".
Root Cause: A producer's CLI is designed to write a self-consistent output dir; the driver's cohort is defined upstream, in a separate artifact. The two are bound only by convention ("the operator pointed the producer at the right output dir, on the right cohort"). When a driver-level conditional invokes the producer in-process, that convention becomes a SOFTWARE responsibility — and absent a binding check, defects flow downstream silently.
Solution: Between the producer call and the next consuming stage, run a pure validator that REQUIRES one-to-one binding between the produced artifacts and the requested cohort:
  1. The validator takes the cohort (canonical keys, e.g., `{(slug, shape_point, arch)}`) and the loaded artifacts as inputs.
  2. For every cohort key, exactly one matching artifact MUST exist (missing keys → fail).
  3. Every artifact MUST be in the cohort key set (extras → fail; this catches stale outputs in the same dir).
  4. No duplicate keys among artifacts (duplicates → fail; catches name-collision regressions in the producer).
  5. Per-artifact invariants the cohort assumes (here: arch == "sm_120" AND cache_layer == "L2") MUST hold on every artifact.
  6. Mismatch fails closed with a TYPED diagnostic (e.g., `headline_runner_<producer>_bundle_set_mismatch`) whose detail enumerates each defect category (missing/extra/duplicate/wrong-arch/wrong-layer/unkeyable). The downstream stage MUST NOT execute on a mismatched set.
Constraints: The validator is pure (no I/O, no side effects); the driver does the I/O of reading artifacts back. Tests cover each defect category in isolation, plus the happy path. The validator's NamedTuple result exposes each defect category as its own field so callers can format diagnostics or pin specific defects in tests. The downstream-stage skip-on-mismatch is REQUIRED — a previously-passing legacy fail-closed check (`len(bundles) == expected`) may still apply to other branches, but the new validator IS the equivalent for the new branch. End-to-end tests for the new branch must include a partial-set negative case that exercises the full path from producer-stub through driver to assertion-of-rc=1; the test must also assert the downstream stage was NOT invoked (record `cr.run_comparison` calls and assert the list is empty).
Validation Evidence: 7 new tests in `tools/local_labels/test_run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py` (6 pure-helper unit tests + 1 end-to-end partial-set test) pass. The Round-17 stub-producer positive test was also updated: the stub now writes bundles matching the requested cohort cells one-to-one (the prior single-stub bundle would correctly trip the new validator). Focused suite: 198 passed, 3 skipped.
Source Rounds: round-17 (introduced producer-invocation chain) → round-18 (added binding check).
Update Note (round-20 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop, 2026-05-29 — the driver must THREAD its selected inputs into the producer's argv, not just the output path): Codex [P2] on `run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py:1704`: the sm_120 first-class path invoked the producer in-process as `sm120_activation_bundle_producer.main(["--output-root", str(output_dir)])` — passing ONLY the output path, so the producer fell back to its hard-coded global corpus (`_ADMITTED_PATH = data/local_labels/label_admitted.json`) and emitted bundles for EVERY globally-admitted sm_120 cell. Under a non-default `--label-admitted-path` / pure-sm_120 cohort, the driver's OWN downstream bundle-set binding check (the very gate this lesson added) then aborted on extra/missing bundles — the binding check is necessary but it catches the symptom; the root cause is the driver not threading its SELECTED input corpus into the producer. GENERAL RULE (CLI-as-library composition, cf. BL-20260527-cli-as-library-via-module-main): when a driver calls a producer's `main(argv)` in-process, it must thread EVERY input it itself resolved (here the runner's `--label-admitted-path`), not just the output location; a producer reading a hard-coded default for an un-passed input silently builds a different cohort than the driver selected. FIX SHAPE: give the producer a CLI flag for the input whose DEFAULT equals its existing hard-coded value (so standalone invocation is unchanged), parameterize the loader (`_load_admitted_sm120(path=...)`), and thread the driver's value at the call site; because the driver's flag default equals the producer's default, threading is a no-op in the common case and only bites under a non-default selection (low-risk). Regression-prove BOTH halves: the loader honors a non-default path (returns only that corpus's cells) AND `main()`'s argparse RECOGNIZES the new flag (both fail pre-fix: TypeError + argparse "unrecognized arguments"). Same producer-reads-a-hardcoded-default-instead-of-the-caller's-selection class as R4 (canonical-device producer) and R17 (fresh-regen cache geometry). TEST-ISOLATION GOTCHA caught by the full sweep (not the focused run): a NEW test that does `import <module>` to reach the real producer can pick up a `_StubProducer` a SIBLING test (`test_run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py`) installed DIRECTLY into `sys.modules["<module>"]` without cleanup (it leaks across the rest of the session). Symptom: `AttributeError: type object '_StubProducer' has no attribute '<real-fn>'`, green in isolation but red in the full sweep. Fix: load the REAL module FRESH from its file path under a UNIQUE name (`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` + `module_from_spec` + `exec_module`), bypassing the polluted `sys.modules` entry — and verify by running the stub-installing sibling test + the new test TOGETHER. Source: round-20 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop; Codex Review-Phase [P2] on run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py:1704-1705.

## Lesson: derive-active-cohort-from-committed-artifacts-instead-of-redundant-manifest
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-derive-active-cohort-from-committed-artifacts
Scope: Driver/orchestrator scripts that need a "cohort to iterate over" and where committed artifacts (a frozen roster + an admitted-cells corpus) already pin the same information that would live in a separate cohort-manifest file.
Problem Description: A driver that requires `--cohort-manifest <path>` as its sole cohort source forces operators to author a manifest that duplicates information already pinned in committed artifacts. When the driver's path is loop-scoped (e.g., "this is the sm_120 active path; the active cohort is the intersection of the frozen sm_120 roster and the admitted sm_120 cells"), requiring a separate manifest creates two problems: (a) operator friction (a hand-authored manifest must be re-built whenever the admitted set changes); (b) drift risk (the manifest can describe a different cohort than the loop's committed artifacts). The Codex review caught this on this project: AC-1 said "the active framework path exercises sm_120 only when --target-arch sm_120 is specified", but the driver was not runnable from committed loop artifacts without a manifest the producer does not emit.
Root Cause: A cohort manifest is a CONVENIENCE artifact, not a primary source of truth. When the primary sources are already committed (frozen roster + admitted-cells corpus), the driver should be able to derive the active cohort from those primaries — not require a hand-built mirror.
Solution: When the driver's loop-scoped path has a canonical primary source for the cohort:
  1. Add a pure helper `_derive_<scope>_cohort_from_committed_artifacts(roster_path, label_admitted_path) -> DerivedCohort` that reads the primaries and returns the intersection.
  2. Build a synthetic CohortManifest-shaped object so the existing downstream gates (publication-grade floor, family-bucket checks) work unchanged — they consume `admitted_cell_count` and a `cells` tuple, both of which the helper can synthesize from the roster + label rows.
  3. The helper fails closed on missing primaries (FileNotFoundError naming the expected path) and on empty intersection (RuntimeError — an empty cohort silently going into the producer would compound the original ambiguity).
  4. In `main()`, gate the helper on the loop-scope marker (here: `args.target_arch == "sm_120"`). The legacy path that has no committed canonical source (here: sm_86) continues to require its manifest.
  5. Cohort iteration order follows the roster's deterministic order field (e.g., `candidate_order`).
  6. Tests: smoke against the real committed artifacts (assert the derived cohort has the expected cell count); negative tests for missing roster and empty intersection; end-to-end positive test for the derived-cohort path; one scope-pin test verifying the legacy path still requires the manifest.
Constraints: The synthetic CohortManifest's `cells` tuple must be built from `CohortManifestCell.from_dict(...)` (or the equivalent) so the downstream gate predicates work unchanged. The synthetic `frozen_at_commit` field can be a placeholder ("0" * 40) because no committed manifest is being signed-against — the primaries themselves carry their own commit-pinning via git history. The helper's `roster_path` and `label_admitted_path` are kwargs (not positional) so tests can override them for synthetic fixtures. The new path MUST NOT silently widen the legacy scope: tests should verify the legacy mode still emits its `<thing>_argument_required` diagnostic when its required arg is omitted.
Validation Evidence: 4 new tests in `tools/local_labels/test_run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py` cover: smoke against real artifacts (`_derive_sm120_cohort_from_committed_artifacts` returns 30 cells matching the active sm_120 cohort); roster-missing → FileNotFoundError naming the path; empty intersection → RuntimeError; end-to-end positive (sm_120 derived-cohort path with stub producer emits 30 matching bundles); 1 scope-pin test verifies the legacy sm_86 path still requires `--cohort-manifest`. Focused suite: 198 passed, 3 skipped.
Source Rounds: round-17 (driver required --cohort-manifest for both paths) → round-18 (sm_120 path derives from committed artifacts).

## Lesson: test-fixture-shape-must-mirror-real-artifact-shape
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-test-fixture-shape-must-mirror-real-shape
Scope: Validators / parsers / consumers that read a structured artifact (JSON bundle, schema-bound document, protobuf, etc.) and ALSO have a test helper that synthesizes the artifact for unit tests. Especially relevant when the artifact's field layout is non-trivial (some fields at top level, some nested) and the test helper bakes in the WRONG layout.
Problem Description: A validator helper checks an invariant against a field at a chosen nesting depth (e.g., `manifest_pairing_index.cache_layer == "L2"`). A test fixture helper synthesizes bundles with the field at THE SAME depth. The validator + fixture agree, so all unit tests pass. But the REAL producer puts the field at a DIFFERENT depth (e.g., top-level `cache_layer`). The validator's check, run against real artifacts, fails on every artifact — but no test catches this because the synthetic fixtures used the validator's assumed depth, not the real producer's. Codex Round 18 review on this project caught exactly this pattern: my `_validate_sm120_producer_bundle_set` checked `mpi.get("cache_layer")`, my `_mk_bundle` test helper put `cache_layer` inside MPI, all 7 synthetic tests passed, but the active sm_120 first-class path would have aborted all 30 committed predictor bundles as `wrong_cache_layer` on the real shape.
Root Cause: A test fixture's job is to mirror real artifact shape, NOT to mirror the implementation's hidden assumptions about where fields live. When the fixture is co-authored with the validator, it is easy to put fields wherever the validator looks for them — which is a circular dependency that hides field-shape skew. The validator and the fixture both share the bug; no test can catch a bug that lives in BOTH halves of the validation pair.
Solution: For any validator over a structured artifact:
  1. Identify the CANONICAL source of the artifact's field layout (the real producer's output, the schema definition, the contract). Cross-check against multiple authoritative readings: the producer's code, the consumer's read sites, the schema (if any).
  2. Author the test fixture to mirror the canonical layout — not the validator's assumed layout. If the producer emits `cache_layer` at top level, the fixture emits `cache_layer` at top level, regardless of how the validator's code looks today.
  3. Add a real-artifact regression test: load a committed real artifact (or call the real producer in a tmpdir) and run the validator over it. Assert the expected outcome. This catches field-shape skew immediately if it ever re-introduces — the synthetic fixtures may pass, but the real-artifact probe will fail.
  4. The real-artifact regression test SHOULD also sanity-check the canonical layout itself (e.g., assert that the real bundles HAVE the top-level field and DO NOT have it inside MPI). This makes the contract the test guards self-documenting; a future producer change that moves the field gets caught by the sanity check before the validator-level failure is investigated.
  5. Cross-reference the validator's reading with adjacent code in the codebase. If two readers agree on a different field path than the validator, the validator is wrong. (Here: `comparison_runner._bundle_join_key` and `cache_predictor.predict_cache_hit_rate` both treat `cache_layer` as top-level; my validator alone read MPI.)
Constraints: The real-artifact regression test can be skipped (via `pytest.skip`) when the artifact is absent (e.g., on a fresh checkout where the active sweep isn't yet built), so it does NOT make the test suite fragile in CI / fresh-checkout settings. The default behavior is to fail with a clear message naming the missing artifact. For test fixtures with optional fields, the synthetic should NOT include the field by default — that pins the absent-field case, which is often the contract's default branch (here: comparison_runner defaults absent cache_layer to "L2"; the validator inherits the same default).
Validation Evidence: 2 new tests in `tools/local_labels/test_run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py`: one pins the L2-default behavior when top-level `cache_layer` is absent (the validator MUST NOT require the field; an absent field is treated as L2 per `comparison_runner._bundle_join_key`'s contract); one loads the actual committed 30 sm_120 predictor bundles + the derived cohort and asserts the validator returns ok with zero defects across all six categories. The second test additionally sanity-checks that the real bundles have top-level `cache_layer` and NO MPI cache_layer field, so a future regression that re-introduces the Round-18 field-shape mismatch is caught at the sanity-check level. Focused suite: 200 passed, 3 skipped. Direct CLI probe on the real bundles: validator returns ok=True (was ok=False with 30 wrong_cache_layer on the Round-18 implementation).
Source Rounds: round-18 (introduced field-shape mismatch) → round-19 (fixed validator + fixture + added real-bundle regression).

## Lesson: same-family-bucket-replacement-pool-inherits-original-split
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-replacement-pool-inherits-original-split
Scope: Any loop that pins a per-cell split assignment (calibration / validation / holdout) on a frozen roster AND uses a same-family-bucket replacement pool to substitute typed-excluded primary cells. The committed effective split partition diverges from the roster's literal split tags whenever any primary is replaced; downstream consumers (calibration fitters, residual block aggregators, leakage scanners) need an explicit mapping to recover the effective split contract.
Problem Description: A plan pins the cohort split sizes (here: AC-2.3's 15/8/7 calibration/validation/holdout for the sm_120 30-cell cohort) AND a replacement pool. When a primary cell typed-excludes during the sweep, a same-family-bucket replacement cell substitutes (per the family-bucket cap discipline). The replacement row's roster `split` field is `"replacement"`, but its EFFECTIVE split when admitted is the typed-excluded primary's split. A naive consumer that reads the roster's literal `split` field will under-count the calibration / validation / holdout buckets by the number of replacements and over-count the `"replacement"` bucket; the AC-2.3 contract is then visibly violated even though the substitution discipline was honored.
Root Cause: The roster's `split` field encodes the candidate's INITIAL ROLE, not its FINAL EFFECTIVE ROLE in the admitted cohort. The substitution mapping (which primary each replacement filled in for) is metadata the roster does NOT carry directly — without explicit substitution metadata, the effective partition is unreconstructible from the committed artifacts alone.
Solution: Commit an explicit `EFFECTIVE_SPLIT_FOR_ADMITTED_REPLACEMENT` map (or equivalent sidecar TOML/JSON) that records each admitted replacement's effective split. The map's keys are the admitted replacement `(slug, shape_point)` tuples; the values are the inherited split string (calibration / validation / holdout). The map is small (typically <10 entries) and easily audited: each entry documents which primary it replaces. Consumers then resolve the effective split as: roster primary split if `split ∈ {calibration, validation, holdout}`; replacement-map lookup if `split == "replacement"`; typed defect if an admitted replacement is NOT in the map. Pin the effective-split partition counts against the AC's pinned contract (e.g., 15/8/7) as a precondition check before the consumer's main logic runs.
Constraints: The map MUST be kept in lockstep with the cohort state. When a new replacement admits in a future round, the map must be extended; the consumer's pre-condition `assert_effective_split_counts(...)` will fire on mismatch and force the update. The map's path is the producer's source file (a module-level dict constant), not a sidecar — this keeps it inside the same git history as the consumer code that reads it, so a substitution-introducing commit and its map update land in the same change. Tests that build synthetic cohort fixtures can override the map via a kwarg to exercise different substitution scenarios.
Validation Evidence: `tools/local_labels/fit_calibration_head_v4_sm120.py` exports `EFFECTIVE_SPLIT_FOR_ADMITTED_REPLACEMENT` with the 2 admitted Round-4/8 replacements (`cross_entropy_fwd_fp32_sm80/correctness_shape` → calibration; `gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80/trace_l2_accuracy_lg` → validation). `resolve_effective_splits` + `assert_effective_split_counts` enforce the 15/8/7 partition; `_derive_sm120_cohort_from_committed_artifacts` is structurally separate (it returns the 30-cell cohort + a CohortManifest-shaped object), but both helpers consume the same roster + label_admitted primaries. The AC-6.2 split-leakage scanner consumes the same map. 14 new focused tests pin the partition + leakage scanner + historical-only retirement; 227 focused tests pass overall.
Source Rounds: round-20 (2026-05-27).

## Lesson: two-commit-landing-sequence-for-source-commit-pinning
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-two-commit-landing-source-commit-pinning
Scope: Producer/fitter scripts that emit artifacts pinning their own `source_commit` (= the git HEAD at emission time). Especially relevant when (a) the producer is NEW in the current commit, AND (b) the emitted artifact is committed alongside the producer.
Problem Description: A producer script is added in commit C1; an operator runs the producer from the worktree BEFORE C1 lands, captures `source_commit = git rev-parse HEAD` (= the PARENT of C1), and writes that into the emitted artifact. The artifact is then committed in C1 (or a sibling commit). On disk the artifact's `source_commit` resolves as a valid SHA (it's a real commit), but `git cat-file -e <source_commit>:<producer_path>` FAILS — the producer didn't exist at that revision. The artifact is not reproducible from the commit it claims. Codex Round 20 review on this project caught exactly this with v4 sidecars: `source_commit = 7a6218f6...` (Round 19's HEAD) where the v4 fitter was introduced by Round 20's `9843acdd`.
Root Cause: `git rev-parse HEAD` at producer-invocation time captures the previous commit when the producer is still uncommitted. The naive "fit then commit both producer and artifact in one commit" flow records the parent SHA in the artifact, but the artifact lands in a child commit that contains the producer. The artifact is "rewindable" but to a different revision than the one it claims.
Solution: Two-commit landing sequence:
  1. **Commit A** — land the producer (and, when adding the contract for the first time, the corresponding provenance regression test). Commit A's HEAD now contains the producer source.
  2. **Commit B** — regenerate the artifact from Commit A's worktree. `git rev-parse HEAD` now returns Commit A's SHA, which the artifact records. Commit B lands the regenerated artifact only (the producer source is unchanged across A→B).
The provenance regression test (added in Commit A) asserts: (i) `git cat-file -e <source_commit>^{commit}` succeeds; (ii) `git cat-file -e <source_commit>:<producer_path>` succeeds; (iii) the producer source at that commit contains the contract-required emitter surfaces (field names, helper names). All three checks block future regressions where the producer is edited but the artifact is regenerated from an uncommitted worktree.
Constraints: For a one-shot producer + artifact landing, the two-commit sequence is mandatory if the artifact's `source_commit` is contract-required reproducible. For a producer that has already landed (e.g., the artifact is being regenerated under an established producer), a single commit is fine — `git rev-parse HEAD` returns the established producer's commit. The provenance test should skip-on-missing the artifact (so fresh checkouts without the artifact don't fail the test suite), but MUST fail loudly when the artifact is present but the source_commit points at a tree that lacks the producer. The producer-emitter-surface inspection MUST use verbatim substrings (e.g., `'"calibration_head_version"'` including the quotes) so a substring search admits only the actual field-literal occurrences and not lossy paraphrases in docstrings.
Validation Evidence: Round 21 closed Codex Round 20 review's blocking issue via this exact two-commit sequence. Step A (`735d8ddd`) landed `tools/local_labels/test_calibration_head_v4_provenance_scanner.py` (6 tests). On the stale Round-20-committed sidecars, 3/6 tests failed deterministically (the head + eval `_contains_v4_fitter` tests + the head `_fitter_has_v4_emitter_surfaces` test). Step B (`03f3c4cc`) regenerated `data/local_labels/calibration_head_v4.json` + `data/local_labels/calibration_evaluation_v4.json` from step-A's HEAD; all 6 tests now pass. Trained-head identity reproduces deterministically (PlattCalibrator, LOOCV p90=0.29046102404...). 233 focused tests pass; claim guard scans 47 paths with 0 defects.
Source Rounds: round-20 (introduced defect) → round-21 (closed via two-commit landing).

Update Note (round-17 → round-18, v5 RLCR loop): the same hazard recurred in a NEW MODE — the producer (v4 fitter) GAINED a new emitted field (`chosen_head_parameters`) in Commit A of a two-commit-landing round, but the artifact regen happened from the worktree BEFORE Commit A landed, so the artifact's `source_commit` resolved to the PRIOR HEAD. The pre-existing provenance scanner enforced "source_commit resolves" + "producer file exists at that commit" + "emitter surfaces (the OLD set) appear in the source at that commit" — but the scanner's surface list was not updated to include the new field's literal, so the stale-`source_commit` artifact passed silently even though the producer at that revision could not have emitted the new field. Fix has two parts: (a) the scanner's emitter-surface list MUST be extended in lockstep with every new field added to the artifact payload (a process-discipline checklist item for every PR that extends the artifact payload); (b) the artifact regen MUST happen AFTER Commit A lands so `git rev-parse HEAD` returns Commit A's SHA (the original two-commit-landing discipline). The Round-18 closure (Commit A `656e474a` adds `'"chosen_head_parameters"'` to the surface list; Commit B `db909778` re-regenerates the v4 artifacts so `source_commit=656e474a` resolves to a HEAD whose fitter contains the new emitter) demonstrates the combined fix. Generalized rule: when adding a new emitted field to a producer's artifact payload, also add the field's literal to any provenance scanner's emitter-surface list in the SAME commit.

Update Note (round-26/27 → round-28, missing-source_commit-on-some-emitters axis): the two-commit-landing discipline implicitly assumes the emitter actually WRITES source_commit. Round 28's audit caught that `tools/local_labels/emit_v5_closure_artifacts.py` — which produces 3 closure artifacts (accuracy_gates, falsification, reliability_diagnostics) — never wrote `source_commit` AT ALL, even though Round 26 + Round 27 summaries claimed "source_commit pinned to Commit A across the full v5 sequence". The fitter (`fit_monotonic_calibration_head_v5.py`) does write source_commit (3 call sites), and other emitters (nugteren2014_rdh_baseline, run_v5_active_path_comparison, emit_v5_robustness_artifact) also write it — but the closure emitter was an under-counted gap. The summaries' claim was based on the OTHER emitters being source-pinned; the closure emitter silently fell outside the scope. The fix: when a multi-emitter pipeline pins source_commit, every emitter must do so (it's not enough for "most" of them to). For artifacts derived from a primary head (e.g., falsification is computed from calibration_head_v5.json), the derived artifact should pin BOTH its own `source_commit` (= emitter's git HEAD) AND `source_v5_head_commit` (= the primary head's `source_commit`); this lets a reviewer cross-check that the derived artifact reflects the same cohort scope the primary head was trained on, AND identify the emitter code that produced the derived artifact. The verification protocol: `for art in <list>; do jq '.source_commit, .source_v5_head_commit' $art; done` and assert no `null` in the output. A scanner test should pin this for the multi-artifact case (Round 28 added `test_falsification_artifact_carries_source_provenance_fields` covering both fields' presence + cross-check that source_v5_head_commit matches the current v5 head's source_commit). Source rounds: round-26/27 (closure emitter never wrote source_commit; summaries claimed pinning that was only true for OTHER emitters; Codex Round 27 review caught via direct `jq` inspection) → round-28 (Commit A adds `_git_head_sha()` helper + writes both fields; Commit B regenerates the artifact). The other two closure artifacts (`accuracy_gates`, `reliability_diagnostics`) STILL lack source_commit and are queued for a follow-on round per Codex's scoped resolution path.

Update Note (round-28 → round-29, scanner-coverage-completes-the-fix axis): the round-28 fix was narrow by design (Codex's required implementation plan named the falsification artifact only). Round 29's audit revealed the gap was 6 of 9 artifacts deep — accuracy_gates + reliability_diagnostics had neither field, robustness + baseline_comparison + active_path_comparison_bundle + per-arch heads had source_v5_head_commit but not source_commit. The Round 28 fix-then-queue-the-rest pattern was correct for round-bounded scope discipline; the follow-on round must close every remaining instance OR the gap recurs the moment a summary claims "source_commit pinned across the full v5 sequence" again. The Round 29 close: 4 emitter modules (`emit_v5_closure_artifacts.py::_emit_ac3_gates` + `_emit_reliability`; `emit_v5_robustness_artifact.py`; `nugteren2014_rdh_baseline.py`; `run_v5_active_path_comparison.py`) each gain a local `_git_head_sha()` helper + add both provenance fields to every artifact dict site (including fail-closed paths, not just the OK path — a fail-closed artifact's source_commit is still useful for auditing WHICH emitter code produced the fail-closed dict). NEW table-driven scanner test (`test_all_derived_v5_artifacts_carry_source_provenance` under `V5CohortCoverageScannerTest`) enumerates ALL 8 derived v5 artifacts via `subTest`, asserts both fields present + 40-char hex SHA shape + `source_v5_head_commit` matches current v5 head's `source_commit`. This is the meta-lesson: when a multi-emitter pattern recurs across a codebase, the scanner test that pins it must enumerate EVERY instance — leaving 7 of 8 to "follow-on rounds" reproduces the original "most but not all" defect class one level up. Source rounds: round-28 (3 of 9 artifacts pinned, falsification fixed + queued the rest with explicit acknowledgement) → round-29 (Queued Side Issue 1 promoted to mainline because Round 28's remaining BSI was operator-blocked; all 6 missing emitters extended; table-driven scanner test pins all 8 going forward; Commit B regen lands all artifacts at the same SHA). Round 29 validation evidence: full sweep 1207 passed (+1 from Round 28's 1206 — the new table-driven scanner test contains 8 subtests one per artifact path); `jq` audit on 9 v5 artifacts confirms every derived artifact carries both fields + the primary v5 head carries source_commit.

Update Note (sm86 cohort regen loop, ledger-artifact_sha256 + provenance-scanner axis): the two-commit-landing pattern is also the canonical answer for a producer-side `artifact_sha256` extension. The sm86 cohort regen ledger emitter (`tools/local_labels/build_sm86_cohort_regen_ledger.py`) gained 3 new sidecar surfaces in a prior round (sector_stream_mpi + divergence_proxies + order_projections) wired into `_resolve_sidecar_paths`, but a subsequent round's `artifact_sha256` emission still hashed only the original 5 keys. The ledger then recorded paths for 6 sidecars without digests for 3 of them — an AC-1/task7 audit-completeness gap. Compounded: the producer's `source_commit` capture happens at emit time via `git rev-parse HEAD`, and on the round that extended the resolver, the artifact was regenerated AFTER the resolver code was staged but BEFORE it was committed; `HEAD` therefore returned the PARENT commit, and the ledger's `source_commit` pointed at a tree that did not contain the resolver extension. An audit replay from the recorded commit produced a 5-key digest ledger (the pre-extension shape), not the 6-key paths + 5-key digests ledger on disk. Fix pattern (mirrors the falsification artifact fix from round-28 of the v5 loop, generalized to the ledger surface): (a) Commit A extends `artifact_sha256` emission to cover all eight audited artifacts (5 original + 3 new), extends the schema's `artifact_sha256.properties` + admitted-arm `required` list, extends the coverage scanner's admitted-entry digest check, and adds a NEW provenance scanner (`test_sm86_cohort_regen_ledger_provenance.py`) that asserts `ledger.source_commit` + every per-cell `producer_commit` value resolves (via `git show <commit>:<producer_path>`) to a tree containing the canonical post-extension resolver markers (here: both `"divergence_proxies"` and `"order_projections"` as quoted literal substrings in the producer source). (b) Commit B re-emits the ledger from a HEAD containing Commit A; the recorded `source_commit` now equals Commit A's SHA, and the provenance scanner's two tests pass. Generalized rule (extends BL-20260527's two-commit-landing rule): when extending an audit-emission producer in a way that changes the artifact's structure, ALWAYS land code in Commit A + add a provenance scanner whose checks fail against the pre-Commit-B ledger (catching the regression at test time), then re-emit in Commit B from the Commit-A HEAD. The provenance scanner's "resolver markers" should be a list of quoted-literal substrings unique to the post-extension producer (here `'"divergence_proxies"'` + `'"order_projections"'`); for future extensions the markers should be the new field names or key constants the extension introduces. The scanner becomes the anti-regression for the NEXT extension, not just a one-time check. Source rounds: sm86 cohort regen loop's Round 28 closure of Codex Round 27 review's two AC-1/task7 audit blockers; commits f3f73949 (Commit A: code + scanner) + fb628aa2 (Commit B: ledger re-emit) + a2941914 (plan-token re-prose).

Update Note (sm86 develop-only loop, AMEND-not-new-commit + digest-CORRECTNESS-arm axis): two coupled corollaries surfaced when a follow-up round had to repair a ledger that a PRIOR round's provenance restamp had left digest-stale. (1) WINDOW MATH — AMEND, don't add a commit. The prior round's restamp (label/bundle `source_commit` = commit X) was committed as commit C with parent X, so the accept-window scanners (`test_post_commit_source_commit_drift`, `test_regeneration_delta_scanner`, which require the recorded commit ∈ `{HEAD, HEAD^}` computed at runtime) passed with X = HEAD^. A NATURAL follow-up commit C2 (parent C) would advance HEAD so that X = HEAD^^ — OUT of window — silently RE-breaking those scanners even though the follow-up changed nothing about the restamp. The fix is to `git commit --amend` commit C (parent stays X) so the follow-up folds into the single post-baseline commit and X stays HEAD^. Generalized rule: once a round lands a restamp at HEAD^, EVERY subsequent same-loop round that must touch tracked files MUST amend that commit (or re-restamp to the new HEAD, which is strictly more churn — it re-runs the whole restamp+propagation cascade and re-changes sidecar bytes → re-syncs digests); a fresh commit is only safe once the restamped artifacts are no longer accept-window-gated. Verify post-amend with `git rev-parse HEAD^` == the restamp value. (2) DIGEST-CORRECTNESS arm, not just presence. The ledger coverage scanner had a non-null arm (`artifact_sha256.* != ""`) but NO arm verifying the recorded digest against the referenced file's actual bytes. So when the prior round regenerated the carveout sidecars (MPI source_commit restamp → new bytes → new SHA-256) but left the ledger's recorded hashes stale, the full sweep stayed green and the drift shipped. A presence-only digest check is a false-confidence guard for any ledger that records hashes of regenerable artifacts. Fix: add a DIGEST-CORRECTNESS arm — for each admitted entry + each `artifact_sha256` role, resolve the path (`predictor_bundle_path`, `trace_dir/dynamic_trace.pb`, `sidecar_paths[role]`); when the file EXISTS locally, recompute SHA-256 and require equality with the recorded digest; host-tolerant skip when absent (sidecars are gitignored host-local, so a fresh checkout / other-arch host legitimately lacks them), counting+surfacing the skip so a fully-absent run reads "0 verified" not a silent pass. Regression-prove it: revert the digest fix → the arm fails catching every drifted role → restore → passes. Source rounds: sm86 develop-only loop Round 2 (introduced the digest drift via the carveout sidecar restamp; full sweep green because the coverage arm was presence-only) → Round 3 (synced the 5 carveout digests recomputed-fresh + added the digest-correctness arm; AMENDED the Round-2 commit 9750b206 → a0af7fe8 to keep eb780459 = HEAD^; full sweep 5053 passed / 0 failed).

Update Note (sm86 multimetric loop, already-committed-bad-artifact + content-vs-window-scanner axis): a third mode where the artifact was committed BAD in a prior round and the producer had ALREADY landed separately. The register-spill build-variant manifest (`data/local_labels/build_variants/register_spill_local_mem_sm_86.json`) was emitted + committed in the SAME commit (be31ace1) as its producer (`emit_register_cap_variants.py`), so it recorded `source_commit=8f3342ef` (the parent, which lacks the producer) — the classic one-commit defect. Codex caught it a round later. The repair is SIMPLER than the canonical two-commit landing precisely because the producer's landing commit (be31ace1) already exists as a distinct ancestor: a SINGLE re-emit commit suffices — re-run the emitter at HEAD=be31ace1 so `git rev-parse HEAD` returns the producer's landing commit, then commit the re-emitted artifact (the producer landing IS "Commit A" already; the re-emit IS "Commit B"). No need to manufacture a fresh producer commit. Only `source_commit` + per-build `binary_sha256` snapshots change; deterministic identity fields (kernel signatures from geometry+regs) regenerate identically, so the regeneration test compares those, not the binary hash. SECOND corollary — choose the scanner's commit predicate deliberately. The accept-window scanners in the note above (`test_post_commit_source_commit_drift`) require the recorded commit ∈ a runtime-computed `{HEAD, HEAD^}` window, which is correct for FRESHLY-restamped artifacts that must track the moving tip but becomes a drift-failure on every later commit and forces AMEND discipline. For a one-shot provenance fact ("is this artifact reproducible from the commit it claims") use a CONTENT+ANCESTRY predicate instead: assert (i) the recorded commit is real and `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sc> HEAD`, (ii) `git show <sc>:<producer_path>` succeeds AND contains the verbatim field-literal substrings the artifact's shape implies, (iii) the same for any documented build-path markers (here the Makefile `SPILL_LADDER_CAPS` + `spill-variants`). A content+ancestry scanner stays GREEN as later commits land (it does not couple to the tip), so it never joins the accept-window drift-failure set and needs no amend discipline. Pin discrimination with a NEGATIVE case: feed the scanner the known pre-producer commit and assert it is flagged producer-absent (proves the positive test is not vacuously green). Skip ONLY when no git work tree is available (the check cannot run at all); never skip on a bad/unrecorded source_commit — that is the failure the scanner exists to catch. Source rounds: sm86 multimetric NCU-replacement loop — register_cap build-variant manifest committed one-commit-bad in be31ace1 (Codex review caught the stale `source_commit=8f3342ef`) → next round re-emitted from be31ace1 (single commit 725023b9) + added the content+ancestry scanner `test_register_cap_variant_provenance.py` (positive test failed against the old commit, passes after re-emit; negative + fail-closed cases pin discrimination); full `tools/local_labels/` sweep 1595 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (+3 new, zero new failures).


## Lesson: cross-host-sidecar-staging-blocks-cohort-closure
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-cross-host-sidecar-staging-blocks-cohort-closure
Scope: cross-host RLCR loop opening; data-staging audit before accepting a plan's cohort-count assumption; CLAUDE.md cross-host survival semantics for gitignored derived artifacts under `data/local_labels/sweeps/*/predictor_bundles/`, `workloads/simbench_bw/results/<slug>/<sku>/<arch>/trace_<shape>/sharing_locality/sharing_locality.json`, `.../hrd_profile/hrd_profile.json`, and `.../sector_stream/sector_stream.jsonl`.
Problem Description: A new RLCR loop's plan body pins a cohort count (e.g. "30 sm_120 + 44 sm_86 NCU-labeled cells") and the loop's lower bound assumes those cells' derived artifacts are reachable on the receiving host. The labels themselves DO survive both git fetch and raw scp (`data/local_labels/label_admitted.json` is tracked), but the cohort's downstream derived artifacts — auxiliary sidecars (sharing_locality, hrd_profile) for activation/tensor slugs under `workloads/portable_lcd/results/`, the simbench_bw L1 sidecars under `workloads/simbench_bw/results/`, and the sm_86 predictor bundles + raw NVBit traces — are gitignored AND were not included in the most recent raw-scp transfer to the receiving host. The loop opens, the producer/fitter code lands cleanly, every test passes, the schema validates, the v5 calibration head's artifact emits — and 72/74 cells report `canonical_pre_calibration_features_absent` / `predictor_bundle_absent` / `sharing_locality sidecar absent`. The accuracy gates land `status=indeterminate_insufficient_data`, the falsification arm lands the same, the reliability diagnostics land the same. The lower bound is not met. Codex Round 0 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop demanded "no new deferral boundary" but the cohort gap is a DATA-staging issue, not a code defect that more coding can resolve.
Root Cause: CLAUDE.md's "Session Handoff" + "Cross-host survival semantics" enumerates three transport-survival tiers: (1) git-tracked (committed) artifacts survive `git fetch`; (2) gitignored artifacts survive raw scp but NOT `git fetch`; (3) host-local built binaries survive neither. The cohort's derived artifacts under `workloads/portable_lcd/results/...sharing_locality/` and `.../hrd_profile/` fall in tier (2): they were generated on the originating host (where the predictor + sharing_locality + hrd_profile producers ran against the live NVBit traces) but are gitignored, so a `git fetch` to the receiving host drops them. A scp transfer that selectively syncs only the committed tree (or selectively scp's `data/local_labels/` without `workloads/*/results/`) leaves the receiving host with the LABELS but not the DERIVED ARTIFACTS. The plan's lower bound implicitly assumed tier (1) for everything; reality is tier (2) for the load-bearing inputs.
Solution: Round 0 of any RLCR loop whose plan body cites specific cohort counts MUST audit data-staging completeness BEFORE coding consumer-side logic. Concretely:
  1. Run `find data/local_labels workloads/simbench_bw/results workloads/portable_lcd/results -name 'sharing_locality.json' -o -name 'hrd_profile.json' -o -name 'sector_stream.jsonl' -o -name 'dynamic_trace.pb' | wc -l` to count the staged derived-artifact set.
  2. Join the count against the plan's cohort-count assumption. If the staging count is below the expected cohort count, raise a Round-0 Blocking Side Issue with a specific operator follow-up (re-scp the missing subtrees, OR rebuild the derived artifacts from the surviving committed inputs, OR explicitly scope the loop to the reachable subset and document in the goal-tracker's Plan Evolution Log).
  3. Document the audit result in Round 0's contract / summary BEFORE pushing into AC-2+ work. The cohort-count audit is the prerequisite that prevents a loop from emitting 5+ rounds of "indeterminate_insufficient_data" artifacts.
  4. When the audit reveals a staging gap, the loop's downstream consumers (backfill scripts, fitters, gate scanners, falsification arm) MUST land with `status=indeterminate_insufficient_data` rather than silently passing OR silently failing. The artifacts exist with the right shape + provenance + the honest methodological state so a future round (post-staging) re-runs them with NO code change.
Constraints: applies to any RLCR loop that opens on a host that did not produce the cohort's derived artifacts (typical case: a new loop on a fresh-scp 3090 Ti host whose cohort originated on a 5060 laptop sweep, or vice versa). The audit step is cheap (one shell command + a comparison) and pays for itself the first time a loop would otherwise spend several rounds on indeterminate consumer-side outputs. The lesson is bidirectional: applies regardless of which arch's data is missing on the receiving host. The lesson does NOT block legitimate "partial cohort" loops that explicitly scope themselves to a subset in the IMMUTABLE plan section; it blocks loops that assume a full cohort without checking.
Validation Evidence: Round 1 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop emits `data/local_labels/calibration_head_v5.json` with `cohort_scope.admitted=2 cohort_scope.skipped=72`; 28 cells with typed_reason `canonical_pre_calibration_features_absent` (sharing_locality + hrd_profile sidecars not staged for sm_120 activation/tensor slugs); 44 cells with typed_reason `predictor_bundle_absent` (sm_86 cohort bundles + traces not staged). The downstream closure artifacts (`calibration_head_v5_accuracy_gates.json`, `calibration_head_v5_falsification.json`, `calibration_head_v5_reliability_diagnostics.json`) all land `status=indeterminate_insufficient_data`. The architecture is in place and re-runs cleanly once the operator follow-up stages the missing subtrees. Commits `c28e304d` + `e9bc2d05` document the data-staging gap as a Round-1 Blocking Side Issue in the goal tracker.
Source Rounds: round-0 (defect missed; plan accepted without staging audit) → round-1 (defect surfaced when backfill admitted 2/74 cells; lesson abstracted).


## Lesson: producer-cli-placeholder-mpi-vs-production-api
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-producer-cli-placeholder-mpi-vs-production-api
Scope: any wrapper script that batch-invokes a producer for a cohort of cells (e.g. `tools/local_labels/regenerate_v5_trace_sidecars.py`); any producer module that exposes both a `__main__` CLI (`if __name__ == "__main__"`) and an in-process production API (a callable like `emit_*_bundle`); the contract between batch-orchestrators and producer modules.
Problem Description: When a wrapper script batch-invokes a producer via the producer's CLI subprocess (e.g. `python tools/hrd_profiler/bundle_from_trace.py --trace-dir <PATH>`), the wrapper silently inherits whatever placeholder/default identity the CLI uses for ad-hoc inspection. The HRD producer's CLI defaults to `slug="dram_stream_stride_mix"`, `shape_point="trace_00"`, zero hashes, and `--device sm_120-laptop` — values designed for an operator running the CLI by hand against a single trace. Running the wrapper against 25 distinct (arch, slug, shape_point) cells via that CLI produces 25 sidecar bundles whose `manifest_pairing_index` does NOT match the actual cell identity. The bundles validate against schema (the CLI's placeholders are syntactically valid) but the 5-field identity tuple is wrong, so downstream consumers that join on (slug, shape_point, binary_sha256, source_commit, kernel_signature_sha256) admit cells against the wrong rows or silently fall through. The producer module's own docstring documents this: "The CLI keeps ad-hoc convenience defaults for operator inspection. The in-process production API accepts real manifest_pairing_index, device, and cache_model_resolver_input from the caller; this function does NOT fabricate placeholders." But a batch-wrapper that uses the CLI for simplicity inherits the placeholders by default.
Root Cause: Producer modules in this codebase typically expose a thin CLI that takes a single positional argument (`--trace-dir <PATH>`) and an in-process production API that takes the full identity + device + cache-model resolver inputs as kwargs. The CLI's purpose is operator-side ad-hoc inspection of a single trace; it fabricates placeholders precisely BECAUSE the operator hasn't supplied them. A batch wrapper that calls the CLI loses access to the per-cell identity that the orchestrator already knows (it has just read it from the staging manifest) — the CLI's placeholders silently win. The hazard is invisible at the wrapper-level (no exception, no schema violation, no obvious error in the report) because the placeholder values are well-formed; only the identity-binding contract downstream catches the corruption, and even then a join that admits the wrong rows can score correctly numerically without surfacing the mis-binding.
Solution: A batch wrapper that orchestrates per-cell producer calls MUST call the producer's in-process production API (e.g. `emit_hrd_bundle(...)` from `tools.hrd_profiler.bundle_from_trace`) NOT the CLI subprocess. The wrapper passes the per-cell identity resolved from authoritative on-disk artifacts (typically the admitted predictor bundle's `manifest_pairing_index` field) + the arch-specific device block + the L2 cache inputs. When the per-cell identity cannot be resolved (e.g. no admitted bundle exists yet), the wrapper MUST fail the cell with a typed reason like `<producer>_mpi_unresolvable:<context>` and write NO on-disk sidecar; fabricating placeholder identity to satisfy the schema is forbidden. The wrapper's test surface MUST include: (a) a positive test that monkeypatches the producer's in-process function and asserts the wrapper passes real per-cell kwargs (not the CLI defaults); (b) per-arch positive tests proving the wrapper passes the correct arch-specific device block (e.g. sm_86 cells use sm_86 device, not the sm_120 CLI default); (c) a typed-fail-closed test proving the wrapper records the typed reason + writes no sidecar when MPI is unresolvable. A subprocess wrapper is acceptable ONLY when the producer's CLI is a real producer (takes `--trace-dir` only because the producer truly needs no other inputs — sector_stream/consume_trace.py is an example: the trace_dir is sufficient input, no per-cell identity needs to be passed because the output sidecar is consumed by joining on the trace_dir's parent path).
Constraints: applies to any batch-orchestrator wrapping a producer whose CLI fabricates ANY input the production API requires. The discriminator: read the producer module's `__main__` block. If it sets default values for any kwarg of the production API (especially identity-binding fields like `slug`, `shape_point`, `binary_sha256`, `source_commit`, `kernel_signature_sha256`, OR device-dispatch fields like `--device`), the CLI is ad-hoc and the wrapper MUST use the in-process API. If the CLI is a thin shell that takes only the same kwargs as the production API + parses them from argparse, the subprocess wrapper is safe. Run a one-time audit on every producer used by a batch wrapper before shipping the wrapper.
Validation Evidence: Round 16 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop patched `_regenerate_hrd_profile` to call `tools.hrd_profiler.bundle_from_trace.emit_hrd_bundle(...)` in-process with real MPI resolved from the admitted predictor bundle. 6 focused tests under `tools/local_labels/test_regenerate_v5_trace_sidecars.py::HRDRegenInProcessDispatchTest` pin the contract: real MPI passed verbatim; sm_86 cell uses sm_86 device (RTX 3090 Ti, 6 MiB L2, 6 slices); sm_120 cell uses sm_120 device (RTX 5060 Laptop, 32 MiB L2); typed-fail-closed when MPI is unresolvable; typed-fail when arch is unknown; typed-fail when emit_hrd_bundle returns contract_ok=False. Live wrapper run (under `uv run --with protobuf`) regenerated 25/25 HRD profiles correctly; v5 fitter admitted_count jumped from 2 to 27 (the 25 newly-regen-sidecar cells now admit, plus the 2 pre-existing). emit_v5_closure_artifacts shifted from `status=indeterminate_insufficient_data` to `ac3.status=ok / fals.verdict=confirmed / reliability.status=ok` for the first time in the loop. Commits `879e3cb6` (Commit A) + `0d092e0a` (Commit B) document the wrapper fix + the downstream 41-artifact regeneration.
Source Rounds: round-15 (defect introduced when wrapper landed using the CLI subprocess; Codex Round 15 review flagged the placeholder-MPI hazard) → round-16 (resolved via in-process API + 6 focused tests + 25-cell live verification).


## Lesson: typed-unavailable-bundle-root-cause-vs-symptom-classification
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-typed-unavailable-bundle-root-cause-vs-symptom-classification
Scope: any per-cell classifier (e.g. `_build_cell_records` in `tools/local_labels/fit_monotonic_calibration_head_v5.py`) that walks a cohort, joins each cell against a producer-emitted bundle, and assigns a typed `typed_reason` to skipped cells; the contract between the producer's typed-unavailability signal and the consumer's audit-trail classification.
Problem Description: A consumer-side classifier walks per-cell bundles in an ordered chain of checks. The first level is "did the bundle exist?" → `predictor_bundle_absent` typed_reason. The second level is "did the bundle have the expected derived field?" (e.g. `canonical_pre_calibration_features`) → `<field>_absent` typed_reason. The classifier OMITS a middle level: "did the producer record a typed-unavailability signal on the bundle itself?" (e.g. `bundle.result_kind == "typed_unavailable"` with a non-empty `bundle.typed_reason` naming the producer-side root cause). Cells whose bundle records `typed_unavailable` + a real root cause (e.g. `cache_model_underconfigured` on cells where the cache-model resolver couldn't configure an L2 model for the trace data) silently fall through to the second level's downstream `<field>_absent` SYMPTOM, because the derived field is None precisely BECAUSE the producer typed-out. The staging manifest and the v5 head end up with DIFFERENT typed_reason values for the same cell — the staging manifest correctly records `BUNDLE_TYPED_UNAVAILABLE:<root_cause>` (since the manifest reads the bundle's `result_kind`), but the consumer's v5 head records the downstream symptom (since the v5 head's classifier never checks `result_kind`). Audit-trail consumers downstream (gate emitters, baseline comparison emitters) propagate whichever value they read first, splitting the cell's accounting across two reasons that mean different things.
Root Cause: Classifier chains are typically built additively as a project discovers new failure modes — `predictor_bundle_absent` lands first when bundles are simply missing, `<field>_absent` lands when bundles exist but the derived field is None, and the intermediate `bundle.result_kind == "typed_unavailable"` case isn't explicitly added because the field-absent fallthrough handles it accidentally. The fallthrough is correct on "field absent" mechanics but wrong on "audit-trail meaning": the root cause is what the operator should investigate, not the symptom. The staging manifest's parallel classifier was built later with the explicit `BUNDLE_TYPED_UNAVAILABLE:<reason>` branch (Round 13's READY classifier fix landed it). The two classifiers diverged because the consumer-side fitter wasn't updated in lockstep.
Solution: When a per-cell classifier chain assigns typed reasons, the chain MUST include an explicit check for the producer's typed-unavailability signal (e.g. `bundle.result_kind == "typed_unavailable"` + `bundle.typed_reason` non-empty) BEFORE any downstream-symptom check. The check propagates the producer's `typed_reason` verbatim onto the cell's skipped row. A defensive fall-through (e.g. typed_unavailable bundle with empty typed_reason → still falls to the symptom path) preserves the safety net for malformed bundles.
