A regression test pins the contract with synthetic bundles: (i) typed_unavailable + reason → root cause propagates; (ii) typed_unavailable + empty reason → symptom fallback; (iii) scalar + features → admit unchanged; (iv) scalar + missing features → symptom path still fires (the new branch only catches typed_unavailable). When the producer's typed-unavailability values are an enumerated set (e.g. `cache_model_underconfigured`), the schema for the consumer-side artifact MUST admit those values — usually as a free-text typed_reason field, not an enum, because the producer side may add new typed reasons over time.
Constraints: applies whenever a producer emits a "typed_unavailable" signal AND a downstream consumer classifies the cell from the bundle. The fix is a 1-branch addition in the classifier chain, but the audit trail's consistency depends on it being added in EVERY downstream consumer that emits typed_reason — the v5 head's `cohort_scope.skipped` + the baseline rows' `<column>_reason` + the gate artifacts' `skipped_cell_reasons` all carry these reasons, and all must read from the same source-of-truth (typically the v5 head's classifier output, propagated downstream). The lesson does NOT apply when the consumer intentionally wants the symptom for its purposes (e.g. a diagnostic that's specifically counting "field-absent" cells); in that case the consumer should still mention the root cause in a sibling field but may keep the symptom for its primary metric.
Validation Evidence: Round 19 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop landed the propagation branch in `_build_cell_records` (Commit A `fecc99d5`) + 4-case regression test under `tools/local_labels/test_fit_v5_typed_unavailable_propagation.py::TypedUnavailableBundlePropagationTest`. After regeneration (Commit B `ff6cddd5`), the 3 sm_120 cells (`l2_persisting_window_sweep`, `working_set_capacity_sweep`, `warm_cold_pair_relaunch`) carry `typed_reason="cache_model_underconfigured"` in `data/local_labels/calibration_head_v5.json::cohort_scope.skipped`, in `data/local_labels/calibration_head_v5_falsification.json::skipped_cell_reasons`, and in `data/local_labels/calibration_head_v5_baseline_comparison.json::rows[*].v5_prediction_reason` — matching the staging manifest's `BUNDLE_TYPED_UNAVAILABLE:cache_model_underconfigured` classification. Schema check confirmed `calibration_head_v5.schema.json` does NOT enumerate typed_reason (free-text), so the new value is schema-valid. The cohort-coverage scanner also reads typed_reason as free-text and passes without change.
Source Rounds: round-18 (defect surfaced by Codex review on the 3 sm_120 cells) → round-19 (resolved via classifier patch + downstream regen + 4-case regression).
Update Note (round-18 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop, 2026-05-29 — an HONEST typed-unavailable arm can still ship INTERNALLY-INCONSISTENT evidence fields): Codex [P2] on `tools/adaptive_sampler/run_sampler.py`: the offline one-window arm correctly emits `trace_convergence_window_count_underconfigured` (honest — it refuses to fabricate K+1 windows from one HRD bundle) with `observed_window_count=1`, but hard-coded `actual_trace_bytes = K_CONSECUTIVE_WINDOWS(3) * TRACE_WINDOW_SIZE_BYTES` (~48 MiB) while the emitted baseline window carried `trace_bytes_cumulative=0`. So the typed REASON was honest but the accompanying NUMERIC evidence was self-contradictory (1 window vs 3-windows-of-bytes vs 0-bytes), letting an operator read ~48 MiB of evidence (~near the 64 MiB requirement) for a run that ingested one 16 MiB window. GENERAL RULE: emitting the correct typed_reason does NOT absolve the evidence payload — every emitted quantity (observed/required window counts, actual/required trace bytes, AND the emitted window rows' cumulative bytes) must describe the SAME ingested evidence; cross-check them as a set, not just the reason string. FIX: report the one-window byte count consistently across the admission's `actual_trace_bytes` AND the baseline window's `trace_bytes_cumulative` (= `TRACE_WINDOW_SIZE_BYTES`, matching sampler.py's window-0 `(k+1)*window` convention); let `required_trace_bytes` carry the gap. SCOPE the per-arm byte value (a `baseline_trace_bytes` defaulting to 0) so sibling arms (disabled / hrd-unavailable, where `observation_count` can be 0) don't gain a NEW bytes>0-for-0-observations inconsistency from a global change — fixing one arm's emitted-value inconsistency must not introduce another's. Pin the cross-field consistency with a regression assertion expressed as RELATIONSHIPS (`actual == baseline_cumulative == required // required_window_count < required`) rather than a hardcoded magnitude, so it survives a window-size change. Inherited pre-loop debt (d629bbb9); amend per BL-20260527 (run_sampler.py is not a predictor/provenance path).


## Lesson: refresh-helper-scope-includes-sweep-bundles-and-per-cell-arrays
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-refresh-helper-scope-includes-sweep-bundles-and-per-cell-arrays
Scope: any project that uses post-commit `source_commit` / `predictor_build_commit` / `regeneration_build_commit` recapture-discipline helpers; any new evidence sub-tree (e.g., per-sweep `predictor_bundles/*.json`, sweep-level `regeneration_delta.json`, downstream audit-evidence artifacts with per-cell arrays) added AFTER the initial helper set landed. ALSO IN SCOPE (added 2026-06-04, seal-lockstep round): any derived artifact that EMBEDS a refreshed row's source_commit — notably the sealed-holdout labels file, which copies holdout rows verbatim from label_admitted. When the drift-managed refresh set intersects the holdout (carveout_sensitivity_probe is a holdout member), EVERY round-close refresh strands the seal unless the close cycle re-seals after the refresh. Cheap insurance: the sealer is deterministic, so commit a temp-path byte-compare reproducibility test per derived artifact (test_seal_holdout_labels_reproducible.py is the model); then a stale derived artifact cannot survive any commit.
Problem Description: A project lands two refresh helpers covering the initial drift surfaces (`refresh_admitted_label_source_commit.py` for `data/local_labels/label_admitted.json` cells and `refresh_predictor_trace_source_commit.py` for `workloads/simbench_bw/results/<slug>/<arch>/trace_<shape>.json` sidecars). Later rounds add new evidence subtrees: per-sweep `data/local_labels/sweeps/<sweep_dir>/predictor_bundles/<arch>__<slug>__<shape>.json` (which carry a `predictor_build_commit` field rather than `source_commit`), per-sweep `regeneration_delta.json` (which carry `regeneration_build_commit`), and downstream audit-evidence artifacts like `data/local_labels/audit_evidence/ac5_variance_precondition_diagnostic/variance_trajectory.json` (which has a `current_per_cell_predictions[*].predictor_build_commit` PER-CELL ARRAY field). Drift scanners for these new sub-trees land and start failing as soon as the first follow-up commit moves HEAD past their pinned SHA, but no helper exists to remediate. The failures get queued as "deferred maintenance" round after round because the existing helpers' scope discriminators (e.g., the trace-sidecar helper's `binary_sha256` safety check tied to a binary path) do not apply to the new file shapes, so a naïve "just use the existing helper" call fails.
Root Cause: Refresh helpers are typically written tightly coupled to the FIRST file class that surfaces the drift — they enforce a safety check (e.g., binary_sha256 match against an on-disk binary) that is correct for that file class but cannot be naively widened. New evidence sub-trees added later have different field names AND different safety-check shapes, so the existing helper either silently misses them or fails closed when pointed at them. The team's incentive structure (close the immediate failure, defer "non-blocking" scanner regressions) lets the gap grow until the queued failure list is large enough that a round explicitly attacks it.
Solution: When a new evidence sub-tree lands that carries a HEAD-derived provenance annotation, the round that lands it MUST ALSO add a matching refresh helper (or extend the existing helper's scope) in the SAME ROUND. If a deferred maintenance round is needed to close accumulated drift from multiple sub-trees, the round's mainline is: (a) write a field-agnostic helper that takes the field name + paths as CLI args (no safety check coupling — let the file class's audit-annotation discipline carry the trail); (b) for per-cell array fields, apply a focused per-cell rewrite (the file-level helper cannot reach into arrays directly — but the audit-annotation pattern works at the artifact-root level when all per-cell entries share the same prior value); (c) include the new helper's unit-test contract (first rewrite records override-from; same-HEAD no-op; HEAD-advances preserves ORIGINAL origin; missing field surfaces as typed defect; field-agnostic across the two target field names). Run the full `pytest tools/` sweep before declaring closure; downstream artifacts that compare-against the refreshed sub-trees (e.g., `Ac5VariancePreconditionArtifactTest::test_current_per_cell_predictions_match_latest_sweep`) will also fail and need lockstep restamp.
Constraints: applies whenever a refresh-discipline project adds a new evidence sub-tree. The lesson does NOT require building One Universal Helper covering every field; per-helper specialization is fine as long as helper coverage matches evidence-sub-tree coverage. The audit-annotation pattern (`<field>_override_from`) is the consistent thread across helpers — every helper's rewrite MUST preserve the prior value somewhere greppable. The idempotency contract (re-running at the same HEAD preserves the ORIGINAL drift origin, not the intermediate value) is non-negotiable: if a future round refreshes a file that has already been refreshed once, the audit trail must not lose the original drift origin.
Validation Evidence: Round 20 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop landed `tools/local_labels/refresh_sweep_build_commit.py` (~135 lines, field-agnostic) + `tools/local_labels/test_refresh_sweep_build_commit.py` (5 cases pinning the contract). The Round-20 commit pair `4915bd78` (Commit A: helper + tests) + `23f29030` (Commit B: 5 drift surfaces re-stamped via the new helper + a focused per-cell rewrite for `variance_trajectory.json`'s 13 per-cell entries) moved the full `pytest tools/local_labels/ tools/baseline_runners/ tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py` regression sweep from 4 failed + 1099 passed (pre-Round-20) to 0 failed + 1108 passed (post-Round-20). The previously-deferred 4 drift scanner classes (`test_post_commit_source_commit_drift::_PostCommitDriftTest::test_admitted_label_source_commit_inside_accept_window`, `..::test_selected_label_artifacts_source_commit_inside_accept_window`, `test_regeneration_delta_scanner::ActiveRoundRegenerationDeltaScanTest::test_every_committed_regeneration_delta_admits`, `..::ActiveRoundPredictorBundleScanTest::test_every_committed_predictor_bundle_in_a0_sweep_admits`) all PASS post-restamp; the downstream `Ac5VariancePreconditionArtifactTest` (8 cases) also PASSES once the per-cell rewrite lockstep-matches the refreshed sweep bundles.
Source Rounds: round-18 (drift first surfaced as out-of-scope) → round-19 (Codex queued the helper-coverage gap) → round-20 (closed: new helper + applied refreshes + full-sweep-green).

Update Note (round-20 → round-21, audit-annotation-shape axis): the helper-coverage lesson covered which file classes a helper must touch, but it did NOT specify the shape of the audit annotation each rewrite must record. A helper that rewrites a HEAD-derived provenance field without recording the immediate-prior value loses the audit trail across multiple refreshes: the existing `<field>_override_from` annotation captures the FIRST prior (set once on the first-ever refresh + preserved across subsequent refreshes — this is the ORIGINAL drift origin), but it does NOT capture the immediate prior of a second or later refresh. Worse, if a helper writes nothing when no override-from exists (the original `refresh_admitted_label_source_commit.py` behavior on selected-label artifacts), the immediate-prior is lost from disk forever. Fix: every helper that rewrites a HEAD-derived field MUST also append the immediate-prior to a sibling `<field>_refresh_history` list (or equivalent) on every non-no-op rewrite. The two annotations are complementary: `_override_from` answers "where did the drift originate?" and `_refresh_history` answers "what was the value just before this rewrite?". The combined contract: (a) `<field>_override_from` = first prior value, set on first refresh when absent, preserved on subsequent refreshes; (b) `<field>_refresh_history` = list of immediate-priors, appended on each non-no-op refresh. A no-op (the cell is already at HEAD) writes nothing to either field. The schema MUST accept both as optional additive fields. For per-cell-array fields (e.g., `variance_trajectory.json::current_per_cell_predictions[*].predictor_build_commit`), use artifact-root annotations of the form `<array_field>_<scalar_field>_override_from` + `<array_field>_<scalar_field>_refresh_history`. Source rounds: round-20 (refresh_sweep_build_commit.py landed override-from only) → round-21 (Codex review flagged the audit-annotation-shape gap; both helpers extended; full pytest tools/ sweep passed; 4-case unit test under `_AuditAnnotationTest` in `tools/local_labels/test_refresh_admitted_label_source_commit.py` pins the contract). Unrecoverable failure mode named: a lost-origin file (where a prior refresh wrote no annotation at all) is unrecoverable from disk; the lesson now says "backfill annotations on the FIRST fix-round opportunity before subsequent refreshes overwrite the origin".

Update Note (round-21 → round-22, false-deferral correction): "unrecoverable from disk" is NOT the same as "unrecoverable from project history". When a HEAD-derived provenance field is overwritten without an audit annotation, the prior value IS still present in any Git commit that touched the file before the destructive rewrite — `git show <pre-overwrite-commit>:<path>` retrieves it deterministically. Round 22 of the v5 RLCR loop closed an audit gap that Round 21 had incorrectly framed as unrecoverable: the SHA `08b930ba...` overwritten by Round 20's restamp was recovered from `git show 4915bd78:<label-file>` (Round 20 Commit A pre-dated the destructive Commit B). The Round 22 backfill restored the complete `refresh_history` audit trail across all three refresh cycles on both label files. Generalized rule: before declaring any provenance value "unrecoverable", run `git log -- <path>` and `git show <recent-commit>:<path>` on every commit that touched the file since the suspected drift origin. If any of those Git-history commits carry the value, the on-disk artifact can be backfilled with that value preserved in a sibling refresh_history entry. The false-deferral pattern this rule prevents: a round closes out a Blocking Side Issue by appealing to "the value was already lost", when in fact the audit trail extends through Git history. Codex's drift-recovery mode is specifically designed to catch this pattern when consecutive rounds accept the false framing without checking Git. Source rounds: round-20 (data loss introduced) → round-21 (false-deferral accepted) → round-22 (drift-recovery + Git-history backfill; Codex Round 21 review correctly identified the recovery path).

Update Note (round-22 → round-23, no-mutation-on-failure axis): a fourth axis surfaced when Codex reviewed Round 22's `_refresh_cells` malformed-history fail-closed path. The validation block was correct — it raised `ValueError` on a non-list `source_commit_refresh_history` — but it ran AFTER `source_commit_override_from` had already been injected on the cell. The CLI's write-on-success-only behavior masked the bug for committed artifacts (the file was never persisted after the exception), but a library caller catching the `ValueError` and reusing the cell object would observe a half-mutated cell. Generalized rule: every audit-annotation primitive MUST validate the FULL input shape (all per-cell fields it reads) BEFORE making ANY field mutation. A `ValueError` rejection MUST leave the cell byte-identical to its pre-call state — no partial writes, no injected sibling fields, no rewrites of existing values. The regression test pattern is to deepcopy the cell pre-call, run the failing call under `assertRaises`, then `assertEqual(cell, before)` to pin byte-identity. Source rounds: round-22 (no-mutation discipline introduced as a primitive contract; the validation ran post-mutation) → round-23 (Codex review caught the half-mutation; validation reordered before any mutation; `test_malformed_history_rejection_leaves_cell_byte_identical` regression added). Generalized to all four audit-annotation axes: (1) helper-coverage; (2) audit-annotation-shape; (3) recoverability; (4) no-mutation-on-failure. Together they define the audit-annotation primitive's discipline.


## Lesson: consumer-side-share-override-for-strict-producer-rules
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-consumer-side-share-override-for-strict-producer-rules
Scope: any consumer that reads a producer-emitted "underconfigured / typed-unavailable" flag and chains it into a downstream fail-closed decision. Especially relevant when (a) the producer's strict rule is "ANY underconfigured bin → whole bundle is underconfigured" and (b) the consumer has access to bin-level data that lets it compute the observation share of the underconfigured bins.
Problem Description: A producer (here, the HRD profiler in `tools/hrd_profiler/profiler.py`) enforces a per-bin sample-count threshold: any bin with `pooled_n < MINIMUM_BIN_SAMPLE_COUNT=50` flips `underconfigured_bin_count += 1`, and `bundle_underconfigured := underconfigured_bin_count > 0`. The downstream consumer (here, `cache_predictor._read_structural_underconfiguration`) reads `bundle_underconfigured` and propagates it as a typed-unavailable bundle (typed_reason=cache_model_underconfigured). The strict rule treats one underconfigured bin with 1 observation the same as one with 50K observations, even when the underconfigured bins' total observation share is negligible (e.g., 1 / 2.1M = 4.8e-7). The bundle's hit-rate uncertainty from ignoring an underconfigured bin is bounded by that bin's observation share (since 0 ≤ bin_hit_rate ≤ 1); when the share is well below the producer's calibration target (e.g., CI_HALF_WIDTH_TARGET=0.05), refusing to emit a scalar is over-conservative and converts admissible cells into typed-falsifications. The v5 RLCR loop had 3 sm_120 cells (l2_persisting_window_sweep, working_set_capacity_sweep, warm_cold_pair_relaunch) classified as typed-falsified for many rounds because each had exactly one underconfigured bin with 1-8 observations out of 1-2.6M total.
Root Cause: Strict producer rules are typically chosen during initial development for safety. They're correct on the worst case (the entire bundle could be uncertain if any one bin's hit-rate is undetermined) but pessimistic in the common case (the underconfigured bin's contribution to the bundle hit-rate is tiny). The producer side can't see the consumer's tolerance budget (e.g., what's "close enough" to the bundle hit-rate). The consumer CAN do this calculation given access to the bin-level data, and can override the producer's strict rule when the math supports it.
Solution: Add a consumer-side share override that wraps the producer's strict flag. The override does two things: (1) compute the observation share of the underconfigured bins from the bin-level data the producer emits; (2) when the share is below a documented threshold (e.g., `_UNDERCONFIGURED_SHARE_THRESHOLD=1e-5`, an order of magnitude or more below the producer's ci_half_width_target), override the strict flag to False and emit a greppable audit annotation (`<field>_share_override`) recording the share, the threshold, and the rationale. The producer keeps its strict rule (so other consumers see the unchanged signal); only the specific consumer that admits the bundle applies the share-based admission. When the consumer also reads downstream consequences of the producer flag (e.g., a separate sidecar's `bundle_underconfigured=True` that derives from the upstream flag), the consumer must SUPPRESS those propagations too when the share-override fires; otherwise the downstream flag silently undoes the override.
Constraints: applies when the producer's bin-level data is preserved in the artifact the consumer reads (so the consumer can compute the share). Does NOT apply when the producer only emits the boolean flag without the per-bin counts. The threshold MUST be documented relative to the producer's calibration target (e.g., "1e-5 = 0.001%, which is 5000x below CI_HALF_WIDTH_TARGET=0.05"); a threshold chosen without explicit relationship to the producer's tolerance budget is suspect. The override MUST NOT silently overwrite the boolean — the audit annotation makes the override greppable from grep / verifier output. The lesson does NOT apply when the producer's strict rule is itself a contract requirement (e.g., regulatory mandate); in that case the consumer must respect the strict rule even when math allows admission.
Validation Evidence: Round 24 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop landed `_UNDERCONFIGURED_SHARE_THRESHOLD=1e-5` in `workloads/simbench_bw/cache_predictor.py` + `_compute_underconfigured_share` helper + 4-case test surface under `workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_cache_predictor.py` (admits when share negligible; keeps strict when share significant; convergence_confidence propagation suppressed when share-override fires; helper handles malformed bins). Re-emitted predictor bundles for the 3 cells (Commit B `13ed0764`) flipped result_kind from typed_unavailable to scalar with predicted_hit_rate=0.997, 0.996, 0.924 respectively + canonical_pre_calibration_features populated. v5 fitter admitted_count moved 27 → 30; the v5 closure artifacts now compute on a 30-cell admitted slice (was 27-cell). The 44 sm_86 cross-host-pending cells remain operator-blocked; same-74 closure still requires scp. Full `pytest tools/local_labels/ tools/baseline_runners/ tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py` sweep: 1116 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed.
Source Rounds: round-19 (3 sm_120 cells first classified as typed_unavailable:cache_model_underconfigured) → round-22 (BitLesson note documented "deeper investigation required") → round-24 (consumer-side share override implemented; cells admit; same-30 v5 sequence regenerated).

Update Note (round-24 → round-25, persist-annotation axis + downstream-consistency scanners): Codex Round 24 review caught two ways the round-24 implementation failed to make the share-override fully auditable. First, the audit annotation was created inside `_read_structural_underconfiguration` but only the rejected-typed-unavailable branch carried it through to the bundle (via `evidence.upstream_underconfiguration`). On the ADMITTED scalar path, the override was discarded — making it structurally invisible to grep / audit consumers reading the on-disk bundles. The fix: in the scalar bundle construction site, also copy the `<field>_share_override` dict from the structural-read result into `bundle["evidence"]["<field>_share_override"]` whenever the override fires. The producer-level regression test pattern: build synthetic upstream sidecars with negligible underconfigured share, stub the launch builder so the test does not depend on the live trace, invoke the producer, assert the resulting scalar bundle's evidence carries the annotation with the measured share + threshold. Second, the staging manifest (an operator-facing surface that classifies cells into `READY` / `CROSS_HOST_SCP` / `BUNDLE_TYPED_UNAVAILABLE` action categories) was NOT regenerated when the v5 head admitted the 3 cells; it kept reporting the cells as `BUNDLE_TYPED_UNAVAILABLE`. The cohort-coverage scanner didn't catch the staleness because it accepted v5-head admission first without asserting the manifest action matches. Fix: add 2 scanner tests — (a) every v5-head-admitted cell MUST have manifest action `READY`; (b) `summary.by_action` aggregate counts MUST equal row-level action counts. Generalizes to: whenever a producer's admission decision changes downstream artifact state, EVERY downstream artifact derived from that admission decision must be regenerated AND a scanner must enforce the consistency. Source rounds: round-24 (annotation created but not persisted on admit path; manifest left stale) → round-25 (persist patch + producer regression test + 2 manifest-consistency scanner tests + manifest regen).

Update Note (sm86 cohort regen loop, threshold-tuning-needs-live-measurement axis): the share-override threshold is a tunable parameter, and the initial value chosen during early wiring (1e-5 = 0.001%) was a maximally-conservative cap rather than the operating point the math actually supports. When the sm86 cohort regen loop ran task5 live for the first time (Round 29), 13 of 20 admitted cells failed with `cache_model_underconfigured` despite having underconfigured-bin observation shares in the 1.29e-5 to 4.58e-3 range — values that are mathematically negligible against the HRD profiler's per-bin `ci_half_width_target` of 5e-2 but above the 1e-5 cap. Round 30 raised the threshold to 5e-3 (0.5%, one order of magnitude below the per-bin CI floor — a 10x safety margin) and the cohort's admitted count jumped from 7 to 18. The general rule extension: when adopting this lesson's pattern in a new producer/consumer boundary, START with the maximally-conservative threshold (e.g., 1e-5) for initial wiring + tests, but EXPECT to re-measure once live cohort data flows and raise the threshold to "order-of-magnitude-below-the-producer's-tolerance-target" once you've seen the live distribution. The maximally-conservative threshold is a debugging convenience, not the final operating point; treat the threshold tuning as a separate scheduled task on the loop's plan rather than expecting the initial value to hold. Concretely: the loop's plan should include a "post-live-measurement threshold review" task that runs after the first full cascade. Source rounds: sm86 cohort regen loop Round 29 (live task5 surfaced the over-conservative threshold) → Round 30 (Commit A `3a787e7a` raised 1e-5 → 5e-3 with boundary regression test; Commit B `231d689b` re-ran cascade + re-emitted ledger with 18 admitted cells).


## Lesson: falsification-report-must-document-plan-mandated-components
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-falsification-report-must-document-plan-mandated-components
Scope: any project where the closure path includes an "engineering-closure" branch satisfied by emitting a falsification report; any artifact whose contract is specified in plan text by enumerating REQUIRED CONTENT COMPONENTS (e.g., "the report MUST document X, Y, and Z").
Problem Description: A plan specifies an engineering-closure-via-falsification path with a content contract: the falsification artifact MUST document (a) the falsification hypothesis verdict, (b) which closure targets were missed, (c) the per-bucket residual structure. The initial implementation focuses on (a) — emitting the verdict + the joint contribution percentage that drives it — because that's the easiest component to compute. Components (b) and (c) get deferred to "future rounds" or "comments in the summary" because they require reading downstream artifacts + aggregating per-cell residuals by bucket. The artifact then ships with only (a); each downstream consumer (operator, reviewer, Codex review) has to manually reconstruct (b) and (c) by reading the closure pipeline's other artifacts + the v5 head's cohort_scope. This silently shifts work from the artifact producer to every artifact consumer.
Root Cause: Plan contracts that say "the report MUST document X, Y, Z" feel like specification but read as wish-list when the implementation only delivers X. The implementation gap is usually because components (b) and (c) require cross-artifact reads + arithmetic — not hard, but the verdict-only path is shorter, and "we can add the rest later" is tempting. Codex review then catches the gap (because the plan text is explicit) and the team adds (b) + (c) in a follow-on round, but the same pattern recurs for the next contract that enumerates components.
Solution: When a plan or contract document enumerates artifact content components, every component MUST land in the same round the artifact is first emitted. Write a scanner test that pins the presence of EACH enumerated component (assertIn on each top-level key, structural correctness on each list/dict shape). The test name should reference the contract source (e.g., `test_falsification_artifact_carries_missed_targets_and_per_bucket_residual`) so a reader sees why each assertion exists. The scanner serves both as regression guard AND as living documentation of the contract — a future round that "extends" the artifact must update the scanner, surfacing the contract review at code-review time. For artifacts whose content depends on other artifacts (e.g., "missed closure targets" requires reading the gate artifacts), the emitter MUST do the cross-artifact reads itself rather than leaving them to consumers; treat the emitter as the contract enforcer. Use domain-named identifiers (e.g., `full_cohort_median_absolute_error`, `cross_arch_transfer`) rather than plan-document terminology in the artifact's enumerated entries; this satisfies the plan-token discipline AND gives consumers stable cross-references to the gate-artifact field names.
Constraints: applies whenever a closure path is satisfied by artifact emission + the artifact contract enumerates content components. Does NOT apply when the closure path is the "research success" branch (all gates pass) — in that case the falsification report may not even need to land. The scanner MUST tolerate the empty-missed-targets case (research success): `missed_closure_targets` may be an empty list. The cross-artifact reads MUST be defensive (`json.JSONDecodeError`, `FileNotFoundError`) so a missing downstream artifact does not crash the emitter; the missing artifact is itself information worth surfacing.
Validation Evidence: Round 26 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop extended `tools/local_labels/emit_v5_closure_artifacts.py::_emit_falsification` with two new helpers (`_compute_per_bucket_residual_reduction`, `_enumerate_missed_closure_targets`) + corresponding artifact-level fields (`per_bucket_residual_reduction`, `missed_closure_targets`). The enumeration reads the accuracy / robustness / active_path / baseline-comparison artifacts and lists every closure sub-criterion that FAILs or is INDETERMINATE; the per-bucket helper aggregates per-cell (v4_err, v5_err) tuples already collected by the verdict-driving measurement loop. NEW `test_falsification_artifact_carries_missed_targets_and_per_bucket_residual` under `V5CohortCoverageScannerTest` pins both fields' presence + structural correctness. The current artifact emits 8 missed_closure_targets entries (4 FAIL on the 30-cell slice + 4 INDETERMINATE pending operator scp) + 4 per_bucket_residual_reduction entries (the four sm_120 family_buckets at n=7-9 each). Full `pytest tools/local_labels/ tools/baseline_runners/ tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py workloads/simbench_bw/tests/test_cache_predictor.py` sweep: 1200 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed.
Source Rounds: round-26 (defect surfaced + closed in the same round — the prior emitter shipped with only the verdict component for many prior rounds; Codex Round 25 review's continued "still not same-74 closure" framing prompted the plan re-read that surfaced the contract gap).

Update Note (round-26 → round-27, failure-state-vs-indeterminate-state distinction axis): Codex Round 26 review caught that the new `_enumerate_missed_closure_targets` helper covered the CURRENT artifact state (cross_arch_transfer + same_74_baseline both INDETERMINATE because the 44 sm_86 cells are operator-blocked) but did NOT cover the eventual FAILURE state once the same-74 cohort lands. The original implementation conflated "no data yet" (INDETERMINATE) with "data lands and computation passes" (no miss to enumerate) and missed the third state: "data lands, computation runs, gate fails" (FAILURE that the helper must enumerate). A synthetic same-74 artifact with both AC-4 transfer gates failing AND both AC-5 baseline-comparison gates failing returned an EMPTY list from the helper, falsely implying no missed targets and silently passing the falsification report completeness contract. The fix generalizes the lesson: when an enumeration helper conditions on artifact status, it MUST cover all THREE states for each gate axis — (a) INDETERMINATE (insufficient data; current same-30 surface), (b) PASS (no miss), (c) FAIL (miss to enumerate). The synthetic-fixture test surface MUST drive each state through the helper directly via `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` + handwritten artifact JSON rather than relying on the current checked-in artifacts; the checked-in artifacts only exercise the current state (here, mostly INDETERMINATE on a 30-cell slice) and miss the future states the helper is supposed to cover when the cohort grows. The synthetic-fixture pattern: build the gate artifact tree under a tempdir, point the helper at it, assert the expected misses appear (or do not appear) with correct threshold + observed values. The "test what the helper will compute once the data lands" discipline matters more for partial-cohort projects (where the current artifact is structurally different from the eventual artifact) than for full-cohort projects (where current ≈ eventual). Source rounds: round-26 (helper added, INDETERMINATE state covered, FAILURE state silently missed) → round-27 (Codex caught the gap via mental simulation of the same-74 artifact; helper extended with 4 new branches; 4 synthetic tests pin all three states per gate axis). Round 27 validation evidence: NEW `MissedClosureTargetsEnumeratorTest` class under `tools/local_labels/test_v5_cohort_coverage_scanner.py` with 4 cases — `test_transfer_relative_gate_failure_emits_miss`, `test_transfer_median_gate_failure_emits_miss`, `test_same_74_baseline_gate_failures_emit_misses`, `test_same_74_all_pass_yields_no_ac4_ac5_misses`. Full sweep: 1204 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed (+4 from Round 26's 1200 — the 4 new synthetic tests).

Update Note (round-27 → round-28, gate-input-completeness-axis-vs-summary-statistic-axis): Codex Round 27 review caught a SECOND axis the Round 27 fix missed. The Round 27 baseline-comparison branch correctly distinguished the three states per gate axis (INDETERMINATE / PASS / FAIL) but the COMPUTABILITY check itself was under-strict. The branch used ONLY `aggregate_metrics.v5_prediction.n == 74` to decide whether the same-74 comparison was computable, while the baseline runner computes aggregate `n` INDEPENDENTLY per prediction column AND separately exposes `v5_vs_nugteren_joint_admit_count`. A future artifact with `v5_prediction.n == 74`, `nugteren_prediction.n == 30`, and `v5_vs_nugteren_joint_admit_count == 30` would skip BOTH the FAIL branches (v5 wins on beat-fraction + median) AND the INDETERMINATE branch (because `v5_prediction.n` is 74), returning `[]` from the helper — falsely implying no missed targets on a structurally incomplete same-74 baseline comparison. The fix: in addition to the three states per gate axis, the helper must check ALL inputs that COMBINE into the gate decision. For baseline-comparison this means ALL SIX aggregate prediction columns at `n == 74` AND `v5_vs_nugteren_joint_admit_count == 74` (not just the headline-column's per-column n). The general principle: when a gate decision depends on multiple input statistics (here, six per-column n values + one joint count), the computability check MUST require ALL of them; using only a single "headline" input (v5_prediction.n in this case) leaves coverage gaps when other inputs are partial. The `observed` payload on the INDETERMINATE entry must name EVERY input value so a reader can see which input blocked the comparison. Source rounds: round-27 (helper covered three states per gate axis but gate-input-completeness gap remained) → round-28 (Codex caught via per-column n inspection in baseline_comparison.json; helper extended; 1 new synthetic test pins the partial-joint-count INDETERMINATE entry). Round 28 validation evidence: NEW `test_same_74_baseline_partial_joint_count_emits_indeterminate_miss` under `MissedClosureTargetsEnumeratorTest`; UPDATED `test_same_74_baseline_gate_failures_emit_misses` + `test_same_74_all_pass_yields_no_ac4_ac5_misses` fixtures to include all 6 aggregate columns + joint_admit_count=74 (otherwise the existing tests would regress under the new tightened gate). Full sweep: 1206 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed.



## Lesson: manual-workflow-wraps-into-CLI-mirroring-scanner-enforcement-scope
Lesson ID: BL-20260527-manual-workflow-wraps-into-cli-mirroring-scanner-enforcement-scope
Scope: any RLCR / iterative-development project where the same multi-step workflow (e.g., artifact regeneration + provenance restamp + per-cell array rewrites) is executed manually each round, AND the workflow has a dynamic discovery surface (drift-managed cells, drift-managed sweep dirs, per-array stale entries) that changes between rounds, AND the project has scanner tests that enforce the workflow's outputs at a defined scope (e.g., "only the LATEST sweep dir's commits are enforced; older sweeps are frozen audit trail"). The wrapping work is autonomous-mainline-paralysis-resistant: it can be done while the project's primary blocker (e.g., cross-host operator data movement) is operator-blocked, because it codifies what the human operator will run when the unblocking event lands.
Problem Description: After 10+ rounds of manually running a multi-command workflow (in this project's case: 8+ commands per round for v5 artifact regen + label restamp + sweep restamp + variance_trajectory restamp), the workflow has stabilized + the dynamic-discovery surface (which cells need refresh, which sweep dirs are drift-managed, which per-array entries are stale) has been worked out via repeated jq queries and grep audits each round. The workflow becomes operator memory + tribal knowledge: every round the operator re-derives the discovery surface from scratch. When the project's external blocker eventually lands (e.g., the cross-host data movement completes + the cohort scope expands), the operator will run this workflow on a LARGER discovery surface (more cells, possibly new sweep dirs), and any drift from the established procedure will introduce silent defects. Concretely: the operator might forget to restamp a per-array entry, or might over-restamp a frozen-audit-trail sweep dir.
Root Cause: Manual multi-step workflows accumulate institutional knowledge in the operator's head + in scattered jq commands across round summaries. When the workflow's discovery surface is dynamic (changes per round), the operator's mental model + the actual surface drift apart. When the workflow's enforcement scope is scanner-defined (e.g., "only LATEST sweep is enforced"), the operator may over- or under-cover the actual enforcement boundary. The fix-by-defects-only round discipline correctly avoids over-engineering, but rounds 10+ of repeated manual execution is itself a defect class: the workflow should be codified once the shape stabilizes.
Solution: When the manual workflow has run 5+ rounds with stable shape, wrap it as a single-command CLI tool. Two non-negotiable design constraints: (1) DYNAMIC DISCOVERY of every per-round-varying surface (cells, sweep dirs, per-array entries) so the wrapper does not hardcode lists that drift; (2) SCANNER ENFORCEMENT SCOPE is the contract source for what to restamp — the wrapper restamps EXACTLY what the active-round scanner enforces, no more (avoid over-touching frozen audit trail) and no less (avoid leaving the scanner red on unrestamped surfaces). The wrapper emits a structured per-step status report at a stable path so reviewers can verify the workflow ran correctly. The wrapper exits non-zero if any step fails but the report still lands. Tests cover the discovery + dispatch logic via synthetic-fixture tempdirs; the end-to-end emitter invocations are already exercised by the manual execution + the broad pytest sweep, so do not duplicate end-to-end testing. Use the existing `regenerate_v5_trace_sidecars.py` wrapper as the project's reference pattern: dispatch table, per-cell-or-per-step status dicts, JSON report at a stable path, --dry-run for safe discovery preview.
Constraints: applies once the workflow has run 5+ rounds with stable shape (premature wrapping is over-engineering). Does NOT apply when each round needs different steps (the wrapper would just become a dispatcher for ad-hoc work). Does NOT apply when the workflow has no scanner-defined enforcement scope (without the scanner contract, the wrapper has no objective scope-correctness criterion). The wrapper MUST mirror the scanner's enforcement scope precisely — over-restamping (e.g., touching older sweep dirs that the scanner treats as frozen audit trail) is a regression that breaks the audit-trail integrity even if no test catches it; under-restamping leaves the scanner red. The wrapper does NOT replace the round's manual review of the discovery surface: when the underlying cohort grows (e.g., sm_86 staging lands), the operator should sanity-check the wrapper's discovery output against the new cohort before trusting it.
Validation Evidence: Round 30 of the per_bucket_monotonic_calibration_counter_free_l2_observability loop introduced `tools/local_labels/regenerate_v5_artifacts.py` (440-line CLI + 13 focused tests) wrapping the 8-step Phase B v5 regen + lockstep restamp workflow that the prior 10+ rounds executed manually. The wrapper's discovery functions (`discover_drift_managed_cells`, `discover_drift_managed_sweep_dirs`, `discover_drifted_predictor_bundles`) read label files + sweep dirs + predictor bundles dynamically; the sweep-dir discovery mirrors `ActiveRoundPredictorBundleScanTest`'s scope-to-latest semantic (returns ONLY the latest sweep dir, NOT historical drift-managed dirs that the scanner treats as frozen audit trail — manual phase B was over-restamping older dirs unnecessarily). The wrapper invokes each step's `main(argv)` in-process (all 8 target modules expose `def main(argv) -> int`); the per-step status report at `data/local_labels/v5_artifact_regeneration_report.json` lands even on failure. Round 30 Commit B is the wrapper's own output: ran `python -m tools.local_labels.regenerate_v5_artifacts`, byte-equivalent numerical output to Round 29's manual phase B (admitted_count=30, falsification verdict=confirmed, missed_closure_targets shape + numbers identical); restamp scope correctly trimmed to LATEST sweep dir only (1 sweep dir vs Round 29's 2). Full pytest sweep: 1220 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed (+13 from Round 29's 1207 — the 13 new wrapper tests). When the operator scp eventually lands the 44 sm_86 cells, running `python -m tools.local_labels.regenerate_v5_artifacts` from a single shell prompt will regenerate the full same-74 v5 sequence + restamp the drift surface correctly + emit a verifiable per-step report — no operator memory or jq spelunking required.
Source Rounds: rounds 20-29 (manual workflow stabilized over 10 rounds; each round restamped slightly different surfaces, over-restamped older sweep dirs that the scanner doesn't enforce) → round-30 (operator-blocked-by-sm_86-scp window provided the autonomous-mainline-paralysis space to codify the workflow once, mirroring the scanner enforcement scope precisely instead of reproducing the manual over-restamp behavior).

Update Note (round-30 → round-31, child-argv-must-not-fall-through-to-process-sys.argv axis): Codex Round 30 review caught a SECOND axis the Round 30 wrapper's initial design missed. The Round 30 wrapper's `_invoke_module_main(module_name=...)` callers omitted `argv` for the 6 default regen steps, so child modules received `argv=None`. Every target module's `parser.parse_args(argv)` interprets None as "use process-level sys.argv" — which includes the WRAPPER'S CLI flags like `--report-path`. Children whose parsers do not define `--report-path` (every emitter except the wrapper itself) would SystemExit on unrecognized arguments. The Round 30 live-run smoke test passed only because it used the no-flag invocation; the documented `--report-path` invocation would have failed in the first child parser. The Round 30 dry-run shape tests skipped every child dispatch + only checked the report shape, so they did not catch the leak. The fix generalizes: when wrapping a manual multi-step workflow into a CLI that itself takes flags, the dispatcher MUST normalize implicit `argv=None` to `[]` before forwarding to children. The `None` value should NEVER reach a child's argparse — child parsers interpret None as "fall through to process sys.argv", which leaks the wrapper's flags. The general principle: a process running in-process child invocations of argparse-based modules must isolate each child's argv from the parent's argv unless it explicitly intends to share. The test surface for this defect class MUST exercise the actual child dispatch path, not just the report-shape; tests that mock or skip the child invocation will silently miss argv-leak defects. Synthetic-module pattern: register a fake module in sys.modules; set process sys.argv to include wrapper flags; invoke the dispatcher with the synthetic module name; assert the synthetic main(argv) receives `[]`. The pattern is small + fast + does not depend on live emitter state. Source rounds: round-30 (wrapper introduced; argv=None left as default; live smoke test used no-flag invocation; defect undetected) → round-31 (Codex caught via static review of `_invoke_module_main` + `mod.main(argv)` call sites; argv normalization added; 3 new tests pin the contract end-to-end via synthetic module). Round 31 validation evidence: NEW `DispatcherArgvNormalizationTest` (2 cases: implicit→[], explicit list forwarded verbatim) + NEW `WrapperNonDryRunWithSysArgvFlagsTest` (1 case: full wrapper run with `--report-path` in sys.argv succeeds via synthetic _REGEN_STEPS). Live smoke: `python -m tools.local_labels.regenerate_v5_artifacts --report-path <path>` (the explicit-flag invocation that pre-fix would have failed) now succeeds end-to-end on the documented CLI surface. Full sweep: 1223 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed (+3 from Round 30's 1220 — the 3 new wrapper-dispatcher tests).

Update Note (round-31 → round-32, audit-report-captures-both-prior-and-new-SHA-for-self-contained-diff axis): Round 30 wrapper's per-step status report recorded only the NEW SHA (via the top-level `head_at_wrapper_invocation`) but did NOT record the PRIOR SHAs of the restamped surfaces. A reviewer who wanted to know "what SHA did this label cell hold before this round's wrapper run?" had to look at `git diff` rather than the report itself. The variance_trajectory_restamp step already captured prior SHAs via its `rewrite_origins` map (Round 30 design); label_restamp + sweep_restamp did not. The fix generalizes: an audit report for a multi-step restamp workflow should be SELF-CONTAINED — each restamp step records BOTH the prior SHA(s) the surface held AND the new SHA the step restamped it to. The downstream reviewer should NOT need to consult `git diff` or another external tool to reconstruct what changed. The pattern: read the artifact files BEFORE invoking the refresh helper to capture prior state; attach the prior-state map to the per-step result dict before writing the report. The discipline applies to ALL restamp surfaces in the workflow, not just the ones where prior SHA capture happens to be easy (e.g., variance_trajectory's per-cell rewrite already captured this; label_restamp + sweep_restamp had to add new read-before-refresh helpers). Source rounds: round-30 (wrapper introduced; only variance_trajectory had prior-SHA capture; label + sweep restamp branches omitted it) → round-32 (audit gap closed; new helpers `read_prior_source_commits_for_cells` + `read_prior_build_commits_for_sweep` read prior state before refresh; report extended with `prior_source_commits` per label_restamp + `prior_build_commits` per sweep_restamp). Round 32 validation evidence: NEW `ReadPriorSourceCommitsTest` (3 cases: returns prior SHAs; returns None for missing key; absent file returns all None) + NEW `ReadPriorBuildCommitsForSweepTest` (1 case: returns structured map) + NEW `WrapperReportCapturesPriorShasTest` (1 case: end-to-end synthetic wrapper proves report includes prior_source_commits with correct values). Live wrapper run lands the extended report on disk: every prior SHA correctly captured as Round 31's HEAD (278853f84c0f). Full sweep: 1228 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed (+5 from Round 31's 1223 — the 5 new prior-SHA-capture tests).

Update Note (round-32 → round-33, independent-sub-actions-per-multi-surface-restamp axis): Codex Round 32 review caught a THIRD axis the earlier wrapper design missed. The Round 30 wrapper's sweep_restamp branch had TWO sub-actions (predictor_bundles refresh + regeneration_delta refresh) but COUPLED them: the branch immediately returned `status="no_action"` and `continue`d when `discover_drifted_predictor_bundles()` returned empty, even if `regeneration_delta.json` was itself stale. Compounding the problem, `discover_drift_managed_sweep_dirs` only checked the regen_delta SHA when deciding whether a sweep was "drift-managed", so a sweep with drifted bundles but in-window regen_delta was never inspected at all. Both gaps were silent across rounds: the wrapper would report `no_action` and the partially-out-of-window sweep would remain broken until manual intervention. The fix generalizes: when a workflow has multiple sub-surfaces that can drift INDEPENDENTLY (e.g., regen_delta + per-bundle commits in a sweep dir; or different artifact fields in a multi-field provenance check), each sub-action MUST decide its own drift state INDEPENDENTLY and the parent step's aggregation MUST treat not-invoked sub-actions as `no_action` (not failure, not skip-without-recording). The OR-of-drift-states selection rule applies: the parent step runs ANY time at least one sub-surface is out-of-window; the inner branches conditionally repair only the drifted sub-surface(s). The test surface for this defect class MUST exercise BOTH cases independently (stale-A-only AND stale-B-only) via synthetic fixtures; testing only the both-drifted case will silently miss the coupled-skip defect. The structurally-complete report shape (sub-result dicts present with `status="no_action"` + empty payload when not invoked) is what lets a downstream reader distinguish "didn't need to run" from "should have run but was silently skipped". Source rounds: round-30 (wrapper introduced; sweep_restamp had coupled sub-actions + `discover_drift_managed_sweep_dirs` only checked regen_delta) → round-33 (Codex caught via static review of the wrapper's sweep_restamp branch logic; decoupling landed; 2 new tests pin both cases via synthetic-sweep harness; helper extended to consider EITHER sub-surface drift). Round 33 validation evidence: NEW `SweepRestampDecoupledSubActionsTest` (2 cases: `test_stale_regen_delta_only_refreshes_regen_delta_not_bundles` + `test_drifted_bundles_only_refreshes_bundles_not_regen_delta`). Both tests use a synthetic-sweep harness that patches `_REGEN_STEPS = ()` + wrapper globals + `_git_head_sha`/`_git_parent_sha` lambdas + recording-stub refresh helper, asserting exact refresh-helper invocation count (1 per stale sub-surface) + per-sub-result status + prior-SHA shape. Full sweep: 1230 passed, 21 skipped, 0 failed (+2 from Round 32's 1228 — the 2 new decoupled-sub-action tests).

Update Note (round-33 → round-34, audit-artifact-shape-pinned-by-scanner-test axis): the wrappers per-step status report lands on disk each iteration as the wrappers own output and is committed. Across the wrappers lifecycle the report shape was extended in three increments (initial top-level + per-step status + variance_trajectorys rewrite_origins; then label_restamp.prior_source_commits + sweep_restamp.predictor_bundles.prior_build_commits + sweep_restamp.regeneration_delta.prior_build_commit; then decoupled sweep_restamp sub-action shape). Each increment landed alongside helper-level tests (test fixtures that synthesize the artifact files + invoke helpers + assert helper outputs) but NO scanner test pinned the EXPECTED SHAPE of the COMMITTED ON-DISK report against the wrappers contract. Future wrapper refactors that omit a field or break the regen sequence + leave a partial report would be silently uncaught: helper-level tests pass synthetic inputs through the helpers; they do not assert that the actual committed report file has the expected shape. The fix generalizes the audit-discipline principle: when a tool produces an audit artifact whose shape is the tools public contract for downstream auditors, write a scanner test that pins the SHAPE OF THE COMMITTED ARTIFACT (not just the helpers that produced it). Per-key assertions + structural-shape assertions + an explicit negative fixture (proving the scanner detects a synthetic-missing-key case) close the regression-guard surface. The committed artifact is the source of truth for the operator audit + the scanner test is the source of truth for the artifacts contract. Source rounds: rounds 30 + 32 + 33 (each landed an increment to the report shape without committed-artifact shape scanner) -> round-34 (audit-discipline gap closed; new scanner test class with 7 cases + 1 class with 2 negative-fixture cases pins the full report contract; broad sweep 1240 passed +10 from prior 1230).
Update Note (round-34 → round-35, scanner-contract-completeness-check-against-its-own-spec axis): Codex Round 34 review caught that my Round 34 scanner (CommittedV5RegenerationReportShapeTest) under-enforced its OWN contract. The round-34 contract required top-level type checks + sweep-restamp presence assertions, but the implementation only asserted key PRESENCE for 4 of the 7 top-level keys + only asserted strings (not SHA-or-unknown shape) for accept_window elements + built a list of sweep_restamp entries but never asserted the list was non-empty + never checked the fallback no_action entry. A future regression that drops the sweep_restamp loop entirely would pass silently. The fix generalizes: when writing a scanner test against an audit artifact contract, audit YOUR OWN SCANNER against the contracts STATED SUCCESS CRITERIA before claiming the contract is pinned. Specifically: (1) type assertions for every contract-named key (not just key presence); (2) shape assertions on container elements that match the shape rules used for the container itself (e.g., accept_window elements should follow the same SHA-or-unknown rule as head_at_wrapper_invocation); (3) presence assertions for ANY restamp-step block the wrapper emits in EITHER its per-target form OR its fallback no-action form (collected via list comprehension + dict-get + assertTrue at-least-one-present); (4) negative fixtures for the absence case (proving the scanner detects an empty-list state). The discipline is recursive: the scanner test itself is an audit artifact whose contract is the round contracts success criteria; pinning that requires going back to the success criteria + checking each criterion is enforced by the test code. The fix is small (4 incremental assertions) but the meta-lesson is that scanner contracts can themselves be under-enforced + a careful follow-up Codex review catches it. Round 35 validation evidence: 2 NEW assertions tightening top-level type checks + accept_window shape rule + NEW test_report_contains_at_least_one_sweep_restamp_entry presence assertion + NEW test_synthetic_report_with_no_sweep_restamp_entry_is_detected negative fixture; full sweep 1242 passed +2 from Round 34s 1240. Source rounds: round-34 (scanner introduced; under-enforced own contract) -> round-35 (Codex caught the gap; tightening landed).

Update Note (round-35 → round-36, cross-artifact-cohort-count-consistency-invariant axis): the prior axes (rounds 30-35) all concerned the wrapper's per-step internals — argv normalization, prior-SHA capture, sub-action decoupling, committed-report shape, scanner-vs-spec completeness. A separate audit gap remained: when MULTIPLE producers each emit a derived artifact carrying its OWN cohort count, no scanner pinned the cross-artifact invariant that those counts MUST sum (or otherwise combine) to a global aggregate. Concretely: the per-arch v5 head emitter writes `sm_120_v5_head.json` + `sm_86_v5_head.json` each carrying an `admitted_count`; the global v5 head writes `calibration_head_v5.cohort_scope.admitted_count`. If the per-arch fitter and the global v5 fitter applied slightly different skip criteria, the per-arch sums would diverge from the global count silently, and downstream per-arch transfer metrics would be computed against an inconsistent cohort set. The fix generalizes: when N artifacts are derived FROM A SHARED INPUT and each carries a piece of the same cohort accounting, write a scanner test that pins the INVARIANT relating their pieces to the global. The invariant must handle indeterminate-status artifacts explicitly — an indeterminate per-arch head contributes 0 to the sum because its admitted set is absent by contract. The test must red-state on synthetic inconsistent states (global=N but per-arch-sum != N). The discipline is broader than counting: any time multiple derived artifacts encode the same fact from different angles (per-arch + global; per-fold + aggregate; per-bucket + cohort-wide), the cross-artifact invariant pinning the consistency is an audit obligation that helper-level tests cannot replace — helper-level tests only verify each producer in isolation; the cross-artifact test verifies they AGREE. The test was trivially satisfied on the current same-30 surface (30 + 0 = 30 because sm_86 is indeterminate) but becomes load-bearing the moment the operator-blocked sm_86 cells land + the per-arch fitter re-runs (then 30 + 44 = 74 MUST equal calibration_head_v5.cohort_scope.admitted_count). Source rounds: rounds 1-35 (per-arch + global v5 heads each carried admitted_count independently; no scanner pinned the cross-artifact invariant) -> round-36 (operator-blocked-by-sm_86-scp window provided autonomous mainline; cross-artifact consistency scanner test + negative fixture added; broad sweep 1244 passed +2 from Round 35's 1242). Round 36 validation evidence: NEW `test_per_arch_admitted_counts_sum_to_global_admitted_count` under `V5CohortCoverageScannerTest` (handles `status=ok` → admitted_count and `status=indeterminate_*` → 0 explicitly; fails on any other status) + NEW `test_per_arch_sum_mismatch_against_global_is_detected` under `CohortCoverageScannerNegativeFixturesTest` (synthetic v5_head.admitted=74 + sm_120.admitted=30 + sm_86.admitted=30; in Round 36 this fixture only asserted the synthetic state was inconsistent — it did not yet wrap the live scanner's assertion in `assertRaises`). Live wrapper run post-tightening: overall_status=ok, head_at_wrapper_invocation=ccec8c2e64ee, cross-artifact invariant 30 + 0 = 30 verified on committed artifacts.

Update Note (round-36 → round-37, negative-fixture-must-actually-exercise-the-scanner-assertion axis): Codex Round 36 review caught that the new `test_per_arch_sum_mismatch_against_global_is_detected` negative fixture only asserted `per_arch_sum != global_admitted` + the hard-coded literals 60 and 74 — it did NOT wrap the live scanner's equality assertion in `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):`. The Round 36 summary/tracker/BitLesson prose all overstated the fixture, claiming it ran the live assertion under assertRaises. The fix generalizes the negative-fixture discipline previously captured by `BL-20260526-ac3-validator-mirrors-contract-not-just-schema`: a negative fixture for a scanner contract MUST actually invoke the scanner's assertion path on the synthetic mismatch + assert that path raises. Constructing the synthetic state and confirming it is inconsistent only verifies the FIXTURE is constructed correctly; it does not exercise the SCANNER. A future regression that softened the live `assertEqual(per_arch_sum, global_admitted)` (e.g., downgraded to a logged warning, swapped to `assertGreaterEqual`, removed entirely) would silently pass the negative fixture because the fixture never reached the scanner's assertion. The fix is small: factor the live `_per_arch_contribution` semantics into the fixture (status=ok int check; indeterminate_* → 0; unexpected → fail-closed via `self.fail`), then wrap `self.assertEqual(per_arch_sum, global_admitted)` in `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):` so the test genuinely exercises and proves the scanner's red-state behavior. The fixture-vs-summary-claim mismatch was itself the secondary signal: a summary that says "raises" should compile to a test method that USES `assertRaises`. Reviewers reading prose without diffing the source will trust the claim; the only durable cure is to write the test that matches the prose. Source rounds: round-36 (negative fixture introduced; only proved synthetic inconsistency; summary/tracker/BitLesson prose overstated) → round-37 (Codex caught the gap; fixture strengthened with mirrored `_per_arch_contribution` + `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError): self.assertEqual(...)`; docstring updated; BitLesson + summary prose corrected). Round 37 validation evidence: the strengthened fixture passes (6 cases in `CohortCoverageScannerNegativeFixturesTest` GREEN); the live scanner's equality assertion is now actually exercised under `assertRaises` so any softening of the live assertion would surface a fixture failure.
Update Note (round-37 → round-38, shared-helper-not-duplicated-assertion-for-red-state-proof axis): Codex Round 37 review caught a SECOND-order defect that the Round 37 strengthening missed. Round 37 added `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError): self.assertEqual(per_arch_sum, global_admitted, ...)` to the negative fixture, but the `self.assertEqual` call lived INSIDE the fixture — it was a DUPLICATE of the live scanner's assertion logic, not a CALL into the live scanner's assertion path. A future refactor that changed the live scanner's `self.assertEqual(per_arch_sum, global_admitted)` at the previous line ~830 (e.g., downgrade to a logged warning, swap to `assertGreaterEqual`, remove entirely) would NOT cause the negative fixture to red-state — the fixture would keep firing AssertionError on ITS OWN local `assertEqual(60, 74)` regardless of what happened in the live scanner. The Round 37 summary's claim that "a future regression that softened the live assertion would surface as `AssertionError` not raised" was therefore incorrect: only a regression to the FIXTURE's local copy would surface that way. The fix generalizes the negative-fixture exercise discipline a level deeper: a negative fixture for a scanner contract MUST CALL THE SAME ASSERTION HELPER the live test calls — not duplicate the assertion logic in the fixture body. Concretely: extract a module-level helper (here: `_assert_per_arch_admitted_counts_sum_to_global(testcase, v5_head, sm_120_head, sm_86_head)`) that owns BOTH the per-arch contribution math AND the `testcase.assertEqual` call. The live test calls the helper directly with committed artifacts (positive evidence). The negative fixture calls the SAME helper with synthetic artifacts under `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):` (red-state proof). Now both tests share one assertion path, but the proof property is ASYMMETRIC, not symmetric: a future refactor that softens the helper's `assertEqual` will ALWAYS turn the negative fixture red (because `assertRaises` no longer sees the expected `AssertionError` on the synthetic 60-vs-74 mismatch), but will NOT necessarily turn the live test red — the live test only catches softening when committed artifacts ARE divergent, and on the current same-30 surface the data is internally consistent so the trivially-satisfied equality assertion is not what red-states the live test. Sanity-verified by temporarily neutering the helper's `assertEqual` (replacing with `_ = per_arch_sum`) and running both tests: the live test PASSED (consistent data), the negative fixture FAILED with `AssertionError not raised` as predicted. The asymmetry is fine and was already implicit in the design — the negative fixture is the regression guard for helper-INTEGRITY (catches softening regardless of data state); the live test is the regression guard for committed-artifact DIVERGENCE (catches real per-arch-vs-global disagreement when it occurs). The Round 38 prose initially overstated this as 'BOTH tests turn red'; Round 39 corrects the docstring, summary, BitLesson, and tracker prose accordingly. Source rounds: round-37 (added assertRaises wrapper but kept duplicated assertEqual inside the fixture) → round-38 (Codex caught the duplication; shared module-level helper extracted; both tests now call it; live-assertion-softening regression-guard is now load-bearing). Round 38 validation evidence: 28 tests pass + 8 subtests in the cohort scanner suite; broad sweep 1244 passed (byte-equivalent to Round 37); proof check confirmed the negative fixture turns red on helper-softening while the live test stays green on consistent same-30 data (asymmetric proof; corrected in Round 39 prose).

Update Note (round-38 → round-39, assertRaisesRegex-pins-source-and-asymmetric-proof-discipline axis): Codex Round 38 review caught TWO more refinements to the negative-fixture discipline. First, the Round 38 fixture used broad `with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):` around the whole shared-helper call. The shared helper has FOUR potential assertion points (assertIsInstance on global int, assertIsInstance on per-arch int, self.fail on unexpected status, and finally the cross-artifact assertEqual). A broad assertRaises would also pass if the helper raised from a PRECONDITION before reaching the equality assertion. The current synthetic state uses status="ok" + int admitted_count so the failure DOES reach assertEqual today, but the test does NOT pin that source. A future fixture edit that accidentally sets status="weird" or non-int admitted_count would still pass — the fixture would no longer be testing the cross-artifact equality path it claims to test. Fix: replace `assertRaises(AssertionError)` with `assertRaisesRegex(AssertionError, "<exact equality message format>")`. Concretely here: `assertRaisesRegex(AssertionError, "cross-artifact cohort-count consistency invariant violated")`. Add fixture-construction sanity checks (status=="ok", admitted_count is int) so the routing is also explicit at the call site. Sanity-prove the regex distinguishes correctly by flipping synthetic state to force a precondition raise (e.g., status="weird_unexpected"): the regex won't match the self.fail message, fixture fails, confirming the source-pinning works. The general principle: a negative fixture's assertRaises wrapper must pin the EXACT raise source, not merely "some assertion in the helper". This generalizes BL-20260527-test-fixture-shape-must-mirror-real-shape: the synthetic FIXTURE must match the real shape, AND the assertRaises pattern must match the real failure CHANNEL. Second, the Round 38 helper docstring + summary + BitLesson + tracker all overstated the coupling between live test and negative fixture as "BOTH tests turn red on helper-softening". The Round 38 sanity check itself contradicted this: with the helper's assertEqual neutered, the live test PASSED because the same-30 surface data IS consistent (the trivially-satisfied equality assertion isn't what red-states the live test on this surface), and only the negative fixture failed with AssertionError not raised. The actual proof is ASYMMETRIC, not symmetric: the negative fixture catches helper-softening regardless of live data state; the live test only catches real divergence in committed artifacts when present. Both are useful, but the "both turn red" claim is factually wrong on a consistent-data surface. Fix: correct the prose in all four locations (helper docstring, Round 38 summary, BitLesson, tracker) inline. The general discipline: when describing test-architecture proof properties, write what the tests ACTUALLY guarantee, not the symmetric-sounding version. Run the symmetry test (neuter the inner assertion, observe what happens) BEFORE writing the prose to avoid this overstatement class. Source rounds: round-38 (negative fixture used broad assertRaises + claimed both-tests-symmetric coupling) → round-39 (Codex caught both gaps; assertRaisesRegex pins source; asymmetric proof prose corrected). Round 39 validation evidence: 28 tests pass + 8 subtests in the cohort scanner suite; broad sweep 1244 passed (byte-equivalent to Round 38); regex-distinguishes-source sanity prove confirmed flipping synthetic status to "weird_unexpected" causes the assertRaisesRegex to mismatch and the fixture to fail.

Update Note (round-39 → round-40, preparatory-test-for-currently-dormant-code-path axis): Codex Round 39 review's Mainline Drift Audit warned that rounds 36-39 had been polishing the same cross-artifact invariant scanner area, while the original numerical SAME-74 ACs remained blocked by operator-pending sm_86 staging. The autonomous-mainline-paralysis pattern is REAL but the "user is asleep + don't block on operator" memory still applies: the answer is to find autonomous preparatory work that exercises CODE PATHS THAT WILL BECOME LOAD-BEARING the moment the operator landing happens, on a DIFFERENT module than the recent scanner refinements. Concretely: `tools/local_labels/run_v5_active_path_comparison.py::_compute_transfer_diagnostics(sm_86_ensemble, sm_120_ensemble, sm_86_cells, sm_120_cells)` contains a both-arch-admitted code branch that short-circuits to `{"n": 0}` whenever sm_86_ensemble is None. Every Round 1-39 invocation hit that short-circuit because sm_86 is operator-blocked. The both-arch-admitted branch had ZERO test coverage; its existence was implicit in the code but no test exercised it. A latent bug in that branch (wrong native lookup, swapped target arch, missing cell filter, off-by-one) would silently emit indeterminate transfers on the eventual same-74 surface and block cross-arch transfer closure during a precious operator-coordinated round. The fix generalizes: when external blockers stall the live data surface from exercising a code branch for many rounds, write a synthetic-fixture unit test for that branch with stub objects that mirror the runtime contract (here: `_StubEnsemble.predict(arch, family_bucket, features) -> float`). Test the both-cases independently (here: both-arch-admitted produces both transfer directions ok + only-source-arch-admitted preserves the current indeterminate-on-both-directions behavior). The test must reference module-level constants (TRANSFER_EPSILON_FLOOR, TRANSFER_REL_GATE_MULTIPLIER, TRANSFER_MEDIAN_ABS_GATE_MAX) so future tuning is also pinned. The general discipline: dormant code paths blocked by external dependencies are exactly the paths that need preparatory test coverage — they have the worst signal-to-noise ratio for bug detection in normal use, and they will surface during high-stakes coordination windows. The autonomous-mainline-paralysis solution is NOT to keep polishing audit scanners in the recent area; it IS to identify the dormant code paths and pin their contract with synthetic-fixture tests BEFORE the external blocker resolves. Source rounds: rounds 36-39 (consecutive cross-artifact scanner refinements; Codex Round 39 FGAC warned of mainline drift) → round-40 (preparatory test for dormant cross-arch transfer code path; targets a DIFFERENT module + new code path rather than yet another scanner refinement). Round 40 validation: 5 cases pass (3 pre-existing + 2 new); broad sweep 1246 passed +2 from Round 39's 1244; plan-token scanner green (3 "AC-4" docstring fragments re-prosed to "cross-arch transfer" before commit).

Update Note (round-40 → round-41, preparatory-test-coverage-extends-to-artifact-writer axis): Round 40 demonstrated the preparatory-test-for-dormant-code-path pattern on a COMPUTATION function (`_compute_transfer_diagnostics`). Round 41 extends the pattern to an ARTIFACT-WRITER function (`_emit_arch_head_artifact`'s status=ok branch) — the writer for sm_86_v5_head.json. The artifact-writer's status=ok branch is currently dormant for sm_86 (always emits status=indeterminate because sm_86 has 0 admitted cells). When the operator-blocked sm_86 cross-host scp lands and the per-arch fitter runs, the status=ok branch fires for sm_86 for the first time. A latent bug in that branch (missing `identity` key handling, wrong `provenance_summary` access, broken feature_schema propagation, wrong key set) would silently emit a malformed sm_86_v5_head.json. The fix generalizes the Round 40 pattern: dormant code paths come in two flavors — computation functions (math/aggregation) AND artifact-writer functions (JSON output emission). Both deserve preparatory test coverage. The artifact-writer pattern has an additional discipline: assert BOTH the in-memory return value AND the file-on-disk JSON have identical shape (catches serialization-format bugs that wouldn't show up in return-only assertions). Also: pair a status=ok test (the dormant branch) with a status=indeterminate test (the current-surface branch) as a regression guard, so accidental refactors of the dormant branch don't leak ok-only fields into the indeterminate path. Both-branch + both-branch-only fields should be separately asserted: both-branch fields (arch, schema_version, calibration_head_version, source_commit, source_v5_head_commit, composite_score_formula, feature_schema) must be in both artifacts; ok-only fields (training_cell_ids, per_bucket_provenance) must be absent from the indeterminate artifact; indeterminate-only fields (reason) must be absent from the ok artifact. Source rounds: round-40 (preparatory pattern for computation function on same module) → round-41 (preparatory pattern extends to artifact-writer function on same module). Round 41 validation evidence: 7 cases pass (3 pre-existing + 2 Round 40 + 2 new Round 41); broad sweep 1248 passed +2 from Round 40's 1246; plan-token scanner green (1 AC-1/AC-2 docstring fragment re-prosed before commit). The preparatory pattern is sustainable: it produces ~2 small unit tests per round targeting a different dormant code path, builds out a coherent "same-74 readiness suite" over multiple rounds, and avoids the side-issue-churn pattern Codex Round 39 FGAC warned about.

Update Note (round-41 → round-42, fixture-must-use-production-construction-path-not-hand-rolled-mock axis): Codex Round 41 review caught a real defect in the Round 41 preparatory test: the status=ok fixture for `_emit_arch_head_artifact` used hand-rolled 4-field identity tuples `(arch, slug, shape_point, family_bucket)`, but the production `_rehydrate_cells_from_v5_head` creates 3-field tuples `(arch, slug, shape_point)`. The live `sm_120_v5_head.json` confirms 3-field training_cell_ids (each entry length 3). The Round 41 test therefore did NOT pin the shape the live writer will emit when sm_86 status=ok fires — it proved only that the writer can serialize a fabricated 4-field shape. The fix generalizes the fixture-shape discipline previously captured by `BL-20260527-test-fixture-shape-must-mirror-real-shape`: a fixture is not free to fabricate the shape of objects the production path constructs. When a test exercises a function downstream of a production constructor (here: the writer is downstream of the rehydrator), the fixture must CALL THE PRODUCTION CONSTRUCTOR to build the input objects, not hand-roll them. The shape mirroring rule is recursive: each layer of the test fixture must use the production-equivalent constructor for the layer ABOVE it, all the way back to the canonical source (here: a synthetic v5 head with an admitted block → rehydrator → per-arch filter → writer). Fabricating any layer breaks the shape contract for every layer below. Concretely the test now: (1) calls the existing `_synthetic_v5_head(admit_count=6)` helper to build the source v5 head with admitted cells (matches the production v5 head shape); (2) calls `runner._rehydrate_cells_from_v5_head(head_v5)` to get production-shape cells with 3-field `identity` tuples; (3) filters to sm_86 cells (mirrors the per-arch filter in production); (4) passes those filtered cells to `_emit_arch_head_artifact`. Additionally adds a fixture-construction guard asserting each cell's `identity` is a 3-tuple before the writer runs, making the production contract explicit at the call site. Also adds `assertNotIn("reason", returned)` on status=ok (matches the existing `assertNotIn(...)` for ok-only fields on the indeterminate test). Drops the dead `_build_synthetic_cells_with_identity` helper that fabricated the 4-field tuples. Source rounds: round-41 (introduced fixture with fabricated 4-field tuples; selected the fixture-shape lesson but violated it in implementation) → round-42 (Codex caught the gap; fixture rebuilt via production constructor; 3-field shape pinned). Round 42 validation: 7 cases pass (3 pre-existing + 2 Round 40 + 2 Round 41 now correctly shaped); broad sweep 1248 passed (byte-equivalent to Round 41); plan-token scanner green. The discipline is now sharper: selecting the fixture-shape lesson means SHIPPING a fixture that calls the production constructor, not just citing the lesson in the BitLesson note.

Update Note (round-42 → round-43, readiness-suite-extends-from-unit-to-integration-level axis): The preparatory-test-for-dormant-code-path pattern (introduced in round-40) has produced a same-74 readiness suite covering function-level units in `run_v5_active_path_comparison.py`: `_compute_transfer_diagnostics` both-arch-admitted branch (Round 40), `_emit_arch_head_artifact` status=ok branch (Round 41/42). The next dormant level is INTEGRATION — the `runner.main()` pipeline with BOTH per-arch ensembles non-None AND `build_selected_ensemble` returning an ok dispatch with a non-None full ensemble. The existing `NonOkDispatchEmitsIndeterminateBundleTest` (3 cases) covers non-ok dispatch via patched `_fit_arch_head` returning None; the complement (both-arch-admitted) is dormant because sm_86 currently has 0 admitted cells. The fix generalizes the dormant-code-path pattern: preparatory test coverage should layer from FUNCTION-level (unit) to INTEGRATION-level (pipeline) when both layers are dormant for the same external blocker. Concretely the new integration test exercises the FULL main() body — argv parsing → v5 head load → cell rehydration → per-arch filter → `_fit_arch_head` (patched) → `_emit_arch_head_artifact` for each arch → `build_selected_ensemble` (patched) → per-cell prediction loop → `_compute_transfer_diagnostics` → bundle emission with `_fitted`-gated aggregate metrics. A SINGLE `_StubEnsembleFull` class consolidates the three single-surface stubs from earlier rounds (`_StubEnsemble`'s .predict + `_StubEnsembleWithProvenance`'s .provenance_summary + a new `_fitted=True` attribute) because main() interrogates the SAME ensemble object through all three call sites.
