Update Note (round-17, TWO producers feeding one consumer must use the SAME model — one silently relying on the consumer's fallback is a producer-consistency gap): Codex [P2]: `predict_cache_hit_rate` is fed by TWO producers — the headline cohort runner (resolves a rich cache-model geometry from the device-properties manifest: 6 L2 slices + CTA interleave + HRD second layer + provenance) and the per-cell fresh-regen bundle builder (`build_predictor_bundle_for_cell.py`, which passed only L2 capacity and so let the walker fall back to num_l2_slices=1 / interleave-off / second-layer-off). So a `REBUILD_BUNDLE_LOCAL` bundle was computed under a DIFFERENT cache model than the trace sidecars / headline runner — a gap the consumer's schema cannot catch (the bundle is structurally valid; only its predictions are wrong). GENERAL RULE: when multiple producers feed one consumer, every producer must establish the SAME model/config the consumer is sensitive to; a producer that omits config and rides the consumer's defaults silently diverges. FIX SHAPE (Codex ask-codex confirmed): factor a SINGLE shared resolver — NOT by importing one producer-script into another (script-to-script coupling), and NOT in the manifest-loader module (which stays a loader) — and call it at BOTH producer sites. Populate only the arch the loop develops (sm_86) and FAIL CLOSED with a typed reason (`unsupported_local_cache_model_geometry_arch:<arch>`) for any other arch rather than emit a wrong-geometry bundle from the fallback; do NOT bake a host-specific manifest key for a deferred arch (sm_120). Use the manifest entry as the single source for capacity AND slices (not a parallel hard-coded table). GUARD AGAINST DRIFT WITHOUT A FORCED REFACTOR: rather than refactor the working producer to delegate (test risk), add a DIVERGENCE-GUARD test that asserts the shared resolver's policy (manifest key + interleave flag + resolved entry/provenance) equals the other producer's — so the two duplicated surfaces can't drift, at far lower risk than rewriting the working path. Latent-fix discipline: the committed bundles predated the new geometry (built at an old commit, not by the current fresh-regen path) → no committed-value change, no regeneration this round. No recapture cascade: `build_predictor_bundle_for_cell.py` is not in the recapture guard's `_PREDICTOR_TOUCHING_PATHS`, so the predictor commit stays F=HEAD^ and the fix amends HEAD. Source rounds: round-17 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop (2026-05-29); Codex Review-Phase [P2] on build_predictor_bundle_for_cell.py:952-959.
Update Note (round-19, the SAME per-device constant table duplicated across two files carries the SAME inherited bug — fix at all sites + mirror the negative-control test): Codex [P2]: `_RTX_3090_TI_PROPS["driver_version"]` hard-coded the CUDA toolkit string `"13.2"` (which belongs in `cuda_version`) instead of the NVIDIA driver version `"595.71.05"` — in BOTH `activation_trace_orchestrator.py` AND `stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py` (a per-device props table mirrored across two producers). A PARTIAL earlier fix had corrected the sibling 5060 entry (and added a 5060-only negative-control test) but left the 3090 Ti slot wrong in both files AND untested. GENERAL RULE (extends R16/R17's all-sites discipline to a duplicated CONSTANT table): when a per-device constant table is mirrored across files, grep the table name and correct EVERY copy; cross-check the correct value against an INDEPENDENT authoritative source (here the committed sm_86 label sidecars + the device-properties manifest, both `595.71.05`) rather than trusting any one in-code copy. NEGATIVE-CONTROL DISCIPLINE: when a sibling case (the 5060) already has a guard test, add the EQUIVALENT for the fixed case (the 3090 Ti) in each file's test home — and make it a negative control that asserts the field is BOTH the right value AND distinct from the look-alike field it was confused with (`driver_version != cuda_version`), so a future copy-paste of the toolkit string is caught. LATENT (the offending in-tree manifests carrying `13.2` are gitignored host-local, not in the branch diff; the committed label sidecars are already correct → no tracked-value change, no regeneration). Amend per BL-20260527 (neither file is a predictor/provenance path). Source rounds: round-19 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop (2026-05-29); Codex Review-Phase [P2] on activation_trace_orchestrator.py:361 + the mirrored stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py table.
Update Note (round-21, an inherited FALLBACK path hardcodes ONE class while the design + schema say it should use the SELECTED class — honor the selection but DISPATCH to preserve the frozen artifact): Codex [P2]: `PerBucketCalibratorEnsemble.fit` hardcoded `PooledIsotonicWithBucketOneHot()` (sklearn) for every sub-`PER_BUCKET_MIN_N` bucket REGARDLESS of the class the pre-registered hierarchy selected, so (1) the documented pygam/LightGBM/XGBoost fallback tiers crashed on the sklearn import for any cohort with a small bucket when sklearn is absent, and (2) with sklearn present + selected!=IR the pooled buckets were trained isotonic while provenance labeled them `{selected}__pooled_fallback_with_one_hot`. NEW twist vs the R6/R9/R10/R14 producer/scaffold and R16/R17/R19 mirrored-surface findings: the bug is in a MODEL FALLBACK path, and the loop's MAINLINE deliverable artifact (the committed `sm_86_v5_head.json`) was PRODUCED by the buggy code — so the fix had to preserve it byte-for-byte. DISAMBIGUATING SIGNAL: the schema (`calibration_head_v5.schema.json`) DOCUMENTED the provenance convention (`model_class_per_bucket` appends `__pooled_fallback_with_one_hot` to "the class name") — i.e. the design already intended the pooled head to BE the selected class in pooled mode; the implementation just didn't honor it. So the correct fix (Codex ask-codex Option A) is to make the fallback HONOR THE SELECTION, NOT to rename the provenance to the hardcoded class + make sklearn a hard dep (Option B, which would MUTATE the committed head's labels + contradict the schema convention for no benefit). FREEZE-PRESERVING TECHNIQUE: introduce a generic wrapper (`PooledMonotoneWithBucketOneHot(base_factory)` realizing `family_bucket` as a per-bucket additive one-hot offset — a categorical main-effect `clip(base(features)+bucket_offset[bucket],0,1)` — over ANY base via the same backfit), but DISPATCH so the already-exercised class (sklearn IR, the only class ever selected on the sklearn-equipped fit host) keeps its EXACT existing implementation (`PooledIsotonicWithBucketOneHot`) and only the never-exercised classes route through the new wrapper. PROVE FREEZE-SAFETY TWO WAYS: (a) re-fit the artifact in the real fit env WITH the fix and with the source fix STASHED → byte-identical output (the change is inert for the exercised selection); (b) the re-fit reproduces EVERY model/provenance field of the committed artifact (only env-derived fields — source_commit — differ). Leave the committed artifact FILE untouched (re-fits go to a temp path). REGRESSION TESTS: a fake non-sklearn selected class proves the fallback no longer imports sklearn (stash-proven: pre-fix raises the sklearn ImportError on a sklearn-absent host) + a generic≡specialization equivalence test (sklearn-guarded) pins the dispatch-safety claim. Inherited (`a90c7047` "true one-hot pooled head" / `c28e304d`, ancestors of `15d9b49b`); not a recapture/provenance path → amend keeps F=HEAD^. Source rounds: round-21 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop (2026-05-29); Codex Review-Phase [P2] on monotonic_calibration_head.py:711-712.

## Lesson: runner-injected-typed-reason-stays-out-of-predictor-enum
Lesson ID: BL-20260529-runner-injected-typed-reason-stays-out-of-predictor-enum
Scope: any consumer/comparison module that INJECTS a typed_reason onto a bundle it mutates (then re-validates the mutated bundle), where the producer module has a typed-reason enum AND a post-commit recapture guard keys "the predictor/producer fix commit" on `git log -1 -- <producer source paths>`. Concrete instance: `comparison_runner.run_comparison`'s v5 active-path block writes `typed_reason="v5_active_path_unscorable"` on a scalar bundle the v5 head cannot score, then `_validate_predictor_bundle_shape` re-validates it against `cache_predictor._TYPED_REASONS`.
Problem Description: The obvious fix for "validator rejects the injected reason" is to add it to the producer enum (`cache_predictor._TYPED_REASONS`). But that edits `cache_predictor.py`, which the post-commit recapture guards (`test_l2_reduction_post_commit_recapture.py` + `test_l2_high_footprint_post_commit_recapture.py`) treat as a predictor-fix: they compute the predictor commit as `git log -1 -- cache_predictor.py <shadow generators>`, so the touch ADVANCES that commit from the prior recapture anchor (3a787e7a) to HEAD, and the 6 drift-sensitive recapture artifacts (still pinning the old anchor) red-state. The guards' intended remedy is a SEPARATE chore-recapture commit C (predictor F = HEAD~1, artifacts pin F) — but in a loop that holds an accept-window restamp (`eb780459`) at HEAD^ via the single-amend strategy, adding commit C pushes that restamp to HEAD^^ and breaks `test_post_commit_source_commit_drift` (`{HEAD, HEAD^}`), forcing a ~40-artifact re-restamp cascade (label + bundles + ledger + predicted_l1 + ac5 + sidecars + trace JSONs + 18 digest re-syncs).
Root Cause: A consumer-injected typed_reason was conflated with a producer-emitted one. The producer (cache_predictor) never emits `v5_active_path_unscorable`; the runner does. Putting it in the producer enum (a) misrepresents ownership and (b) makes an unrelated producer-source edit, tripping the recapture-provenance guard for a non-predictor change.
Solution: Admit the injected reason in the CONSUMER's validator via a CONSUMER-OWNED set, leaving the producer enum + producer source untouched. Concretely: define `comparison_runner._CR_INJECTED_TYPED_REASONS = frozenset({"v5_active_path_unscorable"})` and change the validator to `if typed_reason not in (cp._TYPED_REASONS | _CR_INJECTED_TYPED_REASONS): raise`. Keep the producer's own checks (`cache_predictor._typed_unavailable_bundle`, the predict_l1 structural path) REJECTING the reason (the producer must not emit it). The on-disk schema enum (`cache_predictor.schema.json` typed_reason) is the UNION of producer + consumer-injected reasons (it validates the bundle on disk, which can carry either). Because no producer-source path is touched, `git log -1 -- cache_predictor.py` reverts to the prior recapture anchor and ALL recapture guards pass with ZERO cascade, and the accept window stays intact via amend (no chore commit). Verify: after reverting the producer edit, `git diff <HEAD^> -- cache_predictor.py` is empty (the amended commit does not touch it) and `git log -1 --format=%H -- cache_predictor.py` == the artifacts' recorded source_commit.
Constraints: update any ingress test/comment that asserted the reason is in the producer enum to assert CONSUMER admission instead (assert it is in `_CR_INJECTED_TYPED_REASONS` and NOT in `cp._TYPED_REASONS`, and that the validator admits it). Applies whenever the cheap producer-enum edit would advance a recapture/provenance guard that a downstream restamp depends on; when the reason is genuinely producer-emitted, it DOES belong in the producer enum (then the recapture/cascade is the correct cost). The discriminator: does the PRODUCER ever write this reason? If no, it is consumer-owned. Confirmed via a Codex `ask-codex` consult (the loop's analyze route) before implementing, because the fix diverged from the literal wording of the prior review finding ("widen the runtime enum").
Validation Evidence: Round 8 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop (2026-05-29). Reverted the cache_predictor.py `_TYPED_REASONS` add; added `_CR_INJECTED_TYPED_REASONS` + union validator in comparison_runner.py; updated the ingress test. Post-amend: `git log -1 -- cache_predictor.py` = 3a787e7a (= the 6 artifacts' source_commit); both recapture guards + both accept-window scanners + the v5 tests = 115 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed; HEAD^ = eb780459 (accept window intact, no chore commit). Paired [P2]: the SASS manifest generator now records `binary.relative_to(_REPO_ROOT)` and the 9 committed manifests were rewritten repo-relative (inherited absolute paths from a different host layout).
Source Rounds: round-8 of the sm86_cohort_regen_3090ti loop (2026-05-29); Codex Review-Phase [P1] (recapture artifacts) + [P2] (absolute SASS binary_path).


## Lesson: schema-version-const-bump-migration-active-cohort-scope
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-schema-version-const-bump-migration-active-cohort-scope
Scope: any JSON-schema `schema_version` const bump on a closed-world (`additionalProperties: false`) wire contract that has committed data files AND many test fixtures (e.g. `workloads/simbench_bw/schemas/local_label_admitted.schema.json` const 4->5 and `local_label_sidecar.schema.json` 1->5, with the unit-tagged-metrics additive block).
Problem Description: Bumping a `schema_version` to a new `const` invalidates EVERY artifact validated against the schema. Four traps surfaced in one round: (1) the committed local-label store carried not only the migrate-from version (admitted v4 / sidecar v1) but ALSO older historical versions in the `data/local_labels/sweeps/` subtree (admitted v2 and v3) that were never current-conformant and are NOT schema-validated by any consumer (the aggregator excludes the sweeps subtree) -- a naive "migrate every committed admitted file" pass touched 271 sweep files (unnecessary churn + digest-drift risk against predictor-bundle/ledger sha256 references); (2) tests validate the per-record sub-schema standalone via `schema["$defs"]["admitted_cell"]`, so a `$ref: "#/$defs/<sibling>"` placed inside `admitted_cell` raises `PointerToNowhere` (the resolver root is the sub-schema, which has no `$defs`); (3) the per-launch sidecar (`label_<r>_<l>.json`) and its run-manifest (`label_run_manifest_<r>_<l>.json`) are SEPARATE schemas at independent versions -- bumping the sidecar must NOT touch the manifest (stays v1), and `label_excluded.json`/`label_repeatability.json` are also independent v1; (4) the lockstep surface is large -- producer version stamps live in multiple emitters (`repeatability_aggregator.py`, `merge_admitted_cells.py`, `select_best_config_labels.py`) and ~12 test files build fixtures at the old version.
Root Cause: A closed-world schema's `const` version is a hard parse-time boundary, so "what must migrate" is exactly "what is schema-validated against the live schema by some consumer/test" -- NOT "every file that happens to carry that key." Historical per-config snapshots under a sweeps/ subtree are frozen evidence the active pipeline never re-validates; mixing them into the migration is churn at best and digest drift at worst. And JSON-Schema `$ref` is root-relative, so any sub-schema that is ever validated standalone must be self-contained.
Solution: (a) Scope the migration AND the cohort-uniform scanner to the ACTIVE store only -- the operator-facing `data/local_labels/label_admitted.json` plus the non-sweep per-launch sidecars; `git checkout HEAD -- data/local_labels/sweeps/` to revert any sweep-subtree churn, and exclude `/sweeps/` in the scanner's `git ls-files` walk. (b) INLINE the items schema into the sub-schema's array property (no `$ref` to a sibling `$defs`) whenever a test validates that sub-schema standalone; keep `$ref` only where validation always goes through the root document (e.g. the full sidecar via `import_label.validate`). (c) Migrate value-preservingly with a regex on the exact `"schema_version": <old>` token (word-boundary) so only the version digit changes; confirm via `git diff` that the operator file's diff is schema_version-only (proves the frozen-head fit input is byte-preserved). (d) Bump the sidecar but leave run-manifest / excluded / repeatability at their own versions. (e) Run the FULL sweep to enumerate breakages by file; fix fixtures at the shared-helper source (one edit per `_make_*`/`_valid_*` helper) and bump producer stamps in lockstep; add a hard-fail cohort-uniform-vN scanner test (per BL-20260526-cohort-scanner-test-prevents-uneven-upgrade) so no active-store straggler is left behind. (f) Add an explicit mixed-version boundary test (a complete v(N-1) record/file is rejected by the vN schema) to pin the "old reader rejects new / new reader rejects old" contract.
Constraints: keep historical sweep snapshots frozen (do not re-stamp them). The active-cohort scanner must exclude the sweeps subtree or it red-states on the legitimate historical v2/v3 artifacts. New unit-tagged metric fields stay OPTIONAL (not in `required`) so legacy cells without them remain valid (per BL-20260526-schema-additionalproperties-false-requires-lockstep). The `tools/local_labels` test subset is NOT sufficient to validate a label-schema bump -- the workloads/simbench_bw/tests side (comparison_runner, cross_arch harness, l1_mean_migration) also builds admitted-cell fixtures and MUST be in the breakage sweep.
Validation Evidence: Round 0 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30). Migrated operator label_admitted.json (50 cells) + 60 non-sweep sidecars + 2 committed fixtures to v5 (value-preserving; operator diff = schema_version lines only); reverted 271 sweep admitted + 84 sweep sidecars. Inlined `admitted_unit_tagged_metric` into `admitted_cell.unit_tagged_metrics.items`. New tests: `test_metric_registry.py` (16), `test_schema_v5_lockstep.py` (13, incl. cohort-uniform-v5 scanner + mixed-version + unit-path-mismatch + denylist), `test_frozen_l2_v5_regression_guard.py`. Full host-only sweep: only the 5 pre-existing failures remained (post_commit_source_commit_drift x2, regeneration_delta_scanner x2, no_root_or_ncu_static_guard x1 on the repo-root read_ncu_report.py) -- zero new failures.
Source Rounds: round-0 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).

Update Note (round-0 -> round-1, real-producer/consumer-wiring-completes-the-substrate axis): a schema-version substrate slice is NOT complete when only the in-process APIs + offline JSON-Schema validators enforce it. Codex Round-0 review rejected AC-1 because (a) the real producer (label_driver.sh + the parse_ncu_metrics.py CLI main) still routed every metric through the percentage-only path and never exported SIDECAR_UNIT_TAGGED_METRICS_PATH, so the new extract_unit_tagged_metrics function was unreachable from the actual capture path; and (b) the real consumer ingress (comparison_runner._load_admitted_label_cells / run_comparison) accepted a stale pre-v5 admitted artifact without a typed migration failure, so the schema const-5 boundary was only enforced by a standalone jsonschema.validate in a test, not at the place real data enters. The fix that closed AC-1 in round-1: (1) give the producer CLI a registry-aware path that imports the report ONCE and writes a schema-ready unit-tagged JSON array, wire the driver's repeatable --unit-metric into the single NCU --metrics replay + the parser + the sidecar env, and make the emitter FAIL CLOSED when its unit-tagged-path env var is set but unreadable/malformed/non-array (a silent drop would let a capture claim labels it did not carry); (2) add a consumer-ingress version gate at BOTH the file level (raise a typed AdmittedLabelV5MigrationError on a non-v5 file) and the per-cell level (exclude with a typed reason). Two refinements worth pinning: the per-cell version gate must use PRESENT-AND-!=N semantics (`sv = cell.get("schema_version"); if sv is not None and sv != 5`), NOT strict `!= 5`, or it excludes legitimately-unversioned in-memory test cells (real wire cells always carry the schema-required version, so present-and-wrong is the real stale-artifact signal); and the gate's blast radius spans EVERY run_comparison caller (12 test files), several of which build admitted cells at the old version (test_require_both_arches_for_cross_arch, the test_cli_main v1 file) and must be bumped in lockstep. The producer test MUST drive label_driver.sh with a fake NCU report (not call build_sidecar directly) so it proves the real path — mirrors the test-fixture-shape-must-mirror-real-shape discipline. Generalized rule: "substrate landed" requires the real producer emits it, the real consumer enforces it at ingress, and a fail-closed test exercises each through its real entry point — offline validators + in-process API tests are necessary but not sufficient. Source rounds: round-0 (substrate + in-process APIs + offline validators) -> round-1 (real producer CLI/driver wiring + consumer-ingress typed v5 rejection + registry value-range enforcement; full host-only sweep 5131 passed, only the 5 pre-existing failures).


## Lesson: new-entry-point-script-repo-invariant-checklist
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-new-entry-point-script-repo-invariant-checklist
Scope: adding any new `def main()`-bearing Python script or bash-shebang script under a non-excluded tree (especially tools/local_labels/), and any new NCU-invoking driver; the tools/test_arch_capabilities_coverage.py + tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py repo invariants.
Problem Description: Round 2 of the sm86_unified_multimetric loop added a new NCU metric-availability probe (tools/local_labels/probe_ncu_metric_availability.py, with a `def main()`) plus its tests. Targeted pytest runs of the new files + the directly-edited modules were all green, so the round looked done. The FULL host-only sweep then surfaced two NEW failures the targeted runs never touched: (1) tools/test_arch_capabilities_coverage.py::test_every_discovered_entry_point_is_declared — `discover_entry_points()` walks git ls-files and flags every non-test `.py` with `def main()` (and every bash-shebang `.sh`) that lacks a matching {path, kind, supported_arches, notes} entry in tools/arch_capabilities.json; and (2) tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py — the scanner forbids the plan tokens AC-N / Milestone / Phase / Step / "Round N" in implementation files INCLUDING test files and INCLUDING docstrings (e.g. `"""Tests for ... (AC-4)."""` and a comment `behind AC-3's ...`). Both invariants are global (whole-repo enumeration), so a per-file targeted test run cannot catch them.
Root Cause: Two repo invariants are enforced only by whole-repo enumeration tests, not by any per-module test. A new operator/driver script is "project-owned entry point" the moment it has a main(); a docstring is "implementation file" content the moment the file is a scanned extension. Neither shows up when you run only the new file's own tests or the modules you edited.
Solution: When adding a new script/entry-point, run a fixed checklist BEFORE the full sweep: (1) if the new `.py` has `def main()` (or the new `.sh` has a bash shebang) and is not under an excluded prefix, add a declaration to tools/arch_capabilities.json `entries` with {path, kind (operator_entry_point | feature_extractor | collection_helper | cloud_target_deferred), supported_arches, notes} — kind=operator_entry_point for an operator-invoked CLI/driver; a pure library (no main) needs no entry. (2) Keep ALL implementation files — code AND comments AND docstrings — free of the plan tokens AC-N / AC-D-prefix / Milestone / Phase / Step / "Round N"; reference acceptance criteria only in plans/, references/, .humanize/, or .md (the exempt prefixes/extensions), or by domain language. (3) ALWAYS run `pytest tools/test_arch_capabilities_coverage.py tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py` (the two whole-repo invariants) in the targeted set for any round that adds a script — do not rely on the new file's own tests. Generalized rule: whole-repo enumeration invariants (entry-point coverage, plan-token scan, the no-NCU static guard, cohort-uniform scanners) are invisible to per-file test runs; explicitly include them in the round's targeted verification when the round adds or moves a file.
Validation Evidence: Round 2 (2026-05-30). Full sweep first showed 6 failed = 4 pre-existing drift + 2 new (arch_capabilities coverage + plan-token). Fixes: added a tools/arch_capabilities.json entry for probe_ncu_metric_availability.py (kind operator_entry_point, both local arches); re-prosed 4 AC-N docstring/comment occurrences across probe + 2 test files. Re-run `pytest tools/test_plan_token_scanner.py tools/test_arch_capabilities_coverage.py tools/local_labels/test_probe_ncu_metric_availability.py tools/local_labels/test_no_deployability_leakage_guard.py -q` -> 80 passed, 761 subtests passed. Note: relocating read_ncu_report.py into tools/local_labels/ (a file MOVE, not a new main) did not trip entry-point coverage (no def main) but DID resolve the no-NCU static-guard pre-existing failure (NCU tokens left the non-quarantine root).
Source Rounds: round-2 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).

## Lesson: cache-layer-contract-vs-bundle-vocabulary
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-cache-layer-contract-vs-bundle-vocabulary
Scope: widening `workloads/simbench_bw/comparison_contract.toml` (and the sm_86 submission overlay) `scope.cache_layers` to add a cache layer (e.g. L2-only -> L1+L2); any edit to `comparison_contract.toml`; the comparison_runner cache-layer join and the `...V3LiveScanner` provenance tests.
Problem Description: Round 3 (M1/AC-5) promoting L1/TEX hit rate to a load-bearing scored layer surfaced two traps invisible to per-file test runs. (1) The contract uses contract-side layer labels `"L1"`/`"L2"`, but predictor bundles carry bundle-side labels `"L1TEX"`/`"L2"`; the runner maps bundle->contract via `_BUNDLE_TO_CONTRACT_CACHE_LAYER = {"L1TEX":"L1","L2":"L2"}` BEFORE the membership/join check. The round contract AND the prior-round Codex directive both said widen to `["L1TEX","L2"]` — writing the bundle-side label into the contract makes the mapped contract-side layer (`"L1"`) never equal the literal `"L1TEX"`, so the cache-layer drift gate excludes EVERY L1 bundle. (2) Editing `comparison_contract.toml` changes its sha256, which invalidates the pinned `comparison_contract_sha256` in the LIVE v3 calibration head + evaluation sidecars (`data/local_labels/calibration_head_v3.json` + `calibration_evaluation_v3.json`), raising `sidecar_comparison_contract_sha256_drift` / `evaluation_comparison_contract_sha256_drift` from the `CalibrationHeadV3LiveScanner` / `EvaluationSidecarV3LiveScanner` tests. These two failures only appear in the FULL sweep, not in the directly-edited files' tests.
Root Cause: The comparison contract and the predictor bundles speak DIFFERENT layer vocabularies bridged by one translation dict; the contract must be written in the contract-side vocabulary. And the contract file's hash is a pinned provenance dependency of the live (current-contract-checked) v3 sidecars — but NOT of the historical v1/v2/v4 heads, which are intentionally drift-tolerant. "What must re-stamp on a contract edit" is exactly "the artifacts a LIVE scanner checks against the current contract," not "every artifact carrying the field."
Solution: (a) Use the contract-side label `"L1"` (never `"L1TEX"`) in `scope.cache_layers`; trust `_BUNDLE_TO_CONTRACT_CACHE_LAYER` to bridge the bundle side. (b) Land the driver's L1TEX-bundle emission in lockstep with the widening (append per-cell L1TEX bundles AFTER the L2 fail-closed bundle-count check so L2 discipline is unaffected), else every widened L1 cell is `join_key_unmatched`. (c) Re-stamp `comparison_contract_sha256` in ONLY the two LIVE v3 sidecars (`calibration_head_v3.json` + `calibration_evaluation_v3.json`) to the new contract hash in the SAME change; leave v1/v2/v4 untouched (historical/drift-tolerant). Neither v3 file is in the AC-2 frozen-L2 set, so re-stamping is safe and does not perturb the frozen L2 head. (d) After ANY contract edit, run the two calibration provenance scanners + the FULL sweep, not just per-file tests.
Constraints: the frozen L2 v5 head `sm_86_v5_head.json` and the AC-2 frozen comparison/accuracy artifacts must stay byte-identical — confirm via sha256 + the frozen-L2 regression guard after the widening. `schema_version` is NOT bumped for an additive cache_layers widening. The bundle-side enum `_BUNDLE_CACHE_LAYER_ENUM = {L1TEX,L2}` and the mapping dict are frozen — do not edit them; they already admit both layers.
Solution (process trap, same round): an interrupted tool batch left HEAD pointing at a stray `git commit --amend -m "initial"` that had SQUASHED the prior round's `feat()` commit (rewriting `4b5e399a` "complete M0" into an "initial" message). This repo does NOT use a rolling "initial" commit — each round is its own `feat()` commit on top of the prior. Recover with `git reset --soft <prior-round-commit>` (the squashed commit survives as a dangling object) and re-commit the round as a NEW `feat()` commit. ALWAYS re-verify `git log --oneline` after an amend that an interrupted session may have mis-targeted.
Validation Evidence: Round 3 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30). Widened both contracts to `["L1","L2"]`; fit a separate bounded-[0,1] L1 head (`sm_86_l1_head.json`, frozen L2 byte-identical); emitted the L1 closure scored by comparison_runner (4 scalar / 16 typed_unavailable, meets_floor=false honestly); re-stamped the 2 live v3 sidecars. Full host-only sweep: 4 failed (all pre-existing drift), 5160 passed, 38 skipped — an intermediate sweep had shown the 2 transient `*_comparison_contract_sha256_drift` failures, fixed by the v3 re-stamp. task7 Codex RATIFIED the design for M1/AC-5.
Source Rounds: round-3 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).


## Lesson: fitted-head-must-be-consumed-not-just-emitted
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-fitted-head-must-be-consumed-not-just-emitted
Scope: any round that "fits + commits a calibration head" (e.g. the bounded-[0,1] L1 head sm_86_l1_head.json) whose predictions are supposed to be scored by comparison_runner via a closure/headline driver; per-head coverage floors (DEC-10); regenerating drift-sensitive artifacts.
Problem Description: Round 3 fit + committed sm_86_l1_head.json and its own tests passed, but Codex found the head was DEAD CODE: emit_l1_closure_artifacts.py + run_headline_cohort_predict_compare.py built L1TEX bundles straight from the RAW walker artifact (predicted_l1_per_admitted_cell.json) and called run_comparison WITHOUT ever loading the head. The closure also failed the smoke floor (4 scalar < 5; top-level typed_unavailable, meets_floor=false), and the guard only required relative_error_stats_l1tex.n_cells>=1 so it passed the below-floor closure. AC-5 binds L1 scoring to the smoke-5/publication-10 floor (DEC-10), so "head fitted" is not "head load-bearing".
Root Cause: Emitting an artifact is not the same as consuming it. A calibration head is only load-bearing when the scored path LOADS + VALIDATES + REHYDRATES + APPLIES it and records provenance; a guard that checks only "the layer was scored" (n_cells>=1) cannot distinguish head-calibrated from raw-upstream scoring, nor a met floor from a below-floor diagnostic state.
Solution: (a) The consumer (closure emitter + driver) must load_and_validate the head (fail closed on calibration_head_version/head_kind/cache_layer/fit_status), rehydrate the ensemble via the shared v5_selected_class_dispatch.build_selected_ensemble, and apply it to each scalar row using the SAME feature vector the fitter built (for a single-feature L1 head: zero-fill the L2-coupled canonical features so the frozen projection reduces to raw_prediction). NO silent fallback to the raw prediction when the head cannot score a row -> RAISE. Record an l1_head_provenance block (head path/source_commit/fit_status/selected_class + per-cell raw->calibrated) in the closure. (b) Strengthen the must-be-scored guard to reject below-floor (coverage_gate_verdict.meets_floor != true), top-level non-admitted, vacuous n_cells==0, and missing-l1_head_provenance closures; add the exact below-floor + no-provenance negative fixtures. (c) Meet a per-head coverage floor honestly: the per-cell producer fail-closes to typed_unavailable when the classifier MANIFEST is absent even though a committed trace exists -> regenerate the host-only cuobjdump manifest (only for cells whose on-disk binary sha256 MATCHES the admitted label) to convert them to real scalar predictions; never fabricate values, and keep no-trace / binary-MISMATCH / all-LDGSTS-bypass cells as typed exclusions. (d) Make fit-dependent tests sklearn-reproducible: skip when no model class (sklearn/pygam/lightgbm/xgboost) is importable, and keep a separate sklearn-free committed-artifact provenance test.
Constraints: the frozen L2 v5 head sm_86_v5_head.json must stay byte-identical (the L1 fit/closure must NOT mutate it; verify sha + the AC-2 guard). The in-sample closure geomean (isotonic trains+scores on the same cells) is machinery validation, not held-out accuracy — state this; do not present a tiny geomean as an accuracy claim when N is small. Regenerating a drift-sensitive artifact (predicted_l1) re-stamps its source_commit to the admitted-label value; if the admitted labels are themselves out of the {HEAD,HEAD^} accept window (a queued provenance pass), this surfaces a drift-test failure that is the SAME root cause, NOT a new regression -- verify pre-existing by stashing round changes and running at clean HEAD. The comparison 8-tuple join requires predicted.source_commit == admitted.source_commit, so predicted_l1 cannot be re-stamped to HEAD independently of the admitted labels.
Validation Evidence: Round 4 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30, commit a4f9c3e2). L1 head load-bearing (carveout raw 0.0013->cal 0.9751, int8/tf32 raw 1.0->cal 0.489); regenerated host-only manifests for int8_imma_gemm_traceable + tf32_hmma_gemm_traceable -> 6 scalar; closure top-level admitted, meets_floor=true (6>=5), l1_head_provenance recorded; guard 12 tests incl below-floor + no-provenance negatives; sklearn-free provenance test added; AC-2 byte-identical. Full sweep after the AC-5 repair surfaced 6 additional L2 cascade-recapture failures (test_l2_*_post_commit_recapture) that round-3's predictor-touching cache_predictor.py SCOPE_DOCBLOCK edit had latently introduced (the 6 L2 artifacts stranded at 872f1c3a vs the predictor-touching commit a6180215); re-stamped them 872f1c3a->a6180215 (value-preserving). Final full sweep: 4 failed (the long-standing accept-window drift cluster), 5185 passed -- no new failures, -6 vs the true pre-fix count of 10.
Source Rounds: round-4 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).
Update Note (2026-07-06, Solution (c)'s manifest regeneration reproduces a classifier defect on multi-arch fatbins): regenerating the host-only cuobjdump manifest reproduces the cross-arch contamination, because `tools/cache_predictor/l1_cache_op_classifier._extract_kernel_section` merges fatbin arch slices — it re-arms at every matching `Function :` header (and matches function names by substring), so the manifest concatenates the kernel's SASS across EVERY arch slice, not just the traced one (M1 layernorm: 12 tagged LDG pcs, only 4 real in the traced sm_86 section). The classifier fix + downstream re-run are tracked in the label-pipeline backlog. The ACTIVE Rust sim is unaffected: it classifies cache ops from the control-bits sidecar's opcode_text and consumes no manifests (`sim/mem/src/cache_op.rs`; Rust↔Python vocabulary/eligibility pinned by `tools/cache_predictor/test_cache_op_rust_parity.py`).


## Lesson: nvml-sw-thermal-is-boost-equilibrium-not-a-fault
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-nvml-sw-thermal-is-boost-equilibrium-not-a-fault
Scope: calibrating a per-host NVML reference operating point (SM clock / temp / power) from a READ-ONLY NVML telemetry sweep under sustained GPU load on a desktop Ampere card (RTX 3090 Ti / GA102 / sm_86); any sample-acceptance filter that consults the NVML throttle-reasons bitmask.
Problem Description: Under a sustained user-space CUDA matmul the 3090 Ti reaches ~90C and reports nvmlDeviceGetCurrentClocksThrottleReasons == SW_THERMAL_SLOWDOWN (bit value 32) PERSISTENTLY for the entire steady-state window. A first calibration sweep treated SW_THERMAL_SLOWDOWN as a "slowdown" and put it in the rejection mask, so EVERY active sample was rejected (n_accepted=0). The fail-closed path then correctly refused to emit a reference from rejected data and raised. The result is a calibration that can never succeed, even though the card is at a perfectly valid operating point.
Root Cause: SW thermal slowdown on Ampere is NOT a fault — it is the normal DVFS boost-equilibrium signal. The card boosts its clock until it reaches its thermal/power steady state and then continuously reports SW thermal as the reason it is not boosting higher. The genuine reference operating point the covariate regression / cycles->ns conversion must center on IS that thermally-settled boost clock, so rejecting SW-thermal samples discards exactly the operating point being measured.
Solution: Exclude SW_THERMAL_SLOWDOWN from the hardware-fault rejection mask. Keep HW slowdown / HW thermal slowdown / HW power-brake / SW power cap as rejections (those are genuine faults or external power caps that pull the card BELOW its natural boost). Reject IDLE samples by an active-clock floor (not by the throttle mask). Record sw_thermal_active_fraction (fraction of accepted samples with SW thermal active) in the artifact provenance so a reviewer sees the card ran at thermal equilibrium. Compute the reference per field as the MEDIAN of accepted samples (robust to the warmup ramp + transient dips), reject the warmup window explicitly, and FAIL CLOSED (emit nothing, exit nonzero) when zero samples are accepted — never emit a reference from rejected data. CLAUDE.md Rule 2 (read-only): NEVER set application clocks to force a non-throttled operating point; the reference must be the OBSERVED equilibrium, not a clock-locked one. The downstream consumer (load_reference_operating_point / cycles_to_ns) must detect the unset (nan/-1) and implausible-clock cases and raise a typed diagnostic (nvml_covariate_reference_unset / nvml_clock_implausible) instead of substituting a default.
Constraints: read-only NVML getters only (no clock-lock/persistence/power/MIG/compute-mode mutation, no nvidia-smi clock-lock); the calibration artifact lands under the data/local_labels/ quarantine; sm_86-only (fail closed if the detected device is not an RTX 3090 Ti); the script must exit without writing on a host lacking pynvml/torch so an offline/CI host never overwrites the committed live artifact.
Validation Evidence: Round 6 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30, commit 2f966479). First sweep rejected 3128/3128 samples (SW thermal in the mask) and fail-closed; after excluding SW thermal, 2431/3357 accepted (only warmup rejected), reference SM clock 1350 MHz / temp 90C / power 294.5W / mem 10251 MHz, sw_thermal_active_fraction=1.0, device RTX 3090 Ti driver 595.71.05. Pos+neg fail-closed tests host-independent; AC-2 frozen-L2 byte-identical; full host-only sweep 4 failed (known accept-window drift) / 5173 passed.
Source Rounds: round-6 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).


## Lesson: additive-in-place-splice-when-reaggregation-is-destructive
Lesson ID: BL-20260530-additive-in-place-splice-when-reaggregation-is-destructive
Scope: adding a new ADDITIVE field/block (e.g. unit_tagged_metrics timing labels) to a committed label store (data/local_labels/label_admitted.json) that was built by a multi-pass pipeline (supersession / repair / restamp / merge), where a frozen-head byte-identity guard depends on the existing fields staying byte-for-byte unchanged.
Problem Description: Round 7 / AC-7 needed to add cycles+ns unit_tagged_metrics to the 20 committed admitted sm_86 cells. The "designed" path is to re-run repeatability_aggregator on the per-repeat sidecars. But empirically re-running the bare aggregator on the current sidecars admits ZERO of the 20 committed cells (it reports label_provenance_duplicate / label_repeatability_failed) -- the committed store was produced by a richer multi-pass supersession/repair/restamp pipeline the bare aggregator does not replicate. So "re-run the producer to add a field" would DESTROY the committed store (drop all 20 cells, change the L2 values the frozen v5 head depends on).
Root Cause: A committed artifact built by a multi-pass pipeline is NOT reproducible by re-running a single stage of that pipeline; the bare stage lacks the repairs/supersessions the committed state encodes. Re-derivation is therefore destructive, even though it is the "natural" producer path.
Solution: When the new field is PURELY ADDITIVE (no committed cell carries it yet), splice it in place instead of re-deriving: (1) capture/derive the new block; (2) gate it through the REAL pipeline gate function imported verbatim (do NOT hand-roll the gate -- e.g. call repeatability_aggregator._aggregate_unit_tagged_metrics so the values pass the genuine CV/repeatability gate); (3) read the committed store, add ONLY the new key to each matching cell (keyed on slug/shape_point/arch), preserving every other field; (4) write with the committed file's EXACT json.dumps formatting (match indent AND sort_keys -- check raw==json.dumps(d,indent=2) vs sort_keys variant first) so untouched cells stay byte-identical and only the spliced cells diff. Prove additivity: a field-level protected-key diff vs HEAD must show 0 protected (L2/L1/join-key/source_commit/stddev) fields changed and the only-new-key == the added block, and the frozen-head byte-identity guard must stay green. Provenance: bind a derived label to a cell ONLY when the on-disk source artifact's sha256 MATCHES the cell's committed provenance (e.g. binary_sha256); a mismatch is a TYPED exclusion (timing_binary_sha256_mismatch), never a silent cross-source bind, so the new label shares one source artifact with the existing labels.
Constraints: the frozen L2 v5 head and its byte-identity regression guard must stay green; source_commit must be preserved byte-for-byte by the splice (so the accept-window drift test count does not grow); declare any new capture script in arch_capabilities.json + keep it plan-token-clean; if the capture invokes ncu it must live under tools/local_labels/ (the no-NCU static-guard quarantine allowlist) and use --csv to stdout so no .ncu-rep is written.
Validation Evidence: Round 7 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30, commit 94f5a4e2). Re-aggregation test admitted 0/20 committed cells (proving destructiveness); in-place splice landed timing on 12/20 cells (8 typed exclusions = 7 binary-mismatch + 1 repeatability-failed), git diff 0 protected fields changed (only unit_tagged_metrics added), frozen-L2 guard green, full host-only sweep 4 failed (known drift) / 5183 passed.
Source Rounds: round-7 of the sm86_unified_multimetric_predictor loop (2026-05-30).

## Lesson: uv-requirements-must-pin-full-suite-runtimes
Lesson ID: BL-20260531-uv-requirements-must-pin-full-suite-runtimes
Scope: running this repo's tests/scripts via `uv` instead of a host-local miniforge interpreter; the committed `requirements.txt` + a repo-root `.python-version`; any future third-party import added under tools/ or workloads/; the protobuf-bindings runtime under workloads/simbench_bw/_pb_generated/.
Problem Description: The committed `requirements.txt` pinned ONLY the calibration-fit deps (numpy/scipy/scikit-learn/jsonschema/pytest) because it was authored for the tier-2 calibration subset. Running the FULL host-only suite under a clean `uv` env (the operator's portability directive: use uv exclusively, never the host-local `/home/jakeshea/miniforge3/bin/python`) then failed in two ways that were invisible on the miniforge host (whose ambient env happened to carry the extra packages): (1) ~scanner/manifest producers import `tomli_w` (canonical TOML emitter) -> `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tomli_w'`; (2) the NVBit dynamic-trace contract path (`tools/nvbit_tracer/import_trace_contract.py` -> `import google.protobuf`) and the sharing_locality / order_projections / hrd_profiler observation-builder test surfaces import the generated `*_pb2` bindings, which fail without protobuf, AND fail with a too-old protobuf via `google.protobuf.runtime_version.VersionError: Detected incompatible Protobuf Gencode/Runtime versions ... gencode 7.34.1 runtime 6.33.0. Runtime version cannot be older than the linked gencode version.` Default `uv run` also floated to Python 3.13 instead of the validated 3.12.12.
Root Cause: A requirements manifest scoped to one workflow subset is NOT the dependency closure of the whole test+scanner surface. A host whose ambient interpreter carries extra packages masks the gap; a clean uv env exposes it. protobuf additionally enforces a cross-version rule (runtime >= the linked gencode), so pinning an arbitrary/older protobuf is itself a defect — the runtime must be >= the gencode baked into the committed `*_pb2` bindings.
Solution: Pin EVERY third-party runtime the whole non-vendored suite imports, not just one subset: add `tomli_w==1.2.0` and `protobuf==7.34.1` (== the bindings' gencode 7.34.1; check the gencode with a one-line import or by reading a generated `*_pb2.py`). Add a repo-root `.python-version` (3.12) so `uv run` auto-selects the validated 3.12.12 on any machine. Canonical invocation is `uv run --no-project --with-requirements requirements.txt python -m pytest ...` (a bare `uvx pytest` / uv-tool run does NOT read requirements.txt and still fails). The generated bindings themselves stay a host build step (`make -C workloads/simbench_bw nvbit-pb-python`), like the compiled collectors; only the Python runtime they import is pinned. `openpyxl` is NOT required (its sole importer, tools/local_labels/read_ncu_report.py, is not exercised by collection — verify before pinning anything "to be safe"). To classify a gap: `uv run ... pytest --collect-only` surfaces ModuleNotFound at collection; run one representative failing test to read the exact import/VersionError.
Constraints: Re-confirm the protobuf pin whenever the committed bindings are regenerated (the pin must track the new gencode). Do NOT downgrade to make an older runtime "work" — raise the runtime to >= gencode. The full single-threaded suite takes ~17 min; use `--with pytest-xdist python -m pytest -n auto` for an authoritative number in ~2 min. Keep numbers honest: never write pass/fail counts into a commit/summary before the run completes (a prior round committed "5523 passed, 0 failed" before the run finished; the truth was 5335 passed / 5 failed, all pre-existing).
Validation Evidence: Round 0 of the sm86 non-root wall-clock prediction loop (2026-05-31, commits d4ab6969 chore(deps) + eb9e837d feat(timing_contract) + 52d53fab re-emit). Before: clean uv env failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: tomli_w` and protobuf `VersionError` (gencode 7.34.1 vs runtime 6.33.0). After (`uv run --no-project --with-requirements requirements.txt`, Python 3.12.12 + protobuf 7.34.1 + tomli_w 1.2.0): `--collect-only` collects 5378 items with 0 import errors; full host-only suite `5335 passed, 5 failed, 38 skipped` (the 5 are pre-existing and reproduce at base eed235d9). Same 5335/5/38 reproduced under the prior miniforge interpreter, proving the uv switch changed no outcomes.
Source Rounds: round-0 of the sm86 non-root wall-clock prediction loop (2026-05-31).

## Lesson: static-sass-signature-is-launch-config-input-not-sass-derived
Lesson ID: BL-20260531-static-sass-signature-is-launch-config-input-not-sass-derived
Scope: any static-SASS / cubin feature extractor that emits a record carrying `kernel_signature_sha256` (e.g. workloads/simbench_bw/static_sass_features.py); any producer joining static features to CUPTI/NVBit timing or cache evidence by the canonical kernel signature; per-mnemonic FLOP/byte work estimators built from a cuobjdump histogram.
Problem Description: A static-SASS extractor cannot derive the canonical `kernel_signature_sha256` from the cubin alone: per workloads/simbench_bw/schemas/kernel_signature.spec.md the signature hashes a 10-field LAUNCH record (kernel_name + grid/block dims + shared_mem_static/dynamic + regs_per_thread), and grid/block/shared-mem are launch-time facts absent from a disassembly. A first cut (a) imported a non-existent helper (kernel_signature.compute_static_signature) -- pure fabrication; and (b) after that was replaced, silently fell back to the largest disassembled function while still hashing the original caller-supplied launch config, emitting an INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT record whose kernel_name/regs (gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80_kernel / 36) did not match the kernel its kernel_signature_sha256 was computed for (gemm_hmma_bf16 / 32). Separately, a FLOP/byte sanity-check estimate computed by multiplying CLOSED-histogram family counts charged matrix-movement feeders (LDSM/STSM/MOVM, in the tensor_core family) the full MMA FLOP weight and charged a load-ordering barrier (LDGDEPBAR, mapped to load_store) memory bytes -- both wrong.
Root Cause: (1) The canonical signature is a join key shared with the CUPTI-side C++ collector and the NVBit-side adapter; its inputs are launch metadata, not binary identity (binary_sha256 is the separate binary-identity field). Deriving or guessing those inputs from SASS is fabrication. (2) A silent dominant-function fallback decouples the record's identity fields from the signature's hashed inputs. (3) The closed mnemonic histogram is a coarse FAMILY bucketing; a family count is not a per-op-class count, so multiplying it by one weight miscounts ops that share a family but differ in cost (feeders vs MMA; barriers vs traffic).
Solution: (1) Require the caller to supply the full 10-field launch config; compute the signature through the ONE production implementation nvbit_trace_summary_adapter.compute_kernel_signature_sha256 (reuse, never re-derive or hand-roll -- it is byte-identical to the CUPTI C++ encoder and conformance-fixture-anchored). Import its real exported names (KERNEL_SIGNATURE_FIELD_ORDER, compute_kernel_signature_sha256); verify exports with grep before importing -- do not assume a name like CANONICAL_FIELD_ORDER. (2) Make kernel selection EXPLICIT (match the launch config's kernel_name / --kernel against mangled-or-demangled symbols); NO dominant-function fallback. Cross-check the selected function's demangled name AND static cuobjdump -res-usage REG count against the launch config's kernel_name / regs_per_thread (the two identity fields hashed into the signature) and FAIL CLOSED on mismatch, so the emitted record and its signature always describe the same kernel. (3) Compute the FLOP/byte estimate from EXPLICIT per-mnemonic sets (genuine MMA ops only; genuine memory ops only), never histogram family counts; exclude feeders (LDSM/STSM/MOVM) and barriers (LDGDEPBAR) from FLOPs/traffic; carry a required estimate_convention string + a lossless unknown_mnemonics list (the histogram exemplar list caps at 8 and is NOT lossless).
Constraints: cuobjdump's per-kernel header differs between surfaces -- `--dump-sass` emits `Function : <name>` (colon-space-name) while `-res-usage` emits `Function <name>:` (name-then-colon); parse both. Always pass `-arch sm_86` so the right fatbin slice is disassembled (a portable_lcd `_run` fatbin carries sm_86 + sm_90/sm_90a + sm_120 slices; without -arch the dump may yield no sm_86 functions or the wrong arch). Run via `uv run --no-project --with-requirements requirements.txt`. The closed static_sass_features.schema.json must stay byte-identical (this is a producer, not a schema edit); the FLOP/byte estimate is sanity-check-only (claim_class fixed) -- never a stand-alone identifiability claim.
Validation Evidence: Round 1 of the sm86 non-root wall-clock prediction loop (2026-05-31, commits 9c7d2f5e feat + 8f3c2e1a re-emit). task3 Codex review returned REQUIRED_CHANGES on exactly these three points; all integrated. Live extraction on the real sm_86 slice of gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80_run (RTX 3090 Ti, driver 595.71.05, CUDA 13.2): record validates, families sum to total (192), tensor_core=5 (HMMA), regs_per_thread=36, unknown_mnemonics=[]; re-hashing the emitted record's own kernel_name+regs reproduces its kernel_signature_sha256 (internal consistency). test_static_sass_features.py 25 passed / 6 subtests; guard slice + AC-2 + simbench validation 920 passed / 781 subtests; closed feature schema + frozen L2 head byte-identical. Two earlier broken commits (incl. the inconsistent artifact) were reset and are unreachable from HEAD.
Source Rounds: round-1 of the sm86 non-root wall-clock prediction loop (2026-05-31).

## Lesson: ask-codex-codex-exec-is-autonomous-write-agent
Lesson ID: BL-20260602-ask-codex-codex-exec-is-autonomous-write-agent
Scope: any use of `/humanize:ask-codex` (which runs `tools` -> `scripts/ask-codex.sh` -> `codex exec`) for a "review" or "consult" during an active RLCR loop, especially when `HUMANIZE_CODEX_BYPASS_SANDBOX=true` makes `codex exec` run `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`.
Problem Description: A task5 `ask-codex` prompt that asked Codex to "review the NCU observation contracts and OUTPUT a structured review" did not just review. `codex exec` is an unsandboxed autonomous coding agent with full repo write + `git commit` access; given the loop context it autonomously folded the review's blocking fixes into the code, committed them (`6e36e3db fix(metric-registry): fold ncu contract blockers`), wrote round-1/round-2/round-3 loop-state files, edited `goal-tracker.md`, and advanced the RLCR loop through multiple rounds — all without the orchestrating agent's pre-verification. Two duplicate `ask-codex.sh` processes (plus orphaned `codex exec` node children) were left running and continued to mutate the repo.
Root Cause: `codex exec` is an agent, not a text-completion endpoint. The phrase "output a structured review" does not constrain it to read-only; with write+commit access and an RLCR loop visible in the working tree, it takes the initiative to implement and commit. Orphaned `codex exec` children survive a `pkill` of the parent `ask-codex.sh`, so they must be killed by their own PIDs.
Solution: (1) Treat `ask-codex` as an autonomous actor: either explicitly constrain the prompt ("READ-ONLY. Do NOT edit files, do NOT git add/commit, output ONLY the review text.") or expect and verify autonomous edits. (2) After any ask-codex consult during a loop, independently verify the resulting commit (`git log`, run the full test suite yourself) before trusting it. (3) To stop a runaway, kill the `codex exec` engine children by PID (`ps -eo pid,cmd | grep 'codex exec'`), not just `pkill -f ask-codex.sh`. (4) Keep the orchestrating agent as the integrator/verifier of record.
Constraints: This is about `codex exec` write-mode under bypass-sandbox; a read-only or approval-gated Codex config would not exhibit it. The autonomously-committed code here happened to be sound (verified 109 passing tests + strict-v2 registry), but soundness must be confirmed, never assumed.
Validation Evidence: Round 1 of the sm86_multimetric loop (2026-06-02): a task5 ask-codex "review" produced commit `6e36e3db` + round-1/2/3 loop-state files autonomously; runaway duplicate processes (2845122/2845185 + codex exec children 2845186/2845187/2845194) were detected via `ps`, killed by PID, and the resulting code independently re-verified (`uv run ... pytest`: 109 passed, 44 subtests; registry strict-v2 clean; `dram__sectors_op_*` correctly excluded).
Source Rounds: round-1 (incident); stabilized + verified + one clean follow-up fix (`03fdb562`) by the orchestrating agent.

## Lesson: ncu-launch-occupancy-on-raw-page-not-catalog
Lesson ID: BL-20260602-ncu-launch-occupancy-on-raw-page-not-catalog
Scope: any consumer that needs NCU `launch__occupancy_limit_{warps,blocks,registers,shared_mem}` ground truth out of a committed `.ncu-rep` (`tools/local_labels/validate_static_occupancy_agreement.py`; future T0 occupancy / launch-stat validators), and any reader of `data/local_labels/ncu_metric_availability_probe_sm_86.json` deciding whether NCU "exposes" these metrics.
Problem Description: Two signals jointly imply "NCU has no launch-section occupancy ground truth," which is FALSE. (1) The availability probe marks all four `launch__occupancy_limit_*` as `available:false` with `excluded_reason=launch_config_metric_not_in_counter_catalog`. (2) `parse_ncu_metrics.py` imports with `--page details` and returns ZERO `launch__occupancy_limit_*` rows from the committed captures (those captures only collected the `Command line profiler metrics` + `Memory Workload Analysis` sections, and the details page is section-organized). A consumer trusting either signal concludes the values are absent and cannot build the exact-agreement validator the plan needs — when the values are in fact present in every committed report.
Root Cause: `available:false` refers ONLY to the `--query-metrics` counter CATALOG; the four launch occupancy limits are launch-config-derived quantities NCU computes without a hardware counter, so they are correctly absent from the counter catalog yet still emitted per launch. The details page omits them only because the relevant section was not collected. The RAW page carries every collected raw metric — including the launch occupancy limits — independent of section selection.
Solution: Read them via `ncu --import <rep> --page raw --csv` (NOT `--page details`) and select the four columns from the per-launch data row. The raw CSV layout is: a metric-name header row, then a UNITS row (`"block"`, `"register/thread"`, `"byte/block"`), then float-rendered data rows (`"16.000000"`). Skip the units row (its occupancy cells are all non-numeric), strip US thousands separators before `float()`, and distinguish an empty cell (absent) from `"0.000000"` (a real zero). The `ncu_report.py` Python API also exposes them, but via a soft `0.0`-on-absence fallback that conflates absent with zero — prefer the raw-CSV path.
Constraints: `launch__occupancy_limit_shared_mem` is NOT computable from the pure-static T0 inputs alone: NCU's value uses the launch's CONFIGURED shared-memory carveout (`launch__shared_mem_config_size`, a runtime/driver decision) plus the driver-reserved per-block shared memory (1024 B on sm_86), e.g. `8192 / 1024 = 8`. CORRECTION (round-2): an earlier round excluded shared_mem from the exact-agreement gate as a "known divergence" and emitted a fabricated max-carveout value (16), which let the validator report a false `overall_agree=True` despite the 16-vs-8 mismatch. The correct contract is exact-or-typed-unavailable + gate ALL FOUR limits: the calculator emits shared_mem `typed_unavailable` (reason `requires_runtime_shared_mem_carveout`) when no carveout is supplied, and computes it exactly (`carveout // round_up(static+dynamic+reserved, alloc_unit)`) when one is — never a non-gated carve-out. Do NOT "fix" the probe to mark these `available`: the catalog classification is correct; the fix is to read per-report values directly. Reading an existing `.ncu-rep` with `ncu --import` is read-only (no profiling/replay/clock-control, no new `.ncu-rep` written) and is allowed only from `tools/local_labels/` per the quarantine.
Validation Evidence: Round 1 of the sm86_multimetric loop (2026-06-02, RTX 3090 Ti, ncu 2026.1.0.0, CUDA 13.2). `data/local_labels/sm_86/silu_fwd_bf16_sm80/timing_shape/run_0_0.ncu-rep` raw page yields warps=6, blocks=16, registers=10, shared_mem=8 (with launch__block_dim_x=256, launch__registers_per_thread=18, launch__shared_mem_per_block_static=0). `emit_static_occupancy_prediction.py` + `validate_static_occupancy_agreement.py` produce `overall_agree=True` with the three gated limits exact; `test_validate_static_occupancy_agreement.py` (14 passed) locks the units-row skip, thousands-strip, empty-vs-zero, and fail-closed-on-corrupt-row behaviors.
Source Rounds: round-1 of the sm86_multimetric loop (2026-06-02).

## Lesson: registry-inventory-lockstep-on-producer-contract-change
Lesson ID: BL-20260602-registry-inventory-lockstep-on-producer-contract-change
Scope: any change to a producer/calculator's exact-or-typed-unavailable contract for a metric registered in `tools/local_labels/metric_registry.json` (e.g. `occupancy_calculator.py`, future T1/T2/T3 producers), where the registry and the regenerated `data/local_labels/metric_ontology_inventory.json` are the downstream source of truth for coverage, typed-unavailable budgets, and the planned `(cell, metric_key)` evidence harness.
Problem Description: A round changed `launch__occupancy_limit_shared_mem` from always-available (max-carveout) to typed_unavailable-without-a-runtime-carveout in the calculator/producer/validator, but left the `metric_registry.json` row (`per_arch_availability.sm_86="available"`, `feature_eligible`, notes citing the old `shared_mem_per_sm_bytes`/per-block formula) and the inventory regenerated from it unchanged. The behavior tests all passed (they exercise the calculator/validator), so the drift was invisible to the suite — but the NEXT Codex review flagged it as BLOCKING: the ontology is consumed downstream as truth, so a behavior⇄ontology mismatch would feed a stale availability into coverage/budgets/harness.
Root Cause: the registry/inventory is a SEPARATE source-of-truth surface from the producing code; changing producer behavior does not auto-update it, and no test cross-checked "registry availability matches the producer's actual exact-or-typed-unavailable contract."
Solution: when a producer/calculator's contract for a registered metric changes, in the SAME round: (1) update the registry row's `per_arch_availability` + notes (per_arch VALUES are free-form — not vocab-checked against AVAILABILITY_TOKENS, unlike the top-level `availability` — so a precise token like `requires_runtime_carveout` is allowed, but it MUST be mapped in `emit_metric_inventory._typed_unavailable_reason` or the inventory shows `unmapped_status:X`); (2) regenerate `metric_ontology_inventory.json` (its byte-match drift test forces this); (3) add a registry+inventory regression test that pins the new contract and fails on the old one. Leave sibling metrics that ARE exact (warps/blocks/registers) unchanged.
Constraints: this is about the ontology/source-of-truth surface, not the behavior surface (which has its own tests). A precise free-form `per_arch_availability` token is fine but needs the inventory reason mapping. Do not reclassify the metric_class or deployability if only availability changed (shared_mem stays `static`/`feature_eligible` — it is still a deployable static feature when the carveout is supplied).
Validation Evidence: round-3 of the sm86_multimetric loop (2026-06-02): `occupancy_limit_shared_mem_theoretical` per_arch_availability.sm_86 `available`->`requires_runtime_carveout`; inventory regenerated (reason `requires_runtime_shared_mem_carveout`); `test_emit_metric_inventory.py::SharedMemT0ContractTest` (3 tests) pins registry + inventory and asserts warps/blocks/registers stay available; full `tools/local_labels/` suite 1579 passed / 12 pre-existing failed / 0 new.
Source Rounds: round-2 (behavior changed, ontology left stale), round-3 (drift caught by review and fixed in lockstep).

## Lesson: register-spill-microbench-needs-cap-and-differential-enforcement
Lesson ID: BL-20260602-register-spill-microbench-needs-cap-and-differential-enforcement
Scope: the factorial microbench corpus under `workloads/simbench_bw/` (register-spill / local-memory family, and by extension every factorial family that claims to exercise a phenomenon across a sweep axis); `verify_kernel_contracts.py` SASS-mnemonic locks; the GPU-free `tools/local_labels/verify_spill_sweep.py` differential verifier.
Problem Description: `register_spill_local_mem` built UNCAPPED reported REG:96 / STACK:0 on sm_86 -- i.e. its 64-wide live FP32 set fits in registers and it NEVER SPILLS, so a register-SPILL microbench did not exercise spill at all, and its `live_regs`/`-maxrregcount` sweep was a confounded no-op (the plan's AC negative-test failure state). Separately, the kernel-contract verifier only does PRESENCE grep of one declared SASS mnemonic (count >= 1) -- it cannot prove the family's phenomenon actually varies across its sweep, so "a -maxrregcount sweep that does not change spill is rejected" was unenforceable by registration alone.
Root Cause: (1) spill is driven by register PRESSURE relative to the allocation budget; a small live set never spills regardless of the sweep, and a register array with compile-time-resolvable indices scalarizes into registers (so it only spills when a `-maxrregcount` cap forces it). (2) A presence-only contract grep checks "the opcode appears once", not "the phenomenon changes across the controlling axis" -- two different assertions.
Solution: (1) Build the spill microbench under a per-thread register cap (Makefile `SPILL_MAXREG`, e.g. 32) so the live set is forced to local memory -- LDL/STL in `--dump-sass`, STACK-frame bytes > 0 in `cuobjdump -res-usage`. Measured sm_86 ladder: cap 255 -> STACK 0 (no spill); 64 -> 112; 32 -> 424; 24 -> 472. (2) Enforce the phenomenon DIFFERENTIALLY and GPU-free: compile a `-maxrregcount` ladder, read spill via `cuobjdump -res-usage`, and CONFIRM only if the top cap does not spill, the bottom cap does, spill is monotone as the cap drops, and there is a real delta; REJECT a constant/no-spill/spills-at-top/non-monotone (confounded) sweep with a typed reason. This generalizes: every factorial family should enforce its phenomenon across its sweep axis (differential), not merely presence-grep one opcode. `cuobjdump -res-usage` needs a full executable (the `arch =` header) -- a bare `-cubin` omits it.
Constraints: this is offline/static (nvcc compile + cuobjdump; no kernel run, no counters). Live NCU labeling + repeatability admission is a SEPARATE step before the cells are admitted/evaluatable. The cap makes the binary slower but identical in result (spill is a perf, not correctness, effect). Promoting the slug into the contract lock requires the three-surface lockstep (KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC + CONTRACT_REQUIRED_SLUGS + remove from EXCLUSIONS; strict-bijection test).
Validation Evidence: round-4 of the sm86_multimetric loop (2026-06-02, sm_86 RTX 3090 Ti, nvcc 13.2, cuobjdump 13.x). `tools/local_labels/test_verify_spill_sweep.py` 9 passed incl. an nvcc-gated integration build of the real ladder (top cap 0 spill, bottom > 0); `verify_kernel_contracts.py --row register_spill_local_mem` -> `[OK] ... matches LDL/STL`; `test_verify_kernel_contracts.py` 17 passed; `verify_coverage.py --strict` ok; full `tools/local_labels/` 1587 passed / 12 pre-existing failed / 0 new.
Round-5 addendum (enforcement must be WIRED INTO THE GATE): a standalone differential verifier is NOT enforcement if the contract gate does not call it -- Round 4 committed `verify_spill_sweep.py` but `verify_kernel_contracts.py` still presence-grepped LDL/STL only, so the gate could false-green a confounded sweep (Codex flagged it blocking). Fix: call the differential check from the gate via a `DIFFERENTIAL_CONTRACT_CHECKS` registry AFTER the mnemonic grep, with an INJECTABLE measurement seam (e.g. a `spill_provider` parameter) so the gate's rejection paths are tested host-independently (no nvcc). And a tool meant to be both a library and a `python -m pkg.mod` entry needs a path-robust sibling import (insert its own dir into sys.path): `python -m pkg.mod` puts the package root, NOT the module's own dir, on sys.path, so a bare sibling import raises ModuleNotFoundError.
Round-6 addendum (build-time variant axes must be EMITTED labelable cells, not prose): when a factorial family's controlling axis is a BUILD-TIME variant (here `-maxrregcount` / Makefile `SPILL_MAXREG`), not a runtime axis, the variants must be an EXPLICIT committed build-variant manifest with distinct STABLE per-variant identity -- not prose and not only a contract gate. Route the variant value into the kernel signature so identities differ deterministically (the cap changes regs/thread, a `compute_kernel_signature_sha256` field, so per-cap signatures differ) plus a per-variant binary hash; provide a Makefile build path that takes the variant via `SPILL_MAXREG` (so the collection harness can rebuild/select the matching binary before capture); and add a prose⇒manifest test (a cap documented in the canonical ladder / Makefile / shape prose but absent from the emitted manifest fails). Keep it ADDITIVE -- a new artifact under `data/local_labels/build_variants/`, NOT a change to the shared `(arch, slug, shape_point)` cell-identity 7-tuple (that would destabilize the splits/suite and is reserved for the live-labeling integration). kernel signatures are deterministic (geometry + regs) so they regenerate identically; binary hashes are per-build snapshots, so a regeneration test must compare the deterministic fields, not the binary hash.
Round-7 addendum (the emitted manifest's source_commit must be reproducible): the Round-6 manifest was committed in the SAME commit as its emitter, so its recorded `source_commit` pointed at the PARENT (pre-emitter) commit. See [[BL-20260527-two-commit-landing-source-commit-pinning]] (the already-committed-bad single-re-emit case + the content-vs-window scanner choice): re-emit from the emitter's already-landed commit and guard with a content-and-ancestry provenance scanner (`test_register_cap_variant_provenance.py`).
Round-8 addendum (begin threading the variant into the SHARED capture identity -- ADDITIVE/OPTIONAL): the Round-6 deferral ("reserved for the live-labeling integration") starts here. Make the build-variant axis an OPTIONAL field on the in-memory cell-identity dataclass (`sweep_repeatability_matrix.py` `CellId.register_cap: int | None = None`), NOT a new required tuple element. Two sentinels keep base-only inputs byte-identical: the `None` default (absent => historical 3-field identity, derived `cell_id`/`output_dir_name`/verdict-row unchanged) and a sort-key sentinel (`-1` for absent => base cells sort before any variant of the same shape AND the relative order of a base-only candidate set is unchanged). A candidate token widens to `arch/slug/shape_point/register_cap_<cap>`; `parse` accepts 3 or 4 parts and rejects a malformed suffix with a typed error; the verdict table gains additive `register_cap`/`variant_id` columns (empty for base). KEY PROPAGATION NUANCE for the remaining join steps: keying by the FULL identity OBJECT propagates distinctness automatically to every dict-keyed consumer (`--binaries`/`--measurement-manifests` maps, the matrix, the digest) -- but any consumer that matches on a hand-listed field SUBSET (e.g. an aggregator-summary matcher comparing only device/slug/shape_point, `check_admission_floor.py` dedup, the `evidence_manifest.py` join) does NOT become variant-aware for free and MUST be updated explicitly. The variant routes through binary/manifest SELECTION (the dict key), NOT a `label_driver.sh` flag; the driver-contract extension (per-variant/per-shape measurement manifests + driver validation) and the subset-matcher join propagation are the next steps.
Round-9 addendum (thread the variant into the ADMISSION/JOIN identity -- prefer a MIXED-ARITY key when widening a SHARED dict key): `repeatability_aggregator.py` grouped cells by `(arch, slug, shape_point)`, so two cap variants collapsed into one cell and their differing binary/kernel signatures tripped a FALSE `label_provenance_duplicate`. CRITICAL TECHNIQUE: when widening a shared in-memory dict-KEY tuple that OTHER modules consume, prefer a MIXED-ARITY key -- keep the base cell at its historical arity (the 3-tuple `(arch, slug, shape_point)` verbatim) and append the variant component ONLY for the widened case (the 4-tuple) -- over a uniform sentinel-padded key (a 4-tuple with `""` for base). A first cut used the uniform form and broke 23 tests in a downstream consumer (`restore_admitted_l1_mean_from_source_config.py`) that builds a 3-tuple lookup key against the aggregator's `_load_sidecars_by_cell` grouping -- the exact field-SUBSET-consumer trap the Round-8 addendum warned about. Mixed-arity made every base-cell consumer byte-identical (ZERO blast radius -- `restore_admitted_l1_mean` + `capture_timing_labels` needed no change); only future variant-aware consumers need updating. Schema side is the additive lockstep: `local_label_admitted.schema.json` + `local_label_sidecar.schema.json` gained optional `register_cap`/`variant_id` (not required, additionalProperties stays false so they must be declared, NO schema_version bump per the wire-extension policy + the existing `source_commit_override_from` additive precedent). TWO process points: (1) the FULL `tools/local_labels/` sweep caught the 23-test regression the targeted aggregator run (26 green) did NOT -- re-confirms [[BL-20260530-new-entry-point-script-repo-invariant-checklist]]'s full-sweep discipline; (2) the per-variant MEASUREMENT-manifest emission was RESEQUENCED after this admission-identity work because its join-critical `kernel_signature_sha256` must be DERIVED from the live predictor trace (`stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py` reads it from the trace; the collection manifest's value is a canonical-geometry placeholder grid 8/block 128, and the kernel's REAL launch is grid=(warps,1,1)/block=(32,1,1) while the shape TOML `launch_config` is stale) -- an offline recompute would mint a wrong join key.
Round-12 addendum (MAP each consumer's key before threading -- not every field-subset consumer collapses; reuse the ONE normalizer, don't re-inline): the Round-9 nuance ("a consumer that matches on a hand-listed field SUBSET must be updated when widening a shared identity") needs a discriminator. BEFORE mechanically threading the optional variant through every named consumer, MAP each consumer's ACTUAL key. A consumer whose key already includes fields that DIFFER per variant already disambiguates and must NOT be threaded. Concretely: `check_admission_floor.py` keyed by the bare `(arch,slug,shape_point)` 3-tuple -> COLLAPSES cap variants -> thread it (mixed-arity). But `evidence_manifest.py`'s 7-field (`_JOIN_KEY_FIELDS_7`) + 5-field (`_MPI_KEY_FIELDS_5`) join keys already include `binary_sha256` + `kernel_signature_sha256`, which are DISTINCT per `-maxrregcount` cap (distinct binary + distinct register count -> distinct kernel signature), so they ALREADY disambiguate -- threading `variant_id` there is redundant AND risks an asymmetric-presence match-break (one side carries variant_id, the other doesn't -> the join silently fails). The discipline: map the key, thread ONLY the consumers that actually collapse, and PIN the already-correct ones with a test instead of editing them (correctness > rote). SECOND point: REUSE the single canonical normalizer (`repeatability_aggregator._variant_identity`) across consumers via a path-robust sibling import -- do NOT re-inline a second copy (re-inlining is the exact divergence failure class hardened against in the Round-10/11 addenda: two copies drift). A parallel Understand-phase workflow (5 readers, one per consumer, each returning a structured key-construction + additive-plan + blockers finding) is the efficient way to do this mapping.
Round-13 addendum (the "reuse ONE normalizer" rule collides with a DEPENDENCY constraint when a consumer runs in a minimal interpreter -> extract a dependency-free leaf module): the producer side `sidecar_emitter.py` is invoked by `label_driver.sh` via `python3 sidecar_emitter.py` under a BARE SYSTEM python3 that does NOT have jsonschema. Reusing the canonical normalizer by importing it from `repeatability_aggregator` (which imports jsonschema at module load) crashed the live capture path with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'jsonschema'` -- 21 integration-test failures (label_driver_integration / provenance_enrichment / sweep_repeatability_matrix), caught ONLY by the FULL `tools/local_labels/` sweep, not the focused sidecar unit tests (re-confirms [[BL-20260530-new-entry-point-script-repo-invariant-checklist]] full-sweep discipline). RESOLUTION: extract the shared PURE-LOGIC helper into a DEPENDENCY-FREE leaf module (`variant_identity.py`, imports only `__future__`) that BOTH the heavy consumers (repeatability_aggregator, check_admission_floor -- which also use jsonschema) AND the lightweight minimal-interpreter producer (sidecar_emitter) import; the heavy module RE-EXPORTS it under its historical private name (`_variant_identity`) so its public surface + downstream importers are unchanged + behavior-preserving. GENERAL RULE: a normalizer/helper shared between a full-environment consumer and a minimal-interpreter producer (a script the driver runs under whatever `python3` is on PATH) must live in a leaf module with ZERO heavy imports -- that is how you get single-source reuse WITHOUT coupling the producer to the consumer's dependency stack. Before adding a cross-module import to a driver-invoked script, check what interpreter the driver uses and what that import transitively pulls in. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-12 -- check_admission_floor threaded (mixed-arity, reusing _variant_identity), evidence_manifest verified-not-threaded (test pins binary+kernel-sig disambiguation), split + predictor-result-tree found live/manifest-coupled and queued; full tools/local_labels/ 1621 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (+7, zero new).
Round-10 addendum (a redundant-field identity needs ONE normalizer + a fail-closed CROSS-FIELD gate): the Round-9 admission identity carried TWO redundant optional fields -- `register_cap` (int) and `variant_id` (str `register_cap_<cap>`) -- normalized by TWO divergent helpers (`_variant_id_of` preferred the string for grouping; `_register_cap_of` preferred the int for the admitted record), and the schemas only checked each field's type/pattern. A contradictory pair (`register_cap=64`, `variant_id=register_cap_32`) therefore grouped under register_cap_32 but ADMITTED register_cap=64 -- a self-contradictory cell identity (Codex caught it). GENERAL RULE: per-field schema validation (type + pattern) does NOT catch semantic DISAGREEMENT between two fields; when an identity has a value field plus a derived string form, (a) route BOTH the producer-side grouping AND the consumer-side emission through ONE normalizer (`_variant_identity(record) -> (variant_id, register_cap, error)`: both absent => base; one present => derive the other; both present => require agreement else error; malformed => error) so the two surfaces can never prefer different fields, and (b) add a fail-closed CROSS-FIELD gate at the provenance/admission boundary (a typed `label_variant_identity_inconsistent` exclusion when any run-manifest OR sidecar disagrees) so a contradictory pair is EXCLUDED before any admitted record is built. Pin it with a negative fixture covering exactly the contradictory pair (excluded + emits no admitted record) plus positive controls that a one-field-present record derives the other consistently. Also: exclude `bool` from the int check (`isinstance(x, int) and not isinstance(x, bool)`) so `register_cap=True` is treated as absent, not cap 1.
