Round-11 addendum (validate a value-encoding STRING by CANONICAL-STRING equality, not a lossy parse-and-compare; and INPUT-gate vs OUTPUT-pin schema asymmetry): the Round-10 cross-field gate compared only the PARSED integer suffix of `variant_id` (`parsed_cap == register_cap`), so a leading-zero alias `register_cap_064` for cap 64 passed -- one physical cap admittable under two distinct strings (`register_cap_64` from cap-only records + `register_cap_064` from alias records), splitting variant-aware accounting. GENERAL RULE: when an identity field is a STRING that encodes a value (`variant_id = register_cap_<int>`), a lossy parse-and-compare (parse to int, compare ints) is NOT a sufficient validation -- two distinct strings can parse to the same value. Validate by CANONICAL-STRING equality: parse the suffix (ASCII-decimal only -- `startswith(prefix) and suffix.isascii() and suffix.isdigit()`, since a non-ASCII "digit" passes str.isdigit() but breaks int()), then require `raw == f"register_cap_{parsed}"` (the canonical decimal form, no leading zeros); reject otherwise with the typed reason. SECOND point -- schema-tightening asymmetry: tighten the OUTPUT (admitted) schema pattern to canonical-only `^register_cap_(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$` as a belt-and-suspenders invariant (the producer never emits a noncanonical string post-fix), but DELIBERATELY leave the INPUT (sidecar) pattern loose `^register_cap_[0-9]+$` so a noncanonical INPUT reaches the normalizer's fail-closed gate and yields the precise typed reason (`label_variant_identity_inconsistent`) instead of a generic schema rejection -- and so the aggregate-level negative test that asserts that typed reason actually fires (a tightened input pattern would pre-empt the gate and the cell would silently land in rejected_sidecars instead). See [[BL-20260526-byte-equivalence-not-dict-equality-for-prefix-preservation]] + [[BL-20260526-claim-guard-standalone-token-not-substring]] (exact-string discipline).

round-10 (fixed the register_cap/variant_id semantic-consistency bug via a single normalizer + a fail-closed cross-field provenance gate; 3 new tests); round-11 (closed the residual canonical-alias hole: canonical-string equality rejects leading-zero aliases like register_cap_064 + output-schema-tighten / input-schema-loose asymmetry; 2 new aggregate negatives); round-12 (threaded the variant into check_admission_floor's admission count via mixed-arity + reuse of _variant_identity; mapped all 5 downstream consumers and verified-not-threaded evidence_manifest, queued split + predictor-result-tree as live/manifest-coupled; 18 new tests); round-14 (the normalizer's DERIVE path -- register_cap-only -> f"register_cap_{cap}" -- lacked the non-negativity check the parse path already enforced, so register_cap=-1 derived the schema-invalid register_cap_-1 and the producer wrote a sidecar its own importer rejects; fix put the non-negative value-domain check in the SINGLE shared normalizer so every consumer + producer inherits it; 6 new tests); round-15 (label_driver.sh threads + validates the variant end-to-end: --expected-variant + manifest validation fail-closed before NCU/artifacts + run-manifest/sidecar propagation; 7 new NCU-shim integration tests; OFFLINE variant path now complete end-to-end. GENERAL RULE: when a normalizer DERIVES one representation from another, the derive path must enforce the SAME value domain (minimum/pattern) the parse path enforces; a schema's value constraints are an invariant the PRODUCER must check before write -- a producer must never emit an artifact its own importer would reject -- and the negative test must exercise the DERIVE path, not only the parse path); 3 driver malformed-type negatives + 1 rewritten stale argv test + 3 new argv/dry-run forwarding tests); round-17 (proved the harness-forwarding + driver-gate COMPOSITION end-to-end via a DIFFERENTIAL live-mode sweep regression `LiveVariantManifestGateTest`: a register_cap variant candidate against a base/mismatched manifest aborts every repeat at the variant gate BEFORE NCU, the matching manifest admits + propagates the pair into the sidecars; the forgotten-consumer fix; 8 new tests pinning both reviewer repros); round-20 (landed the variant-aware NVBit trace producer `trace_register_cap_variant_cells.py` + ran it LIVE on the 3090 Ti -- FIRST Verified-live-sm_86: 4 distinct-binary per-cap trace JSONs; KEY FACT: live tracer reports regs_per_thread=0 so the live signature is cap-invariant -- caps distinguished by binary_sha256 + variant_id, with the per-cap behavioral signal being admitted-insts-scale-with-spill); no floors relaxed, no stale labels spliced).
Round-15 addendum (the driver-producer half + two bash-heredoc techniques): label_driver.sh now threads the canonical variant end-to-end (a --expected-variant flag; the measurement-manifest validation heredoc normalizes register_cap/variant_id via the shared variant_identity and fails closed before NCU; the canonical pair flows into the run-manifest + the SIDECAR_* env). Three reusable points: (1) PLACEMENT of a fail-closed gate matters as much as its existence -- put the validation BEFORE the artifact emission AND before the orphan-cleanup trap so a rejected capture leaves NO run-manifest / .ncu-rep / sidecar behind; pin it with a negative test that asserts the cell dir has no artifacts. (2) A bash `python3 - <<PY` heredoc that must return MULTIPLE values emits a single `a|b|c` line (non-whitespace delimiter) parsed with `IFS='|' read -r a b c`; a NON-whitespace IFS preserves empty trailing fields (a whitespace IFS like tab collapses them), so a base record's empty variant fields round-trip. (3) The driver's bare system `python3` (no jsonschema) heredoc can reuse the shared normalizer ONLY because it lives in the dependency-free variant_identity.py leaf (Round-13 addendum) -- import it via an env-passed LD_SCRIPT_DIR sys.path insert; this is the payoff of the leaf-module extraction. With this, the OFFLINE variant-identity path is complete end-to-end (capture identity -> driver validate+write -> run-manifest + sidecar -> aggregator + admission-floor count distinctly); only the LIVE per-cap NCU capture + the live-trace per-variant measurement manifests remain.
Round-16 addendum (a TOLERATE-ABSENCE normalizer needs a STRICT pre-check at each OPERATOR-INPUT boundary; and a producer guard is not enforcement until WIRED into the launching harness): the Round-15 driver gate normalized the measurement manifest's variant via the shared `variant_identity()`, whose deliberate contract TOLERATES ABSENCE (missing field => base cell) so the many internal callers that legitimately lack the field get a base identity. But that tolerance is WRONG at the operator-input boundary: a manifest carrying `register_cap: "64"` (JSON string), `register_cap: true` (bool), or a non-string/empty `variant_id` was read as ABSENT, so the driver silently ran a BASE capture (rc 0) and wrote a base sidecar for a manifest that EXPLICITLY declared a (malformed) variant identity. GENERAL RULE: a shared normalizer with a tolerate-absence contract (missing => default) must be fronted by a STRICT TYPE pre-check at every boundary where an operator EXPLICITLY supplies the field -- present-but-wrong-type is a declared-but-INVALID value (must ERROR), not an absent one (must NOT be defaulted). Put the type check at the BOUNDARY (here the driver's manifest-validation heredoc: `register_cap` present must be a non-`bool` `int`, `variant_id` present must be a non-empty `str`, else exit 2 before NCU + the run-manifest + the orphan trap), so the shared normalizer keeps its absence-tolerance for the internal callers and is not contorted to distinguish absent-vs-malformed for everyone. SECOND point (the one that bit twice now): a producer-side guard is NOT enforcement until it is WIRED into the harness that actually LAUNCHES the work. The Round-15 `--expected-variant` flag did nothing for live safety until `sweep_repeatability_matrix._label_driver_argv()` forwarded `--expected-variant <cell.variant_id>` for variant cells (base cells get no flag). "The flag EXISTS" is not "the flag is PASSED on the launch path" -- pin the forwarding with an argv test AND a dry-run test (variant cell => flag present with the canonical value, NOT leaked into --shape-point or workload args; base cell => no flag). THIRD point (process): when you add a flag that changes a boundary's argv, GREP for the stale test that asserted the OLD boundary -- here `test_driver_argv_does_not_leak_variant_token` asserted `register_cap` was ABSENT from argv and (correctly) went red once `--expected-variant register_cap_64` started appearing; rewrite it to the new contract in the SAME change rather than leaving a misleading red. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-16 -- 2 Codex Round-15 blockers closed (driver strict-type pre-check + harness argv forwarding); 3 driver malformed-type negatives + rewrote 1 stale argv test + 3 new argv/dry-run forwarding tests; full tools/local_labels/ 1658 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); no-NCU/no-root static guard 14 passed; Offline-verified (live per-cap NCU capture queued).
Round-17 addendum (a wired guard still needs a COMPOSITION proof -- a DIFFERENTIAL end-to-end test, not just a forwarding assertion + a separate gate test): Round-16 wired the harness forwarding (`_label_driver_argv` emits `--expected-variant`) and (Round-15/16) the driver gate that rejects a mismatched manifest, but the two were proven SEPARATELY -- a dry-run argv test (the flag is PASSED) and a direct-driver test (the gate REJECTS). Neither proves the two halves COMPOSE on the actual `--live` execution path: the forwarding could target the wrong flag/cell, or a preflight (here `_run_provenance_audit_preflight`, which is NOT variant-aware) could short-circuit before the gate, or the live launcher could pass the manifest differently than the direct test. GENERAL RULE: when two independently-tested halves (a producer/harness that forwards X + a consumer/driver that gates on X) must work together, add ONE end-to-end test that drives the real launch path and is structured as a DIFFERENTIAL -- stage everything identically and vary ONLY the gated field, so opposite outcomes are attributable to the gate alone and nothing else. Here: a live sweep of a 4-part register_cap variant candidate through `label_driver.sh` + a fake-ncu shim, three cases differing only in the manifest's build-variant identity -- base manifest (no register_cap) and mismatched manifest (register_cap_32 vs expected register_cap_64) each abort EVERY repeat at the variant gate; the matching manifest (register_cap_64) proceeds, propagates register_cap/variant_id into all sidecars, and admits (the positive control proves the gate does not false-reject a correct input). Pin the "before NCU" claim on OBSERVABLE side-effects -- no `.ncu-rep`, no success sidecar, and the failed-invocation record's stderr excerpt carries the gate's own diagnostic (`does not match --expected-variant`) -- rather than trusting the return code (many gates share rc 2). COROLLARY: when the abort is mediated by an audit-trail record whose captured stderr is TRUNCATED (here `label_driver_stderr_excerpt[:240]`), first confirm the gated path emits NO leading stderr on the otherwise-well-formed input so the diagnostic survives truncation (the driver's 32 pre-gate stderr writes are all conditional on malformed input and do not fire on the success-up-to-gate path). The variant-suffixed config_subdir (`cell=..__register_cap_64`) doubles as proof the harness keyed the variant cell distinctly through the LIVE path, not just dry-run. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-17 -- `LiveVariantManifestGateTest` (3 tests + a `_stage_measurement_manifest` variant-fields extension); no production code change; focused 76 passed + 13 subtests (+3); full tools/local_labels/ 1661 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; Offline-verified (live per-cap NCU capture still queued).
Round-18 addendum (the strict-pre-check-at-the-operator-boundary pattern generalizes to EVERY tolerate-absence boundary; per-variant artifact PATHS need the same non-collision discipline as mixed-arity KEYS; and an additive schema widening lands with the producer + survives all staging branches + has cross-package blast radius): made the measurement-manifest staging tool variant-aware (the OFFLINE consumer half of per-variant manifests). FOUR reusable points. (1) The Round-16 strict-type-pre-check-before-variant_identity pattern is not driver-specific -- it belongs at EVERY operator-input boundary that feeds the tolerate-absence normalizer. Here the staging tool reads a per-variant trace JSON (operator input): a present-but-malformed register_cap (JSON string/bool) or variant_id (non-string/empty) must ERROR, not be read as absent and silently staged as a BASE cell at a variant path. (2) A per-variant ARTIFACT PATH needs the same non-collision discipline a mixed-arity KEY needs: a dedicated `variants/register_cap_<cap>/` subdir keeps base cell-root paths BYTE-IDENTICAL (zero blast radius for base consumers) while giving each cap its own trace/manifest -- two caps of the same (slug,shape) can never collide. Validate the trace at the variant path declares the SAME canonical cap as the requested one (a base/foreign/inconsistent/noncanonical trace fails closed before any manifest is written). (3) When a staged artifact gains an identity field, the additive schema widening (here `measurement_manifest.schema.json` optional register_cap [int,min 0] + canonical-pattern variant_id, NOT required, additionalProperties stays false, NO schema_version bump -- per [[BL-20260526-schema-additionalproperties-false-requires-lockstep-extension]]) must land in the SAME change as the producer that emits the field, AND the new value must be copied into the manifest AFTER the build/preserve/upgrade branch dispatch (per [[BL-20260527-staging-tool-must-preserve-rich-producer-manifest]]) so it survives all three branches -- not only the absent-build branch. (4) A shared schema's blast radius crosses package boundaries: a `tools/local_labels/` schema edit must be checked against `workloads/simbench_bw/` consumers too. Here a schema-consumer test failed; a `git stash` confirmed it fails IDENTICALLY on the clean tree (a pre-existing `validate.py --all` timing_shapes / sm_count=0 error), proving the additive change was not the cause -- the stash-on-clean-tree check is the discipline for attributing a cross-package test failure. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-18 -- staging tool variant-aware (`_canonical_variant_or_raise` + `_trace_variant_matches_or_raise` + per-variant subdir + 4-part candidate parse) + schema widening; 9 new staging tests (12 pre-existing base tests stay green = byte-compatible); full tools/local_labels/ 1670 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; the trace PRODUCER (live-coupled) + live capture queued; Offline-verified.
Round-19 addendum (an additive widening has MORE consumers than the obvious producer+schema -- ENUMERATE every surface that reads the field OR parses the key form): the Round-18 additive schema widening (optional register_cap/variant_id) + the staging producer landed, but the operator-facing PREFLIGHT validator `validate_measurement_manifest.py` was a SECOND consumer the change forgot, and Codex flagged it blocking. Two failure modes from the SAME omission: (a) the validator's `_validate_schema` checked only the legacy required fields, so a present-but-malformed register_cap="64" / noncanonical variant_id="register_cap_064" / inconsistent pair PASSED preflight (the field-reading consumer was not updated); (b) its `--binary` coverage matched cell keys with `cell.split("/")[-2:]`, so a valid 4-part variant key `<arch>/<slug>/<shape>/register_cap_<cap>` parsed to `(shape, register_cap_*)` and a correct variant manifest was REJECTED as unmatched (the key-PARSING consumer was not updated). GENERAL RULE: when widening an identity (a new optional field AND a new key-suffix form), enumerate EVERY surface that (i) READS the field or (ii) PARSES the key, and update them in the SAME change as the producer+schema -- the wire-extension policy's "consumer side lands in lockstep" is plural. The fix re-applied the EXACT contract already used by the aggregator + driver + staging tool, with no re-inlined copy: import the shared `variant_identity` leaf (sibling import); strict-type-pre-check-then-normalize at this operator-input boundary (so malformed/noncanonical/inconsistent fails exit 1); and a MIXED-ARITY match key generalized from the aggregator's dict key to this validator's coverage match -- base stays a 2-tuple `(slug,shape)` (BYTE-IDENTICAL diagnostics + existing tests untouched), a variant widens to `(slug,shape,variant_id)`, so sibling caps match distinct binaries without collapsing. A `--binary` cell key that may carry a variant suffix is disambiguated by the trailing `register_cap_` prefix (a base shape_point never starts with it) and normalized canonically so a noncanonical binary-key ALIAS is rejected at the same boundary as the manifest field. PROCESS: reproduce the reviewer's exact repros as the first two tests (rejected-valid + accepted-malformed) so the fix is pinned to the reported failure, not an adjacent one. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-19 -- `validate_measurement_manifest.py` variant-aware (`_manifest_match_key` + `_binary_cell_match_key` mixed-arity + `_validate_schema` variant gate); 8 new tests (existing base tests stay green); full tools/local_labels/ 1678 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; Offline-verified; the live-coupled trace producer + live capture remain queued.
Round-20 addendum (FIRST live cells + a tooling fact that changes the variant identity contract: a BUILD-TIME axis may not surface in the LIVE trace, so distinctness must rest on an axis that IS recorded): landed the variant-aware NVBit trace producer `trace_register_cap_variant_cells.py` (mirrors activation_trace_orchestrator.py: select pre-built per-cap binary -> CUDA_INJECTION64_PATH=tracer_tool.so -> parse stats.csv -> write trace_<shape>.json, but with per-cap binary selection + a non-colliding variants/register_cap_<cap>/ subdir + the canonical variant identity) and ran it LIVE on the 3090 Ti for the cap ladder. CRITICAL EMPIRICAL FACT (probe BEFORE designing the identity contract): the NVBit tracer reports `regs_per_thread=0` for these kernels (`cuFuncGetAttribute(BINARY_VERSION)` returns 0 -> device-cc fallback), so the LIVE-derived `kernel_signature_sha256` (kernel_name + real grid/block + shared + regs) is IDENTICAL across register caps -- the `-maxrregcount` cap is NOT observable in the live trace metadata. GENERAL RULE: when a build-time variant axis does not surface in the live trace's recorded fields, the variant's distinctness MUST rest on an axis that IS recorded/derivable -- here `binary_sha256` (distinct per-cap binaries) + the explicit `register_cap`/`variant_id` fields -- and the join key must include that axis (the 7-field evidence-manifest key includes binary_sha256, so caps stay distinct cells despite identical signatures). Do NOT rely on the live signature to separate caps; and do NOT fabricate a per-cap signature from build-time `cuobjdump` regs just to make it look distinct (the build-variant manifest's canonical-geometry per-cap signature is a SEPARATE stable-identity artifact, not the live join key). The genuine per-cap signal that DID appear live is the admitted instruction count scaling with spill (cap 255->1.10M, 64->1.27M, 32->2.05M, 24->2.19M): lower cap => more spill => more instructions -- that is the behavioral evidence to lean on. A 2-line `.pb` parse surfaced the regs=0 fact and prevented a wrong "signature distinguishes caps" design. The producer reuses variant_identity + emit_nvbit_trace_summary + LADDER_CAPS (no re-inline), is declared in arch_capabilities.json (a new `def main(` must be), and is NVBit-only (never NCU). Live trace artifacts land under the gitignored results/ tree (host-local, reproducible). Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-20 -- new producer + 11 offline unit tests; LIVE cap ladder {255,64,32,24} at trace_l2_accuracy_xl -> 4 distinct-binary trace JSONs; producer->staging(R18)->validator(R19) composes on real artifacts; full tools/local_labels/ 1689 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; Verified-live-sm_86.
Round-21 addendum (a LIVE-artifact producer must guard its OWN source_commit reproducibility AT WRITE TIME + be FAILURE-ATOMIC over consumable sidecars): Codex flagged TWO blocking defects in the Round-20 producer before it could seed live NCU admission. (1) PROVENANCE AT WRITE TIME: the producer recorded `source_commit = git HEAD` but the Round-20 live run happened BEFORE the producer was committed, so every trace recorded `source_commit=31c9c484` -- a commit that PREDATES and cannot reproduce the emitting producer (the Round-6/7 [[BL-20260527-two-commit-landing-source-commit-pinning]] class, now at the PRODUCER instead of a committed artifact). GENERAL RULE: a producer must record `source_commit=HEAD` only AFTER verifying (a) HEAD CONTAINS the producer file (`git cat-file -e HEAD:<producer>`) and (b) the provenance-relevant source files (the producer + every module whose code determines the emitted identity fields -- here the signature adapter + the variant normalizer) are committed/clean; else fail closed BEFORE launch (no GPU wasted, prior artifacts untouched). The operational fix order: commit the producer FIRST, THEN run live (HEAD contains it, tree clean). An `allow_dirty` escape hatch must STAMP the artifact non-admissible (`provenance_dirty_override`), never silently emit clean-looking provenance. This is the WRITE-TIME complement to the content-and-ancestry SCANNER (`test_register_cap_variant_provenance.py`): the scanner catches a bad committed artifact after the fact; the guard prevents emitting one. (2) FAILURE-ATOMIC consumable sidecar: the producer deleted only the raw `trace_<shape>/` folder before launch, so a post-launch failure (no stats.csv / protobuf-summary error) left the PRIOR `trace_<shape>.json` in place, and the staging tool reads exactly that file -> it would stage stale join keys for a trace that actually FAILED. GENERAL RULE (extends [[BL-20260528-ncu-export-overwrite-pre-clean]]): a producer that overwrites a CONSUMABLE sidecar must remove the prior consumable artifact (the sidecar + its summary, not just the raw output dir) BEFORE the risky step, and publish the new one via temp-file + `os.replace` ONLY after ALL checks pass -- so no consumable artifact survives a failed rerun and no half-written file is ever visible. Route the risky launch through a thin wrapper (`_run_workload`) so the post-launch failure path is unit-testable without a GPU (seed an old sidecar, force a no-`.pb` launch, assert the stale sidecar is gone). Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-21 -- producer provenance guard + atomic/no-stale sidecar + `_run_workload`; 6 new offline tests (5 provenance + 1 stale-safety); committed the producer THEN re-ran the live ladder from HEAD `45ed15a6` (traces now record a source_commit that contains the producer; staging+validator re-compose, exit 0); full tools/local_labels/ 1695 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; Verified-live-sm_86.
Round-22 addendum (FIRST live repeatability-ADMITTED per-cap variant cells -- the per-cap LIVE MEASUREMENT is the differential signal the cap-invariant signature could not carry; and DO NOT config-hunt a borderline cell past the repeatability gate): ran the per-cap NCU capture of the register-cap ladder {255,64,32,24} at trace_l2_accuracy_xl on the 3090 Ti via the tools/local_labels sweep harness (NCU only via the driver into data/local_labels/; non-root counters work because RmProfilingAdminOnly=0) and repeatability-admitted through the existing aggregator -- the AC-4 positive test for this family. The per-cap LIVE L2 sector hit-rate is the real per-cap signal: it tracks spill MONOTONICALLY (cap 24/32/64/255 -> 99.47/99.41/96.87/88.93%), which is exactly what the cap-invariant live kernel_signature (Round-20, regs=0) could NOT distinguish -- so the variant's payoff shows up in the MEASURED LABEL, not the identity hash. The repeatability stddev gate (<=0.03 over n>=5) admitted the 3 stable spill caps (24/32/64) and EXCLUDED the no-spill cap 255 (stddev_l2 0.0448) whose L2 hit-rate is intrinsically noisier (input-load-dominated, no localized spill traffic). GENERAL RULE: when a cell at the phenomenon's EXTREME (here zero spill) fails the repeatability gate, report it honestly -- do NOT config-hunt to force admission. cap 255 admits on a cache=none re-run (0.0140) and under cache=all (0.0205, but mean 58% vs 87% -- flush-between-replays is a DIFFERENT measurement semantics, not the same cell relabeled), confirming it is borderline-noisy rather than broken; mixing cache semantics across sibling caps to make them all "admit" would be gaming the gate. The gate EXISTS to exclude unstable cells. SECOND point: a live-capture round can be NO-PRODUCTION-CODE (the capture->admission path was wired in Rounds 15-19); its deliverable is the COMMITTED admitted-cell evidence (the per-config _aggregator_out/ verdicts), and committing it mid-loop is safe ONLY after a full-suite run with the new sweep output STAGED confirms zero new scanner failures (the regeneration/ledger/drift scanners could otherwise trip on newly-committed cells) -- verified 12-failed-unchanged before committing. Each admitted record carries evidence_kind=live + canonical register_cap/variant_id + distinct per-cap binary_sha256 + a source_commit that contains the producer + no provenance_dirty_override, so the 4 caps are 4 distinct cells. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-22 -- live per-cap NCU admission (3/4 robust + cap-255 borderline); no production code; committed _aggregator_out verdicts (d76fb05d); full tools/local_labels/ 1695 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new, sweep output staged); static guard 14; Verified-live-sm_86.
Round-23 addendum (extend the mixed-arity recipe to the SELECTOR + MERGE; and a SHARED-CANONICAL-CORPUS materialization cascades into every consumer -- stage + full-suite BEFORE committing it, and revert + sequence-after-lockstep when it cascades): made `select_best_config_labels.py` + `merge_admitted_cells.py` variant-aware via the shared `variant_identity` (the same recipe as the aggregator R9 / check_admission_floor R12 / staging R18 / validator R19): reconstruct identity with the optional variant suffix, bind/group/dedup on a MIXED-ARITY key (base byte-identical, variant widened), and fail closed on a cross-field-inconsistent register_cap/variant_id pair the per-field schema cannot catch. KEY LESSON: materializing a new variant cell into a SHARED CANONICAL corpus (`data/local_labels/label_admitted.json`) has a blast radius FAR beyond the producing tools -- it cascades into EVERY consumer that reads/validates/audits the corpus. Here the variant-aware selector+merge correctly produced a 4-variant-cell corpus (schema-valid; check_admission_floor sm_86=24/5), but the full sweep went 12 -> 19: seven cohort/audit/validator scanners (ac3 validator, capture_timing_labels, relabel_audit cohort union, cohort_regen ledger) are NOT variant-aware. GENERAL RULE: before committing a materialization into a shared corpus, STAGE it and run the FULL suite (12->19 surfaced the cascade); the lockstep discipline means the materialization is SEQUENCED AFTER its consumers are variant-aware, not bundled with the producer/selector fix. When a reviewer's directed step (materialize now) turns out to cascade, the honest move is to land the unblocking fix (selector/merge variant-aware + tests + the selected-artifact audit trail), REVERT the premature corpus change to preserve zero-new-failures, and document the gating consumers as the next-round lockstep scope -- not to commit 7 new failures. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-23 -- selector+merge mixed-arity + 6 tests; selected_*.json audit artifacts committed; canonical materialization deferred (reverted); full tools/local_labels/ 1701 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new, corpus reverted); static guard 14; Offline-verified.
Round-24 addendum (a LABEL-ONLY variant cell can't be materialized into a corpus whose downstream LEDGER demands FEATURE ARTIFACTS it doesn't have -- the gate is the artifact CONTRACT, not just identity keys): consolidated the mixed-arity recipe into ONE shared helper `tools/local_labels/corpus_cell_identity.py` (`cell_variant_id` + `corpus_cell_key(with_arch=...)`, reusing variant_identity) and fixed the two remaining variant-blindness bugs Codex flagged -- `merge_admitted_cells.py` now rejects a duplicate WITHIN --new-from-sweep (Counter(new_keys), not only existing-vs-new), and `capture_timing_labels.py`'s timing key + typed exclusion records carry the variant identity. KEY NEW LESSON: when Codex (rightly) insists the materialization land in lockstep, "thread the variant through the corpus" is NOT a pure key-widening exercise. A register-cap variant cell is LABEL-ONLY (NCU L1/L2 hit-rates from the Round-20 emit-only trace producer); but the regen-ledger coverage test asserts ledger.n_cells == len(admitted), so materializing forces 4 new ledger entries, and an `admitted`-status entry's schema REQUIRES the full predictor-feature artifact set (predictor_bundle + hrd_profile/divergence_proxies/sharing_locality/order_projections/sector_stream digests + bundle_admission_path + validator_status) that the label-only cell does NOT have. So the materialization is gated on a MISSING-ARTIFACT dependency: either run the predictor-feature pipeline on the variant traces (Codex's own step-5 "thread through predictor result trees") or add a new honest "label-only" ledger status + teach the consumers it. GENERAL RULE: discover the downstream ARTIFACT contract (read the consuming ledger/manifest SCHEMA + its coverage test) before committing a corpus materialization; a producer that emits a label-only cell cannot satisfy a consumer that demands a full feature bundle. Do NOT fabricate artifact digests or mislabel the status to force it; document the missing-artifact dependency + sequence the feature pipeline first. This differs from a scheduling deferral: the consumer fixes (merge #1, timing #3) + the shared helper ARE the lockstep work and landed; the materialization is blocked by a concrete absent artifact. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-24 -- shared corpus_cell_identity helper (11 tests) + merge dup-within fix + timing variant-aware (byte-identical for the base corpus); full tools/local_labels/ 1712 passed / 12 pre-existing-failed (zero new); static guard 14; materialization gated on the predictor-feature pipeline; Offline-verified.

Round-25 addendum (the materialization is DONE -- and materializing a variant into the canonical predictor-input corpus CASCADES into the ENTIRE predictor/eval/accounting layer, not just the cohort/audit consumers a reviewer enumerates; MEASURE the blast radius before scoping): resolved Round-24's missing-artifact blocker by RUNNING the feature pipeline on all 4 variant trace dirs -- made `regenerate_sidecars_for_cell`+`_resolve_trace_dir` variant-aware (resolve the `variants/register_cap_<cap>/` subtree + the variant label MPI by (arch,slug,shape,variant_id); the distinct per-cap binary_sha256 flows into the CLOSED 5-tuple sidecar MPI so siblings get distinct identities WITHOUT touching the sidecar schema), produced 24 sidecars + 4 `admitted fresh_regen` bundles (variant-aware `build_predictor_bundle_for_cell` with a collision-free `__<variant_id>` filename), materialized label_admitted.json (+4, 50 base byte-identical via the merge splice), spliced the regen-ledger to 24 cells (kept the 20 HEAD base entries verbatim to avoid regressing the stale base-sidecar-drift cohort; 4 honest admitted variant entries), and threaded every consumer Codex listed via the shared corpus_cell_identity helper. KEY NEW LESSON: materializing a register-cap variant makes its slug a NEWLY-admitted (slug,shape) identity (its base cell was repeatability-excluded), and that cascades FAR beyond the enumerated cohort/audit consumers into the WHOLE predictor/eval/accounting layer -- the v5 head⇄ledger exact-cohort invariant, closure accuracy slices, cohort-accounting, the 1:1 predicted-L1 coverage guard, the task12 substitution-preflight geomean -- because every one of those is fit/evaluated/accounted over label_admitted's admitted set and was built for the base cohort. MEASURE it: a HEAD-vs-changes failure diff (stash the whole working tree, run the suite at HEAD, pop, run with changes, comm the failure lists) showed 33 vs 22 -> 11 new; do NOT guess the blast radius. The honest handling -- without prematurely refitting the predictor (a gated model op) or reverting -- is ONE consistent transitional boundary: the register-cap variants are a SEPARATE FACTORIAL CORPUS not yet folded into the base predictor/headline cohort (folding = the downstream predictor refit), applied via cell_variant_id at each consumer's admitted-cell enumeration, so the materialization lands with zero net new failures while the refit folds them in later. GENERAL RULE: a corpus materialization that touches the predictor-input file is inseparable from the predictor refit; if you cannot refit (gated/out-of-scope), encode the transitional exclusion uniformly + document it, never fabricate or mislabel. CAUTION: a stash-based full-suite diff temporarily reverts the working tree (label_admitted reads base) -- do NOT panic, the changes are in stash@{0} and the script's `git stash pop` restores them. ALSO: a scoping comment that names AC-N or taskN trips the live plan-token scanner -- re-prose in domain language. ALSO (AC-5): the "union the imported 49-cell cohort at floors 15/8/8" instruction predated the relabel that cut the admitted cohort to 20; the 49-cell cohort ∩ the current label_excluded set = 16 relabel-evicted (repeatability-failed) cells, so reaching 15/8/8 (>=31 cells) over the 24-cell post-relabel corpus would re-admit repeatability-failed cells with stale labels -- a corpus-integrity regression; surfaced it as a decision point rather than regenerating the split. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-25 (commit 0a2e6ba4) -- feature pipeline on 4 variant traces (Verified-live-sm_86 artifact production) + materialized corpus/ledger + transitional predictor-cohort boundary; full tools/+simbench 22 failed = HEAD baseline (zero net new); static guard 14; plan-token 24.

Round-26 addendum (an AC-5 split floor written against a PRE-RELABEL admitted-cohort assumption is COUPLED to corpus size: you cannot meet it by re-capturing the relabel-evicted cells -- they fail the SAME repeatability gate again -- so meeting the floor requires NEW admitted cells, coupling the split AC to the corpus-expansion AC; RECONCILE + MEASURE the re-capturable pool before promising a regeneration). The AC-5 split was specified over "the imported 49-cell task6 cohort + factorial cells" at floors cal>=15/val>=8/holdout>=8 (>=31 cells). That premise predated the relabel that cut the admitted cohort to 20. Landed the AC-5 INFRASTRUCTURE: widened cohort_manifest.schema.json + cohort_split.schema.json with optional register_cap/variant_id on cell records AND nested identity_tuple (additive; additionalProperties:false kept; current artifacts still validate); threaded validate_cohort_manifest.py mixed-arity (CohortManifestCell.variant_id via the shared normalizer; uniqueness widened); wrote a read-only provenance-safe reconcile_task6_against_current_corpus.py + committed artifact. EMPIRICAL FINDING: of the 49 task6 cells, 19 are current_admitted, 16 are current_excluded_repeatability_failed, 14 are missing_from_root; the re-capturable CACHE pool is 18 (12 already failed repeatability under ALL Round-9 configs -> reproduce the same failure; only 6 never-captured). Current corpus = 24 admitted (20 base + 4 variants). So even optimistic task6 re-capture reaches ~30-32 -- at/below the >=31 floor with no margin. GENERAL RULE: when a downstream split/floor AC was written assuming a larger admitted cohort than a later relabel left, the floor is not satisfiable by re-running the evicted cells (intrinsic repeatability failure recurs); it requires growing the corpus with NEW admitted cells (new microbench families + live labeling) -- so reconcile (committed artifact) + measure the re-capturable yield (read the typed-exclusion reasons) BEFORE promising the regeneration, and surface the floor<->corpus-size coupling instead of relaxing the floor or splicing stale labels. The variant-capable schemas + mixed-arity validators + the reconciliation artifact are the ready infrastructure so the regeneration is a single pass once the union reaches the floor. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-26 (commit f6b5e5d3) -- cohort schema lockstep + validate_cohort_manifest mixed-arity + task6 reconciliation tool/artifact/tests; recovery_v2 at its 2 pre-existing baseline failures (zero new); static guard 14; plan-token 24; Offline-verified.

Round-26 CORRECTION (the a-priori prediction above was REFUTED by the live sweep -- DO NOT trust it): the claim that the relabel-evicted cells "fail the SAME repeatability gate again" and that "the floor is not satisfiable by re-running the evicted cells (requires NEW microbench families)" is WRONG. I then ACTUALLY RAN the live re-capture (commit 501723b0): after the sanctioned refresh_predictor_trace_source_commit maintenance (ancestor fc455a84 -> HEAD; re-verifies binary_sha256, NOT a dodge) brought the stale-provenanced predictor traces current, sweep_repeatability_matrix --live (cache=none x warmup{0,1,5} x5 repeats) ADMITTED 11 of the 14 re-capturable simbench cells under their per-cell-best config (stddev_l2 <= 0.0089 <= 0.03), fresh source_commit=HEAD. Only 3 genuinely-noisy memory-thrash cells stayed excluded. CORRECTED LESSON: the Round-9 "no_admitting_config_found" verdict means no SINGLE config admitted ALL 49 cells -- it does NOT mean a per-cell-best re-capture fails. RE-RUN the per-cell-best sweep (the harness + select_best_config_labels pick the lowest-stddev config per cell) BEFORE concluding a relabel-evicted cell is intrinsically unadmittable or that new families are needed; a "repeatability_failed" eviction can be a single-config artifact. The fresh admits are provenance-safe (refresh source_commit to HEAD + re-verify binary_sha256 + import ONLY freshly gate-passing records; never splice the stale label). 24 current + 11 fresh = 35 admitted across ~28 slugs -> AC-5's 15/8/8 + >=8-holdout-slug floor is REACHABLE from the current corpus + fresh live evidence WITHOUT new families, floor relaxation, or stale-label splicing. Remaining: the mechanical regeneration (cohort_manifest update to the 35-cell union + a cohort_split generator + floor restore + sealed holdout). Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-26 commit 501723b0 -- live NCU re-capture, 11/14 admitted, Verified-live-sm_86 for the re-capture.

Round-26 regeneration addendum (commit 5665ffc5 -- completed the split regeneration over the 35-cell union). FOUR general lessons: (1) DECOUPLE a split/eval cohort from the predictor-feature corpus. A split AC partitions LABELED cells; it does NOT need predictor features (sidecars/bundles). Merging 11 freshly-admitted label-only cells into the canonical label_admitted.json to grow the "admitted set" CASCADED into the entire predictor-feature layer (regen-ledger coverage, cohort-accounting, relabel-audit, ac3-validator, timing-exclusion, task12-preflight, predicted-L1 report) -- MEASURED at 18 net-new failures, reverted. The fix: keep label_admitted.json canonical, write the fresh labels to a SIBLING labels-dir artifact (data/local_labels/task6_recapture_admitted_labels.json, schema-valid, matches the expected_label_path pattern), and reference them via the manifest's PER-CELL expected_label_path -- then make the label-presence validator honor each cell's OWN expected_label_path (strictly stronger, byte-identical when all cells point at the canonical aggregate). The label-only union (35) and the feature-complete corpus (24) are legitimately different cohorts at this stage; folding the fresh cells into features is a SEPARATE round. (2) When RESTORING a floor that was relaxed for a smaller cohort, the new cohort's slug count can make the OLD slug-floor ARITHMETICALLY UNSATISFIABLE: 28 unique slugs cannot give calibration>=14 unique slugs once validation and holdout each consume 8 (14+8+8=30>28). Restore the PLAN's stated CELL floors (cal>=15/val>=8/holdout>=8) and DERIVE a satisfiable slug floor (cal slug floor 10), documenting that the old 14 was a pre-relabel artifact, not a plan requirement -- do NOT blindly restore the pre-relabel value or it self-blocks. (3) A guard that has a DEDICATED self-test is DELIBERATE, not drift -- do NOT relax it even when the module docstring contradicts the implementation (the trace-axis clause-3 docstring said "(when present)" but a `test_..._axes_must_be_present` self-test enforces PRESENCE). Resolve a new-data interaction with a NARROW, documented, SCOPE-LIMITED exemption (a slug allowlist applied only in the committed-cell SCAN, gated by a default-True param) that leaves the guard + its self-test fully enforced -- never widen/loosen the guard itself. (4) TIER-SEPARATE the membership criterion: the cohort split partitions T2 NCU cache/timing LABELS; the trace-axis check validates T1 NVBit trace sidecars. A cell can be T2-label-admitted (split-eligible) while its T1 trace sidecar is provenance-incomplete (e.g. a re-capture producer that emits no extra_app_args); these are different tiers and one does not gate the other. GENERAL META-LESSON: a deterministic GENERATOR that fail-closes on its own validator before writing (build_union_cohort_manifest validates the manifest; generate_cohort_split validates the split; both refuse to emit an invalid artifact) makes the regeneration reproducible AND self-checking -- the artifact can never be committed in a state its validator rejects. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-26 commit 5665ffc5 -- cohort manifest+split regeneration, floor restoration, per-cell label-presence, sealed-holdout re-seal; full tools+recovery_v2 14 failed (baseline 15, one fixed, zero net new); plan-token 24; static guard 14; Offline-verified.

Round-27 addendum (commit dfbe0db5 -- the variant top-level<->nested identity lockstep + a DEEPER normalizer type-gate bug found by adversarial verification). THREE general lessons: (1) jsonschema `"type": "integer"` (draft 2020-12) ACCEPTS an integral-valued FLOAT (32.0, 64.0) as an integer -- it checks the integral VALUE, not the Python type (bool and str ARE rejected). So a JSON float register_cap survives schema validation and reaches a downstream normalizer. If that normalizer gates field PRESENCE with `isinstance(x, int)` (as variant_identity did: `isinstance(raw, int) and not isinstance(raw, bool)`), the float is silently treated as ABSENT and the OTHER cross-checked field (variant_id) is trusted alone -- masking a real disagreement (a cell physically built at cap 24 admitted as register_cap_64). GENERAL RULE: a TOLERATE-ABSENCE normalizer must FAIL CLOSED on a present-but-wrong-TYPE value (None=>absent; any present value of the wrong type=>ERROR), never silently drop it. This is the same "present-but-malformed must ERROR, not be read as absent" lesson (Round 14/16 driver), now extended to the jsonschema-integral-float case at the SINGLE shared normalizer so every consumer inherits the strict type domain. (2) A correct STRING-LEVEL guard (canonical-variant-id equality) can be DEFEATED by a type-domain gap ONE LAYER BELOW it (the normalizer that produces the string dropped a field). Fixing the surface lockstep (compare cell_variant_id(top) vs cell_variant_id(nested)) was necessary but NOT sufficient; the chokepoint normalizer had to be hardened too. ADVERSARIAL VERIFICATION (independent agents whose ONLY goal is to construct a bypass) found the float gap that the direct fix + a broad hand-written test matrix MISSED -- run an adversarial bypass sweep after any security/integrity guard fix; ~70 vectors across two sweeps confirmed closure. (3) A lockstep/consistency check must run BEFORE the first call that would invoke the normalizer and raise an UNTYPED error (e.g. CohortManifestCell.from_dict / corpus_cell_key both call cell_variant_id) -- otherwise a real violation surfaces as a bare ValueError instead of the typed contract error a caller matches on. Order the typed check first (manifest: _check_variant_lockstep over raw cells before dataclass construction; split: a pre-pass before the disjoint check). MINOR defense-in-depth note: jsonschema's `$` matches before a trailing newline (Python-re), so a `register_cap_64\n` variant_id passes the schema pattern but the normalizer's canonical-string check rejects it (caught, not a bypass); anchoring schema patterns with `\Z` is belt-and-suspenders. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-27 commit dfbe0db5 -- variant_lockstep_error helper + CohortManifest/Split VariantLockstepError + normalizer present-but-malformed-type fail-closed + replacement_history internal-consistency check; full tools+recovery_v2 14 failed (baseline 15, zero net new); plan-token 24; static guard 14; two adversarial sweeps found no bypass; Offline-verified.

## Lesson: paired-atom-vs-red-microbench-and-microbench-family-registration
Lesson ID: BL-20260603-paired-atom-vs-red-microbench-and-family-registration
Scope: adding a factorial microbench family member to the simbench_bw L1 corpus (workloads/simbench_bw/l1/<slug>/); the ATOM-vs-RED observation-point contrast; verify_kernel_contracts.py SASS-mnemonic + differential gates; verify_coverage.py multi-surface registration.
Problem Description: The atomics family had only global_atomic_histogram (a global atomicAdd with the return IGNORED) and NO paired partner exercising the atomic-with-return SASS op; the plan's AC-4 wants "paired ATOM-vs-RED (return-value-used vs ignored)" and AC-3's lockstep must never miscount a RED as an ATOM. Adding the partner touched many lockstep surfaces, and a naive build/verify approach tripped a host-local provenance scanner.
Root Cause / Mechanism: (1) SASS op selection is driven by RETURN-VALUE LIVENESS: an atomicAdd whose return is ignored compiles to a reduction (RED/REDG, no return); whose return is genuinely live (escapes to global memory) compiles to an atomic-with-return (ATOM/ATOMG). A return assigned to a dead local is DCE'd back to RED -- the return must escape. (2) verify_kernel_contracts.count_mnemonic_occurrences matches a mnemonic only at a token boundary (`re.escape(m) + r"(?=\s|$|[^A-Za-z0-9._])"`); a bare head like "RED" does NOT match the dotted opcode "RED.E.ADD.STRONG.GPU" (the internal "." is in [A-Za-z0-9._]), so the convention is to declare the FULL dotted opcode (like the gemm "HMMA.16816.F32.BF16" contracts). (3) A new L1 slug is a MULTI-SURFACE lockstep: roster.toml (+ verify_coverage BENCHMARK_ROW_COUNT), platform_matrix.toml, splits.toml (+ the SEPARATE pinned split_counts in verify_coverage.py), shapes/<slug>.toml, trace_budgets.toml, AND verify_kernel_contracts (KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC + CONTRACT_REQUIRED_SLUGS strict bijection + remove from EXCLUSIONS). (4) Building a kernel to verify its SASS rewrites the host-local gitignored <slug>_run binary, which trips test_nested_provenance_scanner's "sidecar binary_sha256 matches on disk" check for cells in _MICROBENCH_CELLS_8.
Solution: (a) Force ATOM by accumulating the atomicAdd returns and storing the accumulator to a NON-verified scratch buffer (the histo counters, not the verified `out` identity round-trip) -- keeps the return live (-> ATOM) while the correctness reference (out=in) stays byte-identical to the RED sibling. EMPIRICALLY CONFIRM with cuobjdump (return-ignored -> REDG×N/zero ATOM; return-used -> ATOMG×N/zero RED) -- never wire a SASS contract on assumption. (b) Declare the full dotted opcode in KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC, and ADD a DIFFERENTIAL check (DIFFERENTIAL_CONTRACT_CHECKS) with an arch-robust opcode-FAMILY regex (`\bATOMG?\.` vs `\bREDG?\.`) asserting cross-family PURITY (return-used kernel ATOM-only, return-ignored kernel RED-only) so a RED can never be miscounted as an ATOM; make it skip-when-toolchain/binary-absent (clean-checkout safe) and fail-closed otherwise. (c) Update EVERY registration surface in lockstep (use verify_coverage --strict + verify_kernel_contracts as the gate). (d) After build-to-verify-SASS, `make clean` the host-local artifacts (the gate rebuilds on demand; the provenance scanner skips-when-absent), and RE-BASELINE net-new against the CURRENT pre-round HEAD (git stash the round's changes), not a stale baseline file -- failure sets drift as HEAD advances and binaries get rebuilt.
Constraints: The full dotted opcode (incl. the sm_86 .STRONG.GPU memory scope) is arch/toolchain-specific like the gemm contracts; the gate builds sm_86 only, so the family-level differential regex carries the arch-robust distinction. The offline foundation (author + register + build + static-SASS-verify + contract gate) is legitimate incremental AC-4 progress; live NCU repeatability-admission is a separate live round (the register_spill family was built this way: offline Rounds 4-8, live admission Round 22).
Validation Evidence: verify_kernel_contracts 22/22 (global_atomic_histogram REDG.E.ADD.STRONG.GPU×12, global_atomic_return_used ATOMG.E.ADD.STRONG.GPU×14); verify_coverage --strict ok; full simbench_bw/tests 4 failed = pre-existing baseline (stash-diff: zero net new); tools/local_labels + recovery_v2 15 = pre-existing baseline (zero net new); plan-token 24; static guard 14. Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-28 commit ae4664f7; Static-SASS-verified-sm_86 + Offline-verified.
Update Note (round-39, a 2nd opcode-distinct ATOM-vs-RED pair via a different atomic OP — parameterize the differential sibling): to expand global_atomic / global_reduction slug-disjoint coverage toward >=3 (Codex Round-38 directive), add a SECOND ATOM-vs-RED pair using a DIFFERENT atomic operation than atomicAdd: atomicMax. Empirically (cuobjdump on sm_86) atomicMax return-ignored -> REDG.E.MAX.S32.STRONG.GPU (x12) + RED.E.MAX.S32 (x2) [RED-only], return-used (acc escapes to a scratch buffer) -> ATOMG.E.MAX.S32.STRONG.GPU (x14) [ATOM-only] — opcode-distinct from the add-pair's .ADD on the `.MAX.S32` op. KEY GENERALIZATION: the existing `_atom_vs_red_differential_check` hardcoded `sibling = "global_atomic_histogram"`; replace that with a `_ATOM_RED_SIBLING` map {return_used->histogram, max_return_used->max_histogram} and register the check for each ATOM member — the arch-robust family regexes `\bATOMG?\.` / `\bREDG?\.` already match ANY atomic op (.ADD, .MAX, .AND, ...), so the differential needs NO regex change, only the sibling lookup. The kernel mirrors the add-pair EXACTLY (identity round-trip on `out` for correctness + the atomic on a separate non-verified scratch `histo`; the return MUST escape for the ATOM variant). Both members are identity-round-trip slugs (deterministic_seeded_tensor reference) added to FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS + the build_reference identity branch + all the multi-surface registration (roster +BENCHMARK_ROW_COUNT, splits +counts, platform_matrix, trace_budgets, shapes, KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC + CONTRACT_REQUIRED_SLUGS strict bijection). This is the OFFLINE FOUNDATION (R32 cadence); live admission + materialization follow. The CONTRACT_SPEC opcode must be the EXACT emitted full-dotted form (REDG.E.MAX.S32.STRONG.GPU, not RED.E.MAX) — verify against the built binary, not the standalone probe. Source: round-39 (2026-06-03), commit 52b961ad; Static-SASS-verified-sm_86 + Offline-verified.
Update Note (round-40/41, the recipe scales to clear the >=3 slug-disjoint bar — one atomic OP per slug-disjoint member): the atomics family reaches the AC-4 >= 3-slug-disjoint bar for BOTH global_atomic AND global_reduction purely by VARYING the atomic op across 3 opcode-distinct pairs: atomicAdd (ATOMG.E.ADD / REDG.E.ADD), atomicMax (ATOMG.E.MAX.S32 / REDG.E.MAX.S32), atomicAnd (ATOMG.E.AND / REDG.E.AND) — each op emits a clean atomic-with-return ATOMG.E.<OP> (return-used) and a reduction REDG.E.<OP> (return-ignored) on sm_86, so each pair is a distinct slug-disjoint member exercising the same op-class. R40 live-admitted+materialized the max pair (atom/red 1->2; both admit at cache=none warm like the add reduction), R41 the and pair (atom/red 2->3, both load_bearing_eligible). Live cache preference: the reductions (histograms) are most repeatable at cache=none WARM (small bin set stays resident); the and_histogram admitted ONLY at warmup1/cache=none (the cache=all cold configs all exceeded 0.03). GENERAL: to lift an op-class to >=3 slug-disjoint coverage, the cheapest path is N copy-mirrored cells differing only in one opcode-distinct axis (atomic op, vector width, ...), each driven through offline->live->materialize; the differential's `_ATOM_RED_SIBLING` map + the recurrence guard make adding the Nth pair mechanical. Source: round-40 commits 1c628895/73295a55/0af0080c + round-41 commits 50b3de0a/b787fbbc/ba16ad48; Verified-live-sm_86; Codex Round-39/40 review mainline gap #1.

## Lesson: live-ncu-admission-pipeline-for-a-new-simbench-l1-cell
Lesson ID: BL-20260603-live-ncu-admission-pipeline-for-a-new-simbench-l1-cell
Scope: live NCU repeatability-admission of a NEW simbench_bw L1 microbench cell on a local device (RTX 3090 Ti sm_86) via tools/local_labels/; the trace -> measurement-manifest -> sweep -> aggregate -> select pipeline; the launch_config <-> predicted_bytes <-> real-launch relationship; NCU-artifact quarantine.
Problem Description: Codex flagged the offline-contract-gated global_atomic_return_used as an unjustified deferral because it lacked live NCU labels (AC-4 requires labels collected via tools/local_labels/ + aggregator repeatability admission). Driving the full live pipeline for a brand-new cell (no prior trace) required the exact tool sequence + flag shapes, and the cell's TOML launch geometry was flagged as stale vs the binary.
Root Cause / Mechanism: A new L1 cell's live admission is a fixed 5-step pipeline: (1) build the per-kernel binary; (2) `run_suite.py --mode trace --kernel <slug> --shape-point <shape>` LD_PRELOADs tracer_tool.so and writes results/<slug>/<sku>/<arch>/trace_<shape>.json + trace_<shape>/dynamic_trace.pb (a COMPACT .pb -- ~190 bytes is normal; the real record count is admitted_record_count, NOT the .pb byte size); (3) `stage_measurement_manifests_from_predictor_trace.py --candidates <slug>/<shape> --sku <sku> --arch-dir <arch>` (arch component ONLY when --sku is given) derives the measurement manifest's kernel_signature FROM THE LIVE TRACE (the real launch), not the TOML; (4) `sweep_repeatability_matrix.py --live --candidates <arch>/<slug>/<shape> --binaries/--measurement-manifests/--predictor-trace-jsons <id>=<path> --cache-controls none --warmup-counts 0,1,5 --repeats 5 --out-root data/local_labels/sweeps/<dir>` runs NCU only through label_driver.sh and emits per-config _aggregator_out verdicts; (5) `select_best_config_labels.py --sub-sweep <INNER timestamped dir with verdict-table.csv> --out-selected --out-config-manifest` picks the lowest-stddev config. The TOML `launch_config` is BOTH descriptive geometry AND the input to trace_size_estimator's predicted_bytes (warp_count = grid*block/32); a launcher that hardcodes its own grid/block (here grid(1,1,1)/block(32,1,1) striding the axis-product) makes the TOML's larger geometry a stale proxy. Correcting the TOML to the binary's real launch kept predicted_bytes validating (trace_size_estimator --validate) because the estimate folds dynamic instruction counts, not just warp count.
Solution: Drive the 5 steps in order. Correct a stale launch_config to the binary's REAL launch (the signature comes from the trace regardless, but the descriptive geometry must not contradict it, and predicted_bytes is re-validated). The aggregator verdict ("admitting_config_found", per-cell stddev_l2 <= 0.03) IS the AC-4 repeatability admission; commit the _aggregator_out verdicts + verdict tables + the selected-best-config labels/config-manifest. The raw .ncu-rep + per-launch sidecars are gitignored host-local -- `git add` the sweep dir adds only the evidence JSON/CSV; ALWAYS verify `find . -name '*.ncu-rep*' | grep -v data/local_labels/` is empty (CLAUDE.md Rule 1) + the static no-NCU/no-root guard passes. The canonical MERGE into label_admitted.json + cohort manifest/split + ledgers is a SEPARATE step (it grows the count-pinned admitted set and cascades -- the predictor-feature refit), NOT part of the aggregator admission.
Constraints: NCU only via label_driver.sh into data/local_labels/ (Rule 1 local-label exception); read-only nvidia-smi (-L) only, no clock/persistence/power/MIG mutation (Rule 2); no privileged CUPTI. The select_best_config_labels --sub-sweep must be the INNER timestamped dir that holds verdict-table.csv, not the --out-root parent. Atomics admit with low stddev here (L2~95% matches the sibling) but other families may be noisier -- report the honest typed-exclusion verdict rather than config-hunting.
Validation Evidence: global_atomic_return_used admitted at trace_l2_accuracy_xl L2=95.253% / stddev_l2=0.0152 / n=5 / evidence=live (warmup5; warmup0 also admits at 0.0279); within 0.2pp of the RED sibling 95.45%. verify_kernel_contracts 29 passed; static no-NCU/no-root guard 14; no .ncu-rep outside data/local_labels/; tools/local_labels + recovery_v2 15 = baseline (zero net new). Source: sm86 multimetric loop round-29 commit 7e38b2b3; Verified-live-sm_86 (NCU label capture) + Offline-verified.
Update Note (round-38, the cache-control axis preference is OPPOSITE for reuse-heavy reductions vs streaming copies — and a prior single-config exclusion is NOT a verdict): R38 re-ran the paired RED histogram global_atomic_histogram (return-ignored atomicAdd -> REDG), which had been excluded at stddev_l2=0.136 from a SINGLE cache=none config predating the full-matrix discipline. The full matrix (cache{all,none}×warmup{0,1,5}×5) revealed it ADMITS cleanly at cache=none WARM (warmup0 0.0047 / warmup5 0.0033, L2~95.6%) but is NOISY at cache=all COLD (0.10-0.31). This is the EXACT REVERSE of the vector-width copies (R34: 128b float4 needed cache=all cold, noisy at cache=none warm). MECHANISM: a reuse-heavy kernel (the histogram's repeated atomicAdds into a SMALL bin set) is deterministic when NCU RETAINS caches between replays (cache=none -> the bins stay resident -> stable high L2 residency); flushing (cache=all) forces cold reduction ordering that varies run-to-run. A streaming/bandwidth copy is the opposite: it wants cold (cache=all) so each replay starts from the same cold-miss state. RULE: ALWAYS run the full --cache-controls all,none matrix; a cell excluded under one config is NOT a verdict (re-test under the full matrix before trusting a stale label_repeatability_failed). The per-cell-best selector picks each cell's lowest-stddev admitting config independently. Source: round-38 (2026-06-03), commit c6d71915; Codex Round-37 review mainline gap #1 first directive; Verified-live-sm_86.
Update Note (round-40, the stale-launch_config trap recurs on EVERY new mirrored microbench — guard it, don't just fix it): R29 fixed global_atomic_return_used's stale shape launch_config (declared grid[4096/16/4]/block[128] while the launcher hardcodes grid(1,1,1)/block(32,1,1) — a single grid-strided 32-lane block). But the SIBLING global_atomic_histogram stayed stale, and R39's new atomicMax pair (mirrored from histogram) INHERITED the staleness — Codex Round-39 flagged it as a blocking side issue before live admission. ROOT: these microbenches hardcode their launch geometry in the .cu launcher; the shape TOML launch_config is descriptive (+ feeds predicted_bytes), so a copy-mirrored slug carries whatever (possibly wrong) geometry the template had. predicted_bytes is AXIS-PRODUCT-based (samples*bins*160), so it is GEOMETRY-INDEPENDENT and stays valid through the fix — the staleness is a correctness/honesty defect (the TOML lies about the launch), not a predicted_bytes failure, which is why trace_size_estimator --validate passed despite the lie. FIX once + GUARD: correct every stale shape to grid[1,1,1]/block[32,1,1] AND add a recurrence guard (test_atomics_identity_slugs_declare_real_single_block_geometry) enumerating the family's identity-round-trip slugs and asserting each shape's launch_config == the launcher's real geometry — so the next mirrored slug cannot reintroduce it. The committed admitted artifacts (label/trace/bundle) are trace-derived (kernel_signature from the real run), so the descriptive TOML fix does NOT perturb them. GENERAL: when you mirror a microbench template, AUDIT the descriptive config it copies (launch_config, predicted_bytes) against the real launcher, and prefer a SOURCE-OF-TRUTH guard over a one-off fix for a defect that recurs by copy-paste. Source: round-40 (2026-06-03), commit 1c628895 (fix+guard) / 73295a55 (live) / 0af0080c (materialize); Codex Round-39 review blocking side issue; the atomicMax pair admitted (max_return_used warmup0/cache_none 0.0022, max_histogram warmup5/cache_none 0.0145) -> atom 1->2, red 1->2.
Update Note (round-42, FOUR operational gotchas when driving the pipeline for the local-memory family + the per-cell-best-vs-aggregate verdict): R42 landed two slug-distinct local-memory cells (local_dynamic_index_array via DATA-DEPENDENT runtime array indices the compiler cannot resolve -> forced .local; local_volatile_array via a `volatile` per-thread array forcing real memory access -> both clean LDL/STL with NO -maxrregcount cap, so slug-disjoint from register_spill_local_mem; they take traffic_local_ld/st 1->3 -> load_bearing_eligible). FOUR reusable gotchas, all caught live: (1) THE TRACE STEP MUST RUN UNDER THE FULL INTERPRETER. `python3 run_suite.py --mode trace` under the BARE system python3 (no protobuf / jsonschema) SILENTLY skips the nvbit_trace_summary + feature-pipeline steps (hrd/divergence_proxies/order_projections/sharing_locality/trace_convergence all emit `*_skip_reason`), and leaves `binary_sha256` / `kernel_signature_sha256` / `kernel_name` = None in the trace JSON -> stage_measurement_manifests then fails "trace JSON ... missing ... 'binary_sha256'". Re-run with /home/jakeshea/miniforge3/bin/python (protobuf+jsonschema present) and the join keys + 6 sidecars populate. (2) THE BUNDLE BUILDER + LEDGER REGEN MUST RUN AS `python -m tools...`. As a bare script they raise `walker_raised:ModuleNotFoundError:No module named 'tools'` (the fresh_regen walker imports tools.*; the package root must be on sys.path). (3) MERGE BEFORE BUNDLE. build_predictor_bundle_for_cell resolves the cell's label 5-tuple from data/local_labels/label_admitted.json (`label_5tuple_unresolved` if absent) -> run merge_admitted_cells FIRST, THEN build the bundle. (the lesson's recipe lists "build bundle; merge" loosely; the real dependency is merge -> bundle.) (4) THE AGGREGATE SWEEP VERDICT IS CROSS-CELL, NOT PER-CELL. With >1 candidate in one sweep the root verdict.json can read `no_admitting_config_found` meaning NO SINGLE (warmup,cache) config admits ALL candidates simultaneously — even when EACH cell admits at SOME config. select_best_config_labels picks each cell's own lowest-stddev admitting config independently; read the per-cell verdict-table.csv `admitted` column, not the aggregate. Here local_dynamic_index_array admitted ONLY at warmup1/cache_all (stddev 0.0158; noisy warm at 0.037-0.305 because data-dependent indices vary cache behavior run-to-run) while local_volatile_array admitted at warmup0/cache_all (0.0022) + two cache_none configs — different configs, so the aggregate said no_admitting_config_found but both admit per-cell-best. Source: round-42 (2026-06-04), commit 9646699b (offline) / 41194b21 (live) / 625a10c6 (materialize); Codex Round-41 review mainline gap #1 first directive; Verified-live-sm_86; tools+recovery_v2 15=baseline zero net-new (comm-diff identical set), simbench 4=baseline.

## Lesson: materialize-base-factorial-cell-via-slug-transitional-boundary
Lesson ID: BL-20260603-materialize-base-factorial-cell-via-slug-transitional-boundary
Scope: canonically materializing a NEW admitted BASE-slug factorial cell (one with no register-cap variant_id) into the predictor cohort — label_admitted.json + the regen ledger + the cohort manifest/split/sealed-holdout — on a host where the downstream per-cell feature artifacts are host-local-gitignored; the predictor-HEAD refit (fold into the fitted v5 head / closure / robustness / accounting) is deferred to a later pass. Generalizes the Round-25 register-cap-VARIANT transitional boundary to a base-slug cell. Concrete instance: sm86 multimetric loop round-30 materializing global_atomic_return_used.
Problem Description: Codex directs "canonically materialize the admitted cell with sidecars/bundle/label_admitted/manifest/split/ledger/accounting". Three traps: (1) the canonical generators are NOT all idempotent on every host — a FULL regen of the regen-ledger (build_sm86_cohort_regen_ledger, which inventories on-disk sidecars) is DESTRUCTIVE here: it flips the 19 cells whose sidecars are host-local-absent on THIS machine from admitted to sidecar_failed, because the committed ledger encodes a 22-admitted state built on a host that HAD those sidecars. (2) the cohort-manifest generator (build_union_cohort_manifest) only knew about the existing base cells + the re-captured task6 cells + the register-cap variants — it had NO path to fold a brand-new base slug, so a re-run reproduces the manifest WITHOUT the new cell. (3) adding the cell to label_admitted/ledger/manifest cascades ~15 net-new failures into the predictor/eval/accounting layer (v5 head-standalone cohort-equality, closure/accuracy admitted_count, compute_predicted_l1 coverage, task12 substitution-preflight drift, timing-exclusion accounting, the additive-splice freeze test, the path-reconciliation survivor count) — the same layer Round-26 measured at ~18 net-new for the 11 fresh cells.
Root Cause / Mechanism: The committed eval artifacts (fitted v5 head, closure slices, predicted_l1, task12 verdict) describe the BASE predictor cohort, which already EXCLUDES the register-cap variants via `cell_variant_id(cell)` truthiness (the Round-25 transitional boundary). A new base factorial cell is admitted/evaluable (it has a live NCU label + trace + the 6 feature sidecars) and belongs in the slug-disjoint cohort split, but the predictor HEAD was fit WITHOUT it; folding it into the head is a model refit (a separate downstream pass), not a corpus-materialization step. Because the new cell is a BASE cell (no variant_id), the existing variant exclusion does not cover it — every base-predictor-cohort scoping site needs a SLUG-based exclusion too.
Solution: (A) MATERIALIZE the corpus, not the model. Reuse the on-disk trace + sidecars (verify their MPI is the closed 5-tuple, no `arch` key); build a fresh bundle (build_predictor_bundle_for_cell, fresh_regen, fixed --fresh-regen-run-id for a deterministic committed path); merge into label_admitted.json (merge_admitted_cells, additive). For the regen ledger, do a TARGETED splice (BL-20260529-targeted-vs-producer + BL-20260530-additive-in-place-splice): run the FULL regen to a temp, EXTRACT only the new cell's entry (its 8 artifact_sha256 + 6 sidecar_paths + bundle path — verify each digest hashes the present file), append it to the committed ledger, bump n_cells, keep the existing entries + top-level provenance byte-identical (match the committed serialization: json.dumps(indent=2, sort_keys=True)+"\n"). Never let the destructive full-regen overwrite the committed ledger on a sidecar-incomplete host. (B) EXTEND the manifest generator with an additive fold step + a per-cell family-bucket mapping (`_NEW_FACTORIAL_CELL_FAMILY`) selecting the new base factorial slugs from label_admitted; then run the deterministic generator chain (manifest -> split -> seal) and confirm it is an idempotent fixpoint (re-run -> cmp byte-identical). The split refresh is AC-mandated (the split is over the union INCLUDING newly admitted factorial cells); a single-cell slug round-robins via assign_splits and can reshuffle several other single-cell slugs across cal/val/holdout — that is the deterministic policy output, valid as long as floors (cal>=15/val>=8/holdout>=8 + >=8 holdout slugs) + slug-disjointness hold and the holdout is re-sealed (digest-bound to the new manifest). (C) DEFER the head refit via a SHARED slug-based transitional boundary: add a `PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS` frozenset + `is_pending_refit_factorial(cell)` to the shared identity helper (corpus_cell_identity.py, imported by both tools/local_labels and recovery_v2), the base-cell analog of `cell_variant_id`. GREP EVERY base-predictor-cohort scoping site and thread `and not is_pending_refit_factorial(cell)` next to the existing `and not cell_variant_id(cell)` IN LOCKSTEP: the v5 head-standalone `_ledger_admitted_identities`, the closure `_admitted_sm86_count`, the build_interim_diagnostic_report `admitted_by_key` (predicted_l1 coverage), the task12 preflight admitted_keys. This keeps the committed eval artifacts BYTE-IDENTICAL (no refit). build_cohort_accounting needs no change — it iterates the FIXED 13-cell design cohort, which already omits the new slug. (D) The remaining count-pins are honest test updates: the label-count pin (+1), the manifest accounting pins (admitted/attempted +1), the path-reconciliation survivor count (+1 if the cell's expected_trace_path resolves on disk), and the additive-splice freeze test (allow the new base slug via PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS). The relabel-audit is SCOPED to its frozen 44-cell relabel sweep — intersect the admitted set with the sweep keys so a fresh cell admitted by a DIFFERENT sweep does not perturb the 20/24/44 closure (do NOT bump it to 21/45; there is no 45-cell sweep). Add a timing-exclusion entry for the new trace-only cell (no timing-shape capture -> timing_binary_absent with a truthful detail). The cell's label source_commit pins the binary/source; the kernel signature is trace-derived/geometry-independent, so a later geometry-fix commit is not authoritative for it (document, do not "refresh").
Constraints: NCU stays quarantined (no new capture this pass; reuse the on-disk Round-N trace). Avoid plan tokens (`AC-N`, Milestone, Phase, Round N) in implementation code/comments — the plan-token scanner is scoped to tool/impl files (task6/task12 ARE established artifact/file names and allowlisted; AC-N is NOT). Removing a slug from PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS is the explicit signal that the predictor-head refit folded it in.
Validation Evidence: round-30 commit 5d882554: label_admitted 24->25 sm_86 (existing byte-identical), ledger n_cells 24->25 (targeted splice, 8 digests match present files), cohort_manifest 35->36 admitted/53->54 attempted, cohort_split cal 20/val 8/holdout 8 (idempotent fixpoint, floors+disjointness hold, holdout re-sealed), 15 net-new predictor-layer failures resolved by the slug transitional boundary + count-pins. tools+recovery_v2 15 failed = baseline (zero net new), 2272 passed; simbench 4 failed = baseline (zero net new), 2028 passed; plan-token 24; static guard 14; verify_coverage --strict ok; verify_kernel_contracts 29; no .ncu-rep outside data/local_labels/. Offline-verified over the Round-29 Verified-live-sm_86 NCU label.
Source Rounds: round-30 of the sm86_multimetric_ncu_replacement loop (2026-06-03); generalizes the Round-25 register-cap-variant transitional boundary to a base-slug factorial cell.
Update Note (round-35, the shared producer-side boundary makes SUBSEQUENT materializations cheap): R30 materialized the first base factorial cell (global_atomic_return_used) BEFORE the shared is_base_predictor_fit_cell predicate existed (R31 added it across the producers + the test/report scoping helpers, per BL-20260603-vector-width...round-31 note). R35 materialized the TWO vector-width cells (global_vectorized_128b_copy, global_scalar_32b_copy) with the boundary already in place, and the predictor/eval cascade AUTO-RESOLVED: adding the two slugs to PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS alone excluded them from the v5/L1 fitter, the closure/baseline/citation emitters, the per-admitted-cell L1 predictor, the interim-diagnostic coverage, and the task12 preflight — because all of those now route through is_base_predictor_fit_cell. So the per-consumer threading that R30 had to do by hand is now a ONE-LINE slug addition. The only remaining lockstep for an Nth base factorial cell is: (1) merge into label_admitted (additive), (2) TARGETED ledger splice (verify digests vs present files; full regen still destructive on a sidecar-incomplete host), (3) the build_union_cohort_manifest _NEW_FACTORIAL_CELL_FAMILY family-bucket entry + run the idempotent manifest/split/seal generators, (4) the count-pins (ac3 count, validate_cohort_manifest admitted/attempted + drift-observed, path-reconciliation survivors), (5) a timing-exclusion entry, (6) add the slug to PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS. The committed eval artifacts (v5 head/closure/predicted_l1/task12) stay byte-identical (no refit). R35 result: label_admitted 25->27, ledger 25->27, manifest 36->38, split cal22/val8/holdout8 (idempotent), zero net new across both suites. LESSON: invest once in a SHARED cohort predicate (R31) and every later corpus addition collapses from an N-consumer cascade to a single-slug-set edit + the count-pins. Source: round-35 (2026-06-03), commit 8223dbf8.
Update Note (round-38, materializing a cell with an EXCLUDED + RECAPTURE dual pre-state touches MORE lockstep surfaces — and the manifest builder needs a re-point step): R37's vec64 + R35's vecwidth + R30's atom were never-excluded NEW cells (cheap cascade). R38 materialized global_atomic_histogram, which was UNIQUELY in BOTH label_excluded.json (the original single-config label_repeatability_failed) AND task6_recapture_admitted_labels.json (a later recapture that passed, label-only in the manifest). Promoting it to a materialized base-factorial cell required, BEYOND the standard R35 list: (a) REMOVE the stale label_excluded entry (a cell is admitted XOR excluded — leaving it trips the ac3 partition); (b) REMOVE it from the recapture artifact (no longer label-only) — which drops the reconcile_task6 count + the per_cell_exclusions companion entry, so REGENERATE task6_current_corpus_reconciliation.json and DELETE the histogram key from per_cell_exclusions_live_sweep.json; (c) the relabel-audit: because the cell IS in the frozen 44-cell relabel sweep, it legitimately moves excluded->admitted WITHIN the cohort (admitted 20->21, excluded 24->23, union still 44) — UPDATE those pins (this is NOT the R30 "don't bump for a different-sweep cell" case; verify membership via the sweep ledger's cell_key, not slug); (d) the build_union_cohort_manifest builder carries base-manifest cells through unchanged, so a cell already present as a stale recapture-pointer is NOT re-pointed by the append-once step 2b — change step 2b to UPDATE-IN-PLACE (clear+update to the canonical label_admitted base-factorial form) so a materialized factorial cell overrides its prior recapture/exclusion pointer; manifest admitted/attempted stay 39/57 (re-pointed, not added). RULE: before materializing, grep the cell across ALL label stores (label_admitted / label_excluded / task6_recapture) + the manifest + the relabel-sweep ledger; a cell in multiple stores needs each reconciled in lockstep, and the per-cell-exclusion companion + reconcile artifacts are regenerated, not hand-pinned. Source: round-38 (2026-06-03), commit a471b5fc; Codex Round-37 review mainline gap #1.
Update Note (round-34, the cache-control axis is load-bearing for streaming / wide-request cells): R29 admitted the atomics ATOM cell with --cache-controls none. R34 live-admitted the two vector-width copies (global_vectorized_128b_copy, global_scalar_32b_copy) and found cache-control is NOT a free axis: the 128-bit float4 copy is repeatable ONLY with caches FLUSHED between replays (cache=all -> each replay starts cold -> deterministic cold-miss pattern -> stddev_l2=0.0042), but at cache=none (NCU RETAINS caches between replays) the residual-L2 state from the prior replay perturbs the next replay's sector hit-rate, giving stddev 0.045-0.074 (EXCLUDED). The scalar 32-bit copy admits at several configs but its noisy ones differ run-to-run (NCU sampling variance: cache=none/warmup5 was 0.012 in one sweep, 0.185 in another). RULE: run the FULL plan-specified matrix --cache-controls all,none --warmup-counts 0,1,5 from the start (the plan's AC-4 names cache-control {all,none}); the per-cell-best selector picks each cell's lowest-stddev ADMITTING config independently, so the aggregate verdict can be no_admitting_config_found (no single config admits ALL candidates) while every cell still admits at its own best config. Trying cache=all is the standard other axis, NOT config-hunting (R22 admitted the borderline register-spill cap-255 at cache=all the same way). A streaming/bandwidth copy typically wants cache=all (cold); a reuse-heavy cell may want cache=none (warm). Source: round-34 (2026-06-03), commit 912e3458; Codex Round-33 review step 1.
Update Note (round-31, the transitional boundary must cover PRODUCERS, not only tests/report-consumers): Round-30 threaded the slug boundary (PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS + the register-cap cell_variant_id exclusion) into the test/report scoping helpers but NOT the artifact PRODUCERS. Codex Round-30 review caught that the documented v5-head fitter CLI then CRASHED on a duplicate register-cap variant bundle key (the bare (arch, slug, shape_point) join key collides for sibling caps), and once variants were filtered it would have pulled the pending-refit factorial cell into the fit despite the tests excluding it. ROOT CAUSE: a transitional-boundary exclusion expressed only on the consumer/test side is cosmetic — the committed eval artifacts (fitted head, closure, predicted_l1) describe the base cohort, and a producer that does NOT share that scope either crashes or silently regenerates a DIFFERENT cohort than the tests expect (BL-20260527-producer-output-bound-to-requested-cohort + BL-20260529 fix-the-PRODUCER-not-just-one-consumer). FIX: define ONE shared POSITIVE cohort predicate (here is_base_predictor_fit_cell(cell) = sm_86 base cell, no cell_variant_id, no is_pending_refit_factorial; fail closed on a malformed pair) and thread it through EVERY producer that selects the fit cohort: the v5 fitter's ledger-bundle selector (the load-bearing crash fix) AND its label-cell join, the closure emitter's ledger bundle lookup + citation, the per-admitted-cell L1 predictor, and the L1 head fitter + L1 closure emitter. THEN have the test/report scoping helpers call the SAME helper so there is literally one definition and producer-vs-consumer cannot drift.
