ARCH GOTCHA: a helper that hard-requires arch=='sm_86' is correct for the sm_86-only ledger/closure/predicted_l1 contexts but would EMPTY an arch-parameterized fit when run with --arch sm_120; guard the arch-parameterized label filter on the sm_86 case (the ledger-bundle scope is the load-bearing exclusion in every mode — a cell with no scoped bundle is skipped by the join), and at the predicted_l1 site that documents a legacy missing-arch tolerance, exclude via the two underlying predicates rather than the arch-bundling helper to preserve that tolerance. VERIFY by re-running every documented producer CLI to TEMP outputs (committed artifacts untouched — this round makes the DEFERRAL safe, it does not perform the refit) + a regression test that re-runs the producer CLI over the current committed ledger and asserts no duplicate-key crash + the pending cells excluded + the fitted cohort equals the scoped ledger set. Round-31 validation: v5 fit admitted=18 (= committed head), predicted_l1=20 base, L1 head scalar=6, v5 closure accuracy=18; tools+recovery_v2 15 failed=baseline (zero net new), 2279 passed; simbench 4=baseline; plan-token 24; static guard 14; NO data artifacts changed. Source: round-31 (2026-06-03), commit 14a0875d; Codex Round-30 review blocking side issue.

## Lesson: vector-width-request-sector-formation-microbench-axis-selection
Lesson ID: BL-20260603-vector-width-request-sector-formation-microbench
Scope: adding a request/sector-formation AC-4 microbench family with a STATIC-SASS-verifiable offline contract (the Round-28 paired-microbench discipline applied to the request/sector-formation surface); choosing which request-formation axis is opcode-distinct vs runtime-only; preventing ptxas auto-vectorization of a "scalar" partner; the disjoint-class differential regex. Concrete instance: sm86 multimetric loop round-32 adding global_vectorized_128b_copy vs global_scalar_32b_copy.
Problem Description: AC-4 names "request/sector-formation (alignment offsets, vector widths, partial masks, uncoalesced)" as a corpus family. The Round-28 atomics model (offline foundation = author + register + build + static-SASS-verify + fail-closed contract + family-level differential) only works if the contrasted phenomenon shows up as DISTINCT SASS OPCODES. Naively picking "coalesced vs uncoalesced" or "aligned vs misaligned" for the offline contract FAILS: those differ only in the runtime SECTOR count, NOT the opcode (a coalesced and an uncoalesced global load are BOTH `LDG.E` — only NCU/the trace sees the sector difference). A naive contiguous scalar copy also silently defeats a "scalar 32-bit" contract because ptxas AUTO-VECTORIZES `out[i]=in[i]` (i contiguous per thread) into 128-bit `LDG.E.128`.
Root Cause / Mechanism: Request/sector formation has two kinds of axes. (a) OPCODE-DISTINCT axes — vector WIDTH (float4 -> LDG.E.128/STG.E.128 vs scalar -> bare LDG.E/STG.E), cache OPERATOR (.cg/.ca/.cs from __ldcg/__ldca/__ldcs), and ATOM-vs-RED (return liveness) — emit different SASS mnemonics, so a static cuobjdump contract CAN gate them. (b) RUNTIME-ONLY axes — coalescing, alignment offset, partial active mask — produce the SAME opcode and differ only in the per-request sector count at execution, so ONLY a live NCU/trace sector count can gate them (a static SASS contract cannot). Auto-vectorization: ptxas fuses a thread's contiguous consecutive scalar accesses into the widest aligned vector op available, so a "scalar" kernel must AVOID per-thread contiguity to stay 32-bit.
Solution: For the OFFLINE SASS-contract foundation, pick an opcode-distinct axis (vector-width here). (1) Vectorized member: a contiguous float4 copy `out4[i]=in4[i]` over a 16-byte-aligned buffer (cudaMalloc is 256B-aligned) -> LDG.E.128/STG.E.128. Require samples*bins % 4 == 0 at every shape (host driver fails closed otherwise) so the whole buffer round-trips through the 128-bit path with no scalar tail. (2) Scalar member: a GRID-STRIDED copy `for (i=tid; i<n; i+=lanes) out[i]=in[i]` — per-thread accesses are `lanes` apart (non-contiguous), so ptxas cannot fuse them; stays bare LDG.E/STG.E. (3) BUILD BOTH + cuobjdump BEFORE wiring the contract; wire KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC to the OBSERVED full dotted opcodes (here LDG.E.128.CONSTANT/STG.E.128 and LDG.E.CONSTANT/STG.E — the `.CONSTANT` cache hint comes from `const __restrict__`). The count_mnemonic_occurrences matcher (re.escape(m)+r"(?=\s|$|[^A-Za-z0-9._])") requires the FULL dotted opcode because the trailing `.` after a bare `LDG.E` fails the boundary — so a bare `LDG.E` spec never spuriously matches `LDG.E.128`. (4) Differential check (family-level purity, arch-robust on the opcode head): 128b class = r"\b(?:LDG|STG)\.E\.128\b"; scalar class = r"\b(?:LDG|STG)\.E(?!\.128)" — the negative lookahead makes the two classes DISJOINT (`LDG.E` does not match inside `LDG.E.128`). Assert vectorized=128b-present-and-scalar-absent, scalar=32b-present-and-128b-absent; skip-when-SASS-unavailable; inject sass_provider for host-independent accept/reject/skip tests. (5) Registration is the multi-surface lockstep (roster +BENCHMARK_ROW_COUNT, splits +counts +pin, platform_matrix, trace_budgets, shapes, KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC+CONTRACT_REQUIRED_SLUGS strict bijection). predicted_bytes = axis_product*160 at grid(1,1,1)/block(32,1,1) (estimator declared_ops defaults to 1, warp_count=1), +/-10% tolerance — mirroring an existing identity slug's axes reproduces it. (6) Defer the runtime-only request-formation axes (coalescing/alignment/partial-mask) to the LIVE NCU label, not the offline contract.
Constraints: A new identity slug, like global_atomic_return_used, need NOT be in references/mma_microbench.FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS for offline registration (verify_coverage skips non-covered slugs; build_reference only raises at a live correctness RUN) — correctness-reference wiring is a queued live follow-up. Clean the host-local built binaries after the cuobjdump verification (the gate rebuilds on demand; the provenance scanner skips-when-absent) and re-baseline net-new against the current pre-round HEAD via git stash -u. NO NCU is involved in this offline round (Rule 1): the SASS comes from cuobjdump static inspection, not a profiler.
Validation Evidence: round-32 commit 4f6f02fe. Vectorized -> LDG.E.128.CONSTANT x15 / STG.E.128 x15 (zero scalar); scalar -> LDG.E.CONSTANT x15 / STG.E x15 (zero .128). verify_kernel_contracts 35 passed (+6 vector-width synthetic accept/reject/skip); verify_coverage --strict ok; trace_size_estimator --validate ok for both; simbench 4 failed=baseline (zero net new), 2034 passed; tools+recovery_v2 15 failed=baseline; plan-token 24; static no-NCU/no-root guard 14; no .ncu-rep anywhere. Static-SASS-verified-sm_86 + Offline-verified.
Source Rounds: round-32 of the sm86_multimetric_ncu_replacement loop (2026-06-03); extends BL-20260603-paired-atom-vs-red-microbench-and-family-registration to the request/sector-formation vector-width axis.
Update Note (round-33, correctness-reference registration is a CURRENT-SCOPE AC-4 gate — NOT a queued follow-up): Round 32 left the new slugs out of references/mma_microbench.FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS, reasoning by the global_atomic_return_used precedent that an uncovered identity slug is fine for offline registration (verify_coverage skips non-covered slugs; build_reference only raises at a live correctness RUN). Codex Round-32 review showed this was WRONG: `run_suite.py --mode correctness --kernel <slug> --shape-point correctness_00 --dry-run-cpu --component-strict` checks FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS membership BEFORE building the reference and exits 2 with reference_family_slug_mismatch — a correctness-registration gate that fails OFFLINE (no GPU needed), so it is current-scope AC-4, not deferrable to the live admission. CORRECTED RULE: when a new identity-round-trip slug declares reference_family=<F>, it MUST be added to <F>.FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS WITH a build_reference branch in the SAME round — and that branch returns deterministic_seeded_tensor((samples,bins),seed,-1,1) because the host seed_tensor LCG (state=seed*2654435761+1, *1103515245+12345, /2**32, value=lo+(hi-lo)*u32) is byte-identical to deterministic_seeded_tensor, so the reference matches the printed row-major [samples][bins] output exactly (correct for both the --dry-run-cpu self-compare AND a future live correctness run). The --dry-run-cpu path SELF-COMPARES the reference against itself (run_suite handle_correctness), so it validates the reference resolves + the tolerance contract without a GPU. REGRESSION-SCOPE LESSON (BL-20260602-registry-inventory-lockstep): the pre-existing dry-run regression derived its scope from tools/microbench_inventory.py, so a roster row declaring the family but absent from the inventory side-list BYPASSED it entirely (both the R32 pair and the latent R28 atomic shipped broken). The fix is a guard whose scope is the SOURCE OF TRUTH (the roster): assert every row declaring reference_family=<F> is in <F>.FAMILY_COVERS_SLUGS. A coverage/registration regression must enumerate from the producing source-of-truth, never from a parallel inventory that can silently omit new rows. Source: round-33 (2026-06-03), commit 1a07bef4; Codex Round-32 review blocking side issue.
Update Note (round-37, a THIRD width member closes the global ld/st coverage gap + the differential generalizes to N disjoint classes): The Round-36 coverage report (BL-20260603-op-class-coverage-from-contract-signatures-not-load-only-manifests) showed contract-locked global_load/global_store coverage at 2 (the scalar + 128b copies) -> below the >=3 slug-disjoint bar. Adding ONE more opcode-distinct width member lifts BOTH metrics (requests + sectors = 4 metrics) to load_bearing_eligible. The opcode-distinct middle member is a 64-bit float2 GRID-STRIDED copy (`for (i=tid;i<n2;i+=lanes) out2[i]=in2[i]` over float2*; host driver requires samples*bins%2==0): empirically cuobjdump-verified to emit LDG.E.64.CONSTANT / STG.E.64 (the `.CONSTANT` from `const __restrict__`, like the 128b member), neither `.128` nor bare 32b -- completing the vector-width sweep {32b,64b,128b}. THREE-CLASS DIFFERENTIAL: extend `_vector_width_differential_check` from a hardcoded 2-member contrast to a data-driven `_VECTOR_WIDTH_MEMBERS` table where each member asserts its own width-class regex PRESENT and the other two ABSENT; the 32b regex's negative lookahead must exclude BOTH widths (`\b(?:LDG|STG)\.E(?!\.(?:64|128))`) and add `_VEC64_MEM_RE = \b(?:LDG|STG)\.E\.64\b` (the three classes are mutually disjoint on the literal width token: "LDG.E" never matches inside "LDG.E.64"/"LDG.E.128", and ".64" vs ".128" cannot match each other). Register the check for ALL THREE members so any member's contract re-asserts whole-family purity; rewrite the synthetic accept/reject fixtures to the 3-way provider. LIVE-ADMISSION ROBUSTNESS DELTA: unlike the 128b float4 (which needed caches FLUSHED -- only cache=all admitted in R34), the 64b float2 copy admits at ALL SIX configs (stddev_l2 <= 0.0072 across cache={all,none} x warmup={0,1,5}); the per-cell-best selector picks warmup1/cache_all (L2=69.868%, stddev_l2=0.0029). Materialization is the now-cheap R35 cascade (BL-20260603-materialize-base-factorial-cell): merge label_admitted (27->28) + targeted ledger splice (27->28, 8 digests verified) + _NEW_FACTORIAL_CELL_FAMILY +1 + idempotent manifest(38->39)/split(cal 22->23)/seal + timing-exclusion + count-pins (ac3 27->28, validate_cohort_manifest 38->39/56->57 + drift, path-reconciliation 22->23) + PENDING_REFIT_FACTORIAL_SLUGS +1; re-emit the coverage report to confirm the flip. The Round-36 coverage TEST itself is a count-pin: its global_load=2 negative-bar assertion must be re-pointed to a still-below metric (local ld/st = 1) once the 3rd copy lands. Source: round-37 (2026-06-03), commits 171473f5 (offline) / dcd5cc22 (live) / 742aafa7 (materialize); Codex Round-36 review mainline gap #1.

## Lesson: op-class slug-disjoint coverage uses contract-locked SASS signatures, not load-only cache-op manifests
Lesson ID: BL-20260603-op-class-coverage-from-contract-signatures-not-load-only-manifests
Scope: building the load-bearing-metric slug-disjoint coverage report (task6 / the >=3-slug-disjoint structural gate against leave-one-slug-out collapse) that maps each registry metric's op_classes to the admitted+evaluable sm_86 slugs exercising it; choosing a SOUND per-cell op-class provenance basis. Concrete instance: sm86 multimetric loop round-36, emit_ac4_load_bearing_coverage.py.
Problem Description: The natural-seeming op-class provenance source — data/local_labels/cache_op_classifier_manifests/sm_86/<slug>__<shape>.json (per_pc_instruction_cache_op[].raw_mnemonic) — APPEARS to give a per-PC SASS op-class profile per cell, and the round-36 contract named it as the basis. Decoding it (LDG->global_load, STG->global_store, ATOMG->global_atomic, ...) produced a report where EVERY manifest-backed cell reported ONLY global_load — so global_store/atom/red looked under-covered (barred) even for cells (gemms, norms, copies) that obviously store their output. Using it as the op-class basis is UNSOUND.
Root Cause / Mechanism: (1) the cache-op-classifier manifests are LOAD-ONLY BY DESIGN. The classifier exists to determine the L1/L2 CACHE-OP (.ca/.cg/.cs/.constant) of LOAD instructions for hit-ratio modeling; its per_pc_instruction_cache_op list contains only LOAD ops, so it can NEVER witness a store/atomic/reduction. Decoding it for per-op coverage systematically reports zero store/atom/red and biases every gated metric. (2) the manifest is keyed by (slug, shape); a glob/exact-shape mismatch silently drops committed provenance too. (3) NO committed artifact carries a COMPLETE per-cell op-class histogram: the cache-op manifest is load-biased; KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC declares only the HEADLINE opcode the microbench is designed to exercise (a gemm-traceable declares HMMA/IMMA, not its LDG/STG); the only complete witness is the NVBit dynamic_trace.pb, which is heavy and host-local-absent for many cells. So a per-op coverage report cannot be built soundly from any SINGLE partial source, and picking the wrong one produces confident-but-wrong "barred" verdicts.
Solution: SCOPE THE REPORT TO WHAT A SOUND COMMITTED BASIS CAN ACTUALLY WITNESS. (A) For OP-CLASS-GATED count metrics (global/local/texture/surface ld/st/atom/red), score against the CONTRACT-LOCKED factorial-microbench SASS signatures (workloads/simbench_bw/verify_kernel_contracts.py KERNEL_CONTRACT_SPEC) — the authoritative, regression-locked declaration of which memory op-class each DESIGNED microbench exercises (the factorial corpus is exactly what task6/AC-4 builds for per-op slug-disjoint coverage). A slug exercises the metric iff its decoded contract op-class set intersects the metric's op_classes. Imported real-workload cohort cells carry NO contract-locked op-class signature; they are NOT counted toward designed factorial op-class coverage (they vary the LOSO column but are not a contract-locked family pinned to a specific op) — list them separately, do not silently assume their ops. (B) For LAUNCH/INSTRUCTION/AGGREGATE metrics (metric_class=static occupancy_limit_*, empty-op_classes smsp__inst_executed, and the mixed-aggregate-vocabulary L1 total-requests metric) there is NO op-class gate — they are launch-geometry / any-instruction / aggregate-L1 properties of EVERY admitted cell, so coverage = the full admitted+evaluable set. Detect "gated" precisely: metric_class==replay_count AND op_classes non-empty AND every token is a SPECIFIC gateable op-class (a generic aggregate token like 'local'/'atomic'/'reduction'/'surface' in the total's op_classes => not gated). (C) Op-classes with zero admitted contract-locked coverage FAIL CLOSED to typed_unavailable_no_coverage (never silently assumed present). (D) admitted+evaluable = label_admitted.json(arch sm_86) INTERSECT regen-ledger status==admitted (bundle present); register-cap variants collapse to base slug; label-only / bundle_failed / unadmitted excluded.
Constraints: DROPPING a tempting-but-unsound provenance source is the right call even when the round contract named it — a misleading "barred" verdict is worse than a correctly-scoped one. The report's value is to SCOPE the remaining factorial families, so an honest contract-locked count (global ld/st=2, atom=1, local=1, red/texture/surface=0; occupancy/inst/total eligible at 18) that exactly matches the plan's named family-expansion list is more useful than an inflated count. The artifact must be deterministic (json.dumps indent=2 sort_keys=False + trailing newline, NO timestamp/git-head — mirror emit_metric_inventory) so a test byte-matches a fresh emit. The plan-token scanner walks `git ls-files` and scans `*test*.py` too (test files are IN scope unless individually exempted): forbid `AC-N` (hyphenated), Milestone/Phase/Step/`Round N` in BOTH the emitter AND the test — the bare token "ac4" (no hyphen) is fine (matches the ac4_*.json data-artifact convention), and "task6"/"task12" are allowlisted artifact names.
Validation Evidence: round-36 commit 787141d6. ac4_load_bearing_metric_coverage_sm_86.json: 19 load-bearing metrics over 18 admitted+evaluable slugs, 6 load_bearing_eligible (4 occupancy + smsp_inst + l1_total at 18 each), 7 below_slug_disjoint_threshold (global ld/st requests+sectors=2, global atom=1 [the single-slug negative-test bar], local ld/st=1), 6 typed_unavailable_no_coverage (global red + 4 surface + texture). test_ac4_load_bearing_coverage.py 22 passed (byte-match, >=3 gate, single-slug bar, label-only/unadmitted/bundle_failed exclusion, opcode decoder). tools+recovery_v2 15 failed=baseline (zero net new), 2309 passed; simbench 4 failed=baseline (zero net new), 2036 passed; plan-token scanner 0 violations on both new files (24 tests pass); static no-NCU/no-root guard 14; no .ncu-rep anywhere. Offline-verified (a deterministic analysis artifact + validating test over the committed registry/corpus/contract; no live GPU). Cross-arch sm_120 deferred (DEC-6).
Source Rounds: round-36 of the sm86_multimetric_ncu_replacement loop (2026-06-03); Codex Round-35 review mainline gap #1 (the AC-4 structural slug-disjoint coverage report).
Update Note (2026-07-06, on multi-arch fatbins the manifests are unsound even for their DESIGNED cache-op purpose): a review gate found `l1_cache_op_classifier._extract_kernel_section` concatenates the kernel's section across every fatbin arch slice (substring name match), so manifests built from multi-arch fatbins are cross-arch contaminated even for the load-only cache-op modeling they were designed for — a SECOND independent reason the round-36 manifest-drop was right. The Phase-1 label-side L1 predictor consumed these contaminated manifests (the NCU labels themselves are unaffected); classifier fix + downstream re-run tracked in the label-pipeline backlog. The ACTIVE sim classifies from the control-bits sidecar's opcode_text and consumes no manifests (`sim/mem/src/cache_op.rs` + `tools/cache_predictor/test_cache_op_rust_parity.py`).

## Lesson: shape-token-alone-mislabels-axis-flag-binaries
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-shape-token-alone-mislabels-axis-flag-binaries
Scope: every NCU label-capture driver that forwards app args to a simbench_bw per-slug binary (tools/local_labels/orchestrate_sm86_label_recapture.py::_app_args_for_cell, capture_timing_labels.py which imports it, any future single-cell recapture surface); consumers of committed labels whose capture provenance is the bare `--shape-point` era.
Problem Description: simbench_bw binaries parse `--shape-point <name>` as a LABEL-ONLY token — the launch geometry comes from explicit per-axis flags (`--warps`, `--allocation-bytes`, `--m/--n/--k`, `--carveout-percent`, ...). A capture driver that forwards only the shape token profiles the binary's COMPILED DEFAULTS and mis-attributes the resulting NCU ground truth to the canonical shape. The mislabel is invisible to the 5-tuple identity (binary_sha256 and the trace-derived kernel_signature_sha256 are geometry-independent) and passes the repeatability gate (the wrong launch is perfectly repeatable). Concrete damage found: carveout_sensitivity_probe's committed l1tex label 0.9751 was the DEFAULT 4-warp/4KiB launch (canonical 64-warp/16MiB/carveout-25 truth: 0.0014) — which manufactured a phantom "walker intra-block-reuse catastrophic miss" diagnostic (pred 0.0013 vs "truth" 0.9751) and the worst LOSO slug (0.814); tf32 (defaults m16/k32 vs canonical m32/k256), int8 (default K=64 vs 256), dram_streaming (defaults n=131072/blocks=128 vs canonical n=256/blocks=8) were also mislabeled; 11 timing labels captured through the same path had the same exposure.
Root Cause: orchestrate_sm86_label_recapture's `_app_args_for_cell` docstring ASSUMED "the binary parses shape point directly" — true for portable_lcd runners (runner_common resolves the full shape internally) but false for every axis-flag simbench binary. The matrix sweep driver (sweep_repeatability_matrix) always resolved axes from workloads/simbench_bw/shapes/<slug>.toml, so matrix-captured labels were canonical — the two capture paths silently disagreed on geometry.
Solution: (1) PRODUCER: `_app_args_for_cell` resolves canonical axes from the slug's shapes TOML via the SAME helpers the matrix driver uses (`_load_simbench_shape_axes` + `_axes_to_workload_cli_args`; fail-closed ValueError on roster-vs-TOML drift) so the two capture paths cannot diverge; regression test pins the four repaired cells' exact axis vectors. (2) DISCRIMINATOR (3-way NCU comparison, cheap and decisive): run the binary under (a) the bare shape token and (b) the canonical TOML axes, one launch each, and compare hit rates + launch geometry (grid dims in the NCU CSV) against the committed label — a committed value matching (a) is a mislabel. Audit scope = {cells captured via the bare path} ∩ {binaries with axis flags}; check binary main.cu defaults vs TOML axes to predict which cells diverge (defaults can coincidentally equal canonical for SOME axes — int8's grid matched but K differed). (3) REPAIR per cell: re-trace at the current binary where trace binaries drifted, full-matrix re-label (cache{all,none} x warmup{0,1,5} x 5, per-cell-best), targeted label-row replacement + trace-side source_commit restamps (binary-sha verified), downstream cascade (manifest identity refresh, split/seal digest re-bind, bundles, ledger, predicted artifacts, count pins). A cell with no admitting config at canonical axes is typed label_repeatability_failed and leaves the corpus (int8: 45->44, deterministic split re-deal, holdout re-sealed before any evaluation).
Constraints: the canonical axes live ONLY in the shapes TOML (the binary defaults are the correctness_00 shape, not the canonical cell); kernel_signature_sha256 is geometry-independent so identity checks can never catch this class — only the launch-geometry/value discriminator can; NCU quarantine discipline applies throughout (capture via tools/local_labels into data/local_labels on the local devices only).
Validation Evidence: Round-0 commits 4f445d2e (repair+refit) + c007d874 (recapture stamps) of the sm86_ncu_replacement_continuation loop (2026-06-04). carveout canonical truth 0.0014 vs walker 0.0013 (the phantom walker defect dissolved); carveout left the LOSO worst set (0.814 prior); LOOCV gap 0.441->0.3385, LOSO 0.814->0.7076 over the folded 40-cell corpus; L1 scalar coverage 30%->75%; tools+recovery_v2 14 failed = baseline minus the RESOLVED carveout lockstep failure (net -1), 2341 passed; simbench 4 = baseline identical; static guard 14; plan-token OK. Verified-live-sm_86 (NCU canonical-geometry re-captures + NVBit re-traces on the RTX 3090 Ti, driver 595.71.05, CUDA 13.2).
Source Rounds: round-0 of the sm86_ncu_replacement_continuation loop (2026-06-04).

## Lesson: single-output-path-producer-collision-clobbers-fitted-head
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-single-output-path-producer-collision-clobbers-fitted-head
Scope: any pair of producers that can write the same committed artifact path with different derivations — concretely `tools/local_labels/fit_monotonic_calibration_head_v5.py --output data/local_labels/sm_86_v5_head.json` (ledger-driven split-aware fit) vs `tools/local_labels/run_v5_active_path_comparison.py` (whose `--sm-86-head-out` DEFAULTS to the same path and emits a joint-head-rehydrated arch fit); the `regenerate_v5_artifacts.py` wrapper, which sequences both (fitter first, comparison after — i.e., the clobber order).
Problem Description: After the split-aware fitter produced the canonical sm_86 v5 head (admitted=40, fit_scope split-aware), running the active-path comparison runner as part of a "run all wrapper steps manually" regen recipe silently OVERWROTE the head with a stub rehydrated from the sm_120-era joint head (`calibration_head_v5.json`, 30 sm_120 cells, 0 sm_86 cells) — `admitted_count: 0`. The first downstream symptom was remote from the cause: `emit_v5_sm86_closure_artifacts` reported `accuracy.status=indeterminate_insufficient_data admitted=0` on its SECOND run after having reported `admitted=40` minutes earlier.
Root Cause: Two producers own one output path with different data lineages. The comparison runner's per-arch head emission is a DERIVED VIEW (rehydration of whatever joint head exists) but it writes to the canonical fitted-head path by default; nothing fails closed when the rehydrated cell set is empty.
Solution: (1) Do not include the comparison runner in a regen recipe unless its output paths are explicitly redirected away from the canonical fitted heads; (2) recover by re-running the ledger-driven fitter (deterministic) and `git checkout` the comparison-bundle artifact; (3) queued hardening: split the default output path (e.g., `sm_86_v5_head.from_joint_rehydration.json`) or add a fail-closed guard (refuse to write an admitted_count=0 head over an existing admitted_count>0 head). Detection heuristic: any gate emitter that flips from `admitted=N` to `indeterminate_insufficient_data` between two runs WITHOUT a head-refit in between means some intermediate step rewrote the head — diff the head file first, not the emitter.
Constraints: The wrapper `regenerate_v5_artifacts.py` ALSO invokes the fitter with no argv (glob lane, not `--admitted-bundles-from-ledger`, no `--cohort-split`) — the wrapper lane has two distinct head-integrity defects and must not be used for split-aware regens until taught the argv + step-order fix.
Validation Evidence: Round-1 regen: closure run 1 `admitted=40 max_abs_diff=0.0`; after the comparison runner, closure run 2 `admitted=0`; `git diff data/local_labels/sm_86_v5_head.json` showed 1305 deletions (cohort_scope emptied). Re-running the fitter restored `admitted: 40, split_aware: True, n_fit: 34`; closure re-run reported `admitted=40` again.
Source Rounds: round-1 of the sm86 NCU-replacement continuation loop (2026-06-04).

## Lesson: recapture-stamp-is-per-family-predictor-touching-commit-not-head
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-recapture-stamp-is-per-family-predictor-touching-commit-not-head
Scope: the BL-20260508 post-commit recapture flow whenever MULTIPLE code commits land before the artifact commit (e.g., predictor-demotion commit, witness-threading commit, audit-surface commit, then the artifact commit); the recapture scripts `recapture_l2_reduction_artifacts.py` / `recapture_l2_high_footprint_artifacts.py` and their lockstep tests `test_l2_*_post_commit_recapture.py`.
Problem Description: Running the recapture scripts after the ARTIFACT commit stamps `source_commit = HEAD` (the artifact commit), but the recapture lockstep tests compute the expected stamp as "the most recent commit touching the artifact family's producer surface" — and DIFFERENT families have DIFFERENT touch-sets: the l2_reduction family includes its shadow-report generator (changed in a later audit commit), while the high-footprint/carveout family's most recent producer-touching commit was the earlier cache_predictor commit. One HEAD-stamp cannot satisfy both.
Root Cause: The recapture script stamps a single `git rev-parse HEAD`; the tests pin per-family producer-touching commits. The flow was designed for the simple case (one fix commit -> recapture -> chore commit) where HEAD == the fix commit at stamp time.
Solution: After a multi-commit landing, stamp each family with ITS most recent producer-touching commit (read the expected value from the failing lockstep test's message, or compute `git log -1 --format=%H -- <family producer files>`), via a targeted text replace of the `source_commit` field. Verify with the family lockstep tests + the provenance-window guard (which only requires the stamp to be resolvable and to contain the split-aware fitter commit as ancestor).
Constraints: Do not restamp artifacts whose content derivation actually depends on the LAST commit (e.g., a generator-constant change) with an EARLIER commit — the stamp must name a commit whose tree could produce the committed bytes. The recapture scripts also re-serialize (sort_keys) — verify content-equality minus the stamp before committing (parse both sides and compare dicts).
Validation Evidence: Round-1: recapture stamped HEAD=040a2bcd; `test_l2_high_footprint_post_commit_recapture` demanded e56cd825 (cache_predictor commit) while `test_l2_reduction_post_commit_recapture` demanded 999044fa (its generator's constant commit). Per-family restamps turned the full recovery-v2 block green (596 passed).
Source Rounds: round-1 of the sm86 NCU-replacement continuation loop (2026-06-04).

## Lesson: taskstopped-bg-shell-wedges-rlcr-stop-hook-via-wrong-tasks-dir
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-taskstopped-bg-shell-wedges-rlcr-stop-hook-via-wrong-tasks-dir
Scope: any RLCR loop session that launches background Bash/Agent tasks and stops one via TaskStop (no completion notification is ever written to the transcript); the humanize stop hook's `loop-bg-tasks.sh` (`list_pending_background_task_ids` -> `prune_dead_bg_task_ids` -> `is_bg_task_alive` -> `derive_tasks_dir_from_transcript`).
Problem Description: After TaskStop on a background pytest shell, the stop hook reported "1 background task(s) still running - loop has NOT terminated and will resume on completion" on EVERY subsequent stop, parking the loop indefinitely (observed: ~4.6 h idle) and never reaching the Codex review. TaskList was empty and zero processes were alive — the phantom existed only in the hook's transcript arithmetic.
Root Cause: Two stacked defects. (1) TaskStop produces no `task_notification` record, so the stopped task ID stays in the hook's launched-minus-completed set forever. (2) The liveness probe designed to prune exactly this case fails open: `derive_tasks_dir_from_transcript` builds `/tmp/claude-$uid/$slug/$sid/tasks` from the TRANSCRIPT basename, but the harness writes task outputs under a DIFFERENT run UUID (`/tmp/claude-$uid/$slug/<run-uuid>/tasks`), so the probe never finds the output file and "absent -> fail open (treat as still running)".
Solution: Non-invasive unwedge that lets the hook's own machinery observe the truth: copy the genuine `<task-id>.output` from the real run-UUID tasks dir into the transcript-derived dir the probe checks (`mkdir -p` + `cp`). lsof then reports zero open FDs -> the probe prunes the dead ID -> pending set empties -> the hook's same-session stale-marker cleanup removes `bg-pending.marker` itself and the normal gate flow (Codex review) resumes. Do NOT delete `bg-pending.marker` by hand (loop-state edit, forbidden) and do NOT run cancel-rlcr-loop. Verify before yielding: source the lib and check `count_pending_background_tasks "$transcript" ""` returns 0; verify the lsof binary's exit semantics both ways (0=open, 1=closed) first.
Constraints: Prevention inside a loop: avoid TaskStop on harness-tracked background shells — let them run to completion (the completion notification keeps the hook's books balanced). Real fix belongs in the hook: derive the tasks dir by globbing `/tmp/claude-$uid/$slug/*/tasks/<task-id>.output` instead of assuming run-uuid == transcript sid, and/or fail-closed differently for IDs older than N hours. The hook code lives outside this repo (~/humanize) — report upstream rather than patching mid-loop.
Validation Evidence: Reproduced the hook arithmetic from the transcript: launched={b2d49yz2c,b8nlt69bc,biaa9wne5,bigjxzj6u,bslkejlft}, legacy-completed=all except b8nlt69bc (the TaskStop'd shell). `is_bg_task_alive` fail-open confirmed (derived dir absent). After the copy: `pending-after-prune: []`, `count: 0`.
Source Rounds: round-1 -> round-2 transition of the sm86 NCU-replacement continuation loop (2026-06-04).

## Lesson: mutually-restamping-provenance-windows-cannot-both-close
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-mutually-restamping-provenance-windows-cannot-both-close
Scope: any pair of provenance contracts where (A) a drift scanner requires file X's recorded source_commit to sit in a {HEAD, HEAD^} accept window (repair flow: restamp X, land as the round's final commit) and (B) a lockstep test derives an expected fix-commit via `git log -1 -- <touch-set>` with X in the touch-set and requires other artifacts' stamps to EQUAL that expected commit. Concretely: tools/local_labels/test_post_commit_source_commit_drift.py (label_admitted.json window) vs tools/sm_86_predictor_accuracy_recovery_v2/test_l2_*_post_commit_recapture.py (label_admitted.json formerly in _PREDICTOR_TOUCHING_PATHS).
Problem Description: Closing the drift scanner re-opened the recapture lockstep and vice versa, indefinitely: the label-restamp commit moved the recapture tests' expected fix-commit past the artifact stamps; restamping the artifacts to the new expectation required ANOTHER commit, which moved the drift window past the label stamps. Three commit orderings were tried (labels-then-chore, chore-then-labels, single combined commit) — all fail.
Root Cause: A commit's sha is unknowable at stamp time for content carried IN that commit. If the final label-bearing commit is in the lockstep's touch-set, the lockstep expectation becomes that commit while every artifact stamp predates it; if any restamp commit follows, the depth-2 drift window {HEAD, HEAD^} excludes the label stamps. The two contracts are unsatisfiable simultaneously BY CONSTRUCTION — not by operator error.
Solution: Remove the mutually-restamped file from the lockstep's touch-set derivation, with a rationale comment, and restore the per-family code-commit stamps. Coverage is preserved compositionally: content drift through the label cascade is caught by the fresh-recomputation parity tests (generators recompute from CURRENT inputs vs committed bytes), label-vs-artifact identity by ledger-identity + drift scanners, staleness by the ancestor-window provenance guard. Then close the round label-commit-LAST: refresh at HEAD=P, commit -> stamps = P = HEAD^ of the closing state. Any post-label commit (even a 1-line pin fix) re-opens the drift window — re-run the refresh cycle and land it again as the new final commit (cheap: helper + targeted ledger/selected propagation + deterministic predicted_l1 regen).
Constraints: Do NOT widen the drift window or weaken the lockstep equality instead — both windows' tightness is the point. The general design rule: a file policed by a {HEAD, HEAD^}-window scanner must not appear in any other contract's `git log`-derived expectation set.
Validation Evidence: Round-3 of the 2026-06-04 loop: before the fix, three orderings each left one family red (drift 3-red/recapture green -> recapture 6-red/drift green -> drift 2-red again). After dropping the label arm + restoring per-family stamps + label-commit-last: all of drift (3), recapture (6+2 params), frozen guard, ledger identity, task12, predicted_l1 binding green in one battery (358 passed); full tools sweep residual = the 12 unrelated task10-debt scanners. Round-5 reinforcement: the Round-4 fix commit (producer+test only) landed without a closing label refresh and the next Codex review flagged the stamps at HEAD~2 — exactly the lesson's any-post-label-commit clause; the refresh cycle re-ran label-commit-last and the battery now runs POST-commit at the closing HEAD (177 passed), codified as the standing round-close protocol in the round contracts.
Source Rounds: round-3 (review phase) of the sm86 NCU-replacement continuation loop.

## Lesson: sweep-interpreter-must-carry-sklearn-or-baseline-inflates
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-sweep-interpreter-must-carry-sklearn-or-baseline-inflates
Scope: any session on the 3090 Ti host that runs the canonical host-only sweep (`python -m pytest tools/ workloads/simbench_bw/tests/ -q`) or any v5/L1 fitter/producer; interpreter selection between `/usr/bin/python3` (3.14, no pytest/jsonschema/sklearn), the uv-tool pytest python (`~/.local/share/uv/tools/pytest/bin/python3`, 3.13, jsonschema+protobuf but NO sklearn/numpy), and `/home/jakeshea/miniforge3/bin/python3` (3.12, sklearn 1.8.0 + numpy 2.4.4 + pytest 9.0.2 + jsonschema + protobuf).
Problem Description: Running the full sweep under the uv pytest interpreter reported 21 failures — 10 more than the documented residual — with the extras all in test_monotonic_calibration_head.py and test_sm86_v5_head_standalone.py. They look like genuine regressions but are ModuleNotFoundError('sklearn') cascades: environment-induced, not code defects. A summary or zero-net-new reconciliation built on that baseline would be wrong in both directions (false regressions now; masked real regressions later if treated as residual).
Root Cause: The host carries three plausible Pythons; only miniforge3 matches the committed head's recorded dependency set (sm_86_v5_head.json dependency_versions.sklearn == "1.8.0"). Bare `pytest` resolves to the sklearn-less uv tool env; `python -m pytest` on system python has no pytest at all. Side conditions: the dependency-gated validation inside refresh_admitted_label_source_commit silently SKIPS schema validation when jsonschema is absent, so interpreter choice also flips whether label-file schema validation runs at all.
Solution: Always run sweeps, batteries, producers, and refresh helpers with `/home/jakeshea/miniforge3/bin/python3` from the repo root (the `-m pytest` form keeps CWD on sys.path for `from tools...` imports). Cross-check any surprising failure list against the artifact-recorded dependency_versions before treating it as regression. The committed-head dependency block is the canonical witness for which env produced the artifacts.
Constraints: HANDOFF.md's "no conda/mamba/miniforge" discipline applies to the CUDA host-compiler toolchain (g++-14 for nvcc), NOT to the Python test/producer env — do not "fix" the env by uninstalling miniforge. DEC-6 (no new Python dependencies) means do not pip-install sklearn into the other interpreters either; select the right interpreter instead.
Validation Evidence: Round-0 of the 2026-06-04_16-46-11 loop: uv-python sweep = 21 failed/5813 passed; miniforge sweep at the same tree = 11 failed/5849 passed/19 skipped, where the 11 are exactly the documented residual (nested-provenance x3, sidecar-schema x2, ledger digests, simbench ac5 pair) + the round-start label drift x3 repaired by the closing refresh. The 10-failure delta was pure sklearn absence.
Source Rounds: round-0 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop (2026-06-04).

## Lesson: fail-closed-guards-must-audit-values-not-markers
Lesson ID: BL-20260604-fail-closed-guards-must-audit-values-not-markers
Scope: any fail-closed admission guard protecting a deployable/scored channel from a demoted/diagnostic channel that lives in the SAME artifact — concretely `workloads/simbench_bw/timing_accuracy_runner.py::assert_prediction_bundle_deployable_channel` guarding the deployable ridge channel against the demoted same-slug scaled-neighbor diagnostic; generally any guard whose rejection criteria are metadata (model_version strings, channel declarations, marker fields) while the contraband is a NUMBER that can be copied without its metadata. ALSO: any card/config resolver whose dispatch branches on a kind/status MARKER (`kind == "value"`) and then coerces the payload with bare `int()`/`float()` — the coercion silently launders fractional, zero, negative, bool, and string payloads into "card-sourced" geometry constants; every value branch needs a payload-domain audit (integrality, positivity, finiteness, type, cross-field ordering like hit < miss) beside the marker check.
Problem Description: The first guard implementation rejected demoted model versions, missing channel declarations, missing limitation blocks, diagnostic channels posing as deployable, and same-slug marker fields inside the deployable prediction. Codex review demonstrated a clean bypass: copy every cell's `diagnostics.same_slug_scaled_neighbor.predicted_cycles` into `prediction.predicted_cycles` (adjusting `residual_cycles`), strip nothing — no marker fields are introduced, every metadata check passes — and the retired leaky publication-pass headline (geomean 0.0559) regenerates through the v4 deployable channel, cascading closure to research_success.
Root Cause: Marker fields and declarations travel WITH the value only when the producer writes them; an adversarial or buggy intermediate can move the value alone. A guard that audits provenance metadata but never compares the protected quantity against the demoted quantity verifies the label on the bottle, not the contents.
Solution: When the demoted values are present in the same artifact (which the demotion pattern requires for diagnostic preservation), the guard MUST do a value-level comparison, and the forbidden band MUST be a SUBSTANTIVE proximity band, not an exact-copy epsilon. The round-2 fix used rel 1e-6 ("catches copies including float round-trips") and was itself bypassed in review by epsilon perturbation: diagnostic x1.0001 (even x1.01) passed the 1e-6 band and regenerated the retired publication pass with the honest stored error field untouched — an epsilon-perturbed copy is still a same-slug-derived headline. Correct calibration: measure the honest artifact's MINIMUM deployable-vs-diagnostic relative gap (here ~2.417e-2) and set a fixed band no larger than HALF that gap (here 1e-2), so everything tighter than the band is rejected while honest predictions keep >= 2x headroom. The value arm must be load-bearing and independent of any stored error field; keep the error-equality arm (~1e-12) only as a supplementary catch for forged stored errors outside the band. Re-run the same full audit at every downstream consumer that could independently reach a terminal state (closure re-audits the predictions bundle), so a forged intermediate report cannot bridge the gap. Negative tests must reproduce the EXACT bypasses (value copy AND parametrized epsilon perturbations, no markers), and the positive pin must COMPUTE the honest minimum gap and assert the headroom factor, so a future tolerance or artifact change cannot silently eat the separation.
Constraints: Tolerances must be derived from the honest artifact's measured minimum gaps, not guessed — and sized to the THREAT (disguised derivation), not to float noise: a tolerance that only catches byte-identical copies protects nothing. Too loose false-positives legitimate near-misses, so pin the measured gap-to-band headroom in a test. The required metadata (demoted_channels declaration, per-cell diagnostics block, diagnostic channel marker) becomes part of the guard's own preconditions, so a bundle cannot dodge the value audit by omitting the diagnostic data the audit compares against.
Validation Evidence: Round-2 (commit 34e75d15): exact-copy bypass rejected at runner + closure layers. Round-2 review: epsilon bypass (x1.00001/x1.0001/x1.001/x1.01) demonstrated against the 1e-6 band — all four accepted with publication_pass, closure research_success with a forged report. Round-3 (commit 69e29eec): band widened to 1e-2 (half the measured 2.417e-2 min gap); all four epsilon factors rejected typed at the runner, closure twin rejected with the forged passing report, committed honest bundle passes, headroom pinned by test (min gap > 2x band); timing suites 90 passed. Resolver recurrence (walker geometry round, commit 1aa9906f): a pre-commit adversarial lens probed the P-chase card resolvers and showed `resolve_mshr_count` accepting 31.7/0/-5/True/"48" and `resolve_latencies` accepting negative and inverted latencies as source=card (marker-only audit); fixed with `_card_int`/`_card_positive_float` payload audits + hit<miss ordering + kind=value-without-value typed rejection, each pinned by adversarial test arms.
Source Rounds: round-1 (marker-only guard) -> round-1 review (copy bypass) -> round-2 (value audit, exact-copy band) -> round-2 review (epsilon bypass) -> round-3 (substantive band) of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop.

Lesson ID: BL-20260604-readmission-respects-canonical-harness-verdict
Scope: any governed re-admission of label cells whose committed binary pins went stale (rebuilt binaries on disk, original binaries unrecoverable); any admission decision where independent capture campaigns disagree about a cell's repeatability gate outcome.
Problem Description: A stale-binary cell re-captured for re-admission can produce campaign-dependent gate outcomes: the same cell+config passed the L2 stddev gate in two campaigns (0.0004, 0.0092) and failed in two others (0.0316, and a matrix-harness fail) at the SAME rebuilt binary. Transplanting the row from a passing campaign while discarding the failing campaigns admits a label whose trustworthiness is campaign luck; downstream contracts (the launch-provenance config-selection contract) then demand config-selection evidence that the canonical harness refuses to produce (no_admitting_config_found).
Root Cause: The single-campaign aggregator gate measures within-campaign dispersion only; a cell can be within-campaign stable but across-campaign bimodal (cache-state sensitivity, boundary-of-gate variance). The admission decision authority for marginal cells is the config-selection harness (sweep_repeatability_matrix), which the one-shot recapture flow (orchestrate -> aggregator) bypasses.
Solution: For governed re-admissions: (1) recapture via the established pipeline and transplant ONLY whole aggregator-produced rows (no hand-edited pins) — the transplant pattern preserves every other committed row byte-for-byte and keeps the count splice additive; (2) when a cell's campaigns disagree, run the canonical config-selection harness and treat ITS verdict as the admission authority — a no_admitting_config_found verdict withdraws the cell even if individual campaigns passed; (3) commit the LOSING evidence with the decision (per-campaign stddevs + the harness sweep dir) in a re-admission manifest artifact, and make the manifest the data-driven authority for every consumer that must tolerate the re-admission (count-accounting allowlist, frozen-era value cross-check era-scope, fitter-rerun expected-cohort era-scope) — no hardcoded test holes; (4) cells that cannot re-admit stay at their historical identity (their old labels remain valid labels OF the old binary) with capture-evidence-typed exclusions, NOT demoted, when demotion would break sealed-cohort floors or re-roll seal membership.
Constraints: Era-scoped consumers must remain load-bearing for all non-re-admitted cells (enforce the original floors); the manifest entries must carry machine-checkable evidence (failing stddevs above the gate threshold, or the harness verdict) so the allowlist validator can reject evidence-free entries; split/seal regeneration is legal only while the holdout-touch ledger records zero touches, and membership stability must be PROVEN (manifest-to-manifest assignment diff == 0), not assumed.
Validation Evidence: Round-6 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop (commits 5cecf338, e80a31c2, a8dde648): 5 cells re-admitted at fresh pins; softmax withdrawn on the matrix verdict (committed sweep dir sweep_20260604T135257Z_d04ce439b643); bf16/fp16 typed with two-campaign failing evidence; count coverage 615 entries on 41/44; final guarded sweep zero net-new (11 == residual 8 + drift 3); POST-commit battery 343 passed / 0 failed.
Source Rounds: round-6 (2026-06-04).

Lesson ID: BL-20260604-semantic-discovery-needs-candidate-separation-and-scope-probes
Scope: any live discovery harness that pins hardware-counter semantics by matching observed counts against candidate designed-count models (the observation-contract microfixture pattern); future per-arch contract re-discovery (sm_120 leg) and any extension fixtures for new op classes.
Problem Description: The first discovery sweep pinned only 1/15 metrics despite every fixture capturing clean integer-stable counts that, on inspection, matched obvious semantics exactly. Three distinct defects: (1) zero-designed fixtures (cross-pipe contamination gates) were intersected with semantic candidates — their model name never appears in a semantic fixture's candidate set, so the global intersection was ALWAYS empty; (2) measured reality defied every candidate on one dimension (misaligned warp loads: naive touched-sector arithmetic designs 80, the live counter reports 69) and the engine could only express that as a whole-metric mismatch, poisoning a metric whose aligned behavior was perfectly pinned; (3) real semantics were missing from the candidate set entirely (the divergent-path launches execute one EXTRA warp instruction vs the uniform path: deltas (-16, 0); tex/surface pipes form FOUR wavefronts per warp instruction: 64 for 16 warps).
Root Cause: A discovery engine conflates three different evidence roles — semantic constraints (non-zero designed counts that select among candidate models), contamination gates (zero expectations that validate fixture isolation), and scope probes (measurements that test where a pinned model's arithmetic remains valid). Folding all three into one intersection makes the engine fail closed on everything; and a candidate set authored purely from first-principles expectations cannot contain semantics only the hardware knows.
Solution: (1) Partition fixture evidence by role: semantic candidates intersect ONLY across non-zero-designed fixtures; zero-designed fixtures pass/fail independently as contamination gates (any non-zero observation in an unexercised pipe fails the metric); scope probes never join the intersection — an anomalous probe attaches a fail-closed SCOPE RESTRICTION to the pinned semantic (e.g. aligned_128B_warp_footprints_only) that consumers must honor or reject the cell. (2) Treat the first sweep as a hypothesis-generation pass: when observed values reject all candidates but reveal a consistent pattern, NAME the discovered model (issued_warp_instructions_with_divergence_reconvergence, four_wavefronts_per_warp_inst), add it to the candidate set, and re-run the FULL live sweep so the final artifact's evidence is produced end-to-end under the final engine. (3) Keep runtime-parameterized probe arms (same SASS, different W) so the discriminating deltas are code-generation-clean, and keep SASS witnesses (cuobjdump) for op-class attribution fixtures (ATOM-vs-RED) so the fixture proves it exercises what it claims.
Constraints: The re-run-after-extending-candidates step is mandatory — pinning from the first sweep's data under a retrofitted model without a fresh end-to-end run would make the artifact's provenance partially hand-derived. Candidate extensions must be NAMED semantic models justified by the measured pattern, never per-fixture fudge values; an anomaly that fits no nameable model stays a scope restriction (fail-closed), exactly like the misaligned-sector case.
Validation Evidence: Round-8 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop: first sweep 1/15 pinned; after the role partition + two hardware-discovered models + the misalignment scope probe, the final live sweep pinned 13/15 with 0 mismatch / 0 underdetermined / 2 typed no_expressible_fixture, all contamination gates clean, rollup residual zero on all 15 fixtures (commits 18128893 + e24cc85a).
Source Rounds: round-8 (2026-06-04).

Lesson ID: BL-20260605-exact-disagreement-signatures-localize-semantic-gaps
Scope: any deterministic reconstruction validated against integer ground-truth counters (the replay-count producer vs NCU labels; future T2 traffic reconstruction; the sm_120 leg); more broadly, any producer whose output should equal a measured integer quantity exactly.
Problem Description: The first corpus pass of the replay-count producer agreed exactly on 300/314 label-bearing pairs but disagreed on 14, spread over 3 cells. Treating the misses as generic "model error" (tolerances, calibration) would have buried three distinct, fully-determinable semantic gaps.
Root Cause: Deterministic integer reconstructions do not have noise; every disagreement is a missing or wrong SEMANTIC, and its arithmetic structure identifies which one. The 14 misses decomposed into: produced == label x 5 on every metric of one cell (the trace pools 5 launches; the NCU label profiles launch 0 only -> scope mismatch); produced == label - 64 where 64 == the cell's wide-local-access count (a per-32b-word request split the 32b-only fixture could not see); produced == 0 with label == 128 == the cell's LDGSTS count (NCU attributes async-copy loads to the global-ld pipe; the wire routes them to the exclusion sidecar).
Solution: Diagnose by signature before touching code: (1) exact integer MULTIPLE across all metrics of a cell -> scope mismatch (launch/grid/repeat pooling) — fix the producer's scope, never rescale; (2) delta equal to a countable trace subset -> a per-width/per-kind split semantic — extend the OBSERVATION CONTRACT with discriminating fixtures and re-discover live (per the candidate-separation lesson), then implement the pinned semantic; (3) produced zero with non-zero label -> attribution/routing gap — if unfixtured, emit a typed fail-closed reason (async_copy_unfixtured), never guess. After each fix re-run the FULL corpus pass; the end state must be exact-or-typed (312/312 exact + enumerated typed budget), not approximately-right.
Constraints: This discipline only works while the reconstruction stays deterministic and integer-valued; the moment a tolerance is introduced, signatures blur and gaps hide. Cross-source identity must stay closed (the 12 kernel-signature-mismatch cells were surfaced as typed rows, not force-joined, exactly because a weak join would have polluted the signature analysis with wrong-binary traces).
Validation Evidence: Round-9 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop (commits 9fe6317c, badcee98): three signatures -> three named fixes (launch scoping; per_32b_word_split pinned by 2 new live fixtures; async_copy_unfixtured typed gate); final corpus pass 312/312 exact with zero disagreements; 35 synthetic-fixture tests pin all arms.
Source Rounds: round-9 (2026-06-04/05).

Lesson ID: BL-20260605-one-touch-governance-outranks-artifact-regeneration-patterns
Scope: any artifact whose GENERATION consumes a governance budget (sealed-holdout one-touch scoring, future one-shot evaluation panels, any digest-keyed consume-once permission); interaction with the standing two-commit landing pattern (producer commit first, artifact regenerated at that HEAD).
Problem Description: The standing two-commit landing pattern regenerates every artifact at the producer-commit HEAD so source_commit pins a tree where the producer code exists. The lockstep verdict CANNOT follow it: its generation scores the sealed holdout, which the digest-keyed ledger permits exactly once per (seal, scope). Re-running at the producer HEAD would either raise HoldoutTouchRepeatError (correct, blocking the landing) or require resetting/duplicating the ledger entry (a governance violation disguised as provenance hygiene).
Root Cause: Two valuable invariants collide: reproducibility provenance (artifact regenerated at the code's HEAD) and consume-once governance (the budget is spent by the FIRST run). They cannot both hold for the same artifact.
Solution: Governance outranks the regeneration pattern. The governance-consuming artifact lands AS-GENERATED, with: (1) its source_commit recording the INPUT-STATE HEAD (every input artifact committed at that commit — schedule the run against a clean committed tree, e.g. right after the prior round's close); (2) the consuming run recorded in the governance ledger with the artifact path, config digest, and scoring HEAD; (3) the round summary explicitly noting the deviation so reviewers do not flag the source_commit mismatch as drift. Tests must not assume artifact.source_commit == current HEAD for such artifacts; lifecycle tests that pinned the pre-consumption state (the empty-seed ledger test) move to pinning the post-consumption invariant (exactly one touch, digest- and config-bound). GATE ORDERING: the legality checks (touch-repeat AND retune-after-touch) must run BEFORE the consuming computation — before the sealed labels are joined, aggregated, or gap-computed — not merely before the ledger WRITE. A guard placed only at the record/write site still lets an illegal rerun consume the sealed data in memory and then reject the bookkeeping; the reviewer-grade witness is a call-order probe (spy on the join function, assert the sealed-split join never fires for an illegal run), committed as regression arms for both the repeat-touch and the retuned-config cases.
Constraints: The input-state HEAD must genuinely contain every input (run the consuming step BEFORE landing new producer commits, or accept that the artifact references the pre-work tree). If the artifact must be regenerated (a real defect found), the legal path is a fresh re-seal (new digest = new budget), never ledger editing.
Validation Evidence: Round-10 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop: the verdict artifact landed as-generated (source_commit = the round-entry close HEAD, all inputs committed there); the committed ledger carries exactly one touch bound to the seal + frozen-config digests; the second-touch and retune-after-touch negative arms are tested against the real config; the ledger seed test was moved to the post-touch lifecycle in the same round (commits 7184a811, fec00b8a). Gate-ordering recurrence (L1 re-score round, commit a2532030): the re-score checked legality only at record_touch time; the reviewer's call-order probe recorded ['calibration_validation', 'holdout', 'calibration_validation'] before HoldoutTouchRepeatError — the sealed holdout was scored in memory by an illegal rerun. Fixed by moving load_ledger + assert_retune_legal + assert_touch_legal ahead of the holdout join; the probe now records ['calibration_validation'] before the raise, pinned by spy-based regression arms.
Source Rounds: round-10 (2026-06-05) -> round-20 review (call-order probe) -> round-21 (pre-join gates) of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop.

Lesson ID: BL-20260605-property-outcomes-must-be-cross-run-stable-or-typed
Scope: any live-derived artifact whose properties are DECIDED by a detection rule over noisy measurements (latency-step capacity detection, throughput-knee resource counts, policy classification from hit-fraction evidence); the per-arch static measurement substrate and its future sm_120 leg.
Problem Description: The outstanding-miss probe's knee rule ("first k where the next step improves per-load throughput by < 10%") produced DIFFERENT property outcomes on two identical live sweeps minutes apart: one run typed the property geometry_unresolved, the next pinned value=2 — because a single DVFS wiggle at low k crossed the 10% threshold once and the rule triggered on first crossing, while the curve kept improving at higher k.
Root Cause: First-crossing triggers convert measurement noise into decision-rule instability: near-threshold ratios flip sides run to run, and the rule amplifies one noisy interval into a categorical outcome change. The property then depends on WHICH run landed, which is indistinguishable from fabrication.
Solution: (1) Design detection rules for OUTCOME stability: require sustained conditions (every subsequent step below the threshold; cumulative jumps with dominance over all outside jumps; transition-run merging with bracket reporting) rather than first-crossing triggers. (2) Land any new live-derived artifact with a CROSS-RUN WITNESS: run the full sweep twice independently and assert every property's kind AND value agree (within a tight tolerance for continuous values); a property that flips is re-ruled or typed, never committed on the luckier run. (3) When a probe structurally cannot decide (single-thread MSHR overlap continuing through the probed range), the honest outcome is typed with the curve as lower-bound evidence — the consumer treats unresolved geometry fail-closed.
Constraints: The cross-run witness doubles live sweep cost — acceptable for cards regenerated rarely; cache-resident cycle measurements on this device are bit-deterministic so the witness is mostly free there, and the instability concentrates in ratio-threshold rules over DRAM-latency curves. Stability tolerances must be declared in the analysis config (part of the artifact), not chosen post hoc.
Validation Evidence: Round-12 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop: the first-wiggle rule flipped (typed vs value=2) between identical runs; after the sustained-saturation re-rule the double-sweep witness reported zero property-outcome diffs (commits 1b438ba9, 8bee4f9d); the committed card's brackets + typed records derive from declared confidence rules in analysis_config.
Source Rounds: round-12 (2026-06-05).

## Lesson: lazy-event-batches-replay-in-event-time-order
Lesson ID: BL-20260605-lazy-event-batches-replay-in-event-time-order
Scope: any lazily-applied deferred-event mechanism where multiple pending events of DIFFERENT types accumulate between processing opportunities and each event's effect depends on the state AT ITS OWN TIMESTAMP — concretely the warp-pool walker's `_apply_fills` (line-allocation fills vs resident-line sector fills, applied lazily at the next access); generally any event-driven model where the poll points are driven by the workload (accesses) rather than by the events themselves.
Problem Description: The batched applier processed all due events of type A (line fills) before all due events of type B (sector fills) regardless of timestamps. A stale sector fill (its line evicted before its fill time; the documented semantics say it drops) survived until a LATER re-allocation fill of the same line was also pending; the type-batched order applied the re-allocation first, so the stale fill's "is my line resident?" drop-check passed against the WRONG (future) state and the sector resurrected into the new line — a false hit. The bug is invisible under dense polling: if any access lands between the two due times, the earlier event is applied (and correctly dropped) before the later one exists in the same batch.
Root Cause: Type-batched application is a correctness shortcut that holds only when callers poll between every pair of due timestamps. Each deferred event's effect must be decided by the state at its OWN time; processing by type evaluates the guard predicates of earlier events against later events' state. The Round-18 verification stress missed it because randomized streams poll densely — the empirically-observed invariant ("the orphan fill's due time always precedes the re-allocation fill's") was REAL, but the code's type-order processing inverted exactly that ordering inside a single sparse-schedule batch.
Solution: (1) Replay due deferred events in CHRONOLOGICAL order (stable sort by (timestamp, type-priority)), using type priority ONLY to break exact timestamp ties, and preserving issue order within a (timestamp, type) class so dense-schedule behavior is byte-identical to the previous applier. (2) Verification for lazy-event mechanisms MUST include SPARSE schedules: streams where warps retire early (the retire path applies no events) so that two-plus due events of different types accumulate before one late poll. (3) The strongest witness is an independent from-scratch reference simulator driven by a single global event queue (no lazy application at all), differential-fuzzed against the production model — and proven independent by checking it DISAGREES with the known-buggy version.
Constraints: Chronological replay inside one poll is O(k log k) over due events — negligible at MSHR-bounded k. The tie-break (type priority at equal timestamps) is part of the documented contract, not an implementation detail: state it in the docstring and pin it with an equal-timestamp fixture. Empirical invariants observed under stress (orderings, inequalities) justify NOTHING about processing order unless the stress includes the sparse schedules that decouple due times from poll times.
Validation Evidence: Round-19 of the SOTA-grounded non-root perf-model loop (commit 9fa7fcff): the reviewer's three-warp sparse reproducer flipped from hit=2/miss=8/sector_resident=1 (false hit via resurrection) to the chronological hit=1/miss=9/sector_resident=2; an independent global-event-time reference simulator matched the fixed walker over ~11,000 sparsity-designed fuzz seeds (2,394 triggering evict-then-realloc races) with 0 divergences while DISAGREEING with the pre-fix walker on the reproducer; all 76 pre-existing multi-fill applications were shown order-unchanged (the only divergent call is the new regression fixture itself).
Source Rounds: round-18 (type-batched applier + dense-schedule stress that missed it) -> round-18 review (sparse-schedule reproducer) -> round-19 (chronological replay + sparse fuzz + independent reference oracle).

## Lesson: rate-metric-scoring-requires-unit-and-stream-alignment
Lesson ID: BL-20260707-rate-metric-scoring-requires-unit-and-stream-alignment
Scope: any scoring of a rate/ratio metric (cache hit rates, utilization fractions) against the frozen-bundle labels; specifically every label field whose name ends in `_pct`; any future L1/L2 accuracy gate that composes a sim-side rate with a label-side rate.
Problem Description: A 6.85pp "L1 MAE" was manufactured out of nothing by comparing the sim's eligible-load PERCENT against the UNIFIED sector label — two independent mismatches stacked: unit convention (percent vs fraction) and stream composition (eligible-load accesses vs unified sector accesses). Scored against the op_ld label with units aligned, the same sim is exact.
Root Cause: Frozen-bundle hit-rate labels are FRACTIONS (registry parser_transform `percent_to_fraction`, valid range 0–1) even though the field names end in `_pct` and NCU renders 0–100 — the field-name suffix lies about the stored unit. And a rate is a composition of a numerator stream over a denominator stream: two rates with the same name are only comparable when both sides count the SAME accesses.
Solution: Before scoring any rate metric, confirm BOTH (1) the unit convention on each side — read the registry parser_transform, never trust the field-name suffix — and (2) that the sim stream and the label stream count the same accesses (eligible-load vs unified-sector vs op_ld are different populations). Diagnostic signature for the unit half: a ÷100 pattern across cells (50 vs 0.5, 25 vs 0.25, 33.33 vs 0.3333) is a unit mismatch, not model error — check for it before touching the model.
Constraints: The frozen-bundle convention (fractions 0–1 behind `_pct` names) is sealed and cannot be renamed; every new consumer inherits the trap. Alignment must be done at the scoring boundary, never by rescaling committed labels or sim outputs in place.
Validation Evidence: Scored against the op_ld label with units aligned the sim is exact — MAE 0.0003pp, 10/10 cells — versus the manufactured 6.85pp against the unified sector label at mismatched units.
Source Rounds: shared-L2 event-integration review gate (2026-07-07).

## Lesson: event-loop-liveness-guard-and-self-affecting-event-horizon
Lesson ID: BL-20260707-event-loop-liveness-guard-and-self-affecting-event-horizon
Scope: any event-driven co-simulation loop with resumable actors (the sim-engine SmWake/L2Access/MemResponse loop in `sim/sm/src/system.rs`); generally any actor run-ahead bound over a queue where an actor's own emitted events later change its state.
Problem Description: Two distinct failures. (i) Liveness: plain `cargo test` has no timeout — pre-fix binaries spun at 300% CPU for 1.5h on a no-progress wake cycle before anyone noticed. (ii) Causality: the per-SM run-ahead bound covered a request only while the emitting call ran; in the gap between emission and L2 pricing (a stale wake landing in that window) the SM ran past its own response and read stale cache state — the bug survived unit tests and was caught only by an event-vs-constant equivalence differential.
Root Cause: (i) An event loop whose progress condition is implicit has no typed way to distinguish "quiescent" from "spinning"; duplicate stale wakes make a naive guard false-positive (177 duplicate stale wakes at one timestamp on a real cell tripped the first guard draft). (ii) A horizon bound sourced only from already-priced responses (`response_due`) is blind to the window in which a self-affecting event EXISTS but is not yet in the bound's data source: a request is dangerous from EMISSION until its response is PRICED, not just while the emitting call runs.
Solution: (1) Land a typed no-progress guard BEFORE the loop's first long-running use: `SystemError::Livelock` SpinGuard, threshold 2×cores+8 consecutive idle wakes at a frozen clock, plus wake dedup via per-SM pending-wake heaps so duplicate same-time wakes cannot false-positive the guard. (2) For every actor run-ahead bound, enumerate every window in which a self-affecting event exists but is not yet in the bound's data source, and close each with its own lower bound — here a per-SM "unpriced request" bound alongside `response_due` covering emission-to-pricing. (3) Keep an event-vs-constant equivalence differential as the standing witness; it is the only surface that caught the causality hole.
Constraints: The livelock threshold must scale with legitimate same-time fan-out (hence 2×cores+8), and dedup must precede the guard's idle-wake count or real workloads false-positive. The unpriced bound must be maintained on the emission path itself — any later maintenance point reopens the window it exists to close.
Validation Evidence: Post-fix, the event-vs-constant equivalence differential is 1000/1000 exact after the per-SM unpriced-bound fix; the SpinGuard converts the former 1.5h 300%-CPU spin into a typed `SystemError::Livelock`; the 177-duplicate-wake cell passes with wake dedup in place.
Source Rounds: shared-L2 event-integration review gate (2026-07-07).

## Lesson: differential-fingerprints-are-only-comparable-with-their-knobs
Lesson ID: BL-20260707-differential-fingerprints-are-only-comparable-with-their-knobs
Scope: any regression comparison against a recorded differential/fuzz outcome (mismatch counts, seed lists, pass ratios) — concretely the event-vs-const evdiff regimes; generally any "the battery must reproduce N/M with identical seeds" gate.
Problem Description: The §6.4 re-proof appeared to show a regression in the MSHR-stall regime (106/500 — an unlogged first run at the binary's n=500 default — vs the logged "312/1000") and an unexplained shift in the eviction regime (988/1000 vs "992/1000"). The MSHR discrepancy was pure knob mismatch — the logged number came from an unrecorded EV_MSHR value — and cost a full baseline re-derivation to rule out a real regression. The eviction shift was real but benign: 4 new seeds differing ONLY in a NEWLY ADDED counter.
Root Cause: A differential outcome is a function of (code, knobs, seed count), but the progress log recorded only the outcome. Without the knob vector, a later run cannot distinguish "regression" from "different experiment". Separately: adding a new observable (counter field) to an equality-compared result struct can grow the mismatch set WITHOUT any behavior change — the equality predicate got strictly stronger.
Solution: (1) Record every differential fingerprint WITH its full knob vector and seed count (e.g. "cap 8, mshr 2, n=1000 → 211/1000, seeds [...]"), and quote it that way in logs and commit messages. (2) When a re-proof disagrees with a logged number, FIRST rebuild the recorded baseline at the prior commit (git worktree + repointed path deps takes minutes) and compare seed LISTS, not counts — a superset whose new members differ only in newly added fields is an observability change, not a regression; prove it by diffing per-seed counter dumps. (3) When adding fields to an equality-compared result, expect and pre-explain the mismatch-set growth in the same change.
Constraints: Worktree baselining requires all current work to be uncommitted or the prior commit to be identifiable; path-dep tools (evdiff) need their manifests repointed at the worktree. Seed lists, not ratios, are the comparable object — ratios collapse exactly the information needed.
Validation Evidence: Pre-§6.4 worktree at 086b1374 reproduced 992/1000 with seeds [196,403,452,546,676,922,991,992] (eviction) and 211/1000 identical-list (MSHR, cap 8 mshr 2 n=1000) — proving the "312/1000" log entry was a different knob vector, the MSHR regime unchanged, and the 4 new eviction seeds (302/604/712/945) differ only in the new store_tag_hits field with cycles and all prior counters equal.
Source Rounds: §6.4 per-access-class L2 hit rule increment (2026-07-07).

## Lesson: ncu-op-token-membership-is-decided-by-ground-truth-labels-not-taxonomy
Lesson ID: BL-20260708-ncu-op-token-membership-from-ground-truth-not-taxonomy
Scope: any sim-emit observation-contract decision about WHICH sector stream an NCU op-token metric counts (l1tex `op_ld`/`op_st`/`op_atom`/`op_red`/`op_ldgsts_*`); generally any time a metric's numerator/denominator membership is inferred from the metric NAME taxonomy or from web/vendor docs rather than from the frozen bundle's own captured values.
Problem Description: A design fork on "does `l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_global_op_ld` include LDGSTS (cp.async) sectors?" FOUR independent expert sources split into two camps that could not be reconciled by argument: two web-research lenses (metric-definition lens: YES by name taxonomy; hardware lens: NO — distinct `op_ldgsts_cache_access`/`op_ldgsts_cache_bypass` tokens exist, so op_ld excludes them), then a required cross-check with a fresh web subagent (REFUTED — op_ldgsts tokens present since NCU 2020.2/2021, so op_ld excludes) and Codex (CONFIRMED — op_ld includes, from the ground-truth balance + NVIDIA forum "Global Loads to Shared Store" wavefronts). The "op_ld excludes LDGSTS" camp, if followed, would have DROPPED the LDGSTS fold and mis-predicted the frozen contract.
Root Cause: The `op_ldgsts_cache_{access,bypass}` tokens genuinely EXIST in Nsight Compute (Codex confirmed them on NCU 2026.1; release notes show LDGSTS memory metrics from 2020.2) — so the taxonomy camp is not wrong about their existence. But token existence does NOT tell you whether the `op_ld` ROLLUP is a superset that also counts LDGSTS or a sibling that excludes it; that is a property of the specific captured metric, decidable only from the frozen bundle's own numbers. The taxonomy inference over-reached from "a finer token exists" to "the coarser rollup excludes it."
Solution: Decide op-token stream membership from the frozen bundle's OWN captured numbers on a kernel whose global op-mix is pinned from the raw instruction stream (not just the classifier manifest — that manifest lists only the LDGSTS load pcs and OMITS the stores, so it cannot alone prove "no ordinary loads"). Dispositive witness for `gemm_hmma_bf16_sm80`: decode the dynamic trace and classify addressed instructions by `memory_op_access_kind` — every addressed LOAD is `op_class=SHARED` (LDS, 9216 sampled), the only global-space ops are STG stores (256 sampled → scales to op_st=2048 exactly) and the address-stripped LDGSTS; there are ZERO ordinary global LDG loads. NCU nonetheless reports `op_ld` requests 16384 / sectors 196608 / hit_rate 16.25%, and total `l1tex__t_requests` 18432 = op_ld(16384)+op_st(2048) EXACTLY. With no ordinary global load in existence, the 16384 op_ld requests CAN ONLY be the LDGSTS copies ⟹ the captured `op_ld` metric counts LDGSTS. So the sim-emit `op_ld` denominator MUST fold the READ-class bypass (LDGSTS) sectors in (`bypass_sectors - bypass_atom_sectors`); numerator stays modeled eligible hits.
Constraints: The verdict is scoped to THIS frozen bundle's captured `op_ld` metric. If a re-capture (newer NCU, or a query that adds `op_ldgsts_*`) makes op_ld exclude LDGSTS, the fold flips — re-run the total-requests-balance check (`l1tex__t_requests` == Σ per-op request tokens) and the SHARED-vs-GLOBAL access-kind classification on the new trace. Determining metric semantics from captured behavior is contract discovery, NOT target-fitting (a denominator's POPULATION is identified; no free constant is chosen) — never choose a hit-FRACTION to match a label. Separately (the SCORABILITY caveat, distinct from the membership question): the fold is the right contract but is INERT on gemm/conv2d today because the tracer excludes ASYNC_COPY addresses by POLICY (`tools/nvbit_tracer/tracer_tool/tracer_tool.cu:3183` routes them to unsupported_memory_path; it already extracts both MREFs at :1397 via `nvbit_add_call_arg_mref_addr64`, so the addresses ARE obtainable — the exclusion is a flippable policy, NOT a hard NVBit limitation as first assumed). The stock accel-sim NVBit mem-trace tool DOES have a real single-memory-operand limitation on cp.async's two operands, but this project's custom tracer extended past it and then excludes by choice. So gemm op_ld stays unscorable (sim emits `na`, 0/0) until that policy is flipped + re-capture — parallel to the ATOMIC/histogram capture gap, a user-gated campaign.
Validation Evidence: `l1tex__t_requests.sum`=18432 balances exactly against op_ld(16384)+op_st(2048) with op_atom=op_red=0; dynamic-trace access-kind classification shows all addressed loads are SHARED and zero ordinary global LDG; Codex CONFIRMED Q1 from the same balance + forum wavefront guidance; the opposing web/taxonomy verdict was refuted by the captured data, not by argument. Codex also corrected the NVBit Q2 claim (policy exclusion, not NVBit limitation) by reading the actual tracer source. The user-mandated web+codex cross-verification was decisive precisely BECAUSE the two sources disagreed, forcing resolution at the primitive instruction-stream level.
Source Rounds: sim-emit contract layer increment — LDGSTS/op_ld adjudication with web+codex cross-verification (2026-07-08).
